# Pre-registered wire predictions [D]

**This document was written and committed BEFORE any USB capture existed.**
That is its entire point. A prediction made after seeing the data is not a
prediction, and CLAUDE.md §6.2 forbids a pipeline that affirms whatever it
decided. Everything below is therefore falsifiable in advance, and the honest
outcome is that a good fraction of it turns out wrong.

Provenance: generated from workflow run `wf_44543d0d-601`, 11 independent
reading agents (one per area) each followed by an independent auditor with no
knowledge of the expected verdict. Neither was told what the answer should be.
The vendor binaries were never executed (CLAUDE.md §1.5) and the two
third-party projects of §1.1 were never opened.

## The harness measured itself, and it is not clean

CLAUDE.md §6.2: *"A harness that cannot produce a bad result is not evidence...
Report its failure rate; a rate of zero is a red flag, not a pass."*


| | count |
| --- | --- |
| predictions written | **352** |
| rejected by audit | **86** (24.4%) |
| surviving as pre-registered | **266** |
| gaps the auditors flagged as missing | **57** |


Fault mix of the 86 rejections:

| fault | n | what it means |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `citation-wrong` | 33 | the cited address does not say what was claimed |
| `mistagged` | 21 | claimed *derived* or *observed* where the evidence is inference |
| `contradicts-notes` | 18 | disagrees with a committed finding without saying so |
| `not-falsifiable` | 11 | `refuted_if` names no observation a capture could make |
| `vague` | 2 | no numbers where the binary supplies numbers |
| `citation-unverifiable` | 1 | the auditor could not check it at all |

Auditors independently re-checked **149** citations against the
raw bytes; **10** did not hold. `citation-wrong` being the largest
category, and mistagging second, is the expected failure shape for LLM readers
and is exactly why §6.2 says the device question is settled mechanically and
never by anything a reader said.

**Rejected predictions are listed but are NOT part of the pre-registration.**
They are kept so the rejection rate above stays checkable, not to be scored.

## How to use this against a capture

Each prediction has a `refuted_if`. Walk the capture, mark every prediction
CONFIRMED / REFUTED / UNTESTED, and record the tally. A REFUTED prediction is a
finding and belongs in the notes with the correction. An area with no REFUTED
entries deserves suspicion, not celebration — see the fault mix above.



---

## Updater 1.10 — launch, enumeration, version decode, device-absent

*area key: `updater-detect` — 32 written, 6 rejected*


### Pre-registered (26)

#### 1. `free-run-zero-usb` — [D]

**Launching updater 1.10 with the mouse attached, waiting, and closing the window without pressing the button produces ZERO USB traffic attributable to the updater — in particular zero SET_REPORT and zero GET_REPORT control transfers to VID 0x3367.**

- **Expect:** Across the entire process lifetime: 0 URB_CONTROL_TRANSFER packets with bmRequestType 0x21 / bRequest 0x09 (SET_REPORT) and 0 with bmRequestType 0xA1 / bRequest 0x01 (GET_REPORT) addressed to the OP1, i.e. no transfer whose wValue is 0x03A0 or 0x03A1. No SET_IDLE, no SET_PROTOCOL, no GET_DESCRIPTOR, no re-enumeration of the device. Any interrupt-IN traffic present is the mouse's own HID input, generated by the Windows HID stack whether or not the updater is running.
- **Cite:** fw110: HidD_SetFeature IAT slot 0x0051b1d4 is dereferenced at exactly 2 sites in .text, both inside FUN_004012a0 (0x004012ba, 0x00401306); HidD_GetFeature slot 0x0051b1d0 at exactly 3, all inside FUN_00401330 (0x0040134e, 0x00401376, 0x004013f6). The 7 callers of 0x004012a0 (0x40190f, 0x401a60, 0x401b26, 0x401bf6, 0x4037a1, 0x403bf2, 0x403ddd) and 7 of 0x401330 all sit inside FUN_00401890/00401980/00401ad0/00401bb0/00403750/00403960, every one of which is reachable only from FUN_004033b0, whose address 0x004033b0 has ZERO rel32 references in .text and exactly one 4-byte occurrence in the whole file: .rdata 0x00544784, the pfn of the WM_COMMAND/BN_CLICKED(id 1001) entry in the dialog's AFX_MSGMAP at 0x00544740.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any SET_REPORT or GET_REPORT to VID 0x3367 appears in the capture between the updater's process start and process exit while the button was never clicked. (A control transfer issued by some other process — e.g. the Endgame Configuration Tool — does not refute this; correlate by process, and by the fact that the config tool uses input report 0x03 over ReadFile, not feature reports.)
- **Matters:** Our egg-flash must likewise emit nothing before the user commits. It also tells us we can safely open/inspect the device without side effects, and it is the baseline against which every later capture's traffic is attributed.

#### 2. `no-device-handle-at-launch` — [D]

**The updater opens no HID device handle at all until the button is pressed; it does not even call SetupDiGetClassDevsW.**

- **Expect:** A handle listing of the process (Process Explorer / handle.exe) immediately after the window appears shows zero \Device\HID... / \??\HID#VID_3367... handles. An API trace shows zero calls to HidD_GetHidGuid, SetupDiGetClassDevsW, SetupDiEnumDeviceInterfaces, SetupDiGetDeviceInterfaceDetailW or CreateFileW-on-a-HID-path before the first WM_COMMAND for control 1001.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00401000 @0x00401000 is the sole holder of every HID/SetupAPI IAT reference (HidD_GetHidGuid 0x51b1dc@0x401025, SetupDiGetClassDevsW 0x51b498@0x401034, SetupDiGetDeviceInterfaceDetailW 0x51b49c@0x40103a, SetupDiEnumDeviceInterfaces 0x51b4a4@0x401075, CreateFileW 0x51b1fc@0x401040, HidD_GetAttributes 0x51b1d8@0x4010f6, HidD_GetPreparsedData 0x51b1cc@0x40111a, HidP_GetCaps 0x51b1c8@0x401129, HidD_FreePreparsedData 0x51b1c4@0x401133, SetupDiDestroyDeviceInfoList 0x51b4a0@0x401173). Raw E8 scan of all .text: 0x00401000 has exactly 9 callers, at 0x403608, 0x403649, 0x403696, 0x4036c9 (all in FUN_00403600), 0x403805, 0x403836, 0x4038d6 (FUN_00403750), 0x403c81, 0x403c97 (FUN_00403960); and 0x00403600's sole caller is 0x00403499, inside the button handler FUN_004033b0.
- **REFUTED IF:** Process Explorer shows the updater holding any \Device\HID handle, or Process Monitor logs a CreateFile on a \\?\HID# path, at any time before the Update button is clicked.
- **Matters:** Confirms the transport can be opened lazily; and it means any HID handle seen in the capture during the free run belongs to some other process, which is how we attribute traffic.

#### 3. `no-timer-no-devicechange` — [D]

**The updater's dialog handles exactly four Windows messages and none of them is a timer or a device-arrival notification, so nothing is polled.**

- **Expect:** The dialog class's AFX_MSGMAP entry array is exactly four 24-byte records then a zero terminator: (nMessage 0x000F WM_PAINT, nSig 0x13, pfn 0x00403100), (0x0037 WM_QUERYDRAGICON, nSig 0x28, pfn 0x004031F0), (0x0111 WM_COMMAND, nCode 0, nID 0x3E9=1001, nLastID 0x3E9, nSig 0x39, pfn 0x004033B0), (0x0201 WM_LBUTTONDOWN, nSig 0x35, pfn 0x00403EE0). No WM_TIMER (0x0113), no WM_DEVICECHANGE (0x0219). RegisterDeviceNotification is not imported at all. Of the 29 SetTimer (0x0051b850) references in fw110 .text, zero fall in the vendor band [0x00401000,0x004040ad).
- **Cite:** fw110 .rdata: AFX_MSGMAP header at 0x005447b8 = {pfnGetBaseMap 0x0040d24c, lpEntries 0x00544740}; entries read as raw bytes at 0x00544740..0x0054479f, zero terminator at 0x005447a0. GetMessageMap override is code at 0x004030b0 (`movl $0x5447b8,%eax; retl`), vtable slot +0x28 at 0x00544b14. Base map getter 0x0040d24c returns 0x0051e714, MFC's own map. Exhaustive 4-byte scan of all of fw110 .rdata and .data for any value in [0x401000,0x4040ad) returns, for the dialog, only 0x00544754→0x403100, 0x0054476c→0x4031f0, 0x00544784→0x4033b0, 0x0054479c→0x403ee0 plus vtable slots 0x00544af0/0x00544b14/0x00544bec/0x00544c4c/0x00544c60.
- **REFUTED IF:** A Spy++ / message trace shows the dialog receiving and acting on WM_TIMER, or the tool visibly reacts to the mouse being unplugged and replugged while idle (e.g. the status text changes on its own).
- **Matters:** Rules out a background poll that would otherwise contaminate every capture we attribute to a deliberate action, and tells us the vendor tool has no device-presence watcher we would be expected to imitate.

#### 4. `oninitdialog-does-nothing` — [D]

**OnInitDialog sets the window icon and nothing else — it does not enumerate, does not query the device, and does not populate the status field.**

- **Expect:** FUN_004030c0 is 63 bytes and contains exactly three calls: CDialog::OnInitDialog (0x004030c4 → 0x004081aa), SendMessageW(m_hWnd, WM_SETICON=0x80, 1, m_hIcon) at 0x004030e1, SendMessageW(m_hWnd, 0x80, 0, m_hIcon) at 0x004030f5; then `movl $1,%eax; retl`. The icon comes from LoadIconW(hInst, MAKEINTRESOURCE(0x80=128)) at 0x00402fed in the constructor, i.e. GROUP_ICON resource 128 (lang 2052).
- **Cite:** fw110 0x004030c0-0x004030fe (raw objdump); constructor FUN_00402f20 @0x00402f20, LoadIconW slot 0x0051b83c at 0x00402fed; vtable slot +0x174 (VA 0x00544c60) = 0x004030c0.
- **REFUTED IF:** The status box contains any text, or the progress bar is non-zero, in a screenshot taken before the button is pressed.
- **Matters:** Establishes that the whole pre-button phase is inert, which is what makes the free run a clean baseline.

#### 5. `launch-ui-state` — [D]

**At launch the window shows: title 'Endgame Gear OP1 8k v2 Firmware Updater', a 'Status:' label, an EMPTY status box, an empty progress bar, and an enabled 'Update Firmware' button. No firmware version is displayed anywhere.**

- **Expect:** Dialog 102 is a DIALOGEX 375x100 dialog units, 8pt 'MS Shell Dlg', style 0x90CE0048, exStyle 0x00040000 (WS_EX_APPWINDOW). Controls: id 1000 msctls_progress32 at (36,35) size 294x23 style 0x50000000; id 1001 BUTTON 'Update Firmware' at (245,67) size 86x19 style 0x50010000 (plain BS_PUSHBUTTON + WS_TABSTOP, NOT a default button); id -1 STATIC 'Status:' at (36,21) size 24x8; id 1017 EDIT at (63,17) size 266x14 style 0x50010080 (ES_AUTOHSCROLL). At 96 DPI that is roughly a 562x162 px client area. The status EDIT has no initial text and nothing writes to it before the button press.
- **Cite:** fw110 RT_DIALOG name 102 lang 1033, resource RVA 0x1e7f14 size 352, parsed byte-for-byte; control ids corroborated by DoDataExchange FUN_00403060 @0x00403060 which does DDX_Control for 0x3e8=1000 (→ member +0xbc), 0x3e9=1001 (→ +0x130), 0x3f9=1017 (→ +0x1a4).
- **REFUTED IF:** The launched window shows a version number, a device name, a 'connected/not connected' indicator, a menu bar, or any control other than these four.
- **Matters:** If the vendor tool showed a version at launch it would have to read the device at launch, and our whole 'nothing happens before the button' model would be wrong. This is the cheapest visual falsifier of that model.

#### 6. `window-is-resizable-with-minimize` — [D]

**The updater window has a thick (resizable) frame and a minimize box, but no maximize box and no menu.**

- **Expect:** Dialog style 0x90CE0048 decomposes to WS_POPUP|WS_VISIBLE|WS_BORDER|WS_DLGFRAME(=WS_CAPTION)|WS_SYSMENU|WS_THICKFRAME(0x00040000)|WS_MINIMIZEBOX(0x00020000)|DS_SETFONT(0x40)|0x08. WS_MAXIMIZEBOX (0x00010000) is clear. The template's menu field is empty and RT_MENU count is 0.
- **Cite:** fw110 RT_DIALOG 102 header dwords (style 0x90CE0048, exStyle 0x00040000, menu ordinal empty); rsrc.py fw110 shows RT_MENU 0 and RT_ACCELERATOR 0.
- **REFUTED IF:** The window cannot be resized by dragging its edge, or it has a maximize button.
- **Matters:** Same calibration purpose; also, a resizable dialog with an empty AFX_DIALOG_LAYOUT resource (2 bytes, all zero) means the controls do not move when resized, which is a visible oddity worth having predicted rather than 'discovered'.

#### 7. `no-uac-prompt` — [D]

**Launching the updater raises no UAC prompt and needs no administrator rights.**

- **Expect:** The RT_MANIFEST resource (name 1, lang 1033, 607 bytes) contains exactly `<requestedExecutionLevel level="asInvoker" uiAccess="false">` and `<ms_windowsSettings:dpiAware>true</...>`. There is no `<dependency>` element, no requireAdministrator, no highestAvailable.
- **Cite:** fw110 MANIFEST(24) name 1 lang 1033 at RVA 0x1ea134, 607 bytes, read verbatim.
- **REFUTED IF:** Windows shows a UAC consent dialog when the exe is double-clicked, or Explorer shows the shield overlay on the icon.
- **Matters:** Tells us HID feature-report access to this device does not need elevation on Windows, which is the closest available evidence about whether macOS will need Input Monitoring (still untested — CLAUDE.md §5).

#### 8. `classic-unthemed-controls` — [G]

**The updater's button and progress bar render in the classic (unthemed) Windows style, because the manifest declares no dependency on Common Controls v6.**

- **Expect:** The manifest has no `<dependentAssembly>` naming Microsoft.Windows.Common-Controls version 6.0.0.0, so COMCTL32.dll v5.82 binds. The progress bar draws as classic chunks, not the modern themed bar, and the push button has the flat classic 3D border.
- **Cite:** fw110 MANIFEST(24) name 1 (full text read; contains only trustInfo and windowsSettings); COMCTL32.dll present in the import list (rabin2 -il).
- **REFUTED IF:** The progress bar and button render with the modern Aero/Fluent theme.
- **Matters:** Low stakes, but the mechanism (manifest content) was read directly, so getting it wrong means I am misreading Windows behaviour and my other platform-level [G] claims should be trusted less.

#### 9. `no-network-no-instance-guard` — [D]

**The updater performs no network activity at any point and has no single-instance guard.**

- **Expect:** The import DLL list is exactly: ADVAPI32, COMCTL32, COMDLG32, GDI32, gdiplus, HID.DLL, IMM32, KERNEL32, MSIMG32, ole32, OLEACC, OLEAUT32, SETUPAPI, SHELL32, SHLWAPI, USER32, WINMM, WINSPOOL.DRV. No WS2_32, no WININET, no WINHTTP, no URLMON. No CreateMutexW/A and no FindWindowW/A are imported, so two copies can be launched simultaneously and both show a window.
- **Cite:** rabin2 -il and rabin2 -i on fw110 (568 imports enumerated; grep for mutex/FindWindow/socket/WSAStartup/InternetOpen returns nothing).
- **REFUTED IF:** A network capture shows the process opening any socket, or a second launch refuses to start / merely focuses the first window.
- **Matters:** Rules out an online version check that could otherwise explain traffic in the capture, and confirms the firmware image is entirely resident in the exe (CLAUDE.md §1.4).

#### 10. `close-exits-immediately` — [D]

**Closing the dialog terminates the process immediately with no device I/O and no confirmation.**

- **Expect:** CWinApp::InitInstance (FUN_00402ac0) constructs the dialog on a 0x25a50-byte stack frame, sets m_pMainWnd, calls CDialog::DoModal (0x004084bd), destroys it, and returns 0 (`xorl %eax,%eax` at 0x00402b8b) — so AfxWinMain exits the app. Time from clicking X to process exit is well under one second and no USB transfer occurs.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00402ac0 @0x00402ac0-0x00402baf, raw objdump; app vtable InitInstance slot at .rdata 0x00544564 → 0x00402ac0; dialog ctor call at 0x00402b4b → FUN_00402f20.
- **REFUTED IF:** Closing the window prompts for confirmation, leaves a process running, or is followed by any USB transfer.
- **Matters:** Confirms the free run is fully reversible and leaves no state on the device.

#### 11. `vid-pid-search-set-closed` — [D]

**The device search space is exactly VID 0x3367 and PIDs {0x1978, 0x1977}, all three as compile-time literals; nothing is computed, configured, or read from a file.**

- **Expect:** VendorID test is a 16-bit compare: `movl $0x3367,%eax` at 0x004010fa then `cmpw %ax,-0xc(%ebp)` at 0x004010ff. ProductID is the function's stack argument, compared 32-bit: `movzwl -0xa(%ebp),%ecx` at 0x00401105, `cmpl 0x8(%ebp),%ecx` at 0x00401109. All 9 rel32 call sites of FUN_00401000 push a literal, either 0x1978 or 0x1977. No path constructs a PID.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00401000 @0x00401000 (raw objdump 0x4010fa-0x40110c); E8-rel32 scan of all 1,154,048 bytes of .text returning exactly 9 call sites for 0x00401000 (0x403608, 0x403649, 0x403696, 0x4036c9, 0x403805, 0x403836, 0x4038d6, 0x403c81, 0x403c97).
- **REFUTED IF:** The tool is observed opening a HID path whose VID is not 3367, or whose PID is neither 1978 nor 1977, other than as a candidate it immediately rejects during the SetupDi walk.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §1.4's principle applied to device identity: our flasher's identity set must also be two compile-time constants, and this is the evidence that the vendor's is.

#### 12. `usage-filter-ff01-02` — [D]

**After VID and PID match, the tool additionally requires HIDP_CAPS.UsagePage == 0xFF01 and Usage == 0x0002, in that order, and it reads no other field of HIDP_CAPS.**

- **Expect:** `movl $0xff01,%eax` at 0x00401137, `cmpw %ax,0x0056a132` at 0x0040113c (UsagePage is at caps+2), then `cmpw $0x2,0x0056a130` at 0x00401145 (Usage at caps+0). FeatureReportByteLength, InputReportByteLength and every other caps field are never read — the caps buffer 0x0056a130 is referenced at exactly two addresses in the whole .text, 0x00401122 (the HidP_GetCaps out-pointer) and 0x00401148.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00401000 raw objdump 0x00401118-0x0040114e; exhaustive 4-byte literal scan of .text for 0x0056a130 → 2 hits, both inside FUN_00401000.
- **REFUTED IF:** An API trace shows the tool reading caps.FeatureReportByteLength, or shows it opening the 0xFF02 collections for anything other than an immediate reject.
- **Matters:** This is the exact predicate our own device selection must copy (updater-protocol.md §1), and the fact that the tool never checks the declared report length is why CLAUDE.md §6.1's length question had to be settled from the descriptor instead.

#### 13. `createfilew-args-no-overlapped` — [D]

**Each candidate HID collection is opened with CreateFileW(path, GENERIC_READ|GENERIC_WRITE, FILE_SHARE_READ|FILE_SHARE_WRITE, NULL, OPEN_EXISTING, 0, NULL) — synchronous, no FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED.**

- **Expect:** Pushes in order at 0x004010ce-0x004010e1: $0 (hTemplateFile), $0 (dwFlagsAndAttributes — no 0x40000000 FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED), $3 (OPEN_EXISTING), $0 (lpSecurityAttributes), $3 (dwShareMode), $0xC0000000 (dwDesiredAccess), lpFileName = detail buffer + 4. Result is stored to the single global handle 0x0056a174 at 0x004010e8, overwriting the previous candidate's handle.
- **Cite:** fw110 raw objdump 0x004010ce-0x004010ed.
- **REFUTED IF:** A Process Monitor / API trace shows a different desired-access or share mode, or shows the handle opened with FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED.
- **Matters:** It fixes that all vendor HID I/O is synchronous (unlike the config tool's hidapi ReadFile+OVERLAPPED), which is the model our transport should mirror, and the shared read/write mode bears on whether our tool and theirs can hold the device at once.

#### 14. `setupdi-flags-0x12` — [D]

**Device discovery is SetupDiGetClassDevsW(HidGuid, NULL, NULL, DIGCF_PRESENT|DIGCF_DEVICEINTERFACE) and a linear SetupDiEnumDeviceInterfaces walk from index 0 that stops at the first match or when the API returns FALSE.**

- **Expect:** `pushl $0x12` at 0x00401029 (0x02 DIGCF_PRESENT | 0x10 DIGCF_DEVICEINTERFACE), three NULL/0 arguments, class GUID from HidD_GetHidGuid into the 16-byte buffer at 0x0056a120. SP_DEVICE_INTERFACE_DATA.cbSize = 0x1c set at 0x00401049; SP_DEVICE_INTERFACE_DETAIL_DATA_W.cbSize = 6 set at 0x004010bc (correct for x86). SetupDiGetDeviceInterfaceDetailW is called twice per candidate: once with buffer NULL / size 0 to obtain the required size, then with a heap buffer of that size.
- **Cite:** fw110 raw objdump 0x00401015-0x004010cd.
- **REFUTED IF:** An API trace shows different flags, a device-instance rather than device-interface enumeration, or a single detail call per candidate.
- **Matters:** Documents the reference enumeration semantics we are reproducing on macOS with IOHIDManager, and pins that only PRESENT devices are considered — so a device in the bootloader must actually be enumerated by the OS, not merely remembered.

#### 15. `no-dynamic-hid-resolution` — [D]

**The updater resolves no HID or SetupAPI function dynamically; every one is a static import.**

- **Expect:** The whole file contains exactly 6 occurrences of the byte string 'HidD_' and 1 of 'HidP_' — 7 total, equal to the 7 HID.DLL imports — and exactly 4 of 'SetupDi', equal to the 4 SETUPAPI imports, all in .rdata. Zero occurrences of 'hid.dll' or 'Hid.dll' in any other case than the import descriptor's 'HID.DLL'. 'GetProcAddress' occurs once (its own import name). fw104 gives identical counts.
- **Cite:** Byte scan of the whole of fw110 and fw104 for those literals; rabin2 -i import list.
- **REFUTED IF:** An API trace shows LoadLibrary("hid.dll") or GetProcAddress for any HidD_*/HidP_* name.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §1.2a: it closes the encoding form the IAT-slot scan is blind to, so 'all device access is in three functions' survives as a claim about the program rather than about one search method.

#### 16. `absent-mouse-19500ms` — [D]

**Pressing Update with no mouse attached takes 19.5 seconds of pure Sleep before 'Device not found' appears, and the window is frozen for all of it.**

- **Expect:** Sleep totals: loop A 500+1000+1500 = 3000 ms; loop B 3000+2700+2400+2100+1800+1500+1200+900+600+300+0 = 16500 ms. Sum 19,500 ms. Add 15 SetupDi walks, so measured click-to-status-text should land in 19.5-21.5 s and MUST NOT be below 19.5 s. Because FUN_004033b0 runs on the UI thread (AfxBeginThread is only reached on the success branches at 0x004034f9 / 0x00403513), the dialog does not repaint or respond during this time and Windows may mark it 'Not Responding'.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00403600 (sleep-ladder addresses above; Sleep IAT 0x0051b214 loaded into %ebx at 0x00403626, called at 0x00403659 and 0x004036d7) and FUN_004033b0 @0x004033b0 whose call to 0x00403600 is at 0x00403499, before either AfxBeginThread site.
- **REFUTED IF:** A stopwatch shows less than 19.5 s from click to 'Device not found', or the window repaints normally while searching.
- **Matters:** A wall-clock number is the hardest thing to be accidentally right about. If it lands, the sleep-ladder reading of FUN_00403600 — and by extension the identically-shaped ladders in FUN_00403750 and FUN_00403960 that govern the post-erase retry budget — is validated by one free measurement.

#### 17. `absent-mouse-busy-latch-bug` — [D]

**After 'Device not found' the tool is permanently inert: the Update button is re-enabled but every subsequent click does nothing at all — no delay, no status change, no enumeration — until the process is restarted.**

- **Expect:** The not-found path re-enables the button (`pushl $1` at 0x0040351d then CWnd::EnableWindow 0x00413707 at 0x00403529) and sets the busy flag to 0 at 0x0040351f — but then FALLS THROUGH into 0x0040353e, which sets the same member back to 1 (`movl $0x1,0x25a38(%esi)` at 0x00403541). The handler's first act is `cmpl %edi,0x25a38(%esi)` at 0x004033dc with edi = 0, jumping to the epilogue at 0x0040356b when it is non-zero. Nothing else clears it on this path (its only clearing writes are 0x00403003 in the constructor and 0x00403855, 0x00403883, 0x0040393a, 0x004039d0, 0x00403abb, 0x00403ba5, 0x00403c56, 0x00403cd1, 0x00403e77, all inside the bootloader/flash functions, none of which was started). Second and later clicks return in microseconds with the status box still reading 'Device not found'.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_004033b0 raw objdump 0x004033dc-0x004033e2 and 0x0040351a-0x0040354a; complete list of writes to +0x25a38 obtained by grepping a full disassembly of 0x00401000-0x004040b0. Cross-code-base: fw104 does the same at 0x004046c8 (`movl $0x0,0x25a78(%ebx)`) falling into 0x004046e7 (`movl $0x1,0x25a78(%ebx)`).
- **REFUTED IF:** A second click after 'Device not found' produces another ~19.5 s search, or any status change at all.
- **Matters:** A vendor bug I derived rather than a protocol fact, present in both independent code bases, and exactly the 'latched state / reported success' class CLAUDE.md §4.3 says compiles clean. Confirming it validates this level of control-flow reading; refuting it means I am misreading fall-through in the very function that decides when a flash starts.

#### 18. `absent-mouse-usb-silence` — [G]

**The 19.5-second mouse-absent search produces no USB traffic on any device.**

- **Expect:** HidD_GetAttributes, HidD_GetPreparsedData, HidP_GetCaps and HidD_FreePreparsedData are all served from the HID class driver's cached report descriptor and generate no bus transaction; CreateFileW/CloseHandle on a HID collection generate none either. The capture over those 19.5 s therefore shows only whatever the machine's other HID devices were already producing.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00401000 issues no HidD_SetFeature/HidD_GetFeature (those slots, 0x0051b1d4 and 0x0051b1d0, are referenced only inside FUN_004012a0 and FUN_00401330 — 2 and 3 sites respectively); the remainder of its calls are the four SetupAPI functions plus the four cached HidD/HidP queries. The 'cached, no bus transaction' half is Windows architecture, [G].
- **REFUTED IF:** Control transfers appear on some other USB HID device on the machine timed with the updater's search — which would mean opening a HID collection read-write does hit the bus and my model of the Windows HID stack is wrong.
- **Matters:** If opening a collection DOES generate traffic, our macOS tool's 'harmless' enumeration is not harmless, and CLAUDE.md §4.4 stage 1's premise that a read-only round trip is the first thing to touch the device would be false.

#### 19. `version-string-1-07` — [D]

**For this mouse (bcdDevice 0x0107) the status box will read exactly 'Mouse firmware current version 1.07'.**

- **Expect:** Byte chain: HidD_GetAttributes writes VersionNumber (HIDD_ATTRIBUTES+0x8, read at 0x0040118a as `movzwl -0x8(%ebp),%ecx`) to the global 0x0056a0f4. FUN_004011f0 loads it at 0x00401245, formats with the wide string at 0x005443b0 which is exactly L"%x" (2 chars), giving L"107"; passes that to 0x004f7965 which tail-jumps to 0x004f794f, which pushes base $0xa and calls the wcstol core at 0x004fa897 — DECIMAL — yielding 107; truncates with `movzwl %ax,%edi` at 0x00401263. The caller does `fildl` 107, `fdivl 0x00544c78` where that qword is exactly 100.0, rounds through float (`fstps`/`flds` at 0x004034c5/0x004034c8), and formats with L"Mouse firmware current version %.2f" at 0x00544840. Result: '1.07'. By the same rule 0x0110→'110'→1.10, 0x0104→1.04, 0x0143→1.43.
- **Cite:** fw110: 0x0040118a-0x004011a2 (capture of bcdDevice), FUN_004011f0 @0x004011f0 (0x00401245-0x00401263), 0x004f794f (`pushl $0xa`), FUN_004033b0 0x004034ab-0x004034e6; .rdata 0x005443b0 = L"%x", 0x00544840 = the format string, 0x00544c78 = 100.0 (double, read from the file).
- **REFUTED IF:** The string shows anything other than the digits 1, 0, 7 in that order — in particular '2.63' (which is what treating bcdDevice as a plain integer and dividing by 100 gives), '0.10', '263' or '1.7'. A locale whose decimal separator is a comma would legitimately render '1,07'; that still confirms the BCD decode and does NOT refute this — only the digits do.
- **Matters:** The only place the vendor tool exposes a device-reported number in human-readable form, and the check on the BCD claim that our own version display and any future 'refuse an unrecognised bootloader version' guard (CLAUDE.md §4.2) will be built on.

#### 20. `version-comes-from-descriptor-not-a-report` — [D]

**The displayed version is read out of the cached USB device descriptor, not fetched from the device — no USB transfer precedes it.**

- **Expect:** In the capture, the moment the status text changes to 'Mouse firmware current version 1.07' is NOT preceded by any GET_REPORT/SET_REPORT or GET_DESCRIPTOR to the OP1. The value's only source is HIDD_ATTRIBUTES.VersionNumber, which hidclass fills from the device descriptor's bcdDevice captured at enumeration time. The global 0x0056a0f4 is written at exactly one address (0x0040119f) and read at exactly two (0x00401247 in the version formatter, 0x00403d43 in the post-flash success message).
- **Cite:** fw110: exhaustive 4-byte scan of .text for 0x0056a0f4 → 3 references, listed above; HidD_GetAttributes call at 0x004010f4.
- **REFUTED IF:** The capture shows any transfer to the OP1 between the button press and the version text appearing.
- **Matters:** On macOS the same number is available from IOHIDDevice's kIOHIDVersionNumberKey with no device I/O, so our preflight identity check costs nothing and can run before any handle is opened.

#### 21. `version-and-flash-are-inseparable` — [D]

**There is no way to make the vendor updater display the current firmware version without it immediately starting a real flash; the two are straight-line, with no confirmation between them.**

- **Expect:** In FUN_004033b0 the sequence is: 0x004034ab call the version formatter → 0x004034e6 SetWindowTextW(status) → 0x004034eb-0x004034f9 push five zeros/this, then `pushl $0x402f00`, then call AfxBeginThread at 0x0040c6e6. There is no message box, no branch, no user input between 0x004034e6 and 0x004034f9. FUN_00402f00 is a 16-byte thunk that calls FUN_00403750 (enter bootloader) directly.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_004033b0 raw objdump 0x004034ab-0x004034fe; FUN_00402f00 @0x00402f00 (0x00402f07 `calll 0x403750`); 4-byte literal scan shows 0x00402f00 occurs once in the whole file, at 0x004034f5 — as the AfxBeginThread argument.
- **REFUTED IF:** A confirmation prompt appears between the version text and the start of the update, or the version can be displayed by some other action.
- **Matters:** It is a one-way door on our only 1.07 device. It also means our own tool should do what the vendor's does not — print the version and stop — and that any 'let me just check the version with the official tool' instinct must be refused.

#### 22. `post-flash-version-same-decode` — [D]

**If a flash to 1.10 completes normally, the success message will read exactly 'Update Succeed, current firmware version is V1.10' — same decode, re-read after re-enumeration.**

- **Expect:** FUN_00403960 re-reads 0x0056a0f4 at 0x00403d43 (repopulated by the PID-0x1978 re-enumeration loop at 0x00403c7c-0x00403cc3), formats it with the same L"%x" at 0x005443b0, parses base 10, divides by the same 100.0 at 0x00544c78, and formats with the message referenced from 0x00403e2a. bcdDevice 0x0110 → '110' → 110 → 1.10.
- **Cite:** fw110 raw objdump 0x00403d43-0x00403db1; message reference at 0x00403e2a (updater-protocol.md §11.2 row 12).
- **REFUTED IF:** The success message shows a version other than 1.10 after a successful flash of the 1.10 updater's resource 140 — which would mean resource 140 is not the 1.10 image, i.e. CLAUDE.md §1.4's hazard is live.
- **Matters:** The only post-hoc check that the image shipped is the image intended. NOT observable in the free run; pre-registered here so it exists before any flash happens.

#### 23. `fwfile-load-precedes-device-search` — [D]

**The firmware resource is loaded into memory before the device is looked for, so a resource failure is reported without the tool ever touching USB.**

- **Expect:** Order inside FUN_004033b0: busy-flag guard (0x004033dc) → EnableWindow(FALSE) on the button (0x004033f2) → SetWindowTextW(status, L"") using the empty string at 0x005443b8 (0x00403431) → progress = 0 via SendMessageW(m_progress.m_hWnd at member+0xdc, PBM_SETPOS 0x402, 0, 0) at 0x00403450 → call FUN_00403200 (FWFILE load) at 0x00403456 → only then call FUN_00403600 (device search) at 0x00403499. FUN_00403200's sole rel32 caller in the whole .text is 0x00403456.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_004033b0 raw objdump 0x004033dc-0x00403499; E8 scan for 0x00403200 → 1 caller.
- **REFUTED IF:** An API trace shows SetupDiGetClassDevsW being called before FindResourceW/LoadResource on the button press.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.2: every preflight failure must abort before anything reaches the device. This is the vendor doing the cheap offline validation first, and it is the order our own preflight should keep.

#### 24. `op1-presents-six-hid-interfaces` — [G]

**On Windows the OP1 8k v2 appears as six separate HID device interfaces, of which exactly one passes the updater's usage filter.**

- **Expect:** Interface A declares five top-level collections (Generic Desktop/Keyboard; 0xFF01/0x02; Consumer Control; 0xFF02/0x01; 0xFF02/0x02) and interface B declares one (Generic Desktop/Mouse). Windows' HID class driver creates one device interface per top-level collection, so Device Manager / SetupDi will list six paths of the form \\?\HID#VID_3367&PID_1978&MI_xx&Col0y#..., with Col01..Col05 on the composite's HID interface. Only the 0xFF01/0x02 one satisfies the compare pair at 0x0040113c/0x00401145.
- **Cite:** Top-level collection inventory is [O] from notes/device-predictions.md (interface A's 156-byte report descriptor, five TLCs; interface B 69 bytes, one TLC). The filter is fw110 0x00401137-0x0040114d. The one-interface-per-TLC behaviour is Windows hidclass policy, hence the [G] tag on the whole.
- **REFUTED IF:** Device Manager or a SetupDi enumeration lists a number of OP1 HID interfaces other than six, or the vendor collections carry no &Col suffix.
- **Matters:** It sets the upper bound on how many CreateFileW calls one enumeration pass makes against our device, and it is the Windows-side cross-check on the [O] finding that this mouse has a third vendor collection nobody opens.

#### 25. `keyboard-mouse-collections-refuse-rw-open` — [G]

**CreateFileW with GENERIC_READ|GENERIC_WRITE will FAIL on the OP1's Generic Desktop/Keyboard and Generic Desktop/Mouse collections, and the updater does not check for that failure.**

- **Expect:** Those two CreateFileW calls return INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE with GetLastError() = ERROR_ACCESS_DENIED (5), because Windows reserves read/write access to system keyboard and mouse top-level collections. The updater stores the failure value into 0x0056a174 anyway (0x004010e8) and calls HidD_GetAttributes on it (0x004010f4) without testing either return value; the VID/PID compare at 0x004010ff/0x00401109 then reads whatever the previous candidate left in the -0x10(%ebp) stack structure. The vendor collections (0xFF01/0x02, 0xFF02/0x01, 0xFF02/0x02) and the Consumer Control collection open normally.
- **Cite:** fw110 raw objdump 0x004010ce-0x0040110c — there is no `testl %eax,%eax` or `cmpl $-1,%eax` between CreateFileW and HidD_GetAttributes, and none between HidD_GetAttributes and the VID compare. The Windows access-denial policy itself is [G], hence the tag.
- **REFUTED IF:** A Process Monitor trace shows CreateFile succeeding with SUCCESS on the collection with usage page 0x01 / usage 0x06 (keyboard) and on interface B's mouse collection.
- **Matters:** If it is true, the vendor's enumerator filters unopenable collections by accident (stale stack bytes) rather than by design — so our enumerator must check every return value explicitly and must never treat a matching VID/PID as implying a valid handle.

#### 26. `fw104-structurally-identical-startup` — [D]

**Updater 1.04, the other code base, has the same one-button, no-timer, nothing-at-startup shape — so this is a property of the product line, not of one build.**

- **Expect:** fw104's dialog AFX_MSGMAP entry array at .rdata 0x005a2008 is four 24-byte records then a zero terminator at 0x005a2068: WM_PAINT(0x0F)→0x00404240, WM_QUERYDRAGICON(0x37)→0x00404320, WM_COMMAND(0x111) id 0x3E9..0x3E9→0x004044F0, WM_LBUTTONDOWN(0x201)→0x00405050. 0x004044f0 has zero rel32 callers and exactly one 4-byte occurrence in the file, at .rdata 0x005a204c (that map entry). All 11 HID/SetupAPI IAT slots resolve to just three functions: 0x00401bc0 (enumerator), 0x00401e30 (SetFeature), 0x00401ed0 (GetFeature).
- **Cite:** fw104 .rdata 0x005a2008-0x005a2067 read as raw bytes; E8/literal scan for 0x004044f0; IAT-slot scan for 0x56e1b8/1bc/1c0/1c4/1c8/1cc/1d0 and 0x56e4c4/4c8/4cc/4d0.
- **REFUTED IF:** Running 1.04 (not planned) shows startup device activity, or its dialog responds to a timer.
- **Matters:** Two independently compiled builds agreeing turns 'this build does nothing at startup' into 'this vendor's updaters do nothing at startup', which is the assumption behind treating any startup traffic in the capture as somebody else's.

### Rejected by audit (6) — not pre-registered

| id | fault | auditor's evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `config-tool-traffic-is-not-ours` | `mistagged` | Tagged [D], but the [D] evidence supports only a host-side loop, not the bus-level claim. I disassembled cfg107 0x412c60-0x412cf5 myself. The cited instructions are all exactly right (0x412c7e `movb $0x3,-0xc(%ebp)`; 0x412ca8 `calll 0x4031c0` with `pushl $8`; 0x412ce4 `pushl $0x50` then `call *%esi` where esi=0x52d22c=Sleep). But at 0x412c99-0x412c9e the code does `movl 0x4(%eax),%ecx; negl %ecx; sbbl %ecx,%ecx` and pushes that as hid_read_timeout's milliseconds argument: for hidapi's default blocking=1 this is -1, i.e. block indefinitely. So the loop's period is gated by report ARRIVAL, not by the 80 ms Sleep, and there is no 12.5 Hz cadence unless the device emits a report faster than every 80 ms. notes/config-protocol.md 10.4 (which the prediction cites) describes these as unsolicited state-change events - polling-rate changes (buf[1]==0x06), a four-way setting (0x02), and 0xB4 - not a periodic stream. An idle mouse plausibly emits none. The refuted_if ('the capture shows no periodic 8-byte activity ... at roughly 80 ms intervals') will therefore likely fire on a capture where the derivation is entirely correct, and its stated purpose - pre-labelling traffic so it is 'diagnosed, not investigated' - inverts into mislabelling the absence of traffic as a refutation. |
| `absent-mouse-15-passes` | `not-falsifiable` | The pass COUNT half is falsifiable; the PID-split half is not, by any instrument the document lists. refuted_if: 'An API trace of a mouse-absent run counts a number of SetupDiGetClassDevsW calls other than 15, or a PID split other than 9/6.' capture_requirements names only USBPcap, Process Explorer, Process Monitor, a stopwatch and screenshots - no API tracer - and asserts 'Process Monitor gives the 15-pass / 9-vs-6-PID count'. It cannot. I confirmed from fw110 0x401000 that the PID never leaves the process: SetupDiGetClassDevsW is called with the HID class GUID (push $0x12 at 0x401029), the walk enumerates every HID interface, and the PID is used only in the in-register compare at 0x401109 (`cmpl 0x8(%ebp),%ecx`). With the mouse unplugged no VID_3367 path exists at all, so a 0x1977 pass and a 0x1978 pass are byte-for-byte identical in everything ProcMon can see. Note also that the disagreement this prediction exists to settle (notes 5.2 '10 passes' vs the predicted 11) cannot be settled by the sibling timing prediction either - see missing_predictions. |
| `status-edit-is-editable` | `contradicts-notes` | The prediction is CORRECT on the bytes - I parsed RT_DIALOG 102 (RVA 0x1e7f14, 352 bytes, all 352 consumed) and control 3 is class ordinal 129 (EDIT), id 1017, style 0x50010080 = WS_CHILD\|WS_VISIBLE\|WS_TABSTOP\|ES_AUTOHSCROLL, with ES_READONLY (0x0800) clear. But notes/updater-protocol.md line 2549 (11.1) states the committed opposite: 'the updater has exactly one control the user can press, one progress bar, and one read-only status line.' The document flags its other note disagreement explicitly and in-line (absent-mouse-15-passes: 'NOTE: notes/updater-protocol.md 5.2 says 10 passes; I derive 11'), so the omission here is a lapse against its own standard, not an ambiguity in the format. It matters because this prediction is declared to be the calibration check on the whole 11.1 control inventory, and 11.1 is the very section it silently contradicts. |
| `absent-mouse-status-text` | `citation-wrong` | The claim holds; one cited identifier does not. 'Device not found' at .rdata 0x544888 - verified, 16 UTF-16 code units, exactly that casing. Push site 0x40352e - verified (`68 88 48 54 00`). The other two strings and their push sites (0x54480c@0x40345f, 0x544840@0x4034ce) - verified, and I confirmed from the full disassembly of 0x4033b0-0x40357c that those plus the empty string at 0x5443b8 are the only string pushes in the handler. But the citation says 'SetWindowTextW helper 0x004135fa (USER32 slot 0x0051b7d4)'. Slot 0x0051b7d4 is USER32!IsWindow (rabin2 -i), used at 0x41360e as an ASSERT-style guard. The actual SetWindowTextW slot is 0x0051b604, called at 0x413636. The helper identification is right; the slot attribution is wrong. |
| `one-handle-per-candidate-none-closed` | `citation-wrong` | Substance verified, one address wrong. I read all of FUN_00401000 (0x401000-0x4011e9) and there is no CloseHandle: the CloseHandle IAT slot 0x51b200 is referenced 22 times in .text, 6 in the vendor band, at 0x403622, 0x4036e2, 0x4038a7, 0x4039f8, 0x403ae2, 0x403e9f - none inside the enumerator. Not-found exit calls SetupDiDestroyDeviceInfoList (instruction 0x401171); success exit 0x40118a->0x4011e9 calls neither - confirmed. The '- 12' in the consequence reconciles exactly (1 close after pass 1 + 11 in loop B; loop A closes nothing). But 'FUN_00403600 calls CloseHandle ... at 0x00403619 and 0x004036e0 only' is wrong at the first address: 0x403619 is `pushl %ebx`; the CloseHandle calls are at 0x403620 and 0x4036e0 (IAT displacements 0x403622 and 0x4036e2). 0x403619 is the displacement of the handle LOAD `movl 0x56a174,%eax` at 0x403618. Separately, this prediction's refuted_if ('Process Explorer's Handles column ... across a failed device search') is not covered by any capture step: Process Explorer is specified for Run 1 only, where no device search occurs. |
| `present-mouse-first-try-succeeds` | `citation-wrong` | Minor, same slip as above. The substantive derivation is verified: 0x403603 `pushl $0x1978`, 0x403608 `calll 0x401000`, 0x403610 `testl %eax,%eax`, 0x403612 `jne 0x403739`, and 0x403739-0x403743 writes *mode=1 and returns without touching the ladder. But 'the CloseHandle at 0x00403619 ... are skipped' names an address that holds `pushl %ebx`; the skipped CloseHandle call is at 0x403620. |

### Cannot predict — what the capture will teach us (7)

- **In what ORDER does SetupDiEnumDeviceInterfaces return this machine's HID interfaces, and therefore how many CreateFileW calls happen before the OP1's 0xFF01/0x02 collection is reached?**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The order is decided by the Windows PnP database on the specific machine (device instance enumeration order), not by anything in the exe. The exe just walks index 0,1,2,... until the API returns FALSE or a candidate matches.
  - how a capture answers it: Process Monitor filtered to the updater process, Operation = CreateFile, Path begins \\?\HID# — the ordered list of paths tried on one button press, with the last one being the match. Record the total number of HID interfaces present on the machine too.
- **Does CreateFileW(GENERIC_READ|GENERIC_WRITE) actually fail on the OP1's keyboard and mouse collections, and if so does the stale-stack-value path in FUN_00401000 ever produce a false VID/PID match?**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The failure is a Windows access-control decision. The consequence — the tool comparing uninitialised or stale bytes at -0xc/-0xa(%ebp) because it never checks CreateFileW's or HidD_GetAttributes' return value — is visible in the disassembly, but whether it ever bites depends entirely on enumeration order and on what the previous iteration left behind.
  - how a capture answers it: Process Monitor's Result column for each \\?\HID# CreateFile (SUCCESS vs ACCESS DENIED), combined with the ordered path list. If an ACCESS DENIED candidate immediately follows one that had VID 3367 / PID 1978, the hazard is live on this machine.
- **How much interrupt-IN traffic does an 8 kHz OP1 generate in an idle USBPcap capture, and will it drown the (predicted zero) updater traffic?**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: It is a property of the mouse's current polling-rate setting and of how USBPcap logs NAKed microframes, neither of which is in any exe.
  - how a capture answers it: Start USBPcap with the mouse plugged in, touch nothing for 30 s, and count packets. That number sets the practical capture strategy for every later run.
- **Does the tool's '%.2f' render as '1.07' or '1,07' on this Windows install?**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The CRT is statically linked and I did not establish whether anything in MFC's startup path calls setlocale; the answer depends on the machine's locale as much as on the code.
  - how a capture answers it: A screenshot of the status text plus the machine's Region > Number format setting. Either way the DIGITS decide the BCD question; the separator is only bookkeeping.
- **Whether the OP1's 0xFF02/0x02 collection (Input report 0x08, 64 bytes) emits anything on its own.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: No Endgame binary opens it — every hid_open usage filter in the corpus compares usage against 1, and the updaters contain zero 4-byte 0xff02 immediates — so no exe describes its content.
  - how a capture answers it: A long idle USBPcap capture with nothing but the mouse attached shows whether report id 0x08 ever appears unsolicited. Free, and the highest-value thing the free run could teach us that is not already derived.
- **How long the whole pre-button phase takes end to end, and whether Windows flags the frozen window during a mouse-absent search.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The Sleep budget is derived exactly (19,500 ms), but the SetupDi walk cost per pass and Windows' hang-detection threshold are machine behaviour.
  - how a capture answers it: A stopwatch and a screenshot of the title bar during the search.
- **Whether the exe on the Windows laptop is byte-identical to the 1.10 we analysed.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: Trivially — it is a fact about the file on that machine, not about ours.
  - how a capture answers it: certutil -hashfile <exe> SHA256 on the Windows box, compared against notes/binaries.md. Every address in this document is void if it differs.

### Gaps the auditor flagged (5)

- THE EXPENSIVE ONE - initial keyboard focus is on the Update Firmware button, and the document's own Run 1 procedure would press it. From the template the author parsed themselves: control 1000 (progress) style 0x50000000 has no WS_TABSTOP; control 1001 (the button) style 0x50010000 DOES (0x00010000); control 1017 (edit) style 0x50010080 does. OnInitDialog at 0x4030c0 returns 1 (`movl $1,%eax` at 0x4030f8), so the dialog manager assigns focus to the first visible, enabled, WS_TABSTOP control in template order - which is 1001, the Update Firmware button. capture_requirements then says: 'Drive the updater's window with the keyboard (Tab/Space/Alt+F4) ... so interrupt-IN traffic stays near zero.' A single Space (or Enter, which activates a focused pushbutton in a dialog) is BN_CLICKED on id 1001, which is the WM_COMMAND the msgmap routes to 0x4033b0 - i.e. an immediate real flash of the one 1.07 device, contradicting the document's own first irreversible_warning. Neither the focus fact nor the Tab/Space hazard is predicted anywhere, and Tab is safe only until the moment it is not. This should be stated as a prediction ('at launch the Update Firmware button has the focus rectangle') AND as a hard capture rule ('use the mouse pointer or Alt+F4 only; never Space or Enter').
- The 19.5 s measurement cannot settle the 10-vs-11 disagreement it is offered to settle, and nothing says so. absent-mouse-15-passes deliberately contradicts notes 5.2's '10 passes', and absent-mouse-19500ms is presented as validating the sleep-ladder reading. But loop B's 11th sleep is Sleep(0) (esi reaches 0 before the exit test at 0x4036f7), so a 10-pass reading and an 11-pass reading predict the SAME 16,500 ms and the SAME 19,500 ms total. The stopwatch discriminates the ladder's shape but is blind to the exact disagreement. Only a pass count can settle it, which makes the ProcMon burst count the load-bearing observation - and that dependency is unstated.
- No prediction of the Run 2 ProcMon shape, which is the only instrument that could count the passes. With the mouse absent, FUN_00401000 still walks every HID interface on the machine and CreateFileW's each one (GENERIC_READ|GENERIC_WRITE, share 3, OPEN_EXISTING) - 15 times over. So Run 2's ProcMon log should show 15 distinguishable bursts of CreateFile on \\?\HID# paths, each burst covering every HID collection present, with the bursts separated by the 500/1000/1500/3000/2700/... ms gaps. That is a concrete, cheap, pre-registerable prediction; it would deliver the 15-pass count, the per-pass interface list that cannot_predict asks for, and the ACCESS DENIED results that keyboard-mouse-collections-refuse-rw-open needs - all from one free run. The document predicts zero HID CreateFile for Run 1 but never states the expected Run 2 shape.
- Six predictions' refuted_if depends on an instrument the capture plan does not include. no-device-handle-at-launch, usage-filter-ff01-02, createfilew-args-no-overlapped, setupdi-flags-0x12, no-dynamic-hid-resolution and absent-mouse-15-passes all say 'An API trace shows...'. capture_requirements lists only USBPcap, Process Explorer, Process Monitor, a stopwatch and screenshots. ProcMon does not log SetupDi* calls, CreateFileW's dwDesiredAccess/dwFlagsAndAttributes as such (it shows a decoded Desired Access column but not FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED as an API argument), FindResourceW, LoadLibrary or GetProcAddress. Either an API tracer (API Monitor / WinDbg breakpoints) belongs in capture_requirements, or those refuted_ifs should be rewritten against what ProcMon actually reports.
- one-handle-per-candidate-none-closed's observation is not scheduled. Its refuted_if is 'Process Explorer's Handles column ... across a failed device search', but Process Explorer is specified for Run 1 only, which performs no search. The observation belongs in Run 2, whose requirements list USBPcap and ProcMon and not Process Explorer. Given the derivation predicts a specific arithmetic (openable HID collections per pass x 15, minus 12 closes - which I confirmed reconciles: 1 close after pass 1 plus 11 in loop B, loop A closing none), this is a checkable number being left unmeasured.

### Irreversible / one-shot in this area

- Pressing 'Update Firmware' with the mouse attached at PID 0x1978 starts a real firmware flash immediately. There is no confirmation dialog, no 'are you sure' and no Cancel — the vendor's entire UI is one button, and its string table contains no 'Cancel', no 'Do not unplug' and no 'Please wait' (fw110 band literal scan, 18 distinct strings). The free run must not press it.
- The version display and the flash are inseparable. FUN_004033b0 runs the version formatter at 0x004034ab, sets the status text at 0x004034e6, and calls AfxBeginThread at 0x004034f9 with no branch in between. You cannot use the vendor tool to read the firmware version without also starting an update. Read bcdDevice from Device Manager instead.
- The mouse is currently on 1.07 [O] and the updater carries 1.10. Once flashed, the device can never again produce a 1.07 observation with the tools we have — there is no downgrade path in any binary. Every 1.07-conditional prediction in this document becomes permanently untestable the moment that button is pressed.
- A flash that completes normally ends with A1 13, which is factory reset — the mouse's settings are wiped. There is a reachable path (gate (c) failing at 0x00403cc3) where the flash succeeds, the tool reports success, and A1 13 is never sent, so this is 'usually' rather than 'always'; do not rely on either outcome.
- If the mouse is ever in bootloader mode (PID 0x1977), pressing Update flashes it immediately with no application-mode handshake at all (FUN_004033b0 mode == 2 → thunk 0x00402f10 → FUN_00403960 directly). So 'the mouse looks dead, let me just open the updater and see' is itself a flash.
- The mouse-absent test in Run 2 is only safe if the mouse is genuinely disconnected. If it is plugged in and merely idle, the same button press flashes it. Verify absence in Device Manager before clicking, not by looking at the desk.
- Not irreversible, but do not let it look like a failure: after 'Device not found' the tool latches its busy flag to 1 and every further click does nothing. Restarting the exe clears it.


---

## Updater 1.10 — bootloader entry and re-enumeration

*area key: `updater-bootloader-entry` — 31 written, 10 rejected*


### Pre-registered (22)

#### 1. `no-traffic-before-click` — [D]

**From updater launch until the 'Update Firmware' button is clicked, the updater generates zero USB traffic of any kind to the mouse.**

- **Expect:** Between process start and the click there are 0 control transfers with bmRequestType 0x21 or 0xA1 addressed to VID 0x3367 / PID 0x1978, and 0 transfers of any kind attributable to the updater. Any USB traffic in that window belongs to Windows or to other software.
- **Cite:** fw110: the enumerator FUN_00401000 has exactly 9 direct callers (0x403608, 0x403649, 0x403696, 0x4036c9, 0x403805, 0x403836, 0x4038d6, 0x403c81, 0x403c97), all inside 0x403600 / 0x403750 / 0x403960, and all three are reached only from the button handler 0x4033b0 (call at 0x403499, AfxBeginThread starts at 0x4034f4 and 0x40350e). The two device-touching wrappers 0x4012a0 / 0x401330 have exactly 7 callers each (E8-rel32 sweep of all 1,154,048 bytes of .text; notes/updater-protocol.md §3.7a, §5.4a).
- **REFUTED IF:** Any HID class control transfer to PID 0x1978 appears between updater launch and the button click.
- **Matters:** It fixes the zero point of the capture and confirms our own flasher needs no startup handshake — device identity can come from the descriptor cache, exactly as the vendor does.

#### 2. `version-not-read-from-device` — [D]

**The firmware version the updater prints ('Mouse firmware current version 1.07') is decoded from bcdDevice in the cached USB device descriptor, not fetched with a device command.**

- **Expect:** No GET_REPORT precedes the first SET_REPORT. The updater's first HID class request in the whole session is the SET_REPORT carrying A1 3A. bcdDevice on the wire reads 0x0107 and the UI shows 1.07.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x4034ab calls FUN_004011f0, which reads only the global DAT_0056a0f4 written by the enumerator at 0x40119d from HIDD_ATTRIBUTES.VersionNumber (captured at 0x4010f4). Format string at 0x5443b0 is L"%x", then __wtol, then fdivl 0x544c78 (=100.0) at 0x4034bf. notes/updater-protocol.md §1.
- **REFUTED IF:** A GET_REPORT (bmRequestType 0xA1, bRequest 0x01) or any other class request appears before the A1 3A SET_REPORT.
- **Matters:** It settles that there is no 'read version' command to reimplement, and it confirms the BCD decode is a host-side interpretation of a descriptor field, which is what our preflight identity check will rely on.

#### 3. `a1-3a-frame-bytes` — [D]

**The enter-bootloader frame is exactly 64 bytes: A1 3A 00 00 00 5A A5 32 followed by 56 zero bytes.**

- **Expect:** Data stage, verbatim hex: a13a0000005aa532 then 00 × 56. Byte [0]=0xA1 (report id), [1]=0x3A, [2]=[3]=[4]=0x00, [5]=0x5A, [6]=0xA5, [7]=0x32, [8..63] all 0x00.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00403750: memset(-0x44(%ebp),0,0x40) at 0x403788; movl $0x3aa1,-0x44(%ebp) at 0x403793; movl $0x32a55a00,-0x40(%ebp) at 0x40379a; pushl $0x40 / pushl buf at 0x403790–0x403792; calll 0x4012a0 at 0x4037a1.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any byte of the 64-byte data stage differs from that sequence, or the length is not 64.
- **Matters:** This is the exact frame EGGFlashCore emits at stage 2 of CLAUDE.md §4.4. One wrong byte is the difference between entering the bootloader and doing something undefined to the only mouse we have.

#### 4. `a1-3a-wire-length-not-padded` — [D]

**Windows sends the A1 command at its own 64-byte length and does NOT pad it up to the collection's 1041-byte maximum feature report size.**

- **Expect:** wLength = 0x0040 with 64 data bytes, even though the same collection also carries Feature report 0xA0 whose total length is 1041. The report id 0xA1 is present as data[0]; the payload is not shifted down by one byte.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403790 pushl $0x40 (the length argument) against 0x4018e0 pushl $0x411 for the A0 commands — two different lengths handed to the same handle. Device side: MaxFeatureReportSize = 1041 for this collection [O, ioreg 2026-09-04].
- **REFUTED IF:** wLength is 0x0411 with 977 trailing zeros, or wLength is 0x003F with the data stage starting at 0x3A.
- **Matters:** macOS and hidapi never pad. If Windows pads and the device only accepts the padded form, every 64-byte A1 command we send from macOS fails and we would have no way to diagnose it. This one observation validates or kills our entire A1 transport.

#### 5. `a1-3a-count-one` — [D]

**In a run that succeeds, exactly ONE SET_REPORT carrying A1 3A appears on the bus, even though the code is a 9-attempt loop.**

- **Expect:** One occurrence of the 64-byte a13a0000005aa532… data stage. Not 2, not 9. The remaining loop iterations fail inside HidD_SetFeature on a stale handle and put nothing on the wire.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403775 movl $0x1,%ebx; loop head 0x403780; 0x4037ef incl %ebx; 0x4037f0 cmpl $0xa,%ebx; jl → 9 iterations, i = 1…9. FUN_004012a0 retries only for GetLastError ∈ {0x15,0x17,0x1D,0x57,0x65B} (0x4012ce–0x4012e7); ERROR_DEVICE_NOT_CONNECTED / ERROR_NO_SUCH_DEVICE / ERROR_INVALID_HANDLE are not in that set, so a removed device yields one attempt and a return of 0.
- **REFUTED IF:** Two or more A1 3A frames reach the bus in a run that goes on to flash successfully.
- **Matters:** It tells us whether our own implementation should send the command once and then wait, or loop. Sending a mode-switch command repeatedly to a device that has already switched is exactly the kind of well-meant retry that causes undefined behaviour.

#### 6. `nine-attempt-signature` — [D]

**If the device ever fails to leave application mode, the signature is exactly 9 A1 3A frames with a specific widening gap — this is the shape to look for in a failed run.**

- **Expect:** 9 SET_REPORTs of the same 64 bytes; between SET n and SET n+1 roughly 4n ms of sleeping (4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28, 32 ms), each SET followed ~2n ms later by one GET_REPORT wValue 0x03A1 wLength 0x40. Cumulative span from SET 1 to SET 9 ≈ 144 ms of sleeps. Then no vendor traffic for up to 22.5 s, then the status line reads 'send bldr request failed'.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x4037a8–0x4037b3 (movb %bl,%al; addb %al,%al; movzbl %al,%ecx; call Sleep) = Sleep(2i) after every send; 0x4037e9 leal (%ebx,%ebx),%ecx; call Sleep = a second Sleep(2i) on failure; loop bound at 0x4037f0.
- **REFUTED IF:** A failing run shows a number of A1 3A frames other than 9, or gaps that are constant rather than growing by ~4 ms per iteration.
- **Matters:** If the capture goes wrong this is how we tell 'the device refused' from 'the tool never sent it', without which a failed capture teaches nothing.

#### 7. `get-report-count-1-to-4` — [D]

**The number of GET_REPORT attempts immediately after the A1 3A is between 0 and 4, and most likely exactly 1.**

- **Expect:** 1 GET_REPORT if it completes or fails with a non-retryable error; up to 4 (Sleep(50) between) only if GetLastError is one of 0x15, 0x17, 0x1D, 0x57, 0x65B. Never more than 4 within one loop iteration.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x4013af–0x4013ce (the five retryable error codes), 0x4013d6 movl $0x1,%edi, 0x401405 pushl $0x32 (Sleep 50), 0x40140a cmpl $0x4,%edi; jl.
- **REFUTED IF:** 5 or more consecutive GET_REPORTs with ~50 ms spacing appear after a single SET_REPORT.
- **Matters:** Our transport has to decide which errors are worth retrying. Seeing which one Windows actually returns when the device vanishes mid-switch tells us which macOS error we must treat the same way.

#### 8. `no-busy-poll-here` — [D]

**The device-busy poll (status byte 0x04) does NOT fire on the enter-bootloader read.**

- **Expect:** There is no run of GET_REPORT wValue 0x03A1 whose gaps grow 0, 100, 200 … 1900 ms. If such a run appears, the device answered A1 3A with resp[1] == 0x04.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x40135d cmpb $0x4,0x1(%edi); busy loop 0x401365–0x4013a3 with addl $0x64,%esi and cmpl $0x7d0 → 20 passes, sleeps 0…1900 ms, ~19 s. notes/updater-protocol.md §2.
- **REFUTED IF:** A sequence of ≥5 GET_REPORTs with 100 ms-incrementing gaps follows the A1 3A.
- **Matters:** The 0x01-ready / 0x04-busy convention is currently derived only from the tool's code. If the busy path fires here it is the first evidence the device really uses it, and it would tell us the mode switch is not instantaneous.

#### 9. `no-output-reports-ever` — [D]

**The updater can emit no HID OUTPUT report and no interrupt-OUT transfer, at any point, by construction.**

- **Expect:** Zero SET_REPORT transfers with wValue high byte 0x02 (Output). Zero OUT transfers on any endpoint. All updater traffic is Feature (wValue high byte 0x03) on the default control pipe.
- **Cite:** fw110 import directory: HID.DLL contributes exactly 7 entries — HidD_FreePreparsedData (0x51b1c4), HidP_GetCaps (0x51b1c8), HidD_GetPreparsedData (0x51b1cc), HidD_GetFeature (0x51b1d0), HidD_SetFeature (0x51b1d4), HidD_GetAttributes (0x51b1d8), HidD_GetHidGuid (0x51b1dc). No HidD_SetOutputReport, no HidD_GetInputReport. The 8 WriteFile IAT references (0x41475f, 0x4fd1a6, 0x507cfb, 0x507d54, 0x507ec2, 0x507f9c, 0x50809d, 0x5080f4) are MFC/CRT file I/O, none in the device closure; the delay-import directory is empty (notes/updater-protocol.md §6.3).
- **REFUTED IF:** Any Output report, or any interrupt-OUT transfer from the host, appears while the updater is running.
- **Matters:** It bounds what EGGCore's transport must implement — Feature get/set on the control pipe and nothing else — and it means any OUT traffic in the capture is somebody else's.

#### 10. `device-disconnects` — [D]

**The device disconnects from the bus after receiving A1 3A and reappears as a different USB device; PID 0x1978 and PID 0x1977 are never present at the same time.**

- **Expect:** Within a few hundred ms of the A1 3A status stage the PID 0x1978 device stops responding, pending URBs complete with an error status, and a fresh device with a new USB address enumerates. No moment in the capture shows both PIDs enumerated.
- **Cite:** fw110: the enumerator takes the PID as an argument (0x401109 cmpl 0x8(%ebp),%ecx) and the only two literals ever passed are 0x1978 and 0x1977 at all 9 call sites (notes/updater-protocol.md §1.1). 0x403800 pushl $0x1977 is polled only after A1 3A is sent. A USB device has one idProduct, so a PID change requires re-enumeration.
- **REFUTED IF:** PID 0x1978 remains enumerated while PID 0x1977 also appears, or PID 0x1977 never appears while the flash still proceeds.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.2 requires us to re-confirm device identity after any re-enumeration. This confirms there is one, and that our code must expect its open handle to die under it.

#### 11. `bldr-vid-pid` — [D]

**The re-enumerated device is VID 0x3367 / PID 0x1977 exactly.**

- **Expect:** GET_DESCRIPTOR DEVICE for the new device returns idVendor = 0x3367 and idProduct = 0x1977. bDeviceClass = 0x00 and bNumConfigurations = 1 are likely but not derived and not predicted.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x4010fa movl $0x3367,%eax / 0x4010ff cmpw %ax,-0xc(%ebp); 0x403800 and 0x4038d1 pushl $0x1977; notes/updater-protocol.md §1, §1.1.
- **REFUTED IF:** idProduct of the bootloader device is anything other than 0x1977, or idVendor is not 0x3367.
- **Matters:** 0x1977 is a compile-time constant in our flasher per CLAUDE.md §1.4-style reasoning. If the real bootloader PID differed we would search for a device that never appears and hang after the point of no return.

#### 12. `host-enumeration-sequence` — [G]

**The re-enumeration produces the standard Windows control-transfer sequence, and it dominates the transfer COUNT during bootloader entry though not the byte count of the whole session.**

- **Expect:** For the new device: GET_DESCRIPTOR DEVICE (bRequest 0x06, wValue 0x0100); GET_DESCRIPTOR CONFIGURATION with wLength 0x0009; GET_DESCRIPTOR CONFIGURATION with wLength = the returned wTotalLength; GET_DESCRIPTOR STRING wValue 0x0300 (langid); GET_DESCRIPTOR STRING for each nonzero iManufacturer / iProduct index; SET_CONFIGURATION bRequest 0x09 wValue 0x0001; then per HID interface GET_DESCRIPTOR wValue 0x2200 wIndex=<ifnum>. Order of the string fetches is not predicted. Roughly 10–25 control transfers, well under 2 KB, against ~143 KB for the block writes later in the same capture.
- **Cite:** No binary address — this is Windows' USB stack, not vendor code. Stated so the capture's bulk can be told apart from vendor commands.
- **REFUTED IF:** SET_CONFIGURATION is absent, or no GET_DESCRIPTOR with wValue 0x2200 appears for the bootloader device.
- **Matters:** Whoever reads the capture needs to know which packets are the operating system and which are the protocol, or they will chase host noise as though it were vendor behaviour.

#### 13. `no-set-address-visible` — [G]

**SET_ADDRESS is not visible in a USBPcap capture, so its absence is not evidence of anything about the device.**

- **Expect:** No control transfer with bRequest 0x05 appears for the bootloader device. USBPcap filters at the URB layer and the hub / host-controller driver assigns the address below that layer.
- **Cite:** Host-stack behaviour; no binary address.
- **REFUTED IF:** A SET_ADDRESS (bRequest 0x05) transfer appears in the capture.
- **Matters:** Registered so that 'we never saw the device get an address' is not later written down as a finding about the mouse.

#### 14. `no-set-idle-set-protocol` — [G]

**Windows issues neither SET_IDLE nor SET_PROTOCOL to the bootloader device.**

- **Expect:** Zero control transfers with bmRequestType 0x21 and bRequest 0x0A (SET_IDLE) or 0x0B (SET_PROTOCOL), on any interface of PID 0x1977 or PID 0x1978.
- **Cite:** Host-stack behaviour; no binary address. Registered because a stray class request is easy to mistake for a vendor command.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any SET_IDLE or SET_PROTOCOL transfer appears.
- **Matters:** If Windows does send one and the device depends on it, a macOS host that does not send it could see different device behaviour — a difference we would otherwise never think to look for.

#### 15. `ms-os-string-once` — [G]

**A GET_DESCRIPTOR STRING with wValue 0x03EE (the Microsoft OS string descriptor) is requested for PID 0x1977 on the first ever attach on that machine and not on later runs.**

- **Expect:** Present in the first capture, absent in a second capture on the same Windows install. It may be STALLed by the device; a STALL is the expected and harmless outcome.
- **Cite:** Host-stack behaviour; no binary address.
- **REFUTED IF:** The 0x03EE request appears on every run, or never appears on a first-ever attach.
- **Matters:** Two captures of the same operation will otherwise differ for no protocol reason and someone will spend an afternoon on it.

#### 16. `rescan-schedule` — [D]

**The bootloader search is 10 enumeration passes on a fixed schedule and it gives up 22.5 s after it starts.**

- **Expect:** Enumeration attempts at cumulative t = 0, 0, 500, 1500, 3000, 5000, 7500, 10500, 14000, 18000 ms (sleeps of 0, 500, 1000, 1500, 2000, 2500, 3000, 3500, 4000, 4500 ms after each failure). Abandonment at t = 22500 ms exactly. The cmpl $0x2710 (10000) bound at 0x403822 is dead code — the cmpl $0x1194 (4500) test at 0x403814 always fires first.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x4037f5–0x403828 (first loop) and 0x4038cf–0x4038f9 (second loop): identical structure — esi starts 0, Sleep(esi), je 0x403875 / 0x40392c when esi == 0x1194, addl $0x1f4, jl $0x2710.
- **REFUTED IF:** A failing run abandons the search at a wall-clock time materially different from 22.5 s after the last A1 3A, or the failure message appears earlier.
- **Matters:** 22.5 s is the vendor's own budget for how long a mode switch may take. Our post-erase code must not give up sooner, and CLAUDE.md §4.2 says it must not give up at all.

#### 17. `readiness-is-enumeration-only` — [D]

**The tool decides the bootloader is ready purely from successful enumeration. It sends no probe, no ping, no version query, and reads nothing from the bootloader before its first real command.**

- **Expect:** Zero HID class requests to PID 0x1977 between the end of Windows' enumeration of it and the first SET_REPORT with wValue 0x03A0. The only thing between them in the code is sleeping.
- **Cite:** fw110 success path is straight-line: 0x4038e0 jne 0x4038fb → 0x4038fb pushl $0x3e8; call *%edi → 0x403902 pushl $0x3e8; call *%edi → 0x403909 → 0x40390f calll 0x403960, whose first act (0x4039a0–0x4039ae) is FUN_00401890 = A0 03. Search space: every instruction from 0x4038cf to 0x403960 plus 0x403960–0x4039ae, read from raw bytes. Encoding form not covered: a call to 0x4012a0 through a register would be invisible to an E8 sweep; the only indirect call on this path is *%edi, resolved from the import table to KERNEL32!Sleep (0x51b214).
- **REFUTED IF:** Any class request reaches PID 0x1977 before the first 1041-byte A0 SET_REPORT.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.2 wants us to 'round-trip a benign query' before proceeding. The vendor does not, so that guard is entirely ours to invent — and the capture cannot tell us what a benign bootloader query even looks like.

#### 18. `two-second-gap` — [D]

**At least 2000 ms of deliberate sleeping separates the tool finding the bootloader from its first command to it, on both internal paths.**

- **Expect:** The interval from the completion of Windows' enumeration of PID 0x1977 to the first A0 03 SET_REPORT is ≥ 2000 ms and, in a healthy run, ≤ about 6.5 s. Two Sleep(1000) calls account for the floor.
- **Cite:** fw110 path A: 0x4038fb pushl $0x3e8; call *%edi immediately followed by 0x403902 pushl $0x3e8; call *%edi — two consecutive Sleep(1000) with no branch between them. Path B: 0x40382a Sleep(1000) → 0x403831 enumerate(0x1977) → 0x40383e cmpl $0x1; je 0x403902 → Sleep(1000).
- **REFUTED IF:** The first A0 03 arrives less than 2000 ms after the bootloader finishes enumerating.
- **Matters:** It is the only settling delay the vendor gives a freshly-entered bootloader. If we shorten it we are deviating; if the capture shows the device needs even longer, we lengthen it.

#### 19. `path-b-second-enumeration` — [D]

**If the A1 3A read failed, the tool confirms the bootloader identity a SECOND time before flashing; if the read succeeded, it does not.**

- **Expect:** Not directly visible on the wire (enumeration is silent), but it shifts the floor timing: path B is Sleep(1000) → a full SetupDi scan → Sleep(1000), path A is Sleep(1000) → Sleep(1000) back to back. A scan costs tens of ms, so path B's gap runs measurably longer than 2000 ms while path A's sits just above it.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x40382a Sleep(1000); 0x403831 pushl $0x1977; 0x403836 calll 0x401000; 0x40383e cmpl $0x1,%eax; je 0x403902 — versus path A which reaches 0x403902 directly from 0x4038fb. notes/updater-protocol.md §5.3 step 3 describes only path B and does not say that path A skips the re-confirm; that note needs correcting.
- **REFUTED IF:** The measured gap is indistinguishable between a run whose GET succeeded and one whose GET failed, over several runs.
- **Matters:** The vendor's identity re-check is conditional on an unrelated failure. Ours should be unconditional, and knowing the vendor's is conditional is why.

#### 20. `first-bootloader-frame-a0-03` — [D]

**The first command the bootloader ever receives is a 1041-byte A0 03, and its byte 16 and bytes 17-20 are predictable to the exact value.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT, bmRequestType 0x21, bRequest 0x09, wValue 0x03A0, wIndex 0x0001, wLength 0x0411. Data: A0 03 then 14 zero bytes (offsets 2–15), then [16]=0x41 (65 blocks), [17]=0x7D [18]=0xD5 [19]=0x81 [20]=0x00 (little-endian 0x0081D57D), then 1020 zero bytes.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00401890: memset 0x411 at 0x4018bc; movl $0x3a0 at 0x4018e9; movl $0x0,-0x454 at 0x4018f3; movb %cl,-0x448 (offset 16) at 0x4018c4; offsets 17–20 written at 0x4018ce / 0x4018fd / 0x401903 / 0x401909; call site 0x4039a0–0x4039ae supplies (byte)dlg+0x21c and dlg+0x25a20. Block count 65 and remainder 0 from 0x403242 shrl $0xa on a 66560-byte resource. Checksum = plain 32-bit additive byte sum computed at 0x403580; I extracted FWFILE resource 140 from this exact .exe (sha256 97cd23d8…) and summed it: 0x0081D57D.
- **REFUTED IF:** Byte [16] is not 0x41, or bytes [17..20] are not 7D D5 81 00.
- **Matters:** This is the highest-value line in the document. If [17..20] read D6 16 80 00 the tool is loading FWFILE 142, not 140, and CLAUDE.md §1.4 plus notes §8.2 are wrong about which image reaches our one mouse. Full discriminator for this build: 140→7D D5 81 00, 142→D6 16 80 00, 143→37 19 81 00, 133→BE BF 81 00, 135→DA DB 83 00, 137→C7 C2 80 00.

#### 21. `a0-03-retry-and-3s-sleep` — [D]

**Each A0 03 attempt is followed by a 3000 ms sleep and then a single 64-byte A1 read, and the whole thing is retried at most 5 times.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT(0x03A0, 1041) → 3000 ms → GET_REPORT(0x03A1, 64). On failure, sleeps of 100, 200, 300, 400, 500 ms between attempts, 5 attempts maximum, then the status line reads 'send bldr start request failed'.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401917 pushl $0xbb8; 0x40191e call Sleep; 0x40193a–0x401951 memset 0x40, movb $0xa1 into buf[0], calll 0x401330 with pushl $0x40. Retry loop 0x403996 movl $0x64,%ebx; 0x4039ba pushl %ebx; call *%edi; 0x4039bd addl $0x64,%ebx; 0x4039c0 cmpl $0x1f4,%ebx; jle.
- **REFUTED IF:** The gap between the A0 03 SET_REPORT and the following GET_REPORT is not approximately 3.0 s, or a 6th A0 03 attempt appears.
- **Matters:** 3 s is the longest single wait in the whole protocol and is the strongest hint that A0 03 is where the erase happens — the erase boundary that notes §10.2 records as still unknown.

#### 22. `find-device-is-instant-normally` — [D]

**The pre-bootloader device search costs nothing observable when the mouse is present and healthy.**

- **Expect:** FUN_00403600 finds PID 0x1978 on its very first enumeration and returns with mode=1 and zero sleeps, so the A1 3A follows the button click within milliseconds. The retry ladders in it — 500/1000/1500 ms, then 11 alternating passes at 3000, 2700, 2400 … 300, 0 ms (16.5 s total, 6 passes at PID 0x1977 and 5 at PID 0x1978) — never execute in a normal run.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403603 pushl $0x1978; 0x403608 calll 0x401000; 0x403612 jne 0x403739 → *mode = 1, immediate return. Ladders at 0x40363e–0x403667 and 0x40366d–0x403702 (edi = 10 counting down to 0, parity at 0x403682 selects the PID, esi 3000 decreasing by 300 at 0x4036fc, exit on esi == 0 at 0x4036f7). notes/updater-protocol.md §5.2 says '10 passes' for the second ladder; the raw code gives 11 (edi = 10…0) — that note needs correcting.
- **REFUTED IF:** There is a delay of seconds between the button click and the A1 3A frame in a run where the mouse is plainly present.
- **Matters:** If the capture shows a long pre-command delay, our model of which enumeration branch ran is wrong, and that branch is the one that decides whether the tool would have flashed an already-bootloadered device.

### Rejected by audit (10) — not pre-registered

| id | fault | auditor's evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `failure-string-discriminates-path` | `contradicts-notes` | The claim 'A run showing exactly one A1 3A frame followed by "send bldr request failed" is self-contradictory and means our reading of the loop is wrong' is false on the code, and it contradicts this same document's a1-3a-count-one. 0x403875 is reached only from 0x40381a, i.e. after the 9-iteration loop exhausts. An iteration falls to the next one at 0x4037e9 either because HidD_SetFeature returned 0 (je at 0x4037b7) OR because HidD_GetFeature returned 0 (jne not taken at 0x4037e3). Exhaustion therefore does NOT imply nine GET_REPORTs were attempted. The document's own a1-3a-count-one predicts precisely the case that breaks it: 'The remaining loop iterations fail inside HidD_SetFeature on a stale handle and put nothing on the wire.' Under that model a device that takes A1 3A, detaches and never returns produces exactly one A1 3A frame, exactly one GET_REPORT, and then 'send bldr request failed' -- entirely consistent with the code. The exact_expectation's implication ('send bldr request failed => all 9 GET_REPORTs after A1 3A failed') is wrong for the same reason. As written, the most likely real failure capture would be misread as evidence that the loop analysis is broken. Separately, the framing that the two messages discriminate the internal path is incomplete: there is a third failure exit at 0x403847 that emits no message at all. |
| `third-vendor-collection-silent` | `citation-wrong` | The citation states 'litscan finds zero 0xff02 immediates in fw110 or fw104'. I ran Tools/ghidra-export/litscan.py: 4-byte immediates are 0 in both, but 2-byte encodings are n=181 in fw110 and n=149 in fw104. The committed source, notes/device-predictions.md, states it correctly as 'zero 4-byte 0xff02 immediates'; the document drops the qualifier and the unqualified version is false. This is the exact failure CLAUDE.md 1.2a names ('A scan for push imm32 that finds nothing says nothing about movl $imm32'), and the document applies that discipline correctly elsewhere (readiness-is-enumeration-only names its blind spot explicitly), so the omission is inconsistent within the document as well as with the notes. The prediction is also tagged [O] while its content is a claim about future device behaviour -- that the device will not emit report id 0x08 during the capture. No device observation supports that; the [O] evidence covers only the descriptor's existence. Compare bldr-loses-mouse-function, which is the same shape of claim and is honestly tagged [G]. |
| `a1-3a-interface-1` | `mistagged` | Tagged [O], but the observation behind it cannot be an application-mode observation. The VID 0x3367 device attached right now is idProduct 6519 = 0x1977 with USB Product Name 'Bootloader' (ioreg, read this session). Its interface numbering does match what the document reports (if 0: protocol 2, 1 endpoint; if 1: protocol 1, 1 endpoint), but that is the bootloader. The only stored application-mode observation, .analysis/hidobserve_3367.json (ProductID 6520 = 0x1978, VersionNumber 263), contains the 156-byte and 69-byte report descriptors and no bInterfaceNumber field whatsoever, and the only ioreg dump in .analysis is ioreg_bootloader_1977_20260904.txt. So the claim 'the command goes to USB interface 1' -- about the PID 0x1978 device the A1 3A is sent to -- has no application-mode [O] behind it, and the document never says which mode its ioreg reading came from. Additionally the headline number, wIndex = 0x0001, is a HID-spec convention (class requests carry the interface number in wIndex), not something ioreg can show; under CLAUDE.md 1.2 that is not [O]. This matters because 1.3 keys a write decision off the tag and wIndex is a byte that reaches hardware. |
