| `bldr-feature-report-lengths` | `contradicts-notes` | Tagged [G] and its matters_because treats the bootloader's declared feature lengths as unknown ('If the bootloader declares a different maximum, macOS will reject the write... after the point of no return'). notes/bootloader-observed.md section 3, committed at HEAD (68fb508) and not listed in sources_read, records exactly this as [O]: the bootloader's 0xFF01/0x02 collection declares 0xA1 at 8x63 = 64 on the wire and 0xA0 at 128x65 = 1041 on the wire, with MaxFeatureReportSize = 1041, and states 'Both hardcoded lengths are now [O]-confirmed against the device.' The same section says it closes notes/updater-protocol.md 6.1, which is the open question this prediction is written against. Registering an [O] fact as a [G] prediction, without saying the repo already holds it, understates the evidence on a preflight-relevant safety property and spends irreversible capture budget on something already settled. |
| `bldr-report-descriptor-ff01-02` | `contradicts-notes` | Tagged [G] on the reasoning that the collection must exist 'otherwise the vendor tool could never find it'. notes/bootloader-observed.md section 4 records it [O]: the bootloader's 156-byte descriptor carries the same three vendor collections (0xFF01/0x02, 0xFF02/0x01, 0xFF02/0x02) as the running application. Worse, the matters_because directly contradicts that note's operational warning. It says the 0xFF01/0x02 filter 'is precisely the filter our macOS enumerator must apply after the mode switch, and it is CLAUDE.md 4.2's confirm device identity again afterwards made concrete.' Section 4 says the opposite in terms: 'usage page and usage do not distinguish the two modes. Only ProductID and the Product string do. Any code that finds the device by usage alone will happily talk to the wrong mode.' Recommending the usage filter as the post-switch identity re-check is the specific mistake the committed note exists to prevent, and it is recommended here without acknowledging the note. |
| `bldr-loses-mouse-function` | `contradicts-notes` | The citation reads 'Inference only -- the application firmware that drives the sensor is the thing being replaced. No address supports it', and the exact_expectation says 'Whether a Generic-Desktop/Mouse interface exists at all in bootloader mode is not predicted.' notes/bootloader-observed.md section 4 answers that [O]: the bootloader 'presents a full application-shaped descriptor' and 'its first interface still presents a working mouse.' I confirmed the descriptor side live: the attached PID 0x1977 device does expose interface 0 with bInterfaceProtocol = 2. The [G] tag on the emission claim is defensible, but declaring the existence question unpredictable, and citing 'no address supports it' while a committed [O] note bears directly on it, is a consistency failure -- and it is the same ioreg dump the document elsewhere leans on for a1-3a-interface-1. |
| `cannot_predict[3] (bootloader descriptor fields)` | `contradicts-notes` | This entry claims the binaries cannot answer, and the capture is 'the only realistic way to get', a list that explicitly includes 'bcdDevice', 'whether a mouse interface still exists', and 'iManufacturer / iProduct / iSerialNumber strings'. At least four of those are already [O] in notes/bootloader-observed.md sections 2 and 4: VersionNumber 0x0006, Manufacturer 'EGG', Product 'Bootloader', and a first interface that still presents a mouse. The justification given -- 'no address bears on any of them' -- is true about the .exe files and irrelevant, because the evidence in the repo is device observation, not derivation. A pre-registration that files already-observed facts as unanswerable will read, after the capture, as though the capture discovered them. |
| `rescan-invisible` | `mistagged` | Tagged [D], but the load-bearing half of the claim is not derived from the .exe. The citation correctly inventories the API calls (I verified all eight against 0x401000-0x401189: HidD_GetHidGuid at 0x401025, SetupDiGetClassDevsW flags 0x12 at 0x401032, SetupDiEnumDeviceInterfaces, SetupDiGetDeviceInterfaceDetailW, CreateFileW with 0xC0000000/share 3/OPEN_EXISTING, HidD_GetAttributes, HidD_GetPreparsedData, HidP_GetCaps). But the conclusion -- 'Every one is served from cached descriptors; none issues a USB request' -- is Windows host-stack behaviour, with no address behind it, and it is a negative claim about traffic. CLAUDE.md 1.2a requires the search space and blind spots to be stated for exactly this shape of claim. The document tags four other host-stack predictions [G] (host-enumeration-sequence, no-set-address-visible, no-set-idle-set-protocol, ms-os-string-once), so the [D] here is inconsistent with its own convention. |
| `a1-3a-setup-packet` | `mistagged` | Tagged [D] with a citation that reaches only as far as 'HidD_SetFeature(handle, buf, 64)'. Nothing in fw110 encodes bmRequestType = 0x21, bRequest = 0x09, wValue = 0x03A1 or wLength = 0x0040 as a setup packet; those are the Windows HID minidriver's translation plus the HID spec, and the document's own matters_because concedes this ('It is the mapping from HidD_SetFeature(buf,64) to actual USB'). Only the 0x40 length and the 0xA1 report id are [D]. wIndex is explicitly deferred to the interface-numbering claim, which is itself mistagged. Under CLAUDE.md 1.2 this is [G]-with-[D]-components, and 1.3 makes the distinction consequential because these are the bytes our IOHIDDeviceSetReport call has to reproduce. |
| `get-report-follows-2ms` | `contradicts-notes` | The refuted_if includes 'No GET_REPORT follows the SET_REPORT', but the document's own cannot_predict entry 1 registers precisely that as an expected possible outcome: 'Check whether a GET_REPORT wValue 0x03A1 completes with 64 bytes of data after the single A1 3A. If it does, path A; if it errors or never reaches the bus, path B.' A pre-registration whose refutation condition is a scenario it separately predicts may legitimately occur cannot be scored: the same observation would simultaneously refute one entry and confirm another. Either the refuted_if should be narrowed to 'no GET_REPORT is issued even though HidD_SetFeature returned success', or cannot_predict entry 1 should drop 'never reaches the bus'. The underlying code cite is correct (0x4037b3 Sleep(2), 0x4037c4 memset 0x40, 0x4037d2 movb $0xa1 into buf[0], 0x4037d9 call 0x401330, 0x4037e1 testl/jne); the defect is in the scoring rule, not the derivation. |

### Cannot predict — what the capture will teach us (9)

- **Does the device answer the GET_REPORT that follows A1 3A (path A), or has it already gone (path B)?**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The branch at fw110 0x4037e1 depends entirely on whether HidD_GetFeature succeeds about 2 ms after the command, which is a property of the mouse's firmware — how long it waits before detaching, and whether it answers a feature read while switching. Nothing in any .exe encodes that. It forks two materially different code paths (one re-confirms the bootloader identity, the other does not) and picking either would be a guess.
  - how a capture answers it: Check whether a GET_REPORT wValue 0x03A1 completes with 64 bytes of data after the single A1 3A. If it does, path A; if it errors or never reaches the bus, path B. The failure-message prediction gives a second, independent read on the same fork.
- **What 64 bytes does the device return in response to A1 3A?**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The updater reads the response and looks at nothing in it — the test at fw110 0x4037e1 is testl %eax,%eax on the wrapper's return value, and no byte of the buffer at -0x84(%ebp) is ever loaded afterwards. notes/updater-protocol.md §3.7b records this as the only response the tool takes zero fields from, so the content is unconstrained by any code we can read.
  - how a capture answers it: If path A occurs, the capture hands us 64 bytes of entirely new information — most usefully byte [1], which would say whether the device uses the same 0x01-ready / 0x04-busy convention for the mode switch. This is the single most informative thing in this area that cannot be derived at all.
- **Everything about the bootloader's descriptors except the 0xFF01/0x02 collection and the two feature report lengths: bcdDevice, bMaxPacketSize0, bcdUSB, number of interfaces, whether a mouse interface still exists, endpoint count and type, iManufacturer / iProduct / iSerialNumber strings, bConfigurationValue, MaxPower.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The updater reads only VendorID, ProductID, VersionNumber (which it stores but never uses for the bootloader) and the usage pair. Every other descriptor field is invisible to it, so no address bears on any of them.
  - how a capture answers it: Windows fetches the full descriptor set during re-enumeration, so the capture contains the bootloader's device descriptor, configuration descriptor and every report descriptor verbatim. No extra tool is needed — and it is the only realistic way to get them, since the bootloader window is about two seconds wide before flashing starts.
- **How long the device takes to detach and re-attach, and whether it is a clean detach or a bus-visible reset.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The tool's tolerance is 22.5 s, which says the vendor expected it to be slow. That is a bound the vendor chose, not a measurement of the device.
  - how a capture answers it: Timestamp from the A1 3A status stage to the first GET_DESCRIPTOR of the new device. It sets the timeout our own macOS re-enumeration loop needs after the mode switch.
- **Whether the A1 3A control transfer is ACKed or STALLed by the device.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: HidD_SetFeature's boolean return cannot distinguish a STALL from a device that vanished mid-transfer, and the wrapper at 0x4012a0 branches only on the five retryable error codes — ERROR_GEN_FAILURE (0x1F, the usual STALL mapping) is not among them, so the code cannot even tell us which the vendor expected.
  - how a capture answers it: USBPcap records the URB status. A STALL that still enters the bootloader would mean our code must not treat a failed set-report as a failed mode switch — exactly the mistake that would produce nine redundant A1 3A frames.
- **Whether the magic bytes 5A A5 32 are actually required, or whether A1 3A alone would enter the bootloader, and whether a wrong magic is rejected.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The tool always sends the same constant, in both code bases (fw104 builds the identical frame at 0x404760). There is no variant anywhere to compare against.
  - how a capture answers it: It cannot. Answering it needs a deliberate write of a modified frame to the device, which we have no reason to make. Registered so that 'the tool always sends it' is never quietly upgraded to 'the device requires it'.
- **Whether the bootloader survives a power cycle — if the mouse is unplugged while in bootloader mode with the application intact, does it come back at PID 0x1978 or PID 0x1977?**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: No updater tests or reports it. It is the recovery question CLAUDE.md §5 lists as device-gated.
  - how a capture answers it: A capture alone does not answer it. It needs a separate experiment — enter the bootloader, kill the updater before A0 03, unplug, replug, observe the PID — which carries real risk and should be decided on its own merits, not bundled with the capture.
- **Whether the application firmware would also accept A1 3A on interface 0, or on the 0xFF02 collections of interface 1.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The tool only ever opens the collection matching 0xFF01/0x02, so no address speaks to any other target.
  - how a capture answers it: It cannot — a capture shows only what the vendor tool did. Noted because a reader could otherwise conclude that interface 1 is the only interface that works, when all the capture shows is the only one that was tried.
- **Whether Windows would pad a short feature SET_REPORT to the collection's maximum feature length on a different Windows version or driver stack.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: Host-stack behaviour, not vendor code.
  - how a capture answers it: One capture settles it for one machine; a second Windows build would be needed to generalise. The prediction above commits to no padding, so a single capture at least makes the claim falsifiable.

### Gaps the auditor flagged (6)

- The exact bytes of the bootloader's report descriptor. notes/bootloader-observed.md sections 3-5 record the button-forced bootloader's 156-byte descriptor [O], including a one-byte open gap at offset 35 (LogicalMinimum 1 vs 0) that the note explicitly asks to be re-checked. A USBPcap capture of the re-enumeration contains that descriptor verbatim, for free. The document predicts only that it contains '06 01 FF 09 02 A1 01'. Predicting all 156 bytes costs nothing and would (a) validate the capture rig, (b) close the open descriptor-diff gap, and (c) answer a question nobody in the repo has asked: whether the bootloader reached by A1 3A is the same bootloader as the one reached by the button hold. The post-erase recovery argument in CLAUDE.md 4.2 and notes 10.2 rests on those being the same device; the capture settles it at zero marginal cost and this is the single most wasteful omission given the capture is irreversible.
- The bootloader's self-identification: Manufacturer 'EGG', Product 'Bootloader', VersionNumber 0x0006 -- all [O] in notes/bootloader-observed.md section 2. CLAUDE.md 4.2 requires preflight to 'refuse anything unrecognised' if the bootloader reports an identity, so these are exactly the strings our flasher will match on. The capture will contain the GET_DESCRIPTOR STRING responses for iManufacturer/iProduct and the bcdDevice field. The document instead lists these under cannot_predict.
- The up-to-4x inner retry inside FUN_004012a0. nine-attempt-signature commits to 'exactly 9' A1 3A frames and a 4/8/12...ms gap schedule, but each of the nine outer iterations can issue up to 4 HidD_SetFeature calls with Sleep(10) between them whenever GetLastError is one of the five retryable codes (0x4012e9-0x40131e, verified). The failure signature is therefore 9 to 36 frames with gaps inflated by up to 30 ms per iteration. The document cites that retry set elsewhere (a1-3a-count-one) but never reconciles it, so a failing capture showing, say, 12 or 18 frames would be scored as refuting a correct model.
- The third failure exit of FUN_00403750, at 0x403847, which produces NO status message. Verified: it is reached by fall-through when path B's second enumerate (0x403836) returns anything other than 1; it re-enables the button, clears the busy flag at +0x25a38, and returns 0 without pushing a string or calling the status-text setter at 0x4135fa. My exhaustive branch-edge scan confirms it has no incoming branch, only the fall-through. notes/updater-protocol.md 11.2 also omits it. A capture that ends in silence is a real, reachable outcome and the document's two-message discriminator gives the operator no way to recognise it.
- Both interfaces declare bNumEndpoints = 1 [O] (I confirmed this live), so there is no OUT endpoint on either interface. That is an independent and stronger basis for no-output-reports-ever's 'zero OUT transfers on any endpoint' than the import-table argument, and the document lists bNumEndpoints in its own sources_read without using it.
- A precondition the capture plan needs and does not state: the mouse is at this moment enumerated at PID 0x1977 in bootloader mode. capture_requirements bullet 2 correctly says the run must start from application mode, but the repo records neither how the device was put into the bootloader (notes/bootloader-observed.md flags 'WHICH BUTTON IS NOT YET RECORDED' as its most important gap) nor how to get it back to application mode. Without that, the capture cannot be started at all, and the one-shot 1.07 observation the document is built around is at risk.

### Irreversible / one-shot in this area

- THE VENDOR TOOL HAS NO 'ENTER BOOTLOADER ONLY' MODE. Pressing Update runs enter-bootloader and the full erase-and-flash as one sequence: FUN_00403750 falls straight through to FUN_00403960 at 0x40390f with only two Sleep(1000) calls in between. Capturing bootloader entry with the vendor tool therefore means capturing a complete firmware flash. There is no way to get the A1 3A exchange on its own.
- THE 1.07 FIRMWARE'S RESPONSE TO A1 3A CAN ONLY BE OBSERVED ONCE. After the flash the device is at 1.10 and this transition is gone forever — the response bytes, the detach latency, whether it answers the GET at +2 ms. Bootloader entry from 1.10 can be re-captured any number of times afterwards (the tool has no version gate; it will re-flash 1.10 over 1.10), so only the 1.07 behaviour is at stake. Get the capture right the first time.
- A NORMAL COMPLETION ENDS WITH A FACTORY RESET. fw110 0x403dcf builds A1 13 and 0x403ddd sends it after the update succeeds; that frame is byte-for-byte the config tool's Factory Reset (cfg107 0x40479f, same 7-byte encoding c7 45 b0 a1 13 00 00). All user settings — DPI stages, polling rate, button bindings, lift-off distance, lighting — will be wiped. Record every setting from the vendor config tool BEFORE the capture, by screenshot at minimum. We have no config tool of our own yet, so there is no backup to restore from.
- Once flashed, bcdDevice becomes 0x0110 and the baseline in notes/device-predictions.md ('the device is at firmware 1.07', bcdDevice 0x0107) is stale. Re-run the ioreg observation afterwards and update that file, or a later session will reason from a version the device no longer has. The upside: 0x0107 → 0x0110 re-tests the BCD-not-division decode on a second data point for free.
- The updater leaks a Win32 handle for every HID device on the machine and never closes them (fw110 0x4010e8 stores each CreateFileW result to the global 0x56a174 with no CloseHandle anywhere in FUN_00401000; the success path at 0x40118a also skips SetupDiDestroyDeviceInfoList). Harmless for a tool that exits, but while it runs it holds GENERIC_READ|GENERIC_WRITE handles to the laptop's own keyboard and touchpad. Do not run it alongside anything else needing exclusive HID access, and close it when the capture is done.


---

## Updater 1.10 — the start command, its computed arguments, the 3 s wait

*area key: `updater-start` — 22 written, 6 rejected*


### Pre-registered (17)

#### 1. `a003-frame-exact-bytes` — [D]

**The bootloader-start command is one 1041-byte HID Feature report with exactly six non-zero bytes.**

- **Expect:** Length 1041 (0x411). buf[0]=0xA0, buf[1]=0x03, buf[16]=0x41, buf[17]=0x7D, buf[18]=0xD5, buf[19]=0x81. Every other byte — buf[2..15], buf[20], buf[21..1040] — is 0x00 (buf[20] is the checksum's top byte and it is genuinely zero). First 32 bytes: A0 03 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 41 7D D5 81 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00. SHA-256 of the whole 1041-byte frame = e4811bc6261dbf256a5707f6487043978fbd588fbffe15abd0da3d79be7e801e; MD5 = 7f5252f079d5748b274b1dfd16296eb2.
- **Cite:** fw110: memset(base=-0x458(%ebp),0,0x411) @0x4018bc; movl $0x3a0,-0x458 @0x4018e9 (bytes [0..3]); movl $0x0,-0x454 @0x4018f3 ([4..7]); movb %cl,-0x448 @0x4018c4 ([16]); movb %bl,-0x447 @0x4018ce ([17]); movb %dl,-0x446 @0x4018fd ([18]); movb %al,-0x445 @0x401903 ([19]); movb %bl,-0x444 @0x401909 ([20]); pushl $0x411 @0x4018e0, calll 0x4012a0 @0x40190f. notes/updater-protocol.md §3.2.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any byte of the observed SET_REPORT payload differs from the 1041 bytes above — a seventh non-zero byte anywhere, a different value at offsets 16/17/18/19, or any of buf[2..15] non-zero.
- **Matters:** This is the literal frame EGGFlashCore emits at what we have declared the point of no return (notes §10.2). It is the golden-file byte stream; one wrong byte here is the whole project.

#### 2. `a003-block-count-byte` — [D]

**buf[16] carries the block count as a single byte, and its value is 0x41 = 65.**

- **Expect:** buf[16] = 0x41. Rule: buf[16] = (SizeofResource(FWFILE/140) >> 10) & 0xFF, truncated twice — once by movzbl at the call site and again by movb in the builder. For 1.10's FWFILE/140 the size is 66560, 66560>>10 = 65, and 65 & 0xFF = 0x41.
- **Cite:** fw110: block_count computed 0x403242 (shrl $0xa) and stored 0x403245 (movl %ecx,0x21c(%esi)); reloaded as a byte 0x4039a0 (movzbl 0x21c(%esi),%eax) and pushed 0x4039ad; taken as buf[16] at 0x4018c1 (movb 0x8(%ebp),%cl) / 0x4018c4. The only other write to that member anywhere in the vendor band [0x401000,0x4040ad) is 0x403386 (addl %ebx), the remainder-tail increment, and that path is unreachable because 66560 % 1024 == 0 (test at 0x403332).
- **REFUTED IF:** buf[16] is anything other than 0x41 — in particular 0x00, 0x40, or a 16-bit count split across buf[16..17].
- **Matters:** The count and the block indices must be derived from one counter (notes §10.3 invariant 4). If the device were shown a different count than the 65 blocks that follow, our single-counter invariant would be wrong.

#### 3. `a003-checksum-bytes` — [D]

**buf[17..20] carry the host-computed whole-image checksum as a little-endian 32-bit value, and for this image it is 0x0081D57D.**

- **Expect:** buf[17]=0x7D, buf[18]=0xD5, buf[19]=0x81, buf[20]=0x00. The arithmetic, redo it exactly: take PE resource type FWFILE (a STRING-typed resource type), name 140, lang 2052 from 'Endgame Gear OP1 8k v2 Firmware Updater 1.10.exe' — it sits at RVA 0x19d950, is 66560 bytes, SHA-256 8148ebe9f8d2848abe483aee98df6e42bab341c6a17523bfef0f85f1f66754d0. Add all 66560 byte values as unsigned 8-bit ints: total = 8,508,797 = 0x0081D57D. No masking is needed (8.5e6 is far below 2^32). Emit that dword little-endian starting at offset 17. Cross-check: the sum of the 65 individual 1024-byte block sums equals the same 8,508,797.
- **Cite:** fw110: FindResourceW(NULL,0x8c,L"FWFILE") built at 0x403207/0x40320c/0x403211, called *0x51b230 @0x403213; SizeofResource *0x51b220 @0x40323a; FUN_00403580 called 0x40338e and its result stored to 0x25a20(%esi) @0x403393; loaded into ECX at 0x4039a7 and split into bytes at 0x4018ce/0x4018fd/0x401903/0x401909. That member 0x25a20(%esi) has exactly three references in all 1,154,048 bytes of .text — the write at 0x403393, the read at 0x4039a7, the compare at 0x403b95 — so nothing else can alter it.
- **REFUTED IF:** buf[17..20] is not 7D D5 81 00. Big-endian (00 81 D5 7D) refutes the byte order; 08 D4 81 00 means updater 1.07 was run, 0B F4 81 00 means 1.06, 27 F6 81 00 means 1.04; anything else means the checksum rule is not a plain byte sum.
- **Matters:** This value is the only quantity our flasher must compute rather than copy, and the device compares it later (A1 08). Getting the width, endianness or the summed range wrong makes every flash fail the final gate — or worse, pass it against the wrong bytes.

#### 4. `checksum-algorithm` — [D]

**The checksum is a plain 32-bit additive sum of every byte of the image, not a CRC, not a hash, and not a 16-bit sum.**

- **Expect:** sum = (Σ image[i] for i in 0..66559) mod 2^32, computed over block_count × 1024 = 66560 bytes starting at the loaded-image buffer. The vendor unrolls it into four accumulators over 256 iterations per 1024-byte chunk (bytes at chunk+0, +1, +2, +3), resets the three secondary accumulators at each chunk boundary and folds them into the running total, but the result is arithmetically identical to a flat byte sum — verified by re-implementing the loop literally and comparing (both give 0x0081D57D).
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00403580 @0x403580: loop count from 0x21c(%edx) @0x403586; edx += 0x221 @0x403596 (so the first movzbl -0x1(%edx,%ecx) reads image[0]); inner loop 0x4035b0–0x4035d6 with cmpl $0x400 @0x4035d0; fold 0x4035d8–0x4035df; edx += 0x400 @0x4035e2; return -0x4(%ebp) @0x4035ed. notes/updater-protocol.md §8.4.
- **REFUTED IF:** The 32-bit field at buf[17..20] does not equal the flat byte sum of the resource — for example if it matched a CRC32, a 16-bit sum zero-extended, or a sum over only part of the image.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §2's posture rests on this: a plain additive sum can only ever prove received == sent, never that the image is the right image. If it turned out to be a keyed or structured check, that whole safety argument would need rewriting.

#### 5. `three-second-wait` — [D]

**There is a 3000 ms gap between the A0 03 SET_REPORT and the GET_REPORT that reads its answer, and no host-originated traffic occurs inside it.**

- **Expect:** Sleep(3000) is issued immediately after HidD_SetFeature returns and before the response buffer is even zeroed, so the wire gap from completion of the SET_REPORT to the SETUP of the following GET_REPORT is >= 3000 ms and, with Windows' default 15.6 ms timer granularity, <= about 3020 ms. Inside that window the tool issues no SET_REPORT and no GET_REPORT on any report id. There is exactly ONE `pushl $0xbb8` in all 1,154,048 bytes of fw110's .text, and it is this one.
- **Cite:** fw110: calll 0x4012a0 @0x40190f; addl $0x14,%esp @0x401914; pushl $0xbb8 @0x401917; calll *0x51b214 (Sleep, confirmed from the import table) @0x40191e; only then testl %esi,%esi @0x401924, memset(-0x44,0,0x40) @0x40193a, movb $0xa1,-0x44(%ebp) @0x40194d, calll 0x401330 @0x401951. fw104 does the same at 0x404a59. Search-space note: `pushl $0xbb8` is one encoding form only; a whole-disassembly scan for the operand 0xbb8 in ANY form finds a second 3000 in the vendor band, movl $0xbb8,%esi @0x403672, which is FUN_00403600's device-search sleep and is not on this path.
- **REFUTED IF:** The measured gap is under 2990 ms or over 3200 ms, or any vendor feature report appears between the two transfers.
- **Matters:** Our flasher must reproduce this wait. If it is shorter in practice the device may not be ready; if the device is in fact ready sooner, that is a separate finding the capture cannot give us (see cannot_predict).

#### 6. `exactly-one-a003-on-success` — [D]

**On a successful update there is exactly one A0 03 frame in the entire capture.**

- **Expect:** Count of SET_REPORTs whose payload begins A0 03 == 1. The retry loop exits on the first attempt whose returned status byte is 0x01, and nothing else in the binary builds an A0 03 frame — an immediate-store scan of all of .text finds `movl $0x3a0` at exactly one address, and the only two functions that can hand bytes to the device are FUN_004012a0 and FUN_00401330, whose seven call sites each are recovered from a raw E8 sweep of the whole section.
- **Cite:** fw110: je 0x403a0b @0x4039b8 on al==1. Immediate store movl $0x3a0,-0x458(%ebp) @0x4018e9 is the sole such store (Tools/ghidra-export/cmdscan.py, notes §3.7a). Send-wrapper call sites recovered from raw E8 rel32: 0x40190f, 0x401a60, 0x401b26, 0x401bf6, 0x4037a1, 0x403bf2, 0x403ddd. Blind spot stated: this scan sees stored immediates and direct E8 edges; a frame built one byte at a time from a table, or a call through a register, would not appear in it — but any such frame would still have to pass through FUN_004012a0, whose call sites are enumerated exhaustively.
- **REFUTED IF:** Two or more A0 03 frames appear in a run that ends in 'Update Succeed', or an A0 03 appears after the block loop has started.
- **Matters:** Our own flasher must send it exactly once and must never re-send it after the block loop begins. If the vendor re-sends it, our non-abortable region has a shape we have not modelled.

#### 7. `a003-response-frame` — [D]

**The response to A0 03 is fetched on a DIFFERENT report id from the request — a 64-byte Feature report 0xA1 — and the only byte the tool examines is byte 1.**

- **Expect:** GET_REPORT for report id 0xA1, buffer length 64 (0x40), issued 3000 ms after the A0 03 SET_REPORT. Predicted returned bytes: resp[0]=0xA1, resp[1]=0x01. resp[2..63] are not read by the tool at all. The tool's success test is a single `cmpb $0x1,%al` on resp[1] at the call site; the transport wrapper additionally treats resp[1]==0x04 as busy and every other value as not-ready.
- **Cite:** fw110: memset(-0x44,0,0x40) @0x40193a; movb $-0x5f (=0xA1),-0x44(%ebp) @0x40194d; pushl $0x40 @0x40194a; calll 0x401330 @0x401951; movb -0x43(%ebp),%al @0x40195b (that is resp[1], base -0x44); returned; cmpb $0x1,%al @0x4039b6. Busy test cmpb $0x4,0x1(%edi) @0x40135d. notes §3.7b.
- **REFUTED IF:** The response is fetched on report 0xA0 rather than 0xA1, or with a length other than 64, or the byte at offset 1 of the returned data is not 0x01 on a run that proceeds to the block loop.
- **Matters:** The request/response report-id asymmetry is easy to get wrong in a transport layer, and it is the single status byte our preflight has to test.

#### 8. `a1-3a-precedes-and-is-exact` — [D]

**The frame immediately preceding all of this is the bootloader-entry command, a 64-byte report 0xA1 with exactly five non-zero bytes, and it is the last traffic the application-mode device ever sees.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT, report id 0xA1, length 64. buf[0]=0xA1, buf[1]=0x3A, buf[2]=0x00, buf[3]=0x00, buf[4]=0x00, buf[5]=0x5A, buf[6]=0xA5, buf[7]=0x32, buf[8..63]=0x00. Sent to VID 0x3367 / PID 0x1978. Followed after Sleep(2*i) — 2 ms on the first attempt — by a GET_REPORT on report 0xA1, length 64, whose contents the tool does not inspect at all (only whether the call succeeded).
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00403750: memset(-0x44,0,0x40) @0x403788; movl $0x3aa1,-0x44(%ebp) @0x403793 (bytes A1 3A 00 00); movl $0x32a55a00,-0x40(%ebp) @0x40379a (bytes 00 5A A5 32 little-endian at [4..7]); calll 0x4012a0 @0x4037a1; Sleep(2*ebx) @0x4037aa–0x4037b3; movb $-0x5f,-0x84(%ebp) @0x4037d2; calll 0x401330 @0x4037d9; loop bound cmpl $0xa,%ebx @0x4037f0 (ebx = 1..9).
- **REFUTED IF:** The bytes at [5],[6],[7] are not 5A A5 32, or the frame is 1041 bytes rather than 64, or any feature report reaches the device before it.
- **Matters:** It is the landmark that lets the auditor locate A0 03 in the capture, and it is the command our stage-2 bring-up (CLAUDE.md §4.4) sends first.

#### 9. `no-traffic-before-a1-3a` — [D]

**The updater issues zero HID feature reports before the bootloader-entry command — in particular it never asks the device for its firmware version.**

- **Expect:** Between the tool's process start and the A1 3A SET_REPORT there are no SET_REPORT or GET_REPORT transfers on any report id from this process. The version string 'Mouse firmware current version %.2f' is filled from bcdDevice, which Windows already holds from enumeration, not from a command. Only SetupDi enumeration and CreateFileW/HidD_GetAttributes/HidP_GetCaps occur, and none of those generates USB traffic once the device is enumerated.
- **Cite:** fw110: the seven HidD_SetFeature-wrapper call sites recovered by raw E8 sweep are 0x40190f, 0x401a60, 0x401b26, 0x401bf6, 0x4037a1, 0x403bf2, 0x403ddd — none lies inside the Update handler FUN_004033b0 or FUN_00403600. Version path: movl 0x56a0f4,%ecx @0x401245 reads the cached HID VersionNumber, formats with L"%x" at 0x5443b0. notes §1.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any Feature SET_REPORT or GET_REPORT from the updater appears before the A1 3A frame.
- **Matters:** It confirms the version shown to the user comes from bcdDevice and not from a command we have failed to find — an eighth command would break the 'seven commands, complete' claim in notes §3.7a.

#### 10. `gap-a003-to-first-a006` — [D]

**The first block write follows the A0 03 response after a 500 ms pause and nothing else.**

- **Expect:** Sleep(500) between the A0 03 GET_REPORT and the first A0 06 SET_REPORT. That first write is 1041 bytes with buf[0]=0xA0, buf[1]=0x06, buf[2]=0x34, buf[3]=0x00, buf[4..5] = the 16-bit sum of its payload = 0x0273 little-endian (73 02), and buf[16..1039] = the first 1024 bytes of FWFILE/140. Between the A0 03 response and that frame there is no other vendor feature report.
- **Cite:** fw110: pushl $0x1f4 / calll *%edi @0x403a41–0x403a46; block loop head 0x403a48; leal 0x34(%ebx),%ecx @0x403a8b; calll 0x401980 @0x403a8e. Frame layout from FUN_00401980: movw $0x6a0 @0x4019d5, index bytes @0x4019be/0x4019de, payload sum bytes @0x401a54/0x401a5a, rep movsl 0x100 dwords @0x4019ee. Payload sum computed here from the extracted resource's first 1024 bytes.
- **REFUTED IF:** The gap is not 500 ms (+/- one timer tick), the first block index is not 0x34, or buf[4..5] of the first A0 06 is not 73 02.
- **Matters:** It bounds the three-second wait on the far side, and the 0x34 base is the fact notes §3.8 calls the most important derived one.

#### 11. `a003-target-is-bootloader-pid` — [D]

**A0 03 is addressed to the bootloader device, PID 0x1977, never to PID 0x1978.**

- **Expect:** Every A0 03, A0 06, A0 07 and A1 08 frame in the capture is directed at a USB device whose device descriptor reports idVendor 0x3367 and idProduct 0x1977. The only frames on idProduct 0x1978 are A1 3A (before the switch) and A1 13 (after the device comes back). No PID other than 0x1977 and 0x1978 appears — all nine call sites of the enumerator push one of those two literals.
- **Cite:** fw110: FUN_00403750 polls FUN_00401000(0x1977) at 0x403800/0x403805 and 0x4038d1/0x4038d6 and only then calls FUN_00403960 @0x40390f; the handle global 0x56a174 is written by the enumerator at 0x4010e8 and read at all seven send/recv sites. Nine enumerator call sites 0x403608, 0x403649, 0x403696, 0x4036c9, 0x403805, 0x403836, 0x4038d6, 0x403c81, 0x403c97, each pushing a literal 0x1977 or 0x1978. notes §1.1.
- **REFUTED IF:** An A0 03 is seen on idProduct 0x1978, or the bootloader enumerates as any product id other than 0x1977.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §1.4's sibling rule for identity: our device-search space must be closed at two compile-time constants. If a third identity appears on the wire, that closure is false and every enumeration path has to be rewritten.

#### 12. `usb-set-report-framing` — [G]

**On the bus the A0 03 frame is a single class control transfer to the interface, and its data stage includes the report-id byte.**

- **Expect:** SETUP: bmRequestType 0x21 (host-to-device, class, interface), bRequest 0x09 (SET_REPORT), wValue 0x03A0 (report type 3 = Feature, report id 0xA0), wIndex = the bInterfaceNumber of the HID interface carrying the 0xFF01/0x02 collection on the bootloader device, wLength 1041 (0x0411). Data stage: 1041 bytes, first byte 0xA0. Not split across multiple URBs.
- **Cite:** fw110: HidD_SetFeature(handle, buf, 0x411) — IAT slot 0x51b1d4 confirmed from the import table, dereferenced only inside FUN_004012a0 at 0x4012b8 and 0x401304; length immediate pushl $0x411 @0x4018e0. Report type 3 and the wValue encoding are USB HID 1.11 §7.2.2, not derived from this binary. The included-report-id convention is the same one hidapi relies on across Windows/macOS, hence tag G.
- **REFUTED IF:** wLength is 1040 with the data stage starting 0x03, or bRequest/bmRequestType differ, or the transfer is issued on an interrupt OUT endpoint instead of endpoint 0.
- **Matters:** If Windows strips the report id and macOS/hidapi does not, or vice versa, our byte stream would be off by one on the wire even though our buffer is right. This is the cheapest possible place to find that out.

#### 13. `usb-get-report-framing` — [G]

**The response is fetched with a class control IN transfer of 64 bytes whose first byte is the report id 0xA1.**

- **Expect:** SETUP: bmRequestType 0xA1 (device-to-host, class, interface), bRequest 0x01 (GET_REPORT), wValue 0x03A1 (Feature, report id 0xA1), wIndex = same interface as the SET_REPORT, wLength 64 (0x0040). Data stage: 64 bytes, byte 0 = 0xA1, byte 1 = 0x01.
- **Cite:** fw110: HidD_GetFeature(handle, buf, 0x40) — IAT slot 0x51b1d0, dereferenced only inside FUN_00401330 at 0x40134c, 0x401374, 0x4013f4; length pushl $0x40 @0x40194a; buffer's byte 0 preset to 0xA1 at 0x40194d. wValue encoding per USB HID 1.11 §7.2.1.
- **REFUTED IF:** wLength is 63 and the returned data begins with the status byte rather than 0xA1, or the response arrives on an interrupt IN endpoint instead of endpoint 0.
- **Matters:** It decides whether the status byte our code reads as resp[1] is at wire offset 1 or wire offset 0 — a one-byte misalignment that would make every status read wrong in the same direction, so it would look consistent and still be wrong.

#### 14. `no-interrupt-io-from-updater` — [D]

**The updater itself generates no interrupt-endpoint I/O with the device; every byte it sends or receives is a control-pipe feature report.**

- **Expect:** In the capture, every transfer whose source is the updater process is a control transfer on endpoint 0. Any interrupt IN traffic present is the operating system polling the device's input endpoints, not the tool. The updater imports exactly seven HID.DLL functions and none of them is HidD_SetNumInputBuffers or an input-report reader; the device handle global is never handed to ReadFile or WriteFile.
- **Cite:** fw110 import table (loader-authoritative): HID.DLL = HidD_FreePreparsedData, HidP_GetCaps, HidD_GetPreparsedData, HidD_GetFeature, HidD_SetFeature, HidD_GetAttributes, HidD_GetHidGuid — seven, no more. Device handle 0x56a174 has 14 references in .text, all within [0x40101a, 0x403e96]; the ReadFile (*0x51b204) and WriteFile (*0x51b210) dereference sites are at 0x41471f, 0x41475d, 0x4fd1a4, 0x5075f7, 0x5076ad, 0x5078d3, 0x507cf9, 0x507d52, 0x507ec0, 0x507f9a, 0x50809b, 0x5080f2 — none near a handle load. Blind spot: this is a text scan of the disassembly for the handle's absolute address; a handle passed onward in a register from one of those 14 sites is not excluded by this method alone, and is covered instead by the raw-call-edge closure in notes §9.
- **REFUTED IF:** A bulk or interrupt OUT transfer carrying A0- or A1-prefixed data appears, or the tool is seen reading input reports.
- **Matters:** Our transport is feature-report-only. If the vendor uses an interrupt endpoint anywhere in the flash path, our EGGCore design is missing a channel and the mock bootloader models the wrong device.

#### 15. `which-exe-was-run-is-decidable` — [D]

**The four bytes at buf[17..20] identify which updater .exe was actually run, independently of anything the tester writes down.**

- **Expect:** 7D D5 81 00 means updater 1.10 (FWFILE/140 sha256 8148ebe9f8d2848abe483aee98df6e42bab341c6a17523bfef0f85f1f66754d0, byte sum 8,508,797). 08 D4 81 00 means 1.07 (sha256 3922b14157eff4268ca28c8fe7f284ba1ec0880dae2d9137d01eb1c42e297940, sum 8,508,424). 0B F4 81 00 means 1.06 (9f01d732..., sum 8,516,619). 27 F6 81 00 means 1.04 (41d5397e..., sum 8,517,159). All four have block count 0x41, so buf[16] cannot distinguish them.
- **Cite:** Computed here by an independent PE resource walk of all four .exe files, summing FWFILE name 140 lang 2052. Cross-checked against notes §8.1's published SHA-256 table, which matches exactly for all four.
- **REFUTED IF:** The observed buf[17..20] matches none of the four values, meaning either a fifth updater build exists or the checksum is not the flat byte sum of resource 140.
- **Matters:** The whole pre-registration is scoped to updater 1.10. If the wrong .exe was run, every timing and byte comparison must be re-scoped rather than scored, and this field says so without anyone having to remember.

#### 16. `device-checksum-echo-matches` — [D]

**The device's own whole-image checksum, read later with A1 08, returns the identical 32-bit value the host put in A0 03.**

- **Expect:** The A1 08 command is a 64-byte report 0xA1 with buf[1]=0x08, buf[2]=0x34 (first block), buf[3]=0x74 (last block = (byte)block_count + 0x33 = 65 + 51 = 116 = 0x74). Its response is a 64-byte report 0xA1 with resp[1]=0x01 and resp[16..19] = 7D D5 81 00, i.e. the same 0x0081D57D. The host requires exact equality before it will proceed to A1 09 and A1 13.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00401bb0: movw $0x8a1 @0x401be5, movb $0x34 @0x401beb, last block from arg @0x401bef; call site 0x403b6c–0x403b87 computes (byte)block_count + 0x33; result assembled from resp[16..19] at 0x401c55–0x401c7a; compared cmpl 0x25a20(%esi),%eax @0x403b95.
- **REFUTED IF:** resp[16..19] of the A1 08 answer is not 7D D5 81 00 on a run that goes on to report success, or buf[3] of the A1 08 request is not 0x74.
- **Matters:** It is the only external check that our reading of the checksum's width, endianness and coverage matches the device's, and it is free — the vendor's own run performs it.

#### 17. `device-does-not-re-enumerate-during-the-wait` — [G]

**The device does not drop off the bus during the three-second wait after A0 03.**

- **Expect:** No USB disconnect/reconnect of the 0x1977 device between the A0 03 SET_REPORT and the GET_REPORT 3000 ms later. The tool reuses the same file handle across the wait, and a device-gone error (ERROR_DEVICE_NOT_CONNECTED, 0x48F) is not in the wrapper's retry set {0x15, 0x17, 0x1D, 0x57, 0x65B}, so a re-enumeration inside the wait would fail the GetFeature outright and force the whole A0 03 to be retried.
- **Cite:** fw110: the handle global 0x56a174 is loaded fresh at 0x401334 inside FUN_00401330 and is not reopened anywhere between 0x40190f and 0x401951; retry-code compares at 0x4013b5, 0x4013ba, 0x4013bf, 0x4013c4, 0x4013c9. The inference that the device therefore stays present is [G] and rests on the assumption that the vendor tool works in the field.
- **REFUTED IF:** The capture shows the bootloader device detaching and re-attaching inside the 3000 ms window, or the A0 03 GET_REPORT failing and the command being resent on the first pass of an otherwise normal run.
- **Matters:** If A0 03 does erase and the erase resets the USB stack, our non-abortable post-A0 03 region has to survive a disconnect at exactly that point — the hardest case in the whole design (CLAUDE.md §4.2).

### Rejected by audit (6) — not pre-registered

| id | fault | auditor's evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `three-second-wait-happens-on-failure-too` | `contradicts-notes` | The stated observable — "5 x [SET_REPORT A0 03, 3000 ms]" — and the refuted_if "more than five A0 03 frames appear before the abort" both ignore the transport wrapper's own retry, which the committed notes document. I disassembled FUN_004012a0 (0x4012a0-0x40132e): on a failed HidD_SetFeature it calls GetLastError (*0x51b208 @0x4012c8), and for 0x15/0x17/0x1D/0x57/0x65B (compares at 0x4012ce/0x4012d3/0x4012d8/0x4012dd/0x4012e2) it re-issues HidD_SetFeature (*0x51b1d4 @0x401304) with Sleep(10) (pushl $0xa @0x401312), edi=1..3 (cmpl $0x4,%edi @0x40131b) — i.e. up to 4 SET_REPORTs per call. notes/updater-protocol.md §2 states exactly this ("at most 3 retries (4 attempts total), Sleep(10) between"). The outer loop at 0x4039a0-0x4039c6 runs 5 attempts, so an all-fail run can put up to 20 A0 03 frames on the wire, not 5, and the refuted_if would fire on a fully consistent capture. Separately, the sequence omits the GET_REPORT entirely: when the SET succeeds but the status read fails, FUN_00401890 still issues the 0xA1 GET at 0x401951 (and FUN_00401330 can retry it up to 4 times, Sleep(50) @0x401405), so a failed attempt is not always [SET, 3000 ms]. Also minor: only four of the five sleeps (100/200/300/400) actually separate attempts; the 500 ms one is trailing, after the last attempt, before the abort at 0x4039c8. |
| `exactly-one-a003` | `contradicts-notes` | Same root cause, on the success path. The claim "Count of SET_REPORTs whose payload begins A0 03 == 1" and the refuted_if "Two or more A0 03 frames appear in a run that ends in 'Update Succeed'" are stated as if the only multiplier were the outer retry loop. FUN_004012a0's inner retry (verified above, and in committed notes §2) can emit 2-4 A0 03 SET_REPORTs inside the single successful call, and 0x15 = ERROR_NOT_READY is precisely the error a device 2000 ms into a fresh bootloader enumeration would plausibly return. The document's own supporting facts are all correct and I re-verified them independently — `movl $0x3a0` occurs exactly once in all 1,154,048 bytes of .text (0x4018e9; whole-disassembly immediate scan), FUN_00401890 has exactly one call edge (0x4039ae, from my own E8/E9 rel32 sweep over the whole raw .text), and FUN_004012a0/FUN_00401330 have exactly the seven call sites each that the document lists — but none of them bounds the number of wire frames, which is what the refuted_if scores. |
| `a003-busy-poll-shape` | `citation-wrong` | The cited addresses are all correct but do not yield the stated observable. In FUN_00401330 the Sleep is at the END of the loop body, after the read: 0x401371-0x40137a issues HidD_GetFeature, 0x401385/0x40138e test resp[1] for 0x01/0x04, 0x401393 pushl %esi + calll *0x51b214 sleeps, then 0x40139a addl $0x64,%esi and 0x40139d cmpl $0x7d0 / jl 0x401365. So for esi = 0,100,...,1900 the twenty loop reads are separated by gaps of 0 (between the initial read at 0x40134c and loop read 1, no sleep at all), then 0, 100, 200, ... 1800 — and the final Sleep(1900) is followed by loop exit, not by another GET_REPORT. The exact_expectation says the re-issues are "separated by Sleep(0), Sleep(100), Sleep(200) ... Sleep(1900)" and the refuted_if fires on "a re-poll cadence that is not 0/100/200/.../1900 ms", so a faithful capture of this exact code would be scored as a refutation. The totals in the prediction (twenty re-issues, ~19.0 s of sleeping, at most 21 GET_REPORTs) are right; only the cadence-as-observable is wrong. Impact is bounded by the fact that the poll only runs if the device answers 0x04, which is itself unknown. |
| `bootloader-must-expose-ff01-02` | `mistagged` | Tagged [D], but the item-level descriptor content it commits to cannot be traced to any location in the .exe. FUN_00401000 reads HIDP_CAPS fields only — Usage at 0x56a130 (cmpw $0x2 @0x401145) and UsagePage at 0x56a132 (cmpw against $0xff01 @0x40113c) — and never parses a report descriptor; the only other exe-derived facts are the buffer lengths pushl $0x411 @0x4018e0 and pushl $0x40 @0x40194a. The specific encoding "Report ID 0xA1 with Report Size 8 x Report Count 63" and "Report ID 0xA0 with Report Size 128 x Report Count 65 (8320 bits)" comes from the [O] application-mode descriptor in notes/device-predictions.md (I decoded the committed bytes: 06 01 ff / 09 02 / a1 01 / 85 a1 75 08 95 3f ... 85 a0 75 80 95 41), i.e. from PID 0x1978. 1040 bytes could equally be Report Size 8 x Report Count 1040 — nothing in the binary discriminates. Applying an [O] taken on one device mode to a different device mode is inference, so the tag should be [G] (or [O]-extrapolation), not [D]. This also contradicts committed notes/device-predictions.md, whose "Predictions this cannot test, and must not be claimed to" section leads with "Anything about the bootloader. PID 0x1977 is a different device mode with, presumably, its own descriptor" — the prediction does not acknowledge that it is overriding it, though its own matters_because concedes "Nothing has ever tested that". CLAUDE.md §1.3 keys a write decision off the tag, and this prediction is the sole justification offered for the bootloader transport's two buffer lengths. |
| `gap-1977-enumeration-to-a003` | `not-falsifiable` | The claim that distinguishes this prediction from committed notes §5.3 — "the tool does NOT re-confirm the PID a second time — it enumerates once, then sleeps 1000+1000" — has no observable consequence in a USB capture, and the refuted_if clause meant to test it names something no capture can produce. I traced both paths through FUN_00403750. Path B (A1 3A read succeeded, jne 0x4037e3): enumerate 0x1977 @0x4038d6, found -> jne 0x4038fb -> Sleep(1000) @0x4038fb -> Sleep(1000) @0x403902 -> call 0x403960 @0x40390f. Path A (all nine A1 3A attempts exhausted): enumerate @0x403805, found -> Sleep(1000) @0x40382a -> enumerate again @0x403836 -> je 0x403902 -> Sleep(1000) @0x403902 -> same call. Both paths sleep exactly 2000 ms before FUN_00403960, so the >=1990 ms test cannot discriminate them. The second clause — "a second burst of enumeration descriptor traffic between the first 0x1977 enumeration and A0 03" — cannot occur: FUN_00401000 uses SetupDiGetClassDevsW/SetupDiEnumDeviceInterfaces/CreateFileW/HidD_GetAttributes/HidP_GetCaps (*0x51b498, *0x51b4a4, *0x51b1fc, *0x51b1d8, *0x51b1c8), none of which puts a packet on the bus for an already-enumerated device — a point this same document makes correctly in no-traffic-before-a1-3a. Also unsupported: "up to about 6600 ms more". The give-up test is cmpl $0x1194,%esi @0x4038e5 evaluated after Sleep(esi) and before esi += 0x1f4, so the largest sleep that is still followed by another enumeration attempt is 4000 ms, giving a worst case of about 4000 + 2000 = 6000 ms, not 8600. |
| `checksum-is-over-stored-ciphertext` | `contradicts-notes` | The substantive claim (host copies the resource verbatim; checksum input == stored bytes == transmitted bytes) is correct and I verified every citation for it. But the operationalisation — "the concatenation of the 65 A0 06 payloads ... must reconstruct the resource exactly", refuted if "the concatenated A0 06 payloads do not byte-match the extracted FWFILE/140 resource" — assumes exactly 65 A0 06 frames, which the committed notes contradict in three places. (a) notes §5.4 step 3: the write is "retried while it does not return 1, Sleep(200) between, giving up after 5 tries" (loop at 0x403a80-0x403ab1, cmpl $0x5 @0x403aae). (b) notes §5.5 step 8 / §5.6: a repair loop re-issues FUN_00401980 for the same block — my E8 sweep confirms FUN_00401980 has exactly two call edges, 0x403a8e (normal) and 0x401e0d (repair, inside FUN_00401c90), the latter passing pushl $0xa0 @0x401e06. (c) FUN_004012a0's 4-attempt transport retry again. So a run in which the claim is entirely true can put 66+ A0 06 frames on the wire, "the 65 A0 06 payloads" is then not a well-defined set, and naive concatenation refutes a correct prediction. The pre-registration should say the payloads are keyed by the block index at buf[2..3] and that duplicates are expected. |

### Cannot predict — what the capture will teach us (8)

- **What the device actually puts in bytes 2..63 of the A0 03 response.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The updater reads resp[1] and nothing else — the buffer is a 64-byte stack local at -0x44(%ebp) and the only load from it is movb -0x43(%ebp),%al at 0x40195b. Nothing in the binary describes, tests or displays any other byte, so there is no evidence of any kind about their content.
  - how a capture answers it: The GET_REPORT data stage carries all 64 bytes. Dump them verbatim. If any are non-zero they are a channel the vendor's tool ignores — possibly a bootloader version or an error reason, which CLAUDE.md §4.2 explicitly wants us to check for and refuse if unrecognised.
- **Whether the device is actually ready before 3000 ms, i.e. whether the three-second wait is necessary or merely generous.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The tool never asks earlier. Sleep(3000) is unconditional and the first and only GET_REPORT is issued after it, so the binary contains no observation of the device's state during the wait.
  - how a capture answers it: It cannot answer it directly either, and that must be said rather than hoped for. What it CAN show is negative evidence: whether the device does anything on the bus during those 3 s — an unsolicited input report, a stall, a detach. Measuring the minimum viable wait needs a device experiment we would run ourselves, not a capture of the vendor tool.
- **Whether A0 03 erases flash, and if so when within the 3 s.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: There is no erase command among the seven the updater knows, and A0 03's meaning to the device is not described anywhere in the code. notes §10.2 records this as the largest remaining protocol unknown, and A0 03 is where our non-abortable region starts precisely because we cannot answer this.
  - how a capture answers it: Only indirectly: a detach/re-attach, a long NAK stretch, or a stalled control transfer inside the wait would each be evidence about what the device is doing. A clean quiet 3 s is evidence of nothing in particular. Whatever it shows must not be turned into a claim that A0 03 does or does not erase.
- **The bootloader device's own descriptors: how many interfaces and endpoints it has, its bInterfaceNumber for the vendor collection (hence the exact wIndex), its bcdDevice, and its iProduct / iManufacturer strings.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The tool matches on VID, PID, UsagePage and Usage only; it never reads or compares an interface number, an endpoint, a string descriptor or the bootloader's bcdDevice. The application-mode device has been observed [O] but the bootloader is a different device mode and has never been seen.
  - how a capture answers it: USBPcap records the full enumeration of the 0x1977 device — device descriptor, configuration descriptor, every interface and endpoint, the HID report descriptor and any string descriptors. That single burst answers all of it at once, and CLAUDE.md §4.2 wants an identity check on the bootloader that we currently have no basis to write.
- **Whether the device ever answers 0x04 (busy) to A0 03, and whether it ever emits a third status value.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The binary handles 0x01 and 0x04 and treats everything else as not-ready with no logging. That tells us what the vendor anticipated, not what the device emits.
  - how a capture answers it: The observed status byte on a real run settles the normal case. It cannot enumerate the device's full status vocabulary, and a run that never sees 0x04 does not prove 0x04 is unreachable — CLAUDE.md §1.2a applies to conclusions drawn from the capture too.
- **Whether the device validates the block count or the checksum it is given in A0 03 at all.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The host only ever sends the true values, so no binary shows what a wrong value would do.
  - how a capture answers it: It would not. A capture of a correct run cannot distinguish 'the device checks these fields' from 'the device ignores them'. CLAUDE.md §2's assumption that the device validates nothing stays in force, and no observation in this capture may be used to relax it.
- **Whether Windows and macOS agree on the control-transfer convention for a numbered feature report (wLength 1041 vs 1040; report-id byte in the data stage or only in wValue).**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The .exe passes a buffer and a length to HidD_SetFeature; what the Windows HID minidriver puts on the bus is a property of the operating system, not of this binary. That is why usb-set-report-framing and usb-get-report-framing are tagged [G].
  - how a capture answers it: The SETUP packet's wLength and the data stage's first byte settle it outright. If it comes back 1040 / leading 0x03, our hidapi-based transport still works — but the mock bootloader and the golden file must then model the wire form, not the buffer form.
- **What buf[2..15] and buf[21..1040] of the A0 03 frame mean to the device.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: They are zero, written once by the memset and never touched again, so the code carries no information about whether they are reserved, ignored, or meaningful-when-non-zero.
  - how a capture answers it: It cannot. The capture confirms they are zero on the wire, which is all we need under CLAUDE.md §1.3 — we send zeros because the vendor sends zeros, and we never write a byte whose meaning is a guess.

### Gaps the auditor flagged (5)

- The wire-frame count per logical command. FUN_004012a0 emits up to 4 HidD_SetFeature calls (Sleep(10) between, 0x401312) and FUN_00401330 up to 4 HidD_GetFeature calls (Sleep(50), 0x401405) for the five retryable GetLastError values, and committed notes §2 says so. Nothing in the document predicts this, yet it is the multiplier that makes exactly-one-a003 and three-second-wait-happens-on-failure-too unscoreable as written. It is also cheaply falsifiable — a duplicate A0 03 SET_REPORT exactly 10 ms after the first — and it is the first thing our own transport has to decide (do we retry a SET at the transport layer, and on what?).
- How many A1 3A frames appear, i.e. whether the device answers the 0xA1 GET_REPORT that follows the first one. FUN_00403750 loops i = 1..9 (cmpl $0xa,%ebx @0x4037f0) and breaks on the first successful read (jne 0x4037e3); notes §3.1 and §3.7b give the shape but the document commits to no number. The capture settles it for free and it is the single most valuable fact for CLAUDE.md §4.4 stage 2 — whether the device acknowledges the mode switch before dropping off the bus, or just vanishes. Since this run spends stage 2, not predicting it wastes the spend.
- Whether the 0x1978 device detaches and the 0x1977 device attaches as a new USB address, versus both being present at once or the same address changing identity. The document assumes a clean re-enumeration throughout (gap-1977-enumeration-to-a003, a003-target-is-bootloader-pid) but never states it as a falsifiable prediction, and it is exactly what CLAUDE.md §4.2's "confirm device identity again afterwards" has to be written against. Only this capture can answer it.
- The A0 07 read-back frame and its 1041-byte response. Immediately after the first A0 06 the tool calls FUN_00401c90 -> FUN_00401ad0 (calll 0x401c90 @0x403b0c, calll 0x401ad0 @0x401d68), which sends A0 07 with an 8-bit block index at buf[2] and then issues the only 1041-byte GET_REPORT in the whole flow, on report 0xA0. Per notes §5.5 the device's per-block checksum comes back at resp[6..7] little-endian and the payload at resp[16..1039]. None of that has ever been device-confirmed, it is what CLAUDE.md §4.4 stage 3 (read-back with zero writes) rests on entirely, and it sits one frame past where the document stops. Predicting it costs nothing and the capture will contain it regardless.
- DOCUMENT-LEVEL CONSISTENCY NOTE, not a prediction: the whole capture plan requires executing 'Endgame Gear OP1 8k v2 Firmware Updater 1.10.exe' on a Windows machine with USBPcap. CLAUDE.md §1.5 says "Do not execute any of the vendor binaries. Static analysis only. Nothing about this project requires running them." and §2's constraints say "No Windows. No Boot Camp, no VM, no USB capture of the official tool." The document neither cites an override nor notes the conflict. Circumstances have evidently changed, but CLAUDE.md must be amended in the same commit, or a future session will read the rules file, disbelieve the capture, and treat every scored result as illegitimate — precisely the §1.7 failure mode.

### Irreversible / one-shot in this area

- A0 03 is the point of no return by our own rule (notes §10.2). Once the vendor tool sends it, the device may have no valid application until the block loop and A1 09 complete. Do not interrupt the tool, close its window, sleep the laptop, or unplug the mouse from that moment until the updater reports 'Update Succeed'.
- The three-second wait can only be observed once per flash. A successful run emits exactly one A0 03. If the capture was misconfigured — wrong root hub, started late, control transfers filtered out — there is no way to recover that frame except by running a whole second flash. Check the filter before launching, not after.
- The vendor's run ends with A1 13, which is factory reset (notes §5.4a). It WILL wipe the mouse's settings. If any configuration is worth keeping, capture the settings blob with the config tool's read path first, or accept the loss. Note also there is a reachable path where the flash succeeds, the tool reports success, and A1 13 is never sent — so 'a flash always resets settings' is not quite true, but plan as if it does.
- The device is currently at firmware 1.07 and will end at 1.10. This is reversible in principle — updater 1.07 ships FWFILE/140 with SHA-256 3922b141... and never gates on the installed version — but a downgrade has never been attempted and must not be relied on. Treat the version change as one-way for planning.
- Entering the bootloader is CLAUDE.md §4.4 stage 2 and is not free. Running the vendor tool spends that stage. The compensation is that the bootloader's descriptors, which nothing in the corpus can supply, get captured for free at the same time — so make sure the enumeration burst is actually in the capture, because it is the single most valuable thing here that no static analysis can produce.
- If the run fails after A0 03, the device is expected to remain enumerable at PID 0x1977 and the vendor tool will flash a device it finds already in the bootloader with no application handshake (notes §5.1 case 6). That is the recovery path: re-run the same updater. Do not power-cycle repeatedly, do not open the mouse, and do not try our own flasher on it — EGGFlashCore is unbuilt and untested.


---

## Updater 1.10 — the block write loop

*area key: `updater-write-loop` — 27 written, 1 rejected*


### Pre-registered (26)

#### 1. `image-is-fwfile-140-verbatim` — [D]

**Every firmware byte on the wire is FWFILE resource 140 of updater 1.10, copied verbatim with no transform, in file order.**

- **Expect:** The concatenation of the 65 payload regions (frame bytes [16..1039] of the 65 A0 06 SET_REPORTs, in send order) is exactly 66560 bytes with SHA-256 8148ebe9f8d2848abe483aee98df6e42bab341c6a17523bfef0f85f1f66754d0. Its first 16 bytes are 29 C4 D0 EA 8A E8 D6 1C EF 8D AB 9E 78 03 F7 D9. If updater 1.07 is run instead expect SHA-256 3922b141... and first bytes CA 77 4E 19 7B 45 71 09 B2 FC 21 4F D8 3B EA D7; 1.06 -> 9f01d732... and 49 31 DA 12 2B EF 4A A4 45 46 78 46 2E D7 92 52; 1.04 -> 41d5397e... and the same 49 31 DA 12 ... (1.04 and 1.06 share block 0).
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403207-0x403213 FindResourceW(NULL, 0x8c, L"FWFILE"); 0x403295 movl $0x1,%ebx (copy stride 1); 0x4032c0-0x403313 byte copy; resource re-extracted from the PE by a parser written this session
- **REFUTED IF:** The concatenated payloads hash to anything other than 8148ebe9f8d2848abe483aee98df6e42bab341c6a17523bfef0f85f1f66754d0, or the first payload byte is not 0x29, when updater 1.10 is the binary that was run. Any per-byte transform (XOR, endian swap, offset) between resource and wire refutes it.
- **Matters:** EGGFlashCore extracts resource 140 by hardcoded constant (CLAUDE.md §1.4) and ships those bytes untouched (§8.6). If the vendor transforms the image on the way out, every byte our flasher sends is wrong and nothing downstream catches it (§2).

#### 2. `write-frame-shape` — [D]

**Each block write is one 1041-byte feature report whose first six bytes are A0 06 <idx_lo> <idx_hi> <sum_lo> <sum_hi>, bytes 6..15 zero, payload at 16..1039, and byte 1040 zero.**

- **Expect:** [0]=0xA0, [1]=0x06, [2]=block_index & 0xFF, [3]=(block_index>>8)&0xFF (always 0x00 for this image), [4]=payload_sum & 0xFF, [5]=(payload_sum>>8)&0xFF, [6..15] ten zero bytes, [16..1039] 1024 firmware bytes, [1040]=0x00. Total 1041 = 0x411 bytes. The first such frame begins A0 06 34 00 73 02 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 29 C4 D0 EA 8A E8 D6 1C EF 8D AB 9E 78 03 F7 D9 ... and the complete 1041-byte frame has SHA-256 4afda90317bd6fdb2089dedeaceeaf8b88edd891af5fff771da0725595993c30.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00401980: memset base -0x458(%ebp) len 0x411 at 0x4019b9; 0x4019d5 movw $0x6a0 -> [0],[1]; 0x4019be movb %bl,-0x456 -> [2]; 0x4019de movb %bl,-0x455 -> [3]; 0x4019e4 movl $0,-0x454 -> [4..7]; 0x4019cf leal -0x448(%ebp),%edi (= base+0x10) with 0x4019ca movl $0x100,%ecx and 0x4019ee rep movsl; 0x401a54 movb %al,-0x454 -> [4]; 0x401a5a movb %cl,-0x453 -> [5]; length 0x401a4e pushl $0x411
- **REFUTED IF:** Any A0 06 transfer is not 1041 bytes; or the payload does not start at offset 16; or bytes 6..15 are non-zero; or byte 1040 is non-zero; or index/checksum sit at different offsets.
- **Matters:** This is the exact byte layout EGGFlashCore's write phase emits. CLAUDE.md §4.3 names 'wrong chunk boundary' and 'wrong opcode' as the failure modes that compile clean; this frame is where both would live, and the golden-file test freezes precisely these bytes.

#### 3. `block-index-16-bit-at-2-3` — [D]

**The write command carries a 16-bit little-endian block index at bytes [2..3]; the read-back command carries an 8-bit index at [2] only.**

- **Expect:** For every A0 06 frame, [3] == 0x00 and [2] runs 0x34..0x74. For every A0 07 frame, [2] runs 0x34..0x74 and [3..7] are all 0x00 — [3] there is written as part of a dword zero, not as an index high byte.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x4019be / 0x4019c7 shrl $0x8,%ebx / 0x4019de for the 16-bit write index; fw110 0x401b14 movb %cl,-0x416(%ebp) (single byte) with 0x401b1a movl %ebx,-0x415 and 0x401b20 movb %bl,-0x411 zeroing [3..7] in the A0 07 builder at 0x401ad0
- **REFUTED IF:** An A0 06 frame has a non-zero [3]; or an A0 07 frame carries its index anywhere but [2]; or either index field is a different width than stated.
- **Matters:** The asymmetry is real and our two frame builders must not share one index encoder. It also bounds the invariant in §10.3 item 3: if we ever widened the index we would silently write past what the read-back can address.

#### 4. `per-block-checksum-algorithm` — [D]

**Bytes [4..5] of every A0 06 frame are a plain 16-bit little-endian additive sum of exactly the 1024 payload bytes at [16..1039] — no CRC, no seed, no header bytes included.**

- **Expect:** sum = (sum of frame[0x10..0x40F]) mod 65536; [4]=sum&0xFF, [5]=sum>>8. All 65 values as <index>:<[4]> <[5]> in send order: 34:73 02, 35:07 F6, 36:25 01, 37:10 02, 38:99 05, 39:2C EC, 3A:DB F2, 3B:52 08, 3C:27 08, 3D:44 F7, 3E:DF FB, 3F:3F 04, 40:A0 02, 41:65 04, 42:4B FC, 43:4A FD, 44:D8 FB, 45:6B F8, 46:6E FB, 47:98 02, 48:BC FE, 49:AB FD, 4A:75 01, 4B:19 FD, 4C:BF 05, 4D:E0 EF, 4E:CA 03, 4F:9C F0, 50:38 0A, then indices 51 through 73 inclusive all 88 00, and 74:A6 F8.
- **Cite:** fw110 loop 0x401a00-0x401a34: eax starts at 0x10 (0x4019f7 movl $0x10,%eax), four movzbl at base+eax+0/+1/+2/+3, 16-bit addw into %cx, addl $0x4,%eax, cmpl $0x410 jl — summed range is offsets 0x10..0x40F inclusive; stored 0x401a36 movw %cx,-0x460, split 0x401a3d/0x401a4b/0x401a54/0x401a5a
- **REFUTED IF:** Any single row of that table does not match the captured frame's [4..5]. One wrong row refutes the algorithm; the 35-row filler run (88 00) is the cheapest place to spot an off-by-one in the summed range.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.2 says mirror the vendor's verification exactly. If our summed range is off by one byte at either end, every block we send carries a checksum the device will reject — or worse, accepts while we believe we verified something.

#### 5. `filler-run-is-35-identical-payloads` — [D]

**Block indices 0x51 through 0x73 inclusive — 35 consecutive writes — carry byte-identical 1024-byte payloads, so those 35 A0 06 frames differ from one another only in byte [2].**

- **Expect:** Frames for [2] = 0x51..0x73 are identical except byte [2]; each has [4..5] = 88 00, payload beginning 7E 84 7E 92 C9 78 92 D9 B9 2E 68 5C 90 0A 46 65 and ending ... FC B6 44 CF 50 8A 10 9B. Exactly 35 of them.
- **Cite:** fw110 FWFILE/140 chunk hashes (notes §8.0/§8.5, independently recomputed this session: chunks 29..63 are one chunk repeated 35 times, SHA-256 cefe77fb6c23f0d4cb19fc709232bed0035ba41cc1413d46688172d3e6c6ffda)
- **REFUTED IF:** Fewer or more than 35 consecutive identical payloads appear, or they sit at different indices, or the tool deduplicates or skips them.
- **Matters:** §8.0 says do not 'optimise' by skipping the filler, and §8.5a makes this exact chunk the wrong-image guard our flasher runs before the first byte. The capture is the only check that the vendor really does send all 35.

#### 6. `followup-read-after-each-write` — [D]

**Immediately after each A0 06 write the tool issues one 64-byte GET on feature report 0xA1 — not 0xA0, not 1041 bytes — and the device answers 0x01 in byte [1].**

- **Expect:** Exactly 65 GET_REPORT(Feature, id 0xA1) transfers of 64 bytes, one after each A0 06 write, each ~50 ms after its write. Response [0]=0xA1, [1]=0x01. The tool reads only byte [1].
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401a68 pushl $0x32 (Sleep 50) then 0x401a89 pushl $0x40 / 0x401a96 movb 0x8(%ebp),%cl / 0x401a9f movb %cl,-0x44(%ebp) / 0x401aa2 calll 0x401330; the caller pushes the report id at fw110 0x403a86 pushl $0xa1
- **REFUTED IF:** The follow-up read uses report id 0xA0, or is 1041 bytes, or is absent, or the device answers [1] != 0x01 on a run that nevertheless succeeds.
- **Matters:** Command on 0xA0, status on 0xA1 is a cross-report-id pairing our transport must implement deliberately. Getting it backwards means every write appears to fail and our post-A0 03 loop — which by design never gives up — would spin forever on a healthy device.

#### 7. `readback-frame-and-response` — [D]

**After each write-and-status the tool sends a 1041-byte A0 07 read-back for the same index and reads a full 1041-byte response on report 0xA0 that echoes the payload and carries a 16-bit per-block checksum at [6..7].**

- **Expect:** Request: [0]=0xA0, [1]=0x07, [2]=index, [3..1040] zero (all 65 A0 07 frames concatenated have SHA-256 afd4afba5cba80770490a92626d601d47ad525c541f1afe8463a957241f26980; the first is A0 07 34 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 ...). Response: 1041 bytes, [0]=0xA0, [1]=0x01, [6..7] = the same 16-bit LE sum the write put in [4..5] (index 0x34 -> resp[6]=0x73, resp[7]=0x02), and [16..1039] byte-identical to the payload just written.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00401ad0 0x401b0b movw $0x7a0, 0x401b14 index, 0x401b26 send len 0x411, 0x401b6d movb $0xa0 response id, 0x401b74 recv len 0x411, 0x401b8b-0x401b98 rep movsl 0x104 + movsb = 0x411 copied out; consumer fw110 0x401d90-0x401dab (resp[7]<<8 | resp[6]) and 0x401dc0-0x401dd9 (1024-byte compare)
- **REFUTED IF:** The read-back response is not 1041 bytes; or resp[6..7] does not equal the write's [4..5]; or resp[16..1039] differs from the written payload on a successful run; or the per-block checksum turns up at a different offset.
- **Matters:** This is CLAUDE.md §4.4 stage 3 — flash read-back with zero writes — and the only way to validate our address arithmetic without erasing anything. It is also the verification §4.2 requires us to reproduce correctly while discarding the vendor's §5.6 bug.

#### 8. `four-transfers-per-block-in-order` — [D]

**Each block costs exactly four control transfers, always in this order, with no other command interleaved.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT(0xA0, 1041, A0 06 …) -> GET_REPORT(0xA1, 64) -> SET_REPORT(0xA0, 1041, A0 07 …) -> GET_REPORT(0xA0, 1041). 65 iterations, 260 transfers in the block phase. No A1 08 / A1 09 / A1 13 / A1 3A appears between the first and the last block.
- **Cite:** fw110 block loop 0x403a70-0x403b5f: the only calls are 0x403a8e -> 0x401980 and 0x403b0c -> 0x401c90 (which calls 0x401ad0 at 0x401d68), plus SendMessageW 0x403b3d and Sleep 0x403b45
- **REFUTED IF:** Any fifth transfer type appears inside a block iteration; or the read-back precedes the write; or the block phase contains other than 260 transfers on a clean run.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.3 says keep the write phase small and auditable in one file. This is the state machine that file implements; an extra command we did not model would be a hole in the middle of the non-abortable region.

#### 9. `indices-ascend-contiguously-34-to-74` — [D]

**Block indices start at 0x34, ascend by exactly 1 with no gaps and no repeats, and end at 0x74. Nothing below 0x34 or above 0x74 is ever addressed.**

- **Expect:** The [2] values of the 65 A0 06 frames, in order, are 0x34,0x35,…,0x74. Same for the 65 A0 07 frames. 65 distinct indices.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403a48 xorl %ebx,%ebx; 0x403a8b leal 0x34(%ebx),%ecx (write); 0x403aff leal 0x34(%ebx),%eax + 0x403b02 movzbl %al,%ecx (verify); 0x403b58 incl %ebx; 0x403b59 cmpl 0x21c(%esi),%ebx jl
- **REFUTED IF:** Any index outside [0x34,0x74]; a repeated index on a clean run; or a descending or non-contiguous order.
- **Matters:** §10.3 invariant 3 is asserted in our code: every emitted index in [0x34,0x74], no exceptions and no fallback. It is also the whole basis of §10.2's claim that the bootloader is never a target and a failed update is survivable.

#### 10. `block-count-65-no-partial` — [D]

**Exactly 65 write commands are sent, one per 1024-byte block, and the vendor's partial-final-block path never runs.**

- **Expect:** 65 A0 06 SET_REPORTs and 65 A0 07 SET_REPORTs. 66560 firmware payload bytes sent upstream in total and 66560 read back. No short frame, no zero-padded tail block.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403242 shrl $0xa (65) and 0x40324b shll $0xa; subl -> remainder 0 for a 66560-byte resource; tail path 0x403332-0x403386 unreachable; loop bound 0x21c(%esi) at 0x403a4a and 0x403b59
- **REFUTED IF:** Any count other than 65 A0 06 frames on a run with no visible retry; or a 66th frame carrying a partly-zero payload.
- **Matters:** §10.3 invariants 1 and 2: block_count == size>>10, size % 1024 == 0, and refuse anything but 65 rather than emulate a vendor path that has never run in any shipped version.

#### 11. `no-retries-on-a-healthy-device` — [D]

**On a device that answers 0x01 every time, no block is written twice and the repair path never runs.**

- **Expect:** Exactly 65 A0 06 SET_REPORTs total. The repair path is identifiable in a capture because it re-sends A0 06 for an already-written index AND then issues a 64-byte GET on report id 0xA0 — a malformed pairing that appears nowhere on the normal path.
- **Cite:** fw110 retry counter -0x94(%ebp) at 0x403a70 / 0x403aa1-0x403ab1 (cmpl $0x5 — 5 attempts max, Sleep(0xc8)=200 ms between); repair loop fw110 0x401e00-0x401e3d with 0x401e06 pushl $0xa0
- **REFUTED IF:** More than 65 A0 06 frames appear; or any 64-byte GET_REPORT with report id 0xA0 appears. Either means the retry/repair path ran.
- **Matters:** If the repair path ran during the capture, §5.6's defect was live — the vendor decides repair success from byte 1 of the firmware image, not from the device — and the capture's 'success' says less than it appears to. Our flasher deliberately deviates here and the deviation needs to know which path the vendor actually takes.

#### 12. `inter-frame-pacing` — [D]

**The tool's own sleeps set the block cadence: 50 ms write-to-status-read, ~0 ms status-read-to-readback-command, 50 ms readback-command-to-response, 120 ms response-to-next-write.**

- **Expect:** Per-block wall period ≈ 220 ms plus USB time. The block phase (first A0 06 to last A0 07 response) lasts at least 65 × 220 ms = 14.30 s and, on a healthy run, under about 20 s. Medians: t(GET 0xA1) − t(SET A0 06) ≈ 50 ms; t(GET 0xA0 response) − t(SET A0 07) ≈ 50 ms; t(next SET A0 06) − t(GET 0xA0) ≈ 120 ms (30 + 90).
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401a68 pushl $0x32 (50, in the write builder); 0x401b2e pushl $0x32 (50, in the read builder); 0x401d78 pushl $0x1e (30, after the read-back returns); 0x403b43 pushl $0x5a (90, per-block pacing)
- **REFUTED IF:** The measured block phase is under 14 s, or any of the three intervals differs from 50/50/120 by more than ~30 ms in the median case.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §5 lists 'real inter-command timing vs the vendor's padding' as device-gated. If the device tolerates far less than 220 ms per block we can pace faster; if it needs all of it, cutting the sleeps is a way to brick a mouse that our tests would never catch.

#### 13. `start-command-a0-03-declares-count-and-checksum` — [D]

**Before the first block the tool sends one A0 03 frame declaring 65 blocks and the whole-image 32-bit additive checksum, then waits 3 s.**

- **Expect:** 1041 bytes: [0]=0xA0, [1]=0x03, [2..15]=0, [16]=0x41 (=65), [17..20]=7D D5 81 00 (little-endian 0x0081D57D), [21..1040]=0. Complete frame SHA-256 e4811bc6261dbf256a5707f6487043978fbd588fbffe15abd0da3d79be7e801e. Then Sleep(3000) and a 64-byte GET on report 0xA1 whose [1] must be 0x01. For 1.07 the checksum bytes would be 08 D4 81 00; 1.06 -> 0B F4 81 00; 1.04 -> 27 F6 81 00.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00401890: 0x4018e9 movl $0x3a0; 0x4018c4 movb %cl,-0x448 (offset 16, from the stack arg); 0x4018ce / 0x4018fd / 0x401903 / 0x401909 store the four checksum bytes at offsets 17..20; 0x4018e0 pushl $0x411; 0x401917 pushl $0xbb8 (Sleep 3000); call site fw110 0x4039a0 movzbl 0x21c(%esi),%eax and 0x4039a7 movl 0x25a20(%esi),%ecx; host sum routine 0x403580-0x4035ed
- **REFUTED IF:** [16] != 0x41; or [17..20] != 7D D5 81 00 when 1.10 is run; or the frame is not 1041 bytes; or the checksum bytes sit at different offsets; or the gap to the following GET is not ~3 s.
- **Matters:** A0 03 is the point of no return in our design (§10.2) and the first frame our non-abortable region emits. Its declared block count and checksum are computed by us from the image; a wrong offset here means the device is told to expect the wrong thing before anything is written.

#### 14. `whole-image-checksum-query` — [D]

**After the last block the tool asks the device to checksum blocks 0x34 through 0x74 and the device's answer equals the host's 32-bit additive sum of the image.**

- **Expect:** Request 64 bytes: A1 08 34 74 00 00 then 58 zeros. Sleep(100). Response 64 bytes on report 0xA1: [1]=0x01 and [16..19] = 7D D5 81 00 (little-endian 0x0081D57D). Any mismatch aborts with 'get all check sum error'.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00401bb0: 0x401be5 movw $0x8a1; 0x401beb movb $0x34,-0x42 (offset 2); 0x401bef movb %dl,-0x41 (offset 3 = block_count+0x33); 0x401bf2 movw %bx,-0x40; 0x401bfe pushl $0x64; result assembled 0x401c55-0x401c7a; last-block computation fw110 0x403b6c movb 0x21c(%esi),%al / 0x403b72 addb $0x33
- **REFUTED IF:** Byte [3] of the request is not 0x74; or the device's [16..19] differ from 7D D5 81 00 on a run the tool declares successful; or the result appears at an offset other than [16..19].
- **Matters:** This is the only device-side confirmation the protocol offers and §4.2 requires us to mirror it. It also tests the device's own checksum convention: a match proves the device sums the same 66560 bytes we do, which is the one thing our host-side sum can be checked against.

#### 15. `block-to-flash-address-mapping` — [G]

**Block index 0x34 being the first application block is derived; that it equals flash byte offset 0xD000 is a guess, and the capture cannot settle it.**

- **Expect:** The wire will show index 0x34 first and 0x74 last with 1024 bytes per index. It will NOT show any absolute flash address, because no command carries one. addr = index * 1024 (0x34*1024 = 0xD000 through 0x1D3FF) stays [G].
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403a8b leal 0x34(%ebx),%ecx and 0x403aff leal 0x34(%ebx),%eax; notes/updater-protocol.md §3.8 and §10.1, which state the index is [D] and the address mapping [G]
- **REFUTED IF:** Any captured frame contains a field that is plainly a byte address (e.g. 00 D0 00 00 for block 0x34), which would promote the mapping to [D] and mean we mis-modelled the command. Also refuted if a block index appears with a payload size other than 1024.
- **Matters:** §1.3 forbids a [G] reaching hardware. Our code must address blocks by index and never compute a flash address; this prediction exists so a reader of the capture does not quietly upgrade the guess to a fact.

#### 16. `total-transfer-accounting` — [D]

**A clean end-to-end update is 135 feature-report SET transfers and 135 GET transfers, 208,772 bytes of feature-report data in total.**

- **Expect:** SETs: 1 × A1 3A (64) + 1 × A0 03 (1041) + 65 × A0 06 (1041) + 65 × A0 07 (1041) + 1 × A1 08 (64) + 1 × A1 09 (64) + 1 × A1 13 (64) = 135 transfers, 136,627 bytes. GETs: 64 + 64 + 65×64 + 65×1041 + 3×64 = 135 transfers, 72,145 bytes. Grand total 208,772 bytes. Firmware payload upstream: 66,560 bytes, sent exactly once.
