- **Cite:** fw110 send-wrapper call sites recovered by raw E8 scan (0x40190f, 0x401a60, 0x401b26, 0x401bf6, 0x4037a1, 0x403bf2, 0x403ddd), receive-wrapper call sites (0x401951, 0x401aa2, 0x401b74, 0x401c3d, 0x4037d9, 0x403c24, 0x403e10), loop bound 0x403b59, lengths pushed at 0x4018e0, 0x401a4e, 0x401b05, 0x401be2, 0x403790, 0x403be1, 0x403dcc
- **REFUTED IF:** The counts differ and the difference is not explained by visible bootloader-entry retries (A1 3A may repeat up to 9), block retries, or busy (status 0x04) polling. A count difference with every status 0x01 refutes it outright.
- **Matters:** This is the dry-run total our golden file must reproduce byte for byte (§4.3). If our dry run emits a different number of transfers than the vendor's real run, one of the two models of the protocol is wrong.

#### 17. `enter-bootloader-frame` — [D]

**The only command sent to the application-mode device before the flash is a 64-byte A1 3A frame with the magic bytes 5A A5 32 at [5..7].**

- **Expect:** 64 bytes: A1 3A 00 00 00 5A A5 32 then 56 zeros. [4] is 0x00; the magic starts at [5]. Sent to VID 0x3367 / PID 0x1978. Followed by Sleep(2*i) and a 64-byte GET on report 0xA1; the loop runs i = 1..9 and exits on the first GET that succeeds.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403793 movl $0x3aa1,-0x44(%ebp) and 0x40379a movl $0x32a55a00,-0x40(%ebp); send 0x4037a1 with 0x403790 pushl $0x40; sleep 0x4037a8-0x4037b3 (bl doubled); loop bound 0x4037f0 cmpl $0xa,%ebx jl
- **REFUTED IF:** The magic bytes are at [4..6] rather than [5..7]; the frame is not 64 bytes; more than 9 A1 3A frames are sent; or another command precedes it.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.4 stage 2 is bootloader entry and exit with no erase and no write. This frame is the entire stage-2 payload, and getting the magic byte offset wrong by one is the difference between a mode switch and an unrecognised command.

#### 18. `complete-and-factory-reset` — [D]

**After the checksum matches, the tool sends A1 09 to the bootloader, waits for re-enumeration as PID 0x1978, then sends A1 13 — factory reset — and ignores the answer.**

- **Expect:** A1 09: 64 bytes A1 09 00 00 00 00 00 00 + zeros, Sleep(50), 64-byte GET on 0xA1 requiring [1]==0x01; retried up to 10 times with Sleep(1),(2)…(10) ms. Then a USB disconnect of PID 0x1977 and reconnect as PID 0x1978. Then A1 13: 64 bytes A1 13 00 00 + 60 zeros, Sleep(900), one 64-byte GET on 0xA1 that is never inspected.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403be4 movl $0x9a1,-0x50(%ebp), send 0x403bf2, Sleep 0x403bfa pushl $0x32, status compare 0x403c30 cmpb $0x1,-0x8f(%ebp), retry bound 0x403c49 cmpl $0xa jle; A1 13 built 0x403dcf movl $0x13a1,-0x50(%ebp), sent 0x403ddd, Sleep 0x403de9 pushl $0x384, read 0x403e10 with no consumer
- **REFUTED IF:** A1 13 is sent to PID 0x1977 rather than after re-enumeration to 0x1978; or it carries a payload; or the tool sends something else after A1 09; or A1 13 is not sent at all on a run that reports success.
- **Matters:** working-memory 'Flagged for decision #1' is exactly whether our flasher should send A1 13. Whether the vendor sends it to the bootloader or to the application device changes what the command even means, and it is the step that destroys the user's settings.

#### 19. `vendor-collection-only` — [D]

**Every command in the session goes to the single vendor collection; no feature report is sent to the mouse, keyboard, consumer or 0xFF02 collections.**

- **Expect:** Zero SET_REPORT or GET_REPORT(Feature) transfers with any report id other than 0xA0 and 0xA1 for the whole session. Input reports 0x01/0x02/0x03/0x06/0x08 may appear as interrupt traffic but the updater never solicits or reads them.
- **Cite:** fw110: HidD_SetFeature IAT 0x51b1d4 dereferenced only inside 0x4012a0; HidD_GetFeature IAT 0x51b1d0 only inside 0x401330; the 13 report-id immediates found by a whole-.text scan are all 0xA0/0xA1 (notes §3.7a); litscan finds zero 4-byte 0xff02 immediates in fw110
- **REFUTED IF:** Any feature report with a third report id appears, or any traffic addressed to the 0xFF02 collections originates from the updater.
- **Matters:** The device presents a third vendor collection (0xFF02/0x02, Input 0x08, 64 bytes) that no Endgame binary opens [O]. If the updater turns out to touch it, our confinement argument (§9) is incomplete and the command set of seven is not the whole protocol.

#### 20. `usb-control-encoding` — [G]

**Each HidD_SetFeature becomes one SET_REPORT class request and each HidD_GetFeature one GET_REPORT class request, with the report id in the low byte of wValue and repeated as the first data byte.**

- **Expect:** SET: bmRequestType 0x21, bRequest 0x09, wValue 0x03A0 or 0x03A1, wIndex = the vendor interface number, wLength 1041 or 64, data[0] = 0xA0 or 0xA1. GET: bmRequestType 0xA1, bRequest 0x01, same wValue, wLength 1041 or 64, data[0] = 0xA0 or 0xA1.
- **Cite:** This is the Windows HID stack's mapping, not anything in the binary — tagged [G] for that reason. The binary side is fw110 0x4012a0 (HidD_SetFeature, IAT 0x51b1d4) and 0x401330 (HidD_GetFeature, IAT 0x51b1d0) with lengths pushed at their call sites.
- **REFUTED IF:** wValue's low byte is 0x00 rather than the report id; or the report id is absent from the data stage; or the transfers appear as interrupt OUT rather than control.
- **Matters:** On macOS we call IOHIDDeviceSetReport(kIOHIDReportTypeFeature, id, buf, len). Whether the report-id byte is expected inside buf or only in the id argument is the difference between a correct frame and one shifted by a byte — and a shifted A0 06 frame writes the payload one byte off for all 65 blocks.

#### 21. `a1-lengths-not-padded` — [G]

**The 64-byte A1 commands go out as 64 bytes on the wire, not padded to the collection's 1041-byte maximum.**

- **Expect:** wLength == 64 for A1 3A, A1 08, A1 09, A1 13 and for the status GETs on report 0xA1, even though the collection's FeatureReportByteLength is 1041.
- **Cite:** fw110 pushes 0x40 at 0x403790, 0x401be2, 0x403be1, 0x403dcc, 0x401a89, 0x40194a, 0x401c33, 0x4037cf; device-side [O] MaxFeatureReportSize = 1041 (.analysis/hidobserve_3367.json); notes/updater-protocol.md §6.1 flags this as unresolvable from the binaries
- **REFUTED IF:** The capture shows wLength 1041 for any A1-id transfer, or 977 trailing zero bytes appended to a 64-byte A1 frame.
- **Matters:** §6.1 is explicit that macOS does no padding while Windows may. If Windows normalises to the collection maximum, the device has only ever received 1041-byte A1 frames and our exactly-64-byte frames are an untested shape on the very first bootloader command.

#### 22. `progress-bar-sequence` — [D]

**The progress bar takes exactly the values (100*i)/65 + 2 with integer division, and lands on 100.**

- **Expect:** 2 (set once before the loop), then per block: 2, 3, 5, 6, 8, 9, 11, 12, 14, 15, 17, 18, 20, 22, 23, 25, 26, 28, 29, 31, 32, 34, 35, 37, 38, 40, 42, 43, 45, 46, 48, 49, 51, 52, 54, 55, 57, 58, 60, 62, 63, 65, 66, 68, 69, 71, 72, 74, 75, 77, 78, 80, 82, 83, 85, 86, 88, 89, 91, 92, 94, 95, 97, 98, 100.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403a1b movl $0x2,0x25a24(%esi) before the loop; in-loop 0x403b18-0x403b37: movl -0x98(%ebp),%eax / cltd / idivl 0x21c(%esi) / addl $0x2,%eax, with -0x98 incremented by 0x64 at 0x403b51
- **REFUTED IF:** The bar starts at 0, does not reach 100, or advances in 65 equal steps rather than the uneven integer-division sequence above.
- **Matters:** It is the cheapest end-to-end cross-check available without parsing the pcap: if the bar follows this exact sequence, the loop really ran 65 times over 65 blocks, confirming block_count from the UI as well as from the wire.

#### 23. `getfeature-failure-return-is-zero` — [D]

**NOTES CORRECTION. When the follow-up GetFeature fails, FUN_00401980 returns 0x00, not the request's command byte 0x06 — notes/updater-protocol.md §3.4a has the offset arithmetic backwards. Same for FUN_00401890, which returns 0x00 and not 0x03.**

- **Expect:** -0x459(%ebp) is one byte BELOW the frame base -0x458(%ebp), not base+1. It is set to zero by movb $0x0 (0x4019a8 in FUN_00401980, 0x4018b5 in FUN_00401890), lies outside the memset'd region, and is never written again, so the value returned at 0x401ab1 / 0x401960 is 0x00. Both still fail closed (!= 0x01).
- **Cite:** fw110 0x4019a8 movb $0x0,-0x459(%ebp); 0x4019b9 memset(-0x458, 0, 0x411); 0x401ab1 movb -0x459(%ebp),%al; and 0x4018b5 / 0x4018bc / 0x401960 for the A0 03 builder. notes/updater-protocol.md line 301 states '-0x459(%ebp) = base -0x458 + 1', which is wrong by two.
- **REFUTED IF:** Re-disassembly shows the frame base is -0x459 rather than -0x458, or shows a second write to -0x459. Not observable in a USB capture — recorded here because it is in the notes and would otherwise propagate.
- **Matters:** notes §3.4a is cited for the rule that our transport must return a distinct error type rather than reusing the status byte. The rule survives, but the stated evidence for it does not, and a fact that cannot be re-derived is the failure mode CLAUDE.md §1.2b exists to catch.

#### 24. `source-pointer-advances-once-per-block` — [D]

**The source pointer advances by exactly 1024 once per verified block, never inside a retry, so a retried block re-sends byte-identical content.**

- **Expect:** If the capture ever shows two A0 06 frames with the same [2], their [16..1039] are byte-identical and their [4..5] are identical.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403b47 addl $0x400,-0xa0(%ebp), placed after the verify call at 0x403b0c and outside the retry loop 0x403a80-0x403ab1
- **REFUTED IF:** Two frames with the same index carry different payloads, which would mean the pointer advances somewhere we did not find.
- **Matters:** §10.3 invariants 4 and 5: index and source offset derive from one counter, and the pointer advances only after a verified block. A retry that also advanced the pointer would skip 1024 bytes of firmware and nothing in the protocol would notice.

#### 25. `minimum-wall-clock` — [D]

**From the first A0 03 to the success message, a clean update takes about 19–25 seconds of the tool's own sleeps plus USB and re-enumeration time.**

- **Expect:** 3000 (post A0 03) + 500 (status pause) + 65 × 220 = 14300 (block phase) + 200 + 100 (A1 08) + 50 (A1 09) + 900 (A1 13) = 19,050 ms of mandatory sleeps, plus ~2000 ms of bootloader-entry sleeps before it and the 0x1978 re-enumeration wait (first attempt immediate, then 800 ms steps up to 16000).
- **Cite:** fw110 Sleeps at 0x401917 (3000), 0x403a41 (500), 0x401a68 (50), 0x401b2e (50), 0x401d78 (30), 0x403b43 (90), 0x403b65 (200), 0x401bfe (100), 0x403bfa (50), 0x403de9 (900); bootloader-entry 0x4038fb and 0x403902 (1000 each)
- **REFUTED IF:** Total elapsed time from the Update click to the success dialog is under 18 s or over about 60 s without a visible retry or a long re-enumeration gap in the capture.
- **Matters:** It tells the user how long our own flasher should appear to take, and — more importantly — how long the non-abortable region lasts. A tool that looks hung for 20 s invites the exact interruption §4.2 says guarantees the bad outcome.

#### 26. `one-disconnect-before-and-one-after` — [D]

**The device disconnects and re-enumerates exactly twice: once into PID 0x1977 after A1 3A, once back into PID 0x1978 after A1 09.**

- **Expect:** Two USB disconnect/connect pairs in the capture. No re-enumeration between A0 03 and the last block. The bootloader-mode descriptor sequence appears once, between the A1 3A GET and the A0 03 SET.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403800-0x403828 polls FUN_00401000(0x1977) after A1 3A; 0x403c7c-0x403cc3 polls FUN_00401000(0x1978) after A1 09; no enumerator call sites exist inside 0x403a70-0x403b5f (raw E8 scan of the loop body)
- **REFUTED IF:** A third disconnect appears, or a disconnect occurs during the block loop, or the device never changes PID at all (i.e. the bootloader shares PID 0x1978).
- **Matters:** §4.2 requires re-confirming device identity after any re-enumeration, and our post-A0 03 recovery loop is built to re-enumerate and resume. If the device drops mid-loop on the vendor's own run, that recovery path is not hypothetical and must be exercised in the mock harness first.

### Rejected by audit (1) — not pre-registered

| id | fault | auditor's evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `bootloader-presents-ff01-usage-2` | `citation-wrong` | The citation reads "the same function is called with 0x1977 at 0x403805, 0x403836, 0x4038d6, 0x403c81, 0x403c97". Two of those five are 0x1978, not 0x1977. Disassembled fw110 0x403c7c-0x403ca0: `0x403c7c pushl $0x1978` / `0x403c81 calll 0x401000`, and `0x403c92 pushl $0x1978` / `0x403c97 calll 0x401000`. Confirmed independently by an exhaustive E8/E9-rel32 scan of .text for edges into 0x401000, which finds exactly nine call sites (0x403608, 0x403649, 0x403696, 0x4036c9, 0x403805, 0x403836, 0x4038d6, 0x403c81, 0x403c97), and by notes/updater-protocol.md §1.1 which lists the same nine and says the pushed literal is "either 0x1978 or 0x1977". The document's own `complete-and-factory-reset` and `one-disconnect-before-and-one-after` predictions both correctly say 0x403c7c-0x403cc3 polls for 0x1978, so the document contradicts itself here. The underlying point (one enumerator, one usage predicate, applied to both PIDs) survives — but as written the citation asserts the wrong PID at two of the five addresses offered as its proof. |

### Cannot predict — what the capture will teach us (9)

- **What HID report descriptor the bootloader device (VID 0x3367 / PID 0x1977) presents: how many interfaces and top-level collections, which interface carries the vendor collection, and what feature-report byte lengths it declares for 0xA0 and 0xA1.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: FUN_00401000 calls HidP_GetCaps but reads only Usage and UsagePage from the result — it never touches FeatureReportByteLength. The tool hardcodes 0x411 and 0x40 and would work against any device the Windows stack tolerates. Nothing in any of the four updaters describes the bootloader's descriptor. We have an [O] descriptor for PID 0x1978 only; nobody has ever seen 0x1977.
  - how a capture answers it: The re-enumeration after A1 3A produces a full descriptor exchange, including the HID class request bRequest 0x06 / wValue 0x2200 that returns the report descriptor verbatim. Dump those bytes. This is the highest-value item in the whole capture: on macOS IOHIDDeviceSetReport does not pad, so if the bootloader declares lengths other than 1041/64 our flasher sends a wrong-sized report on its very first bootloader command — after A1 3A, with the device already out of application mode.
- **What the device puts in bytes [2..63] of the 64-byte status response to A0 06, and in bytes [2..5] and [8..15] of the 1041-byte A0 07 response.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The tool reads only [1] from the status report (fw110 0x401aac) and only [1], [6..7] and [16..1039] from the read-back (0x401b7f, 0x401d80, 0x401d90-0x401dab, 0x401dc0). Fields it never loads leave no trace in the code, so no amount of reading the binary can recover them.
  - how a capture answers it: The bytes are on the wire whether the vendor reads them or not. If any are non-zero and vary per block, the device is saying something the vendor throws away — an error code, a written-address echo, a bank number. notes §3.7b already observes the protocol has no error-reason byte; this is the only place one could be hiding.
- **Whether the device ever answers 0x04 (busy) during the block loop, and if so at which block and for how long.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The busy path exists (fw110 0x401330, poll with sleeps 0,100,…1900 ms) but whether it is ever taken is device behaviour, not code. The 3000 ms wait after A0 03 hints the vendor expected a long operation there, but that is an inference about intent.
  - how a capture answers it: Every status GET is visible with its [1] byte. Count how many return 0x04 and where. If none ever do, our busy-poll code has never been exercised by anything and the mock bootloader must inject it deliberately (CLAUDE.md §4.3).
- **What A0 03 actually does — whether it erases, and how the device behaves during the 3 s wait.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: notes §6 and §10.2 record this as the project's largest [G]. The name comes from the string 'send bldr start request failed'; the tool sends it and sleeps. There is no erase command among the seven.
  - how a capture answers it: Only partly. If the device NAKs, STALLs, or stops answering interrupt IN during those 3 s, that is evidence of a long internal operation. Answering a status GET immediately at t+3 s tells us little. It cannot prove an erase happened — only a read-back of a block before and after A0 03 could, and the vendor tool never does that.
- **Whether the device validates the block index, the per-block checksum, or the image at all.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The vendor tool never sends a bad index (the index is computed, not chosen — fw110 0x403a8b) and never deliberately corrupts a block, so no rejection path is exercised anywhere in the binary.
  - how a capture answers it: A normal capture cannot answer it either, and we must not provoke it on the only mouse. CLAUDE.md §2 already assumes the device does not validate; that stays assumed. Listed explicitly so it is not later mistaken for something this capture settled.
- **The interface number (USB wIndex) of the vendor collection, in application mode and in bootloader mode.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: Windows addresses the collection by device path; the interface number never appears as a constant anywhere in the code, and the macOS ioreg dump does not record it either.
  - how a capture answers it: wIndex is in the setup packet of every SET_REPORT/GET_REPORT, and the configuration descriptor names both interfaces. We need it because our enumeration must select the same interface and the device presents two.
- **Whether the mouse's normal input reports continue during the flash — i.e. whether the bootloader still enumerates a mouse interface at all.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The updater never opens the mouse collection and never references it, so nothing in the binary bears on it.
  - how a capture answers it: Directly — the capture shows every interrupt transfer. It also tells us whether a user can tell by feel that the device is in the bootloader, which would be the cheapest recovery signal our flasher can offer after a failed update.
- **What the device answers to A1 13 (factory reset) — the updater fetches the response and discards it.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: fw110 0x403e10 issues the GetFeature and the next instruction consumes an unrelated float; neither the return value nor any buffer byte is examined, so the code cannot tell us what a good answer looks like.
  - how a capture answers it: The response bytes are on the wire. working-memory 'Flagged for decision #1' asks whether our flasher should send A1 13 at all; knowing what the device says is one input, and capturing the settings blob before and after is the bigger one.
- **Whether the flash disturbs the settings region independently of A1 13 — i.e. whether the device's settings live inside blocks 0x34..0x74.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: Where the device stores settings is [G]; block indices are all the protocol exposes, and the config tool's 1024-byte blob has no address in it either.
  - how a capture answers it: Only indirectly. Read the settings blob with the config tool immediately before the flash and again after — but A1 13 fires in between and confounds it. A clean experiment needs a run where gate (c) fails and A1 13 is skipped (fw110 0x403cc3), and that cannot be arranged deliberately. Recorded so nobody concludes from a single dirty run either way.

### Gaps the auditor flagged (5)

- The A0 03 retry ladder is unpredicted, and its absence risks a false refutation of the document's headline A0 03 claim. fw110 0x403996-0x4039c6: ebx starts at 0x64 and the start command is re-sent while `addl $0x64,%ebx` keeps ebx <= 0x1f4, i.e. up to 5 A0 03 attempts with Sleep(100), (200), (300), (400), (500) between them, before `L"send bldr start request failed"` at 0x4039df. notes/updater-protocol.md §5.4 step 1 records this ladder verbatim, so the notes plainly support predicting it. `start-command-a0-03-declares-count-and-checksum` says "the tool sends one A0 03 frame" and its refuted_if lists no escape for retries (unlike `total-transfer-accounting`, which explicitly excuses A1 3A and block retries). A capture showing 2-5 A0 03 frames would read as a refutation when it is the documented vendor path. Worse, this is the one measurement the capture can make about cannot_predict #4 ("What A0 03 actually does - whether it erases"): how many attempts it needed and how long the device took to answer is the evidence, and nothing in the document tells the capture's reader to record it.
- No prediction commits to the 500 ms interval between the A0 03 status GET and the first A0 06 write, though it is [D] at fw110 0x403a41 (pushl $0x1f4 immediately after the 'send firmware block data...' status message at 0x403a31). `inter-frame-pacing` gives falsifiable medians for all three intra-block intervals but stops at the block boundary, and `minimum-wall-clock` only folds the 500 ms into a lump sum. That gap is precisely where a long device-side operation after A0 03 would show up as extra latency beyond the tool's own sleep, and it is the cheapest available discriminator for the project's largest [G]. Predicting "t(first SET A0 06) - t(GET 0xA1 status after A0 03) is approximately 500 ms" costs nothing and makes any device-side stall visible as a deviation.
- Nothing predicts the post-flash bcdDevice of the re-enumerated PID 0x1978 device. notes/updater-protocol.md §1 establishes [D] that the version the tool displays is HIDD_ATTRIBUTES.VersionNumber round-tripped through "%x" and __wtol and divided by 100.0 (FUN_004011f0 at 0x401245-0x401263; I confirmed the same idiom inline at 0x403d43-0x403da1 feeding the L"Update Succeed, current firmware version is V%.2f" string at 0x544a50), and .analysis/hidobserve_3367.json records VersionNumber 263 = 0x0107 today. So "the device descriptor read during the final 0x1978 enumeration carries bcdDevice 0x0110" is concrete, falsifiable, visible in the capture the requirements already ask for, and is the single most direct confirmation that the 65 blocks actually changed the resident image rather than merely being acknowledged. The irreversible_warnings section notes the device is at 0x0107 and will end at 1.10 but registers no prediction, so nobody is instructed to check it.
- Nothing predicts or asks anyone to record the bootloader's identity fields beyond its PID. CLAUDE.md §4.2 requires that "if the bootloader reports a version or an identity of any kind, refuse anything unrecognised", which makes PID 0x1977's bcdDevice and its iManufacturer/iProduct/iSerialNumber string descriptors direct inputs to our preflight - and this capture is the only source that will ever exist for them. capture_requirements bullet 4 asks for GET_DESCRIPTOR(Device), GET_DESCRIPTOR(Configuration) and the HID GET_DESCRIPTOR(Report) for 0x1977, which covers bcdDevice, but the string descriptors are fetched by a separate GET_DESCRIPTOR(String) exchange that no bullet names and no prediction commits to. A one-line expectation ("the 0x1977 device descriptor reports bcdDevice X and product string Y") would turn an irreplaceable observation into a checkable preflight constant.
- No prediction registers how many A1 3A frames a healthy run actually produces. The loop at 0x403780-0x4037f3 leaves only on a GetFeature that succeeds, and the GetFeature is issued against the application-mode handle the mode switch is in the process of invalidating - so more than one A1 3A is a likely outcome on a perfectly healthy device, and the count is a direct measurement of how long the device keeps answering after being told to reboot. `enter-bootloader-frame` predicts the loop shape (i = 1..9) but no value of i; `total-transfer-accounting` mentions the repeat only as an excuse in its refuted_if. CLAUDE.md §4.4 stage 2 is bootloader entry and exit, and how many times our own flasher should expect to re-send A1 3A before the handle dies is exactly what stage 2 needs from this capture.

### Irreversible / one-shot in this area

- THE FLASH IS REAL AND IT IS THE POINT OF NO RETURN. Running Firmware Updater 1.10 writes 65 blocks to the mouse. This is CLAUDE.md §4.4 stage 4 performed by the vendor's tool rather than ours, with all of the risk and none of our safeguards. There is one mouse and no spare.
- The 1.07 -> 1.10 upgrade can be observed exactly once. The device is currently at bcdDevice 0x0107 [O]. After this capture it is at 1.10 and our own flasher can never again be tested on a genuine version upgrade. The tool does NOT gate on version (fw110 0x4011f0 only formats and displays it), so re-running 1.10 re-flashes the same image and the wire protocol stays re-capturable — but the 'device at 1.07' state is gone, and no downgrade path has been derived or tested.
- A completed update sends A1 13 = FACTORY RESET (fw110 0x403dcf / 0x403ddd, byte-identical to the config tool's Factory Reset frame at cfg107 0x40479f). All current mouse settings will be destroyed. Capture the settings blob first; it is the only backup.
- Entering the bootloader (A1 3A) makes the device disappear and reappear as PID 0x1977, a device mode nobody has ever observed. If the update then fails, the mouse stays there. The reading that this is survivable — that the bootloader lives below block 0x34, is never written, and keeps answering at 0x1977 — is [G] (notes §10.2), not [D], and this capture is the first test of it.
- Do not unplug the mouse, sleep the laptop, or stop the capture between the A0 03 start command and the success dialog. The vendor tool gives up after 5 retries on a block and aborts — unlike our design, it does not keep driving to a resident image.
- Installing USBPcap requires a driver install and, on some systems, a reboot, and it forces USB devices to re-enumerate. Do all of that BEFORE the mouse is plugged in for the capture run, never mid-flash.
- If a block retry or the repair path runs during the capture (visible as a 64-byte GET_REPORT with report id 0xA0), flag it prominently: §5.6's defect means the vendor's repair loop decides success from byte 1 of the firmware image rather than from the device, so a 'successful' repair in that run verified nothing and the capture's success proves less than it appears to.


---

## Updater 1.10 — read-back verification and the repair loop

*area key: `updater-verify-repair` — 25 written, 6 rejected*


### Pre-registered (19)

#### 1. `readback-request-bytes` — [D]

**Every read-back is a 1041-byte SET_REPORT on feature report 0xA0 whose first three bytes are A0 07 <block index> and whose remaining 1038 bytes are all zero.**

- **Expect:** Buffer length exactly 0x411 = 1041. [0]=0xA0, [1]=0x07, [2]=block index (0x34..0x74), [3..1040]=0x00. For the very first read-back the full frame is: A0 07 34 followed by 1038 bytes of 0x00. Built at fw110 0x401ad0: memset(-0x418(%ebp),0,0x411) at 0x401af7; movw $0x7a0 at 0x401b0b writes [0..1]; movb %cl,-0x416(%ebp) at 0x401b14 writes [2]; movl %ebx(=0),-0x415(%ebp) at 0x401b1a zeroes [3..6]; movb %bl,-0x411(%ebp) at 0x401b20 zeroes [7]; sent with pushl $0x411 at 0x401b05 through the HidD_SetFeature wrapper at 0x401b26.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x00401ad0-0x00401b26; notes/updater-protocol.md §3.4
- **REFUTED IF:** Any A0 07 frame in the capture has a length other than 1041, or has any non-zero byte at offsets 3..1040, or has [1] != 0x07.
- **Matters:** EGGFlashCore emits this frame verbatim in stage 3 (CLAUDE.md §4.4), which is the only zero-write way to validate our block arithmetic against the device.

#### 2. `readback-index-is-8-bit` — [D]

**The read-back command carries the block index in ONE byte at [2] and always writes 0x00 to [3], while the write command carries a 16-bit index at [2..3]; because the tool only ever uses 0x34..0x74 the two are byte-identical on the wire, so the asymmetry is invisible in the capture.**

- **Expect:** For every matched pair in the capture: A0 06 frame [2]=idx, [3]=0x00 (movb %bl,-0x456 at 0x4019be then shrl $8 and movb %bl,-0x455 at 0x4019de, with idx<=0x74 so the high byte is 0); A0 07 frame [2]=the same idx, [3]=0x00 (unconditional zero store at 0x401b1a). Expect 65 pairs with byte [3] = 0x00 on both sides, 65 times out of 65.
- **Cite:** fw110 write index stores 0x4019be/0x4019c7/0x4019de; read index store 0x401b14 with the zero fill at 0x401b1a; notes/updater-protocol.md §3.4 "Asymmetry worth carrying"
- **REFUTED IF:** Any A0 07 frame has a non-zero byte at [3], or any A0 06 frame has a non-zero byte at [3], or the A0 07 index at [2] ever differs from the index of the A0 06 that immediately preceded it.
- **Matters:** It tells us the asymmetry is a latent bug in the vendor's builder rather than a protocol feature, so our read-back frame must zero [3] and our preflight must refuse any index above 0xFF rather than assume the device accepts a 16-bit read index.

#### 3. `readback-response-field-map` — [D]

**In the A0 07 response the host reads exactly three things: status at [1], a 16-bit little-endian per-block checksum at [6..7], and the 1024 payload bytes at [16..1039]. Nothing else is ever examined.**

- **Expect:** resp[1] must be 0x01 (movzbl -0x417(%ebp),%ebx at 0x401d80, cmpl $0x1 at 0x401d87). Device checksum = resp[7]<<8 | resp[6] (movzbl -0x411 at 0x401d90 = [7], movzbl -0x412 at 0x401d97 = [6], shlw $8 / orw at 0x401da4-0x401da8). Payload compared at 0x401dc0-0x401dd9 over exactly 0x400 bytes from resp[16]. Bytes [0], [2..5], [8..15] and [1040] are read by nobody: whatever the device puts there will be visible in the capture and is completely unconstrained by the binary.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401d80-0x401dd9; notes/updater-protocol.md §3.7b, §5.5 steps 4-7
- **REFUTED IF:** The device's per-block checksum turns out not to be at [6..7], or the payload does not start at [16], or resp[1] is not the status byte.
- **Matters:** Our verifier reads the same three fields; if the device also reports something useful at [2..5] or [8..15] we would otherwise never look at it.

#### 4. `readback-checksum-exact-values` — [D]

**The device's 16-bit checksum at resp[6..7] equals the plain 16-bit sum of the 1024 bytes that were written, and for updater 1.10's FWFILE 140 those 65 values are known exactly in advance.**

- **Expect:** resp[6]=low byte, resp[7]=high byte of these values, indexed by block: 34=0273 35=F607 36=0125 37=0210 38=0599 39=EC2C 3A=F2DB 3B=0852 3C=0827 3D=F744 3E=FBDF 3F=043F 40=02A0 41=0465 42=FC4B 43=FD4A 44=FBD8 45=F86B 46=FB6E 47=0298 48=FEBC 49=FDAB 4A=0175 4B=FD19 4C=05BF 4D=EFE0 4E=03CA 4F=F09C 50=0A38 51..73=0088 (all 35 of them) 74=F8A6. The same value also appears in the preceding A0 06 frame at [4]=low, [5]=high (host-side sum loop 0x401a00-0x401a34, stores at 0x401a54/0x401a5a). Host-side expected value recomputed independently at 0x401ce1-0x401d15 and compared with cmpw at 0x401de2.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401a00-0x401a5a and 0x401ce1-0x401de2; values computed from .analysis/blobs/fw110-140.bin (sha256 8148ebe9…, present exactly once in the .exe at file offset 1653072)
- **REFUTED IF:** Any A0 07 response's [6..7] differs from the table, or differs from the [4..5] of the A0 06 that preceded it. (A systematic difference — e.g. a 1's-complement, or a sum that includes the header bytes — would show as a constant offset and would refute this while teaching us the real algorithm.)
- **Matters:** This is the vendor's own free verification (CLAUDE.md §4.2 "mirror whatever verification the protocol provides"). If the device's checksum is computed differently from the host's, the vendor tool would abort on block 0x34 — so a mismatch here is a much bigger finding than a wrong constant.

#### 5. `readback-payload-is-verbatim-ciphertext` — [G]

**The 1024 bytes the device returns at resp[16..1039] are byte-identical to the 1024 bytes just written — i.e. the device echoes the FWFILE ciphertext, not a decrypted image.**

- **Expect:** For every block, memcmp(A0_07_response[16..1039], A0_06_request[16..1039]) == 0. Concretely, block 0x34's read-back payload begins 29 c4 d0 ea 8a e8 d6 1c ef 8d ab 9e 78 03 f7 d9 and ends 14 ed 34 cf 4f 1e ab 20; block 0x74's begins b3 07 f9 cc a2 b2 ee 59 12 e6 a9 f2 89 98 8e d2. The host's compare is a strict byte-for-byte loop over 0x400 bytes at 0x401dc0-0x401dd9 that sets the repair flag on the first difference.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401dc0-0x401dd9; payload bytes from .analysis/blobs/fw110-140.bin blocks 0 and 64
- **REFUTED IF:** The read-back payload differs from the written payload in any byte while the update still succeeds — which would mean the host's compare is not doing what the disassembly says, or the device transforms the data and the vendor tool tolerates it somehow.
- **Matters:** It settles that FWFILE 140 is stored verbatim in flash, so §8.3's "the host never decrypts" extends to "the device stores what it is given", and our dry-run golden file can be diffed directly against a device read-back.

#### 6. `readback-interleaved-not-a-pass` — [D]

**Read-back is interleaved one-per-block immediately after each write, NOT a separate verification pass after all 65 writes.**

- **Expect:** The trace shows the strict repeating quartet, 65 times: A0 06 (idx) SET 1041 -> GET 0xA1 64 -> A0 07 (same idx) SET 1041 -> GET 0xA0 1041 -> next block with idx+1. The loop body is 0x403a70-0x403b5f: write call at 0x403a8e with ECX = leal 0x34(%ebx) at 0x403a8b, verify call at 0x403b0c with the index recomputed as leal 0x34(%ebx) / movzbl %al at 0x403aff-0x403b02, source pointer advanced once per iteration by addl $0x400,-0xa0(%ebp) at 0x403b47, loop-back jl at 0x403b5f. Confirmed exhaustively by raw call-edge scan over all 1,154,048 bytes of .text: 0x401c90 has exactly ONE caller (0x403b0c) and 0x401ad0 has exactly ONE caller (0x401d68, inside 0x401c90), and neither address occurs as a 4-byte literal anywhere in the file.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403a70-0x403b5f (esp. 0x403a8e and 0x403b0c); E8/E9 rel32 scan of the whole .text plus whole-file literal scan, my own script
- **REFUTED IF:** Any A0 07 appears before the first A0 06, or after the A1 08 checksum command, or the capture shows all 65 A0 06 frames consecutively followed by 65 A0 07 frames, or any two consecutive A0 06 frames appear with no A0 07 between them (outside a repair burst, see repair-burst-shape).
- **Matters:** This is the question the parent asked to be settled with an address. It fixes our write-phase structure: one verify per block inside the non-abortable region, and it means a verification failure is detected 1 block late at worst, never 64.

#### 7. `readback-block-order-ascending` — [D]

**Block indices ascend by one from 0x34 to 0x74 with no gaps, no repeats and no index below 0x34 or above 0x74, and the A0 07 index always equals the index of the A0 06 that immediately preceded it.**

- **Expect:** The 65 A0 06 [2] bytes, in order, are 0x34,0x35,…,0x74. The 65 A0 07 [2] bytes are the same sequence. Both derive from the same counter %ebx with the same 0x34 base (0x403a8b and 0x403aff), and %ebx is incremented once at 0x403b58.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403a48 (xorl %ebx,%ebx), 0x403a8b, 0x403aff-0x403b02, 0x403b58
- **REFUTED IF:** Any emitted block index is outside [0x34,0x74], or the sequence repeats/skips an index in a clean run, or a write and its read-back carry different indices.
- **Matters:** Invariant 3 in notes/updater-protocol.md §10.3 is an assert in EGGFlashCore; this is the observation that validates it.

#### 8. `readback-payload-entropy-discriminator` — [D]

**In the block phase the 1041-byte SET_REPORTs alternate between high-entropy and near-all-zero, which is how the two 0xA0 commands are told apart at a glance in the trace.**

- **Expect:** Odd-numbered 1041-byte SETs (the A0 06 writes) carry 1024 bytes of high-entropy ciphertext (~7.94 bits/byte). Even-numbered ones (the A0 07 read-backs) carry 1038 consecutive 0x00 bytes after the 3-byte header. Additionally, blocks 0x51 through 0x73 — 35 consecutive blocks — carry byte-identical 1024-byte payloads beginning 7e 84 7e 92 c9 78 92 d9 b9 2e 68 5c 90 0a 46 65 and ending fc b6 44 cf 50 8a 10 9b, all with checksum 0x0088. Only 31 of the 65 blocks are distinct.
- **Cite:** `python3 Tools/pe/blobcensus.py --check` (recomputes the census for all 21 FWFILE resources and asserts it); notes/updater-protocol.md §8.5. *Was cited to `.analysis/blobs/fw110-140.bin`, a path in nobody else's tree — corrected 2026-09-06 during the citation audit.*
- **REFUTED IF:** The 35 payloads for blocks 0x51..0x73 are not byte-identical to each other, or the A0 07 frames are not near-all-zero.
- **Matters:** It gives whoever reads the pcap a zero-effort sanity check that the capture is complete and untruncated, and it independently confirms the source pointer advances exactly 0x400 per block (a stride bug would break the identical run).

#### 9. `host-comparison-is-two-tests-both-must-pass` — [D]

**The host accepts a block only if BOTH the 1024-byte memcmp passes AND the device's [6..7] checksum equals the host's own sum; either failure alone sends it into the repair loop.**

- **Expect:** Order of operations in FUN_00401c90: (1) copy source to a local and sum it 0x401cb0-0x401d15; (2) memset the 1041-byte response buffer at 0x401d59 — note -0x418+0x10 == -0x408, so the memset deliberately destroys the local copy, the sum having already been taken; (3) A0 07 at 0x401d68; if it returns 0 the function returns 0 immediately (0x401d70-0x401d72 -> 0x401e52); (4) Sleep(30) at 0x401d78; (5) require resp[1]==0x01 else return resp[1] (0x401d80-0x401d8a); (6) byte compare, first mismatch sets flag EBX=2 and exits the loop early (0x401dc0-0x401ddd); (7) cmpw device-vs-host checksum at 0x401de2, mismatch sets EBX=2; (8) cmpl $0x2,%ebx at 0x401df2 — flag 2 enters the repair loop, otherwise return 1.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401c90-0x401df5
- **REFUTED IF:** Nothing in the capture alone can refute the ordering; it is refuted by re-disassembly showing a different sequence, or by a run in which a block whose read-back differed from the source was nevertheless accepted without a repair burst.
- **Matters:** Our verifier must do both tests, and must not treat the device's checksum as a substitute for the memcmp — the vendor does both and CLAUDE.md §2 says nothing downstream of us catches a wrong-but-well-formed image.

#### 10. `readback-nonready-status-is-not-retried` — [D]

**A non-0x01 status on the read-back is NOT retried at the block level; it collapses to 0 and aborts the whole update.**

- **Expect:** If HidD_GetFeature succeeds but resp[1] != 0x01, FUN_00401ad0 skips the 1041-byte copy (cmpb $0x1,%al / jne at 0x401b87-0x401b89) and returns the true status, but the caller's buffer is still the memset zeros — so FUN_00401c90 reads resp[1] = 0x00 at 0x401d80, falls to 0x401e3f and returns 0. FUN_00403960 then takes cmpb $0x1 / jne at 0x403b14-0x403b16 to 0x403ab3: busy flag cleared, button re-enabled, status text set to the string at 0x544998 (L"send block data request failed"), CloseHandle(0x56a174) at 0x403ae0, thread returns 0. No retry, no second A0 07 for that block.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401b7c-0x401b99, 0x401d70-0x401d8a, 0x403b14, 0x403ab3-0x403afa
- **REFUTED IF:** A block's A0 07 returns a status other than 0x01 and the capture then shows a second A0 07 for the same index, or the update continuing to the next index.
- **Matters:** Our post-A0 03 rule is the opposite (never return, keep driving — §10.2). This is the exact vendor behaviour we deliberately do not copy, and knowing it is one-shot means a single transient on the wire ends the vendor's update.

#### 11. `busy-poll-shape-on-readback` — [D]

**If the device ever answers a read-back with status 0x04 (busy) the busy handling is inside the GetFeature wrapper: it re-issues GET_REPORT up to 20 more times over about 19 seconds, and then — this is the defect — returns SUCCESS with the buffer still saying busy.**

- **Expect:** FUN_00401330: first GET, then cmpb $0x4,0x1(%edi) at 0x40135d. If busy, loop 0x401365-0x4013a3 with esi = 0,100,200,…,1900 (addl $0x64 at 0x40139a, cmpl $0x7d0 at 0x40139d), Sleep(esi) only when buf[1]==0x04 (0x401393). So up to 21 GET_REPORT transfers for a single A0 07, spaced 0,100,…,1900 ms, ~19.0 s total. On exhaustion it returns the last transport BOOL (1), so the caller sees success with buf[1]=0x04, and per readback-nonready-status-is-not-retried the update then aborts.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401330-0x4013a5
- **REFUTED IF:** A 0x04 status appears in the capture and is followed by a number of retry GETs other than up to 20, or by retries at a spacing other than 0/100/200/…/1900 ms.
- **Matters:** Our transport must distinguish "still busy after the budget" from "ready"; the vendor's wrapper cannot, and that is a documented reason to return a distinct error type rather than reusing the status byte (§3.4a).

#### 12. `repair-does-not-trigger-clean-run` — [G]

**In a successful capture the repair loop never runs: there is exactly one A0 06 per block index and no index is ever written twice.**

- **Expect:** Zero repeated A0 06 indices across the whole trace. Every A0 06 is followed by exactly one 64-byte GET on 0xA1 and then by the A0 07 for the same index.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401df2 (cmpl $0x2,%ebx gate), 0x403b14
- **REFUTED IF:** Any block index appears more than once in an A0 06 frame during a run that completes successfully.
- **Matters:** If a repair burst ever appears in a run the vendor reports as successful, then either the read-back compare is not what the disassembly says or the device is returning something we have not accounted for — and either would invalidate the whole read-back model.

#### 13. `repair-burst-shape` — [D]

**If the repair loop does trigger, it emits EXACTLY 20 A0 06 frames for the same block index back to back with no A0 07 between them, and it never issues another read-back.**

- **Expect:** Loop at 0x401df7-0x401e3d: esi starts 0 (xorl %esi,%esi at 0x401dfb), addl $0x64 at 0x401e34, cmpl $0x7d0 / jl at 0x401e37-0x401e3d. Each iteration calls FUN_00401980 at 0x401e0d with ECX = the same block index (movzbl 0x8(%ebp),%edi at 0x401df7, movl %edi,%ecx at 0x401e0b) and EDX = the same source pointer (movl -0x424(%ebp),%edx at 0x401e00, a slot written once at 0x401caa and never reassigned). So 20 identical 1041-byte A0 06 frames — identical index at [2..3], identical checksum at [4..5], identical 1024-byte payload — and zero A0 07 frames until the loop ends.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401df7-0x401e3d
- **REFUTED IF:** A repair burst is observed with any count other than 20 identical A0 06 frames (other than the single-frame case ruled out by repair-cannot-succeed-on-this-image), or with an A0 07 interleaved inside the burst, or with a changing index or payload.
- **Matters:** It is the signature to grep the pcap for, and it is the concrete shape of the behaviour build-design.md §2.3 says we deliberately do not copy.

#### 14. `repair-vs-write-retry-discriminator` — [D]

**A repair burst and a write-retry burst are different things and are distinguishable in the capture by count, spacing and the report id of the interleaved GET.**

- **Expect:** Write-retry (the A0 06 status came back != 0x01): at most 5 A0 06 frames for the index, spaced ~250 ms (Sleep(50) inside FUN_00401980 at 0x401a68 plus Sleep(200) at 0x403a9a), each followed by a 64-byte GET on report 0xA1, loop bound cmpl $0x5 / jl at 0x403aae-0x403ab1. Repair (the read-back compare failed): exactly 20 A0 06 frames, spaced ~50 ms, each followed by a 64-byte GET on report 0xA0 or by no GET at all. A write-retry burst is followed by an A0 07 if any attempt succeeds; a repair burst never is.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403a80-0x403ab1 vs 0x401df7-0x401e3d
- **REFUTED IF:** A burst of repeated A0 06 frames is observed that matches neither shape — e.g. 8 frames, or 20 frames at 250 ms spacing.
- **Matters:** If the capture contains any repeat at all, this is how we tell whether the device rejected the write or returned bad data — two different bugs on our side and two different mitigations.

#### 15. `abort-leaves-silence-and-bootloader` — [D]

**After a read-back failure the vendor tool emits no further HID traffic at all: no A1 08, no A1 09, no A1 13, no re-enumeration wait, and the device is left enumerated at PID 0x1977 with a partially written image.**

- **Expect:** At 0x403ab3 the abort path pushes $1 and calls 0x413707 (re-enable button), zeroes the busy flag 0x25a38, sets the status string at 0x544998, calls CloseHandle(*0x56a174) via *0x51b200 at 0x403ae0, zeroes the two words at 0x56a0f8/0x56a0fc and returns 0 via 0x403eb7. Nothing between 0x403ab3 and the return touches HidD_SetFeature or HidD_GetFeature. So: last frame in the capture is the last A0 06 of the repair burst, then only the device's own periodic traffic (if any) until the process exits.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403ab3-0x403afa; call-edge census in notes/updater-protocol.md §5.4a (send wrapper has exactly 7 call sites, none on this path)
- **REFUTED IF:** After an aborted update the capture shows any further A0/A1 feature report on the device, or a USB reset / re-enumeration initiated by the host.
- **Matters:** It confirms that a vendor abort mid-flash is exactly the state our recovery path must handle: device alive at 0x1977, application region incomplete, nothing further sent. Our rule is to keep driving from there (§10.2).

#### 16. `whole-image-checksum-frame-and-value` — [D]

**Immediately after the last read-back the tool sends A1 08 34 74 and the device must return the 32-bit little-endian value 0x0081D57D at [16..19].**

- **Expect:** Sleep(200) at 0x403b65, then A1 08 built at 0x401be5-0x401bf2 as [0]=0xA1 [1]=0x08 [2]=0x34 (movb $0x34 at 0x401beb) [3]=0x74 (movb %dl at 0x401bef, argument = block_count+0x33 computed at 0x403b6c-0x403b72) [4..5]=0 and the rest zero, 64 bytes. Then Sleep(100) at 0x401bfe, a 64-byte GET on 0xA1, require resp[1]==0x01 at 0x401c51, and assemble resp[19]<<24|resp[18]<<16|resp[17]<<8|resp[16] at 0x401c55-0x401c78. Expected bytes on the wire: resp[16]=0x7D resp[17]=0xD5 resp[18]=0x81 resp[19]=0x00. That is the plain 32-bit additive sum of all 66,560 bytes of FWFILE 140 computed by the host at 0x403580-0x4035f5. The same value appears earlier in the A0 03 start frame at [17..20] as 7D D5 81 00, with [16]=0x41 (block count 65). Mismatch aborts with the string at 0x5449d8 (L"get all check sum error"). Note the tool discards FUN_00401bb0's return value entirely and compares only the out-parameter (0x403b8c-0x403b95), so a failed query yields 0 and also aborts.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401bb0-0x401c7a, 0x403b65-0x403b9b, 0x403580-0x4035f5, 0x4018c4-0x401909; value computed from .analysis/blobs/fw110-140.bin
- **REFUTED IF:** The A1 08 response's [16..19] is not 7D D5 81 00, or the request's [2..3] are not 34 74, or the A0 03 frame's [16..20] are not 41 7D D5 81 00. (For other updaters the whole-image sums are: 1.04 = 0x0081F627, 1.06 = 0x0081F40B, 1.07 = 0x0081D408.)
- **Matters:** This is the vendor's second, independent verification and the gate on A1 13. It is also the one number that proves the device summed what we think it stored, across all 65 blocks at once.

#### 17. `block-phase-timing-floor` — [D]

**The per-block sleeps are fixed at 220 ms, so the block phase has a hard floor of about 14.2 s and should measure 14.5-18 s wall clock.**

- **Expect:** Per block: Sleep(50) after the A0 06 SET (0x401a68), Sleep(50) after the A0 07 SET (0x401b2e), Sleep(30) after the read-back returns (0x401d78), Sleep(90) at the loop tail (0x403b43). Sum 220 ms. From the first A0 06 SET to the last A0 07 GET completion = 65*220 - 90 = 14,210 ms of sleeps plus transfer and device time. Bracketing gaps: the A0 03 SET is followed by a 3000 ms gap before its 64-byte status GET (pushl $0xbb8 at 0x401917), then a 500 ms gap before the first A0 06 (pushl $0x1f4 at 0x403a41); after the last read-back there is a 200 ms gap before A1 08 (0x403b65) and a 100 ms gap before its status GET (0x401bfe).
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401a68, 0x401b2e, 0x401d78, 0x403b43, 0x401917, 0x403a41, 0x403b65, 0x401bfe
- **REFUTED IF:** The interval from the first A0 06 to the last A0 07 GET is under 14.0 s, or any A0 06 -> A1 GET gap is not ~50 ms, or any A0 07 SET -> A0 GET gap is not ~50 ms, or the A0 03 SET is not followed by a ~3000 ms gap.
- **Matters:** The vendor's padding is the only timing reference we have; the capture's actual device latencies tell us how much of it is real and how much is superstition, which is CLAUDE.md §5's "real inter-command timing vs the vendor's padding".

#### 18. `all-commands-are-control-transfers` — [D]

**Every byte of this protocol moves over the default control pipe as HID class SET_REPORT / GET_REPORT; nothing uses an interrupt OUT endpoint.**

- **Expect:** Only two device APIs are ever called: HidD_SetFeature (IAT 0x51b1d4, dereferenced at exactly 2 sites, both inside FUN_004012a0) and HidD_GetFeature (IAT 0x51b1d0, exactly 3 sites, all inside FUN_00401330). Wireshark should show, for each command, bmRequestType 0x21 / bRequest 0x09 / wValue 0x0300|reportID for the send and bmRequestType 0xA1 / bRequest 0x01 / wValue 0x0300|reportID for the read. wValue is 0x03A0 or 0x03A1 and nothing else.
- **Cite:** fw110 IAT slots resolved by rabin2 -i; call-site census in notes/updater-protocol.md §5.4a
- **REFUTED IF:** Any command payload appears on an interrupt endpoint, or a wValue other than 0x03A0/0x03A1 carries protocol data, or a bRequest other than 0x01/0x09 appears.
- **Matters:** It confirms hidapi's setFeature/getFeature is the whole transport surface we need on macOS, with no interrupt-OUT path to reproduce.

#### 19. `report-id-in-control-data-stage` — [G]

**The report ID byte is carried as the first byte of the control transfer's data stage, so wLength is 1041 for the 0xA0 reports and 64 for the 0xA1 reports.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT wValue 0x03A0 with wLength 1041 and data[0] = 0xA0; GET_REPORT wValue 0x03A1 with wLength 64 and data[0] = 0xA1. The vendor always passes the full buffer including buf[0] to HidD_SetFeature/HidD_GetFeature (pushl $0x411 at 0x401b05/0x401a4e, pushl $0x40 at 0x401a89).
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401a4e, 0x401a89, 0x401b05, 0x401b67; Windows HIDCLASS behaviour is not derivable from the binary
- **REFUTED IF:** wLength is 1040 / 63 and the data stage begins with the command byte (0x06, 0x07, …) rather than the report ID.
- **Matters:** It fixes whether hidapi's buffer convention on macOS (ID in byte 0) produces the identical wire frame, which is a preflight assertion in EGGCore. If Windows strips the ID we would be sending one byte more than the vendor.

### Rejected by audit (6) — not pre-registered

| id | fault | auditor's evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `readback-count-exactly-65` | `contradicts-notes` | The exact_expectation says "GET_REPORT wValue 0x03A1 = 65 (block status) + 1 (A0 03 status) + 1 (A1 08) + 1 (A1 09) = 68". That omits two status reads the same paragraph's SET line counts. I disassembled the whole post-block tail and the bootloader-entry function: A1 3A at 0x403750 pairs every SetFeature (0x4037a1) with a 64-byte GetFeature on 0xA1 (movb $-0x5f,-0x84(%ebp) at 0x4037d2, call 0x401330 at 0x4037d9), and A1 13 pairs its SetFeature (0x403ddd) with Sleep(900) at 0x403de9 then a 64-byte 0xA1 GetFeature (movb $-0x5f at 0x403e09, call at 0x403e10). So a clean run has A1 3A:N + A0 03:1 + blocks:65 + A1 08:1 + A1 09:1 + A1 13:1 = 69+N, i.e. 70 for N=1, not 68. This also contradicts committed notes updater-protocol.md §3.7a, whose 13-entry immediate census explicitly lists 0x4037d2 and 0x403e09 as 'response report id' stores inside 0x403750 and 0x403960 — the notes already record the reads the prediction drops. The 0xA0 numbers in the same prediction (131 SETs, 65 GETs) are correct and I verified them; only the 0xA1 line is wrong. |
| `progress-bar-2-to-100` | `citation-wrong` | The cited addresses are all real and I read them (0x403a1b movl $0x2,0x25a24(%esi); 0x403b18 movl -0x98(%ebp),%eax; 0x403b1e cltd; 0x403b1f idivl 0x21c(%esi); 0x403b2d addl $0x2,%eax; 0x403b51 addl $0x64,-0x98(%ebp)), but the stated expectation does not follow from them. exact_expectation says "For i=0..64 with block_count 65 that is 2,3,4,…,100". Computing (100*i)//65 + 2 for i=0..64 gives 2,3,5,6,8,9,11,12,14,15,17,18,20,22,23,25,... ,97,98,100 — 65 distinct values that skip 34 of the integers in [2,100] (4,7,10,13,16,19,21,24,27,30,33,36,39,41,44,47,50,53,56,59,61,64,67,70,73,76,79,81,84,87,90,93,96,99). The endpoints (first value 2, last value exactly 100) and the formula are right; the enumeration is not. capture_requirements instructs the operator to "Watch and record the progress bar", so an operator holding the stated sequence will see 2,3,5,6,8 and read a normal run as an anomaly. |
| `repair-cannot-succeed-on-this-image` | `mistagged` | Tagged [D], but the claim is universally quantified — "can NEVER report success on any firmware image any of the four updaters ships ... no shipped block has byte 1 equal to 0x01" — while the citation covers only 'byte census over .analysis/blobs/{fw104,fw106,fw107,fw110}-140.bin', i.e. 4 blobs. notes/updater-protocol.md §8.5a establishes 21 blobs across the corpus (six FWFILE names, 1.10 ships all six); 9 are extracted on disk. I ran the census over all 9: the byte[1] values for the four 140s match the prediction's list exactly (C4 28 DF 33 FD A2 E7 CE FC EE 35 05 84 C4 3A D7 75 8A CA 81 1D 51 57 92 CC AF D2 95 56, then 0x84 x35, then 0x07), and none of the 9 has byte[1]==0x01. But fw110-137.bin DOES contain a block with byte[1]==0x04, which falsifies the identically-scoped sibling assertion 'and NONE is 0x04' the moment it is generalised the same way. So the generalising move is demonstrably unsafe on data already in the repo, and CLAUDE.md §1.2a requires the search space be stated: the derived part is 'FWFILE 140 in the four updaters', which is [D]; the extension to every shipped image is [G]. The load-bearing consequence for the capture (FWFILE 140 in 1.10, burst of exactly 20, no Sleep in the loop) is unaffected and I verified it at 0x401e19-0x401e3d. |

What is derivable, and what I verified, is only: Sleep(50) at 0x401b2e, memset(-0x82c,0,0x411) at 0x401b5c, movb $-0x60 (0xA0) to -0x82c at 0x401b6d, HidD_GetFeature wrapper called at 0x401b74 with (buf,0x411); and for A0 06 the 64-byte read with buf[0]=the caller's 0xA1 (0x401a9f, from pushl $0xa1 at 0x403a86). The claims 'bmRequestType 0xA1, bRequest 0x01 GET_REPORT, wValue 0x03A0, data stage 1041 bytes' and the refuted_if 'The GET following an A0 07 is not 1041 bytes, or its wValue low byte is not 0xA0' are HIDCLASS/HID-spec framing, not derivation. The document itself agrees in two other places: report-id-in-control-data-stage makes the identical inference and tags it [G] with the note 'Windows HIDCLASS behaviour is not derivable from the binary', and cannot_predict #5 says 'The tool hardcodes 0x411 and 0x40 and the binary says nothing about how the stack treats them.' Same inference, three different tags. CLAUDE.md §1.2/§1.3 key on the tag, and this one sizes a transport buffer. |
| `notes-3-4a-return-value-correction` | `not-falsifiable` | refuted_if is 'Re-disassembly shows -0x459(%ebp) lying inside the request buffer, or shows a store to it other than the two zero stores named.' No USB capture can produce that observation — it is a re-read of the same bytes the claim was derived from, which is confirmation rather than falsification, and this document is pre-registration for a capture. (The substance is correct and I checked it: FUN_00401890's buffer base is -0x458 with memset 0x411 at 0x4018bc, -0x459 is base-1, zeroed once by movb $0x0,-0x459(%ebp) at 0x4018b5 and read at 0x401960; FUN_00401980 likewise 0x4019a8 / 0x401ab1. So notes §3.4a's '0x03' and '0x06' entries are indeed wrong and both are 0x00.) The count in matters_because is itself wrong: it says 'Three of the six values in it were wrong', but §3.4a's two failure columns hold six values (0x02,0x02,0x02 / 0x03,0x06,0x00) and exactly TWO are wrong. What is additionally wrong in that table is an address, not a value — it cites 'movb $0x2,%al, 0x401949 region' for FUN_00401980's SetFeature failure, where the actual instruction is at 0x401a79 (0x401949 is inside FUN_00401890). |
| `repair-status-read-uses-wrong-report-id` | `citation-wrong` | exact_expectation states "FUN_00401980's status read length is a fixed pushl $0x40 at 0x401a89". 0x401a89 is not the status read's length argument. The bytes are: 401a89 pushl $0x40 / 401a8b leal -0x44(%ebp),%eax / 401a8e pushl $0x0 / 401a90 pushl %eax / 401a91 call 0x4f83a0 — that is memset(buf,0,0x40), and 0x401a89 is memset's n. The GetFeature wrapper (cdecl, buf at 0x8(%ebp), len at 0xc(%ebp)) is called at 0x401aa2 with 401a9c pushl $0x40 / 401a9e pushl %edx, so the length argument is the push at 0x401a9c. Both instructions are the same two bytes (6a 40), which is exactly why this survives a casual re-check. The same wrong address is in committed notes updater-protocol.md §5.6, in a block headed 'RE-DERIVED 2026-09-04 from raw bytes' that says 'cited as 0x401a8a before this check — corrected' — so the correction landed on the wrong one of the two identical pushes and the prediction inherited it. The substantive claim (fixed 64-byte length, 0xA0 passed at 0x401e06 vs 0xA1 at 0x403a86) is correct and I verified it. |

### Cannot predict — what the capture will teach us (9)

- **What the device puts in A0 07 response bytes [2..5], [8..15] and [1040].**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: FUN_00401c90 reads only [1], [6..7] and [16..1039]; FUN_00401ad0 copies the whole 1041-byte frame up but nothing downstream ever touches those offsets. No instruction anywhere in the 1,154,048 bytes of fw110 .text loads them (they are inside the memcpy'd region, so a literal-scan for their displacements is not conclusive, but no consumer exists in either code base). So the binary places zero constraint on their contents.
  - how a capture answers it: The pcap shows them directly, 65 times, alongside a known index and known payload. If they echo the index, or carry a per-block address, or a status/erase-count, that is a whole field of the protocol the vendor tool discards and we would otherwise never see.
- **Whether the device's [6..7] checksum is computed over what it stored in flash or over what it received into a buffer.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The host only ever asks for a read-back of a block it has just written, so the two hypotheses give the same answer on every path the vendor tool can take.
  - how a capture answers it: Only partially. A clean capture cannot separate them. It becomes answerable only if a read-back is ever issued for a block that was not just written — which the vendor tool never does — so this stays open until our own stage-3 read-back, and that itself depends on the next item.
- **Whether A0 07 works at all outside the write loop — i.e. whether CLAUDE.md §4.4 stage 3 (flash read-back with zero writes) is possible.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: Exhaustive raw call-edge scan over all of fw110 .text: 0x401ad0 has exactly one caller, 0x401d68 inside FUN_00401c90, which itself has exactly one caller, 0x403b0c inside the block loop; neither address appears as a 4-byte literal anywhere in the file. So the vendor never issues A0 07 except immediately after an A0 06, and never in application mode at all. (Blind spot: a target computed at runtime, which no call site in either code base does.)
  - how a capture answers it: It does not — this is the highest-value thing the capture will NOT teach us, and it is worth saying so in advance. The capture will show whether A0 07 works after a write in bootloader mode. Whether it works before any write, or after A1 09, or in application mode, remains ours to find out on the device at our own risk.
- **The device's true response latency for A0 06 and A0 07 — how soon after the SET the status or the data is actually ready.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The vendor sleeps a fixed 50 ms and never polls earlier, so the binary encodes a bound, not a measurement.
  - how a capture answers it: USBPcap timestamps the completion of each control transfer. If the device NAKs or delays the GET's data stage, the gap between the GET's submit and complete is the device's real think-time; if it answers instantly, the vendor's 50 ms is pure padding and our flasher can be paced by the device instead.
- **Whether Windows' HIDCLASS validates a feature-report buffer against the specific report ID or against the collection maximum, and what interface number (wIndex) the control transfers carry.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: FUN_00401000 calls HidP_GetCaps but reads only Usage and UsagePage from the result; it never looks at FeatureReportByteLength (notes §6.1). The tool hardcodes 0x411 and 0x40 and the binary says nothing about how the stack treats them.
  - how a capture answers it: The 64-byte 0xA1 GETs appearing on the wire at all proves per-report-id validation. The repair path's 64-byte 0xA0 GET (if a repair ever triggers) proves it the other way. wIndex is read straight off any setup packet.
- **Whether the bootloader (PID 0x1977) presents the same report descriptor as the application device — in particular whether reports 0xA0 and 0xA1 keep the 1041/64 lengths and the 0xFF01/0x02 usage pair.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: FUN_00401000 is called with the PID as a parameter and applies the identical VID/PID/UsagePage/Usage filter to both modes, so the tool assumes sameness rather than deriving it. Nothing in either code base reads a bootloader descriptor.
  - how a capture answers it: The re-enumeration after A1 3A carries GET_DESCRIPTOR(Report) for the 0x1977 device. That is the only way to get the bootloader's descriptor without spending another stage-2 entry, and it is free in this capture.
- **What A0 03 actually does to the device (erase? unlock? 'prepare for N blocks'?).**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The command carries the block count at [16] and the whole-image checksum at [17..20] and nothing in the tool interprets the device's answer beyond resp[1]==0x01. Both readings fit.
  - how a capture answers it: It does not answer it directly, but the A0 03 -> status gap, and whether a read-back of a block before it was written returns 0xFF filler or old data (not observable in a vendor run), bound it. Our post-A0 03 no-return rule (§10.2) does not depend on the answer.
- **Whether a 0x04 (busy) status ever occurs in practice during the block loop, and after which command.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The busy path exists in the wrapper and in the repair loop; whether the device uses it is a device fact.
  - how a capture answers it: Any resp[1]==0x04 in the trace, with the command that provoked it and how long the device stayed busy, tells us whether our own budget of 20 polls / 19 s is generous or tight.
- **What the device does with a second write of a block it has already accepted (the repair case).**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The vendor's repair loop is unreachable-as-successful on every shipped image, so the vendor never learns this either.
  - how a capture answers it: Only if a repair burst happens to trigger. It almost certainly will not, and we must not provoke one — deliberately corrupting a write would violate CLAUDE.md §1.3 and §4.2.

### Gaps the auditor flagged (5)

- The 64-byte A0 06 block-status response is never predicted. FUN_00401980 reads exactly one byte of it (movb -0x43(%ebp),%al at 0x401aac = resp[1]) after memset+GetFeature(-0x44, 0x40); bytes [2..63] have no consumer anywhere. That is precisely the argument cannot_predict #1 makes for the A0 07 response's unread offsets — 'a whole field of the protocol the vendor tool discards and we would otherwise never see' — but it is not made for A0 06, and the capture would hand it over 65 times alongside a known index and a known 1024-byte payload. Same omission for the A0 03 start response (only resp[1] at 0x40195b; [2..63] free, one observation only) and for the A1 08 response (only resp[1] and [16..19]; [2..15] and [20..63] free, one observation only).
- A1 09 'complete' is inside FUN_00403960 — the function this area names — and is the last command before the device leaves the bootloader, yet nothing predicts it. The binary gives it exactly: 0x403be4 movl $0x9a1,-0x50(%ebp) → 64-byte frame [0]=0xA1 [1]=0x09, 0x403beb movl $0x0,-0x4c(%ebp) → [4..7]=0; SetFeature at 0x403bf2; Sleep(50) at 0x403bfa; 64-byte 0xA1 GetFeature at 0x403c24; resp[1]==0x01 gate at 0x403c30; and a retry loop 0x403bd1-0x403c4c of up to 10 attempts with Sleep(1),Sleep(2),…, failing to the string at 0x544a08. The A1 08 → A1 09 ordering and the ~200 ms gap into A1 08 are predicted; the command that follows is not.
- The re-enumeration budget is stated as '~136 s (sleeps 800,1600,…,16000 ms)' in both capture_requirements and irreversible_warnings, and the arithmetic does not match the cited sleep list. The loop is 0x403c8d movl $0x320,%ebx / 0x403c92 probe PID 0x1978 / 0x403caa Sleep(%ebx) / 0x403cad addl $0x320,%ebx / 0x403cb3 cmpl $0x3e80,%ebx / 0x403cb9 jle — twenty sleeps of 800…16000 ms summing to 168,000 ms = 168 s, not 136 s (136.8 s is the 18-term sum). This is the number that tells the operator how long a stalled run may sit before A1 13, so it should be right in guidance that is meant to keep an irreversible capture running long enough.
- Nothing predicts the shape of an A0 03 retry burst, which is the only repeat that can occur *before* any block is written and therefore the one an operator is most likely to see and misread. The binary fixes it: 0x403996 movl $0x64,%ebx, call FUN_00401890 at 0x4039ae, on al!=1 Sleep(%ebx) at 0x4039ba-0x4039bb, addl $0x64 at 0x4039bd, cmpl $0x1f4/jle at 0x4039c0-0x4039c6 — up to 5 A0 03 SETs spaced 100/200/300/400/500 ms, each followed by Sleep(3000) and a 64-byte 0xA1 status GET, failing to L'send bldr start request failed' at 0x544920. Without this registered, a repeated 1041-byte 0xA0 SET before the block phase has no expectation to be checked against.
- readback-interleaved-not-a-pass's refuted_if includes 'any two consecutive A0 06 frames appear with no A0 07 between them (outside a repair burst)'. It exempts repair bursts but not write-retry bursts, which the document itself predicts elsewhere (repair-vs-write-retry-discriminator: up to 5 A0 06 frames for one index, spaced ~250 ms, loop bound cmpl $0x5/jl at 0x403aae-0x403ab1). A normal, expected write-retry would therefore trip this prediction's refutation clause. Worth adding the same exemption before the document is frozen, since the point of pre-registration is that the clause gets applied mechanically.

### Irreversible / one-shot in this area

- The mouse is currently on firmware 1.07 [O, bcdDevice 0x0107]. Running updater 1.10 changes that permanently. Anything wanted from a 1.07-resident device — the settings blob, an A1 12 read, the application-mode report descriptor, bcdDevice — must be taken BEFORE the update. Whether any FWFILE blob we hold would restore 1.07 is [G] (notes §8.5a leaves 'which blob is which version' open), so treat 1.07 as unrecoverable.
- The update ends with A1 13, which is factory reset (notes §5.4a; byte-identical to the config tool's Factory Reset frame). The user's settings are wiped. Capture the settings blob first or they are gone. Note the reachable corner case: if the device fails to re-enumerate as PID 0x1978 within ~136 s, the tool reports success and never sends A1 13 (gate (c) failing at 0x403cc3) — so a run that succeeds without wiping settings is possible and would itself be a finding.
- If any block fails read-back verification, the vendor tool emits 20 futile A0 06 frames and then ABORTS, leaving the device at PID 0x1977 with a partially written application region and no further traffic. That state has never been exercised. The documented recovery is to re-run the same updater, which flashes a device found already in the bootloader with no application handshake (§5.1 case 6). Do not power-cycle repeatedly, do not reinstall drivers, do not open the mouse — re-run the updater.
- A repair burst, if it ever triggers, is a one-shot observation: it cannot be provoked deliberately without corrupting a write, which CLAUDE.md §1.3 and §4.2 forbid. If one appears in the trace, preserve the capture with extra care — it is the only evidence we will ever get about how the device answers a duplicate write and about the 64-byte GET on report 0xA0.
- Entering the bootloader is what makes the 0x1977 report descriptor observable. If the capture does not include that enumeration, the descriptor is unobtainable until the next flash, and each flash is a real write to the one device we have.


---

## Updater 1.10 — completion, the post-flash command, final state

*area key: `updater-completion` — 25 written, 9 rejected*


### Pre-registered (16)

#### 1. `a108-checksum-frame` — [D]

**The last command of the write phase is a single 64-byte feature report A1 08 34 74 followed by 60 zero bytes.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT(Feature): bmRequestType 0x21, bRequest 0x09, wValue 0x03A1, wLength 0x0040. Data stage exactly: A1 08 34 74 00 00 then 58 more 0x00 (64 bytes total). Byte [2] is the literal 0x34; byte [3] is block_count + 0x33 = 65 + 51 = 116 = 0x74; bytes [4] and [5] are explicitly re-zeroed after the memset.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00401bb0 @0x00401bb0: memset(base=-0x44(%ebp),0,0x40) at 0x401bd7; movw $0x8a1,-0x44(%ebp) at 0x401be5; movb $0x34,-0x42(%ebp) at 0x401beb; movb %dl,-0x41(%ebp) at 0x401bef (dl = 0xc(%ebp)); movw %bx,-0x40(%ebp) at 0x401bf2 (ebx zeroed 0x401bca); pushl $0x40 at 0x401be2; send calll 0x4012a0 at 0x401bf6. Last-block argument computed at fw110 0x403b6c movb 0x21c(%esi),%al / 0x403b72 addb $0x33,%al / 0x403b77 pushl %ecx. fw104: movw $0x8a1 @0x404c68, movb $0x34 @0x404c73.
- **REFUTED IF:** Bytes [2]/[3] on the wire are not 0x34/0x74, or wLength is not 0x0040, or more than one A1 08 frame appears on a run whose checksum matches on the first try.
- **Matters:** EGGFlashCore builds this frame; a wrong byte [3] asks the device to checksum the wrong block range and turns gate (a) — our only whole-image verification — into a no-op.

#### 2. `a108-checksum-value` — [D]

**The device's reply to A1 08 carries the 32-bit little-endian value 0x0081D57D at bytes [16..19], and the same four bytes appeared earlier in the run inside the A0 03 start frame.**

- **Expect:** GET_REPORT(Feature): bmRequestType 0xA1, bRequest 0x01, wValue 0x03A1, wLength 0x0040. Reply: [0]=0xA1, [1]=0x01, [16]=0x7D, [17]=0xD5, [18]=0x81, [19]=0x00 (= 8,508,797 decimal). Corroborating: the A0 03 start frame sent at the beginning of the same run has [16]=0x41 (65 blocks) and [17..20] = 7D D5 81 00.
- **Cite:** Value = plain 32-bit additive sum of all 66,560 bytes of resource FWFILE/140 in fw110 (sha256 8148ebe9f8d2848abe483aee98df6e42bab341c6a17523bfef0f85f1f66754d0), computed the way fw110 FUN_00403580 @0x00403580 does it (four byte lanes, 0x400 stride, loop 0x4035b0–0x4035df). Reply assembly: fw110 0x401c49 (status), 0x401c55/0x401c59/0x401c5d/0x401c66 read [19]/[18]/[17]/[16], shifted and or'd 0x401c61–0x401c78, written out at 0x401c7a. Compare gate: fw110 0x403b8c/0x403b95 cmpl 0x25a20(%esi),%eax. A0 03 stores: fw110 0x4018c4 ([16]=count), 0x4018ce/0x4018fd/0x401903/0x401909 ([17..20]).
- **REFUTED IF:** On a run the updater declares successful, resp[16..19] is anything other than 7D D5 81 00; or the A0 03 frame's [16] is not 0x41 or its [17..20] are not 7D D5 81 00.
- **Matters:** This single number is the only end-to-end proof the whole 66,560-byte image landed. Our flasher computes it host-side and compares; if the device's sum is computed differently (e.g. 16-bit, or excluding a header) our verification is worthless.

#### 3. `completion-timing` — [D]

**The gaps around the completion commands are fixed sleeps and are exactly these.**

- **Expect:** Last A0 07 GET_REPORT -> A1 08 SET_REPORT: 320 ms (Sleep 30 + Sleep 90 + Sleep 200). A1 08 SET -> A1 08 GET: 100 ms. A1 08 GET -> A1 09 SET: under 5 ms, no sleep at all. A1 09 SET -> A1 09 GET: 50 ms. A1 13 SET -> final GET: 900 ms.
- **Cite:** fw110: Sleep(30) pushl $0x1e @0x401d78; Sleep(90) pushl $0x5a @0x403b43; Sleep(200) pushl $0xc8 @0x403b65; Sleep(100) pushl $0x64 @0x401bfe; no sleep between the checksum compare 0x403b9b and the memset 0x403bd9; Sleep(50) pushl $0x32 @0x403bfa; Sleep(900) pushl $0x384 @0x403de9. %edi is Sleep, loaded movl 0x51b214,%edi @0x40398e, and 0x51b214 resolves to KERNEL32!Sleep in the import table.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any of these inter-transfer gaps differs from the stated value by more than ~25 ms of host jitter, or a sleep appears between the A1 08 reply and the A1 09 send.
- **Matters:** Our pacing table copies these constants verbatim. Windows' HID stack absorbs a missing settle time that macOS's IOHIDDeviceSetReport will not.

#### 4. `a109-frame` — [D]

**The bootloader-complete command is a single 64-byte feature report A1 09 00 00 followed by 60 zero bytes.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT(Feature): bmRequestType 0x21, bRequest 0x09, wValue 0x03A1, wLength 0x0040. Data stage: A1 09 then 62 bytes of 0x00. Exactly one such frame on a run that succeeds.
- **Cite:** fw110 FUN_00403960: memset(-0x50(%ebp),0,0x40) @0x403bd9; raw bytes c7 45 b0 a1 09 00 00 = movl $0x9a1,-0x50(%ebp) @0x403be4; movl $0x0,-0x4c(%ebp) @0x403beb; pushl $0x40 @0x403be1; calll 0x4012a0 @0x403bf2. fw104: identical 7 raw bytes c7 45 b0 a1 09 00 00 @0x404d85, send calll 0x401e30 @0x404d93.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any byte after [1] is non-zero, or wLength is not 0x0040, or two or more A1 09 frames appear on a run that ends in 'Update Succeed'.
- **Matters:** This is the command our post-erase phase must reach before it is allowed to stop driving. Getting its shape wrong leaves the device sitting in the bootloader with no application, which is exactly the state CLAUDE.md §4.2 says we must never return from.

#### 5. `reattach-bcddevice` — [G]

**The re-attached device reports bcdDevice = 0x0110 in its USB device descriptor.**

- **Expect:** USB device descriptor bcdDevice field changes from 0x0107 (observed on this mouse, [O]) to 0x0110. HidD_GetAttributes VersionNumber = 272. Under the vendor's BCD decode that displays as 1.10.
- **Cite:** [G] as to the number: nothing in fw110 reads a version out of FWFILE 140 and nothing compares one. Supporting [D]: the decode is BCD not division — fw110 FUN_004011f0 @0x401245 movl 0x56a0f4,%ecx, pushl $0x5443b0 (verified L'%x'), calll 0x4014a0 CStringT::Format, calll 0x4f7965 __wtol, movzwl %ax,%edi, caller divides by the double 100.0 at 0x544c78 (verified). The mouse in hand reads bcdDevice 0x0107 = 1.07 [O] and the corpus contains an updater 1.07, so updater version == firmware version delivered.
- **REFUTED IF:** bcdDevice after the flash is anything other than 0x0110. 0x0140 in particular would mean the FWFILE resource NAME is the firmware version, which would overturn notes §4's reasoning.
- **Matters:** It is the only observable that confirms the image we shipped is the image now running, and it settles whether resource names 133/135/137/140/142/143 are versions or product codes — which bears directly on CLAUDE.md §1.4's wrong-image guard.

#### 6. `reenum-budget-168s` — [D]

**The give-up budget for the device coming back is 21 enumeration attempts and 168,000 ms of sleeping — not the 136 s that notes/updater-protocol.md §5.4 step 6 currently states.**

- **Expect:** 1 immediate attempt plus 20 loop attempts. Sleeps executed: 800, 1600, 2400 ... 16000 ms — 20 sleeps, sum = 800 x (1+2+...+20) = 800 x 210 = 168,000 ms = 168 s. The loop continues while ebx <= 0x3e80, and the 16,000 ms sleep does execute before the exit test fails.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403c8d movl $0x320,%ebx; 0x403caa pushl %ebx / 0x403cab calll *%edi; 0x403cad addl $0x320,%ebx; 0x403cb3 cmpl $0x3e80,%ebx; 0x403cb9 jle 0x403c92. fw104 identical constants: 0x404e33 movl $0x320,%esi; 0x404e54 addl $0x320,%esi; 0x404e5a cmpl $0x3e80,%esi; 0x404e60 jle.
- **REFUTED IF:** A run in which the device never re-appears gives up materially sooner or later than ~168 s. (Do not create this condition deliberately — see the warnings.)
- **Matters:** Our own post-A0 03 recovery loop is being written against the vendor's budget as a reference point, and a quoted number that is 32 s short would propagate straight into it. The notes need correcting either way.

#### 7. `a113-frame` — [D]

**The post-success command is a single 64-byte feature report A1 13 00 00 followed by 60 zero bytes, byte-identical to the config tool's Factory Reset frame.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT(Feature): bmRequestType 0x21, bRequest 0x09, wValue 0x03A1, wLength 0x0040. Data stage: A1 13 then 62 bytes of 0x00. The builder's raw bytes are c7 45 b0 a1 13 00 00 in both the updater and the config tool.
- **Cite:** fw110: memset(-0x50(%ebp),0,0x40) @0x403dc4; c7 45 b0 a1 13 00 00 = movl $0x13a1,-0x50(%ebp) @0x403dcf; movl $0x0,-0x4c(%ebp) @0x403dd6; pushl $0x40 @0x403dcc; calll 0x4012a0 @0x403ddd. fw104: c7 45 b0 a1 13 00 00 @0x404f4c, remaining 56 bytes zeroed by movlpd %xmm0 stores 0x404f56–0x404f7c, send calll 0x401e30 @0x404f88. cfg107: same 7 bytes at 0x40479f inside FUN_00404720, reached from the 'Factory Reset' button (dialog 102, control 1039) via handler 0x00413f90 (0x413f9d pushl $0x1978, 0x413fa2 open, 0x413faf calll 0x404720).
- **REFUTED IF:** Any byte after [1] is non-zero, or wLength is not 0x0040, or the updater's frame differs in any byte from the config tool's Factory Reset frame.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.1 gates every config write on factory reset working first. That gate is only implementable if this frame is exactly what we think it is, and the two tools sending the identical frame is the evidence that the updater's post-flash command IS the factory reset.

#### 8. `a113-target-and-discipline` — [D]

**A1 13 is sent to the re-enumerated APPLICATION-mode device (PID 0x1978), exactly once, never retried, and its reply is fetched 900 ms later and thrown away.**

- **Expect:** One SET_REPORT to VID 0x3367 / PID 0x1978 — never to 0x1977 — on a handle freshly opened by the post-flash enumeration. If the send fails the tool skips the read entirely and still prints the success text. If it succeeds: Sleep(900) then exactly one GET_REPORT wValue 0x03A1 wLength 0x0040, whose return value and every byte of whose buffer are ignored.
- **Cite:** fw110: gate 0x403cc3 je 0x403e6f (only a found 0x1978 reaches the send); send 0x403ddd; testl %eax,%eax / je 0x403e18 @0x403de5 (failed send skips the read and lands on the same continuation as success); pushl $0x384 @0x403de9; recv calll 0x401330 @0x403e10; the next instruction 0x403e18 flds -0x94(%ebp) consumes the version float, not the response buffer. The handle used is DAT_0056a174, written by FUN_00401000 at 0x4010e8 during the post-flash enumeration. fw104: 0x404f8d testl/je, 0x404f91 Sleep(900), 0x404faa recv.
- **REFUTED IF:** A1 13 appears while the device is at PID 0x1977; or appears more than once; or is retried after a failure; or the gap to the following GET_REPORT is not ~900 ms; or the tool visibly reacts to the reply's contents.
