- **Matters:** build-design.md §2.4 proposes that if we send A1 13 we use the config tool's checked form (1100 ms + require resp[1]==1) rather than the updater's fire-and-forget. This prediction is what makes that a deviation we can name rather than an accident.

#### 9. `a113-is-last-transfer` — [D]

**The GET_REPORT 900 ms after A1 13 is the last USB transfer the updater causes. Nothing follows but CloseHandle.**

- **Expect:** Zero further class or vendor requests from the updater after that GET. In particular: no second A1 09, no second A1 08, no A0 11, no A1 12, no re-verification read-back, no settings write.
- **Cite:** fw110 control flow after 0x403e15: 0x403e18–0x403e30 formats L'Update Succeed, current firmware version is V%.2f' (0x544a50, verified), 0x403e45 calll 0x4135fa (CWnd::SetWindowText, whose first import is USER32!IsWindow at 0x51b7d4), 0x403e4a–0x403e6b CString release, 0x403e96 movl 0x56a174,%ecx / 0x403e9d calll *0x51b200 = KERNEL32!CloseHandle, 0x403ea3–0x403ead return 1. Transport census: HidD_SetFeature IAT slot 0x51b1d4 is dereferenced at exactly 2 sites, both inside wrapper 0x4012a0, and that wrapper has exactly 7 callers recovered by an E8-rel32 sweep of all 1,154,048 bytes of .text — 0x40190f 0x401a60 0x401b26 0x401bf6 0x4037a1 0x403bf2 0x403ddd — of which 0x403ddd is the last.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any feature report from the updater process appears after that GET_REPORT.
- **Matters:** It fixes the end of the sequence our flasher must reproduce. If the vendor does something after A1 13 that we have not derived, our 'done' condition is wrong and the mock bootloader's success criterion is wrong with it.

#### 10. `no-third-reenumeration` — [G]

**The device does NOT detach again after A1 13. It stays enumerated as 3367:1978 with the new bcdDevice.**

- **Expect:** No detach/attach in the 30 s following the A1 13 GET_REPORT. The mouse remains usable throughout; the cursor does not stall.
- **Cite:** [G] as to the device's behaviour. Supporting [D]: cfg107's Factory Reset handler FUN_00413f90 does not re-open the device after 0x413faf calll 0x404720 — it calls 0x413db0 twice (0x413fb9, 0x413fc3) to reset local copies and refreshes the UI; and FUN_00404720 itself waits 1100 ms (pushl $0x44c @0x4047af) then requires resp[1]==1 (cmpb $0x1,-0x8f(%ebp) @0x4047e2) on the SAME handle. A device that reset or re-enumerated could not answer that.
- **REFUTED IF:** USBPcap shows a detach/attach within ~10 s of the A1 13 transfer.
- **Matters:** If factory reset re-enumerates, then CLAUDE.md §4.1's 'implement factory reset first and confirm it works' needs a handle-reopen step, and our config tool must survive its own device disappearing mid-operation.

#### 11. `final-success-string` — [D]

**The status line reads exactly 'Update Succeed, current firmware version is V1.10'.**

- **Expect:** Format string L'Update Succeed, current firmware version is V%.2f'; the %.2f argument is the BCD-decoded bcdDevice of the RE-ENUMERATED device divided by 100, so two decimal places: '1.10'. The version is read before A1 13 is sent, from the enumeration that found PID 0x1978.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403d43 movl 0x56a0f4,%ecx — that global is written only on a full match, at 0x40119d from movzwl -0x8(%ebp) @0x40118a, which is HIDD_ATTRIBUTES.VersionNumber (struct base -0x10, Size at -0x10 set to 0xc @0x4010ed, VID at -0xc, PID at -0xa, VersionNumber at -0x8). Format 0x403d50 pushl $0x5443b0 (L'%x') / 0x403d56 calll 0x4014a0; 0x403d62 calll 0x4f7965 (__wtol); 0x403d67 movzwl %ax,%ebx; 0x403d93 fildl / 0x403da1 fdivl 0x544c78 (=100.0); 0x403e2a pushl $0x544a50.
- **REFUTED IF:** The message shows 1.07, 1.40, 2.72, 3.23, or anything but 1.10 — each of which identifies a different failure (no version change, resource-name-as-version, integer-divide decode, integer-divide of 0x143 respectively).
- **Matters:** Our own tool must display a version, and the BCD-round-trip decode is easy to get wrong in exactly the way notes §1 warns about. This is the cheapest confirmation that the decode is right.

#### 12. `wire-encoding` — [G]

**The report ID travels both in wValue's low byte and as byte 0 of the data stage, and wLength counts it.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT: bmRequestType 0x21, bRequest 0x09, wValue 0x03A1 or 0x03A0, wLength 0x0040 or 0x0411, data[0] = 0xA1 or 0xA0. GET_REPORT: bmRequestType 0xA1, bRequest 0x01, same wValue/wLength, reply[0] = the report id.
- **Cite:** [D] for the lengths: fw110 passes 0x40 (0x403be1, 0x403dcc, 0x401be2) and 0x411 (0x4018a5, 0x4018e0) to HidD_SetFeature with the id already at buf[0] (0x403be4, 0x403dcf, 0x401be5, 0x4018e9). [O] for the device side: the report descriptor in notes/device-predictions.md declares Feature 0xA1 as 0x3f bytes = 63 payload and Feature 0xA0 as 1040 payload, i.e. 64 and 1041 on the wire. [G] for the Windows HID stack's framing convention.
- **REFUTED IF:** wLength is 0x003F / 0x0410, or the data stage omits the report id byte.
- **Matters:** macOS IOHIDDeviceSetReport takes the report id as a separate argument from the buffer. Getting this convention wrong is a silent off-by-one on every frame we send.

#### 13. `updater-never-touches-settings` — [D]

**The updater never reads or writes the settings blob. A1 13 is its only settings-related act.**

- **Expect:** No frame with report id 0xA1 and [1]=0x12, and no frame with report id 0xA0 and [1]=0x11, anywhere in the updater's capture. Its entire command vocabulary in the capture is exactly seven values: A1 3A, A0 03, A0 06, A0 07, A1 08, A1 09, A1 13.
- **Cite:** fw110: the only HidD_SetFeature caller is FUN_004012a0 (IAT slot 0x51b1d4 dereferenced at exactly 2 sites, both inside it), and an E8-rel32 sweep of all 1,154,048 .text bytes gives it exactly 7 callers — 0x40190f 0x401a60 0x401b26 0x401bf6 0x4037a1 0x403bf2 0x403ddd. An independent whole-.text immediate scan (Tools/ghidra-export/cmdscan.py) finds 13 report-id stores, all inside the same six functions. fw104 reduces to the same seven commands in four functions (0x402a90, 0x402c00, 0x404760, 0x404980).
- **REFUTED IF:** Any eighth command byte appears from the updater process, or any A1 12 / A0 11 does.
- **Matters:** It settles that the vendor makes no attempt to preserve settings across a flash, so any preservation in our tool is entirely ours to design. It is also the strongest available test of §3.7a's completeness claim, which is what bounds the whole protocol.

#### 14. `progress-then-pause` — [D]

**The progress bar reaches 100 while the last block is still being written, then the window sits at 100 with unchanged text for roughly 3.5–4 s before 'Update Succeed' appears.**

- **Expect:** progress = (100 * i) / 65 + 2 for i = 0..64, i.e. 2, 3, ... 98, 100 — sent as PBM_SETPOS (0x402). The bar is never updated after the last block. Remaining wall time to the success text: 320 ms (to A1 08) + 100 ms + ~0 + 50 ms + re-enumeration wait (~2400 ms predicted) + 900 ms ~= 3.8 s.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403b18 movl -0x98(%ebp),%eax / 0x403b1e cltd / 0x403b1f idivl 0x21c(%esi) / 0x403b2d addl $0x2,%eax / 0x403b31 pushl $0x402 / 0x403b3d calll *0x51b838 (USER32!SendMessageW); the dividend -0x98 starts at 0 (0x403a5c) and gains 0x64 per block (0x403b51), and the update happens BEFORE the increment (incl %ebx @0x403b58). Sleep(90) @0x403b43.
- **REFUTED IF:** The bar stops short of 100, or the pause after it hits 100 is under 1 s or over 10 s on a run that succeeds.
- **Matters:** It is a UI-side cross-check on the whole completion timeline that anyone can observe without a capture, and it gives a human-visible marker for where in the traffic the block loop ended.

#### 15. `gate-c-ui-says-failed` — [D]

**On the reachable path where the device does not come back at PID 0x1978, the tool displays 'Update failed, try again' and never sends A1 13 — even though FUN_00403960 returns 1. notes/updater-protocol.md §5.4a's phrase 'the tool reports success' is wrong at the UI level and should be corrected.**

- **Expect:** Path: 0x403cc3 je 0x403e6f -> busy flag 0x25a38 = 0 and EnableWindow(button, TRUE) -> SetWindowText with 0x544ab4 = L'Update failed, try again' -> CloseHandle -> xorl %edx,%edx; xorl %eax,%eax; movw %ax,0x56a0fc; leal 0x1(%edx),%eax -> return 1. If a capture ever shows this, the image was written AND whole-image-checksum-verified AND A1 09-acknowledged, and the settings were NOT reset.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403cc3, 0x403e6f–0x403e91 (0x403e8c pushl $0x544ab4, string verified as L'Update failed, try again'), 0x403e96–0x403ead. fw104 has the same gate at 0x404e7f testl %ebx,%ebx / je 0x405006.
- **REFUTED IF:** This path also emits A1 13, or displays the success string, or does not display 'Update failed, try again'.
- **Matters:** It is the one outcome where the UI's message and the device's actual state disagree. Our tool must not inherit that, and if it shows up in a capture we must not read it as 'the flash failed' and re-flash on a false premise.

#### 16. `codebase-b-agrees` — [D]

**Every completion constant in the capture will match updater 1.04 as well as 1.10 — the two independently compiled code bases agree exactly on this phase.**

- **Expect:** A1 09 frame builder c7 45 b0 a1 09 00 00; Sleep(50) after it; 10-attempt bound; re-enumeration sleeps 800 step 800 up to 16000; the gate on the found flag; A1 13 frame builder c7 45 b0 a1 13 00 00; Sleep(900); one discarded read. All present in both binaries at different addresses.
- **Cite:** fw104: 0x404d85 (A1 09 build), 0x404d93 (send, calll 0x401e30), 0x404d98 (Sleep 50), 0x404dc7 (recv, calll 0x401ed0), 0x404de8 cmpl $0xa,%ebx / 0x404deb jle, 0x404e23 + 0x404e40 (movl $0x1978,%edx then calll 0x401bc0), 0x404e33 movl $0x320,%esi, 0x404e54 addl $0x320, 0x404e5a cmpl $0x3e80, 0x404e7f testl %ebx,%ebx / je 0x405006, 0x404f4c (A1 13 build), 0x404f88 (send), 0x404f91 pushl $0x384, 0x404faa (recv). fw110 counterparts as cited in the other rows.
- **REFUTED IF:** The timings observed on the wire (50 ms after A1 09, 900 ms after A1 13, 800 ms first re-enumeration sleep) do not match these constants — which would mean the constants read out of both binaries are not the ones reaching the device, and the whole static derivation of this phase is suspect.
- **Matters:** Two independently compiled builds agreeing is the strongest confirmation static analysis alone can produce, and the capture is the first chance to check that the agreement is about the device rather than about a shared misreading.

### Rejected by audit (9) — not pre-registered

| id | fault | auditor's evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `settings-wiped-blob` | `mistagged` | Tagged [D], but the claim is a device-effect claim: "After a normally-completing flash the mouse's stored settings are at the firmware's factory defaults, byte for byte." The committed notes say the opposite about provenance. notes/updater-protocol.md §6, on A1 13: "Currently `[D]` for the frame and the send; `[G]` only for what the device does on receipt." The document's OWN cannot_predict entry #1 repeats this ("notes/updater-protocol.md §6 records the effect as [G]"). I verified every byte of the citation chain and it only ever supports the frame and the button caption: cfg107 dialog 102 control 1039 caption 'Factory Reset' (dlgdump.py), handler 0x00413f90 (0x413f9d pushl $0x1978, 0x413faf calll 0x404720), builder 0x40479f `c7 45 b0 a1 13 00 00`, and fw110 0x403dcf byte-identical. None of that says what the device does. CLAUDE.md §1.2/§1.3 key a real safety decision off this tag (Flagged-for-decision #1 is exactly whether our flasher sends A1 13), so a [G] wearing a [D] here is the most expensive mistag in the set. Correct tag: [G]. |
| `settings-user-visible` | `mistagged` | Same fault as settings-wiped-blob and same citation chain ("Same chain as settings-wiped-blob"). The claim "The user sees settings revert without touching anything" is a device-behaviour claim tagged [D]. notes/updater-protocol.md §6 explicitly holds the effect of A1 13 at [G]. The prediction is careful to disclaim the byte->UI-field mapping but not the underlying effect, which is the part that is underived. |
| `reattach-timing` | `mistagged` | Tagged [D], but the claim is device re-enumeration latency: "The tool's immediate post-A1 09 poll fails; the device is found on the second or third poll." I disassembled the whole cited region (0x403c7c immediate attempt with no preceding Sleep; 0x403c8d movl $0x320,%ebx; loop 0x403c92; 0x403caa pushl %ebx / calll *%edi; 0x403cad addl $0x320; 0x403cb3 cmpl $0x3e80; 0x403cb9 jle) — every cited byte is correct, and it supports only the sleep schedule. Nothing in fw110 determines whether poll #1 succeeds. The document's own cannot_predict #4 says so: "how long after the reply the USB detach happens ... The host code ... never measures or reasons about the interval." The sibling predictions reattach-bcddevice and no-third-reenumeration correctly tag device-side claims [G]; this one does not. Correct tag: [G] for the 2nd/3rd-poll claim, [D] only for the schedule. |
| `post-complete-detach` | `mistagged` | Tagged [D]. The host-side half is genuinely [D] and I verified it (0x403c7c and 0x403c92 both `pushl $0x1978`; FUN_00401000 predicate at 0x4010fa/0x4010ff VID 0x3367, 0x401109 PID vs 0x8(%ebp), 0x401137/0x40113c UsagePage 0xff01, 0x401145 Usage 0x02; a whole-.text immediate scan finds 0x1978 pushed only at 0x403602/0x403643/0x403690/0x403c7b/0x403c91 and 0x1977 only at 0x4036c3/0x4037ff/0x403830/0x4038d0). But the claim sentence is about the device — "The device drops off the bus after answering A1 09" — and nothing in the binary asserts a detach; the tool polling for 0x1978 is consistent with no detach at all. Worse, exact_expectation asserts the re-attached 1.10 device's descriptor contents ("two HID interfaces, iManufacturer 'Endgame Gear', iProduct 'Endgame Gear OP1 8k v2 Gaming Mouse'"). Those come from .analysis/hidobserve_3367.json, which I read: entries [0] and [3] give Manufacturer 'Endgame Gear', Product 'Endgame Gear OP1 8k v2 Gaming Mouse', VendorID 13159, ProductID 6520, VersionNumber 263 — an [O] of the device at firmware 1.07. Projecting it onto post-1.10 firmware is [G], and the document's cannot_predict #6 concedes the descriptors may change across the flash. |
| `transfer-counts` | `citation-wrong` | The exact counts (135 SET / 135 GET, GET-0x03A1 = 70) assume one HID transfer per wrapper call, and the cited evidence does not support that. I disassembled both wrappers. FUN_00401330 (0x401330-0x40141b): on a transport-success read it inspects buf[1] at 0x40135d and, while buf[1]==0x04, re-issues HidD_GetFeature in a loop (0x401365-0x4013a3) with Sleep(esi), esi = 0,100,...,1900 while esi < 0x7d0 — up to 20 EXTRA GET_REPORTs and ~19 s per command; and on transport failure it retries up to 4 attempts (0x4013e0-0x40140d, `cmpl $0x4,%edi`) with Sleep(50). FUN_004012a0 (0x4012a0-0x40132e) likewise retries the SEND up to 4 attempts (0x4012f0-0x40131e, `cmpl $0x4,%edi`, Sleep(10)). notes/updater-protocol.md §2 documents both verbatim ("20 passes, ~19 s total"; "at most 3 retries (4 attempts total)"), so the prediction contradicts the committed notes without saying so. The prediction's only caveat covers A1 3A and A0 03 retries. The consequence is in the refuted_if, which mis-states the diagnosis: "If the real capture has an extra transfer we cannot account for, there is an eighth command we never found" — an unaccounted 0xA1 GET is far more likely a busy-poll of a known command, and reading it as an eighth command on an irreversible capture is exactly the wrong inference. (Same wrapper-level issue makes a113-target-and-discipline's "exactly once, never retried" an absolute the SetFeature wrapper can break.) |
| `no-version-gate-second-run` | `citation-wrong` | Citation states: "the only cmp instructions in the whole handler are 0x4033dc, 0x40349e, 0x4034a6 and 0x403500." I disassembled 0x4033b0-0x40357c in full. There are SIX cmp instructions: those four plus 0x403401 `cmpl %edi,%eax` and 0x403406 `cmpl %edi,%ecx`. It also omits the `test` form entirely — 0x40345b `testl %eax,%eax` (a live control-flow gate on FUN_00403200's return, branching to L"Read firmware file failed"), 0x40348e, 0x40355d. CLAUDE.md §1.2a names this exact failure: a scan for one encoding form says nothing about the forms it cannot see, and the claim here is a negative one. For the record the substantive conclusion does survive independent check — a whole-file 4-byte scan for 0x0056a0f4 gives exactly three sites: the write at 0x40119f and the reads at 0x401247 (FUN_004011f0) and 0x403d45 (FUN_00403960), both display-only — but the stated basis for it is wrong and would not be reproducible by a reader following it. |
| `relaunch-version-string` | `vague` | The claim is "Relaunching updater 1.10 immediately after a successful flash shows 'Mouse firmware current version 1.10' before anything is written." The cited function is FUN_004033b0, which is the Update BUTTON handler, not launch or dialog init: the MFC message-map entry at file offset 0x143784 reads nMessage 0x111 (WM_COMMAND), nCode 0, nID 0x3e9 (=1001, dialog 131 control 1001 captioned 'Update'), nSig 0x39, pfn 0x004033b0, and notes/updater-protocol.md §5.1 calls it "the Update button". A whole-file scan for the string pointer 0x00544840 finds exactly one reference, 0x4034cf, inside that handler. So relaunching alone shows nothing; the line appears only after the operator clicks Update, and clicking Update immediately posts the flash worker (0x4034f4 pushes 0x00402f00 -> 0x403750). The refuted_if — "or reads nothing while the mouse is plugged in and working" — will therefore fire on a perfectly correct device if the operator does what the claim literally says. A refuted_if that a correct model can trip is not a clean falsifier. |
| `a109-response` | `citation-wrong` | exact_expectation is correct and I verified every address (Sleep(50) 0x403bfa; memset 0x403c0f; movb $-0x5f -0x90(%ebp) 0x403c1d; recv 0x403c24; cmpb $0x1,-0x8f(%ebp) 0x403c30; retry sleep 0x403c39-0x403c40; incl %ebx 0x403c42; cmpl $0xa,%ebx / jle 0x403bd1 at 0x403c49/0x403c4c; L'send bldr complete request failed' confirmed at 0x544a08). But matters_because states "The vendor treats it as failure after 10 tries in 55 ms, which is a very short window if the device resets on receipt." 55 ms is only the sum of the retry sleeps (1..10). The retry target is 0x403bd1, i.e. the whole build-send-Sleep(50)-read cycle, so each of the 10 attempts also pays the Sleep(50) at 0x403bfa: the floor is 10*50 + 55 = 555 ms, and more whenever the read wrapper retries (Sleep(50) x4) or busy-polls (up to 19 s). The window is an order of magnitude wider than stated, and that number is what sizes our own completion retry. |
| `vendor-interface-index` | `citation-wrong` | The citation describes entry [0] of hidobserve_3367.json as "the 156-byte descriptor interface (keyboard boot protocol, the two vendor collections, MaxFeatureReportSize 1041)". It has THREE vendor top-level collections, not two. notes/device-predictions.md carries a section headed "The device presents a THIRD vendor collection, and nothing in Endgame's software opens it [O]" and tabulates 0xFF01/0x02, 0xFF02/0x01 and 0xFF02/0x02. I confirmed it in the raw [O] descriptor bytes in the JSON: usage-page items 06 01 FF at indices 47-49, 06 02 FF at 108-110, and 06 02 FF at 132-134. The IORegistryEntryIDs cited are correct (4295408007 for the 156-byte interface, 4295408008 for the 69-byte one) and the wIndex=0 conclusion is untouched, but the description contradicts a committed note that exists specifically to stop that collection being forgotten. |

### Cannot predict — what the capture will teach us (10)

- **What the device actually DOES when it receives A1 13 — wipe a settings region, reload a defaults image from ROM, or something broader such as clearing calibration or sensor tuning.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: Both tools only send 64 bytes with no payload and, in the updater's case, discard the reply. Nothing on the host side describes or depends on the effect. notes/updater-protocol.md §6 records the effect as [G]; only the button caption 'Factory Reset' in cfg107's dialog 102 names it at all.
  - how a capture answers it: The four-blob experiment (D_107 / S / P / D_110) plus a walk of every config-tool page before and after. If P == D_110 exactly, A1 13 is a settings-region reset and nothing more. If D_107 and D_110 differ in fields whose UI meaning is stable, the firmware's own defaults changed across versions — a distinct finding.
- **Whether writing blocks 0x34..0x74 disturbs the settings independently of A1 13 — i.e. whether the settings region overlaps anything the flash touches.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The block-to-flash-address mapping is [G] (notes §3.8, §10.1: index 0x34 is [D], 0x34*1024 is not), and both hypotheses — 'the flash wiped it' and 'A1 13 wiped it' — predict the same observation: defaults after a normal run.
  - how a capture answers it: It cannot, from a normal run. Separating them requires a flash that completes and then does NOT send A1 13, which is only reachable by preventing the device from re-enumerating — i.e. unplugging it mid-flash. That is not worth doing on one mouse. Working-memory 'Flagged for decision' #2 should stay open after this capture.
- **Whether the FWFILE resource names 133/135/137/140/142/143 are firmware versions or product codes.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The tool hardcodes 140 and never reads a version out of the blob; the blobs are encrypted at H~7.94 bits/byte and the cipher is unidentified (working-memory open gap #4). Both readings survive every static check (notes §4).
  - how a capture answers it: The post-flash bcdDevice settles it in one packet: 0x0110 means the names are not versions, 0x0140 means they are.
- **Whether the bootloader answers A1 09 before it resets, and how long after the reply the USB detach happens.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The host code requires resp[1]==0x01 and then immediately starts polling for PID 0x1978; it never measures or reasons about the interval. The 50 ms sleep at 0x403bfa is the vendor's guess, not a device fact.
  - how a capture answers it: The capture timestamps the A1 09 reply and the detach directly. If the reply never arrives on a run that nonetheless succeeds, gate (b) in notes §5.4a is not what we think it is and our completion logic needs rewriting.
- **The bootloader's own USB and HID descriptors at PID 0x1977 — how many interfaces, which one carries the vendor collection, its wIndex, its report descriptor, its bcdDevice.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: FUN_00401000 reads only VID, PID, Usage and UsagePage from the bootloader device and never inspects report lengths (notes §6.1). Nothing else in either updater touches the bootloader's descriptors.
  - how a capture answers it: USBPcap records the full enumeration of the 0x1977 device the moment it appears. This is the single highest-value item in the whole capture for us, because CLAUDE.md §4.2 requires refusing an unrecognised bootloader identity and we currently have nothing to recognise it by.
- **Whether firmware 1.10 changes the application-mode report descriptor — in particular whether the unexplained fifth top-level collection (UsagePage 0xFF02 / Usage 0x02, Input report 0x08, 64 bytes) still exists.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The updaters contain zero 4-byte 0xff02 immediates and the config tools only ever filter on Usage 1, so no binary in the corpus references that collection at all. It was found on the device, not in any binary.
  - how a capture answers it: The GET_DESCRIPTOR(Report) in the post-flash re-enumeration gives the new descriptor verbatim, comparable byte-for-byte against the 156-byte 1.07 descriptor already recorded in notes/device-predictions.md.
- **What bytes [2..63] of the A1 09 and A1 13 replies contain.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The updater reads only [1] of the A1 09 reply and literally nothing of the A1 13 reply (notes §3.7b). Whatever the device puts there has never been looked at by any code in the corpus.
  - how a capture answers it: The reply data stages are in the capture in full. If either carries an error code or a reason byte, it is the first diagnostic channel this protocol has been shown to have, and notes §3.7b's 'there is no error code, no reason byte' would need amending.
- **Whether the device would accept A1 13 in bootloader mode, and whether A1 13's 60 zero payload bytes are ignored or meaningful.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: No vendor tool ever sends A1 13 to PID 0x1977, and no vendor tool ever sets a non-zero byte after [1]. What the device tolerates is entirely outside the search space the binaries define.
  - how a capture answers it: It would not, from a vendor capture. Only our own experiments could, and neither is worth attempting on one mouse.
- **Whether a downgrade is possible — running updater 1.07 after 1.10.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: All four updaters load the same hardcoded resource 140 (FindResourceW(NULL,0x8c,L"FWFILE") at fw110 0x403200 and fw104 0x404330) with different blob contents, so the mechanism exists; whether the device or bootloader refuses an older image is [G], and CLAUDE.md §2 assumes the device validates nothing.
  - how a capture answers it: Only by doing it, which is a second full flash with all the same risk. It is not part of this capture, but the answer determines whether the 1.07 state is recoverable at all.
- **Whether the reachable 'flash succeeded but device did not come back' branch (gate (c)) ever occurs in practice.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: It is a race between the device's re-enumeration time and a 168 s budget. Nothing static bounds the device side.
  - how a capture answers it: Only by observing it accidentally. It must not be provoked deliberately, since provoking it means unplugging a mouse whose application region has just been rewritten.

### Gaps the auditor flagged (5)

- No prediction commits to the status byte NOT being 0x04 on any completion read, even though the notes make 0x04 a first-class device answer. notes/updater-protocol.md §2 (which I verified against 0x40135d-0x4013a3) says resp[1]==0x04 means busy and turns one read into up to 21 GET_REPORTs spread over ~19 s of 100 ms-stepped sleeps. The completion phase is exactly where a device finalising flash would plausibly report busy. A free, falsifiable prediction is available — 'every completion read (A1 08, A1 09, A1 13) returns resp[1]==0x01 on the first GET; no repeated 0xA1 GET with 100 ms-stepped spacing appears' — and without it an operator who sees extra 0xA1 GETs has no reference point and, per transfer-counts' refuted_if, would misread them as an undiscovered eighth command.
- Nothing predicts what resp[1] values other than 0x01 and 0x04 the device can emit, or what the A1 08 reply carries outside [1] and [16..19]. §3.7b's 'there is no error code, no reason byte' is a statement about the TOOL, not the device, and the capture is the only opportunity to test it. cannot_predict #7 covers bytes [2..63] of the A1 09 and A1 13 replies but silently omits the A1 08 reply's unread bytes ([2..15] and [20..63]) and the A0 03 / A0 06 status replies — and A1 08 is the one whose semantics the whole verification story rests on. If those bytes are not extracted from this capture they cost another full flash.
- Nothing predicts that the Update button is re-enabled and the re-entrancy flag cleared BEFORE A1 13 is sent, which is both observable and an operational hazard for the capture. At 0x403cd1 the handler clears the busy flag (`movl $0x0, 0x25a38(%esi)`) and at 0x403cdb calls 0x413707, which I confirmed is CWnd::EnableWindow reaching USER32!EnableWindow at 0x51b840 — both roughly 900 ms plus a round trip before the success text appears at 0x403e45. The Update handler's own entry gate is 0x4033dc `cmpl %edi,0x25a38(%esi)` / jne-return, so clearing it to 0 re-arms the click. So there is a window in which a second Update click is accepted while a factory reset is in flight. capture_requirements says nothing about not clicking Update again while the bar sits at 100.
- The capture_requirements have the operator press the config tool's Factory Reset twice (steps 1 and 6) with the capture running, yet no prediction covers the config tool's own A1 13 exchange — despite it being the exact 'checked form' build-design.md §2.4 proposes we adopt. cfg107 gives literals: 0x40479f builds the identical frame, 0x4047af `pushl $0x44c` = Sleep(1100), then a 64-byte 0xA1 GET whose resp[1] must equal 1 at 0x4047e2 (`cmpb $0x1,-0x8f(%ebp)`), on the same handle. Predicting 1100 ms and resp[1]==0x01 costs nothing and is the only chance to validate the form we intend to ship before we ship it.
- a109-response describes the retry as 'the whole build-send-sleep-read cycle', but on a FAILED send no read is issued at all: 0x403bf2 stores the send result in ebx and 0x403c00/0x403c02 `testl %ebx,%ebx` / `je 0x403c39` jumps straight to the retry sleep. So a send-failure retry shows as a SET with no following GET, which is a distinguishable signature worth naming in advance if it appears.

### Irreversible / one-shot in this area

- The 1.07 -> 1.10 transition happens once on this mouse. The pre-flash settings blob, the 1.07 report descriptor and the 1.07 bcdDevice cannot be recovered afterwards except by a downgrade flash, which is itself untested and carries the same risk as the update. Capture all three BEFORE running the updater.
- A1 13 destroys the user's stored settings. There is no undo other than re-entering them by hand. Capture blob S (the deliberately non-default state) before the flash or the settings-wipe prediction cannot be tested at all.
- The bootloader at PID 0x1977 is only on the bus during a flash. Its descriptors are observable only while the capture is running inside that window. If the capture is started late or filtered to the application device, that observation is lost for this flash and costs another full flash to retake.
- The gate-(c) branch — flash completes, A1 13 never sent, UI says 'Update failed, try again' — is reachable only by preventing the device from re-enumerating within ~168 s, i.e. by unplugging it after A1 09. Do NOT provoke it to test the prediction. It is the one state where the device has a fresh image but the tool has abandoned it.
- Once the updater has been run, the device's firmware is 1.10 for every subsequent experiment in this repo. Any observation whose value depends on the device being at 1.07 must be taken first.


---

## Which firmware bytes actually reach the wire

*area key: `firmware-image-identity` — 22 written, 4 rejected*


### Pre-registered (18)

#### 1. `only-140-reaches-wire` — [D]

**Only FWFILE resource 140 is loaded. Resources 133, 135, 137, 142 and 143 are unreachable and none of their bytes appears anywhere in the capture.**

- **Expect:** Exactly one FindResourceW call site in the whole binary can name a FWFILE: 0x403207 pushl $0x5447c0 (L"FWFILE") / 0x40320c pushl $0x8c (=140) / 0x403211 pushl $0x0 / 0x403213 calll *0x51b230. The VA 0x5447c0 occurs as a 4-byte literal exactly ONCE in all 2,133,504 bytes of the file. EnumResourceNamesW/A are not imported. On the wire, no 1024-byte payload will begin with any other resource's block-0 signature: 133=FA 70 11 F5…, 135=2E EB 73 13…, 137=8E 39 98 3E…, 142=30 F0 B6 67…, 143=E5 8A 72 E2…
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403207–0x403213 (raw objdump); IAT slot 0x51b230 resolved to KERNEL32!FindResourceW from the import table; whole-file count of the 4-byte literal 0x005447c0 = 1 (scratchpad scan). Same shape in fw104 at 0x40433a with the string at 0x5a1cd8, also whole-file count 1.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any 1024-byte payload in the capture matches a chunk of FWFILE 133/135/137/142/143, or the total distinct payload content exceeds 66,560 bytes.
- **Matters:** This is the entire basis for hardcoding name 140 (CLAUDE.md §1.4). If a second resource reached the wire our compile-time constant would be wrong and we would flash a partial or foreign image.

#### 2. `load-path-verbatim` — [D]

**The host applies NO transform between the resource and the wire — no decryption, no decompression, no reordering, no content re-checksum. The bytes in the .exe are the bytes in the SET_REPORT data stage.**

- **Expect:** fw110 FUN_00403200: 0x40323a SizeofResource; 0x403242 shrl $0xa gives chunk count 65 stored to obj+0x21c; 0x40324b/0x40324e give remainder 0; 0x403258 LoadResource; 0x40327b LockResource -> src pointer at obj+0x25a3c; 0x403295 movl $0x1,%ebx = copy stride 1; 0x4032a6 memset(obj+0x220 + chunk*0x400, 0, 0x400); 0x4032c0–0x403313 a 4x-unrolled loop of movzbl (src) / movb, src advanced by ebx=1 per byte. The remainder tail path 0x403336–0x403386 never executes.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403200–0x4033a0, raw objdump. IAT slots resolved from the loader-authoritative import table: 0x51b220=SizeofResource, 0x51b228=LoadResource, 0x51b224=LockResource, 0x51b230=FindResourceW.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any A0 06 payload differs from the corresponding 1024-byte slice of FWFILE 140 — even one byte, even ordering — while the update still completes successfully.
- **Matters:** Our flasher ships resource 140 unmodified. If the host did transform, our byte stream would be wrong at every block and no downstream check would catch it (CLAUDE.md §2).

#### 3. `payload-total-bytes` — [D]

**Exactly 66,560 firmware payload bytes travel host-to-device, in 65 A0 06 frames of 1024 payload bytes each, with every image byte sent exactly once.**

- **Expect:** block_count = 66560 >> 10 = 65, remainder = 0. Loop at fw110 0x403a48–0x403b5f runs i=0..64; the source pointer -0xa0(%ebp) starts at obj+0x220 (0x403a56) and advances by exactly 0x400 once per verified block (0x403b47), never inside the 5-try write-retry loop, so a retried block re-sends the same 1024 bytes. Total = 65 x 1024 = 66,560 payload bytes; total A0 06 control-OUT data = 65 x 1041 = 67,665 bytes.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403242 (shrl $0xa), 0x403a48–0x403b5f (block loop), 0x4019ee (rep movsl with ecx=0x100 dwords = 1024 bytes, edi = frame base + 0x10).
- **REFUTED IF:** The capture contains a number of distinct-index A0 06 frames other than 65, or any A0 06 whose payload is not 1024 bytes, or a short/partial final block.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.3 names 'wrong chunk boundary' as a top failure mode; §10.3 invariants 1–5 assert exactly this arithmetic.

#### 4. `payload-sha256` — [D]

**The SHA-256 of the 65 A0 06 payloads concatenated in transmission order equals the SHA-256 of FWFILE/140 itself, because the concatenation IS the resource.**

- **Expect:** sha256 over the concatenation of bytes [16..1039] of the 65 A0 06 frames in index order 0x34..0x74 = 8148ebe9f8d2848abe483aee98df6e42bab341c6a17523bfef0f85f1f66754d0, length 66,560. MD5 = 7e2e055514a90e81b61fb8572421124f. If instead the full 1041-byte frames are concatenated: sha256 = 6053a5897ec7e7772bef0419a6cabe5e2d9f1749c156de388cf5b966fa7b94eb, length 67,665.
- **Cite:** Resource bytes at fw110 file offset 0x193950 length 66560; frames reconstructed exactly per fw110 0x4019be, 0x4019d5, 0x4019de, 0x401a54, 0x401a5a, 0x4019ee and the length immediate pushl $0x411 at 0x401a4e.
- **REFUTED IF:** Either hash differs. This single comparison turns 'did we pick the right image' from an argument into a test.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §2 requires the wrong-image guard to be ours and to run before the first byte goes out. This hash is that guard's ground truth.

#### 5. `payload-first-last-16` — [D]

**The first 16 and last 16 firmware payload bytes on the wire are fixed and known.**

- **Expect:** FIRST 16 (block index 0x34, frame bytes [16..31]): 29 C4 D0 EA 8A E8 D6 1C EF 8D AB 9E 78 03 F7 D9. LAST 16 (block index 0x74, frame bytes [1024..1039]): 68 5B FE 62 53 1D 05 8C 68 D4 9C 03 F6 1D D3 41. Byte [1040] of every 1041-byte A0 06 frame is 0x00, because the payload occupies [16..1039] only.
- **Cite:** FWFILE/140 in fw110 at offsets 0x193950 and 0x1A3D40..0x1A3D4F; payload placement from fw110 0x4019cf (leal -0x448(%ebp),%edi = base+0x10) and 0x4019ee (rep movsl, 0x100 dwords).
- **REFUTED IF:** The first A0 06 payload does not start 29 C4 D0 EA, or the last does not end F6 1D D3 41, or byte [1040] is non-zero.
- **Matters:** Cheapest possible eyeball check that the right resource reached the wire — usable directly in Wireshark's hex pane with no scripting.

#### 6. `a003-start-frame` — [D]

**The first command of the flash proper is a 1041-byte A0 03 SET_REPORT declaring the block count and the host-computed 32-bit additive checksum of the whole image.**

- **Expect:** 1041 bytes: [0]=0xA0 [1]=0x03 [2..15]=0x00 [16]=0x41 (65 blocks) [17]=0x7D [18]=0xD5 [19]=0x81 [20]=0x00 (LE32 0x0081D57D = 8,508,797 = the sum of all 66,560 image bytes mod 2^32) [21..1040]=0x00. SHA-256 of the whole 1041-byte frame = e4811bc6261dbf256a5707f6487043978fbd588fbffe15abd0da3d79be7e801e. Followed by Sleep(3000), then a 64-byte A1 GET.
- **Cite:** Frame built at fw110 0x4018e9 (movl $0x3a0), 0x4018c4 ([16] = count byte), and 0x4018ce/0x4018fd/0x401903/0x401909 ([17..20] = LE32 checksum, from ecx via shrl 8/16/24). Count and checksum supplied at the call site 0x4039a0 (movzbl 0x21c(%esi)) and 0x4039a7 (movl 0x25a20(%esi)). Checksum computed at 0x403580–0x4035ed: four 32-bit accumulators, byte-wise, combined at 0x4035d8–0x4035df.
- **REFUTED IF:** [16] is not 0x41, or [17..20] is not 7D D5 81 00, or the frame is not 1041 bytes, or a different command precedes the block writes.
- **Matters:** A0 03 is our declared point of no return (notes §10.2). A wrong count byte or a byte-swapped checksum is a single-byte error that reaches the device before anything is verifiable.

#### 7. `a006-frame-shape` — [D]

**Every firmware block is a 1041-byte A0 06 SET_REPORT with a 16-bit little-endian payload checksum at [4..5] and ten zero bytes at [6..15].**

- **Expect:** [0]=0xA0 [1]=0x06 [2]=index low byte [3]=index high byte (always 0x00 here) [4]=sum16 & 0xFF [5]=(sum16>>8)&0xFF [6..15]=0x00 [16..1039]=1024 payload bytes [1040]=0x00. sum16 is the plain 16-bit sum of exactly those 1024 payload bytes, and it excludes byte [1040]. Worked examples: i=0 frame begins A0 06 34 00 73 02 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 29 C4 D0 EA…; i=64 frame begins A0 06 74 00 A6 F8 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 B3 07 F9 CC…
- **Cite:** fw110 0x4019d5 (movw $0x6a0 into [0..1]), 0x4019be ([2]), 0x4019de ([3]), 0x4019e4 (movl $0 into [4..7]), 0x4019ee (payload), sum loop 0x401a00–0x401a34 with eax running 0x10..0x40C and addw accumulation, stores at 0x401a54 ([4]) and 0x401a5a ([5]), frame length pushl $0x411 at 0x401a4e.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any A0 06 frame has non-zero bytes in [6..15] or at [1040], or its [4..5] is not the 16-bit sum of its own payload, or its length is not 1041.
- **Matters:** This is the exact frame our flasher emits 65 times. A wrong offset for the checksum or the payload is invisible to us and destroys the image.

#### 8. `a006-index-sequence` — [D]

**Block indices run 0x34 to 0x74 inclusive, strictly ascending, each sent exactly once on a clean run; no index below 0x34 or above 0x74 ever appears.**

- **Expect:** 65 A0 06 frames with [2] = 0x34, 0x35, 0x36 … 0x74 in that order and [3]=0x00 throughout. Index computed as leal 0x34(%ebx),%ecx with ebx running 0..64. The paired A0 07 read-back uses the SAME index, truncated to a byte at 0x403b02 (movzbl %al).
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403a8b (write index), 0x403aff–0x403b02 (verify index), 0x403b58 (incl %ebx), 0x403b59 (loop bound against obj+0x21c).
- **REFUTED IF:** Any A0 06 or A0 07 carries an index outside [0x34,0x74], or the sequence is not monotonically ascending apart from retries that repeat an index already seen.
- **Matters:** notes §10.3 invariant 3. An index below 0x34 would write over the bootloader region, and that is the one error class with no recovery path.

#### 9. `a006-block-checksums` — [D]

**The 65 per-block 16-bit checksums are fixed known values, and their untruncated sum reconciles exactly with the A0 03 whole-image checksum.**

- **Expect:** sum16 for i=0..28, in order: 0273 F607 0125 0210 0599 EC2C F2DB 0852 0827 F744 FBDF 043F 02A0 0465 FC4B FD4A FBD8 F86B FB6E 0298 FEBC FDAB 0175 FD19 05BF EFE0 03CA F09C 0A38. For i=29..63 (35 blocks): all 0x0088. For i=64: 0xF8A6. The 65 untruncated byte-sums add to 0x0081D57D, identical to the A0 03 checksum — an internal consistency check needing no device.
- **Cite:** Computed over FWFILE/140 (fw110 offset 0x193950). Per-block algorithm from fw110 0x401a00–0x401a34; whole-image algorithm from 0x403580–0x4035ed.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any frame's [4..5] disagrees with this table, or the 65 values do not sum to 0x0081D57D.
- **Matters:** Our flasher recomputes this per block. A committed table of expected values makes a golden-file dry-run diffable against a real capture byte for byte.

#### 10. `filler-run-signature` — [D]

**Blocks at indices 0x51 through 0x73 — 35 consecutive frames — carry IDENTICAL 1024-byte payloads. The updater does not skip the filler.**

- **Expect:** 35 consecutive A0 06 frames, indices 0x51..0x73, each with [4..5] = 88 00 and payload starting 7E 84 7E 92 C9 78 92 D9 B9 2E 68 5C 90 0A 46 65 and ending 18 9A C3 2B B0 44 AB 9B FC B6 44 CF 50 8A 10 9B; SHA-256 of that chunk = cefe77fb6c23f0d4cb19fc709232bed0035ba41cc1413d46688172d3e6c6ffda. That chunk value is byte-identical in updaters 1.04, 1.06, 1.07 and 1.10.
- **Cite:** Per-chunk hashing of FWFILE/140 across all four updaters (independent extraction). Transmission is unconditional per the loop at fw110 0x403a48–0x403b5f, which contains no skip predicate.
- **REFUTED IF:** Fewer than 35 identical consecutive payloads appear, or the updater skips any block, or the repeated payload is not cefe77fb…
- **Matters:** notes §8.5a makes this chunk hash the discriminator that would catch a swapped resource (143's filler is e0cac456c107…). It is also the most obvious visual landmark in a 67 KB capture, so it locates the block phase instantly.

#### 11. `a007-readback-echo` — [D]

**Every block is read back immediately after it is written, and the device returns the SAME ciphertext bytes it was given — the vendor byte-compares all 1024 and aborts on any difference.**

- **Expect:** Per block: a 1041-byte A0 07 SET_REPORT ([0]=0xA0 [1]=0x07 [2]=index [3..1040]=0x00), then Sleep(50), then a 1041-byte A0 GET_REPORT whose [1]=0x01, whose [6..7] is the same 16-bit LE payload sum as that block's A0 06 [4..5], and whose [16..1039] is the identical 1024 payload bytes. Total device-to-host firmware bytes = 65 x 1024 = 66,560, hashing to 8148ebe9f8d2848abe483aee98df6e42bab341c6a17523bfef0f85f1f66754d0.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401b0b/0x401b14/0x401b1a/0x401b20 (request frame), pushl $0x411 at 0x401b05 (request length), Sleep(50) at 0x401b2e, 0x401b6d (response report id 0xA0), 0x401b96 (rep movsl 0x104 + movsb = 1041 bytes copied out). Comparison loop 0x401dc0–0x401dd9 over 1024 bytes; device sum read at 0x401d90/0x401d97 as resp[7]<<8 | resp[6] and compared at 0x401de2.
- **REFUTED IF:** An A0 07 response payload differs from what was written while the update still succeeds; or a read-back does not follow every write; or the response length is not 1041.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.4 stage 3 is flash read-back with zero writes. This confirms the device stores ciphertext verbatim and that a read-back is a usable pre-flash validation of our address arithmetic.

#### 12. `a108-whole-image-checksum` — [D]

**After the last block the tool asks the device for a whole-image checksum over blocks 0x34..0x74 and requires it to equal the host's 0x0081D57D.**

- **Expect:** 64-byte A1 SET_REPORT: A1 08 34 74 00 00 then zeros. Then Sleep(100), then a 64-byte A1 GET whose [1]=0x01 and whose [16..19] = 7D D5 81 00 (LE32 0x0081D57D). Preceded by Sleep(200).
- **Cite:** fw110 0x401be5 (movw $0x8a1), 0x401beb (movb $0x34 into [2]), 0x401bef ([3] from arg), call site 0x403b6c–0x403b72 (movb obj+0x21c then addb $0x33, giving 0x74), Sleep(100) at 0x401bfe, response assembly 0x401c55–0x401c7a, comparison at 0x403b95 against obj+0x25a20.
- **REFUTED IF:** [3] is not 0x74, or the device-returned LE32 at [16..19] is not 0x0081D57D on a successful update.
- **Matters:** It is the only device-side confirmation the protocol offers, and per CLAUDE.md §2 it proves only received==sent — so we mirror it (§4.2) without ever treating it as image validation.

#### 13. `alternate-updater-payloads` — [D]

**If a different updater version is run, the payload is that binary's own FWFILE/140 — all four share the shape and differ in content.**

- **Expect:** All four: 66,560 bytes, block_count 65, remainder 0, A0 03 [16]=0x41. Updater 1.07 (.exe sha256 d355346d…): payload sha256 3922b14157eff4268ca28c8fe7f284ba1ec0880dae2d9137d01eb1c42e297940, sum32 0x0081D408 so A0 03 [17..20] = 08 D4 81 00, first16 CA 77 4E 19 7B 45 71 09 B2 FC 21 4F D8 3B EA D7. 1.06 (221c1c49…): sha256 9f01d732913fcfc6799d699d19a92878c97a5f4dc76ed18b4a307125b35514dd, sum32 0x0081F40B so 0B F4 81 00, first16 49 31 DA 12 2B EF 4A A4 45 46 78 46 2E D7 92 52. 1.04 (0fd2016f…): sha256 41d5397ec84c4f167e6dce4d05f649f425e5c38ee066b8931721898d5dc013c3, sum32 0x0081F627 so 27 F6 81 00, first16 identical to 1.06's.
- **Cite:** `Tools/pe/fwfile.py` over `old-firmware-executables/*.exe` and the 1.10 updater; every value above is re-derived and pinned by `Tests/test_fwfile_set.py::PredictionThirteenAlternateUpdaterPayloads`, which runs in `ctest`. .exe SHA-256s re-verified against notes/binaries.md and against disk. *(Citation corrected 2026-09-06: it previously named `scratchpad/fw.py`, which is not in the repo, so the route could not be reproduced — §1.2. Every number re-derived unchanged with the committed parser.)*
- **REFUTED IF:** The A0 03 checksum bytes in the capture match none of the four values above, which would mean the running .exe is not one of the four we have analysed and our ingest gate must refuse it.
- **Matters:** Every other prediction here is keyed to which .exe was run. The A0 03 checksum alone identifies it from the capture, with no filesystem access needed.

#### 14. `config-tool-sends-no-firmware` — [D]

**The Configuration Tool cannot flash. If the config tool is captured instead of the updater, no firmware payload appears at all.**

- **Expect:** Config tools 1.00, 1.01, 1.04 and 1.07 each expose 74 resource leaves and ZERO of type FWFILE; no leaf of any type is 66,560 bytes; the UTF-16 string L"FWFILE" occurs 0 times in any of the four files. Their traffic is 64-byte A1 and 1041-byte A0 feature reports only, with no A0 03, A0 06, A0 07, A1 08, A1 09 or A1 3A.
- **Cite:** Resource directories of all four config .exe files walked with `Tools/pe/fwfile.py`; whole-file UTF-16 string scan. Pinned by `Tests/test_fwfile_set.py::PredictionFourteenConfigToolsCannotFlash` (74 leaves each, zero FWFILE, no 66,560-byte leaf, zero UTF-16 `FWFILE`), with the same check run against two updaters to show it can fail. Independently reproduces notes/updater-protocol.md §6.2.1. *(Citation corrected 2026-09-06 for the same reason as #13; all four counts re-derived unchanged.)*
- **REFUTED IF:** Any config-tool capture contains an A0 06 frame or any run of 1024 high-entropy payload bytes.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §3's two-executable split assumes the config tool has no flash path. If it did, the contradictory-quit-semantics argument would need revisiting.

#### 15. `wire-transfer-shape` — [G]

**Each HidD_SetFeature becomes one USB control-pipe SET_REPORT and each HidD_GetFeature one GET_REPORT, with the report ID as the first byte of the data stage.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT: bmRequestType 0x21, bRequest 0x09, wValue 0x03A0 for report 0xA0 or 0x03A1 for 0xA1, wIndex = the vendor interface number, wLength 0x0411 (1041) or 0x0040 (64). GET_REPORT: bmRequestType 0xA1, bRequest 0x01, same wValue, same wLength. Data-stage byte 0 is the report id, so the firmware payload sits at data-stage offsets 16..1039.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x4012a0–0x4012be: HidD_SetFeature(handle=*0x56a174, buffer=arg1, length=arg2) through IAT 0x51b1d4; fw110 0x401330 likewise for GetFeature through 0x51b1d0. The report ID occupying buffer[0] is [O] from the device's own descriptor (notes/device-predictions.md: macOS MaxFeatureReportSize 1041 for report 0xA0 and 64 for 0xA1, both including the id byte). The step from HidD_SetFeature to a control-pipe SET_REPORT is Windows HID stack behaviour, not read out of the .exe — which is why this row is tagged G rather than D.
- **REFUTED IF:** The capture shows feature reports on an interrupt endpoint, or wLength values other than 1041 and 64, or a data stage that omits the report ID byte — the last of which would shift every payload offset above by one, to 15..1038.
- **Matters:** Whether the report ID is on the wire decides the offset arithmetic of every prediction here. The descriptor already says reports are numbered, so it should be, but this is the one framing assumption not derived from the binary.

#### 16. `bootloader-declares-same-report-lengths` — [G]

**The bootloader-mode device (VID 0x3367 / PID 0x1977) declares the SAME feature reports as application mode: 0xA0 at 1041 bytes and 0xA1 at 64 bytes.**

- **Expect:** At the re-enumeration following A1 3A, the PID 0x1977 device's HID report descriptor declares Feature report 0xA0 with 1040 payload bytes and Feature report 0xA1 with 63 payload bytes on a vendor usage page. The updater passes hardcoded lengths and never queries a report length in the flash path: only the enumerator FUN_00401000 calls HidP_GetCaps, and it tests caps.Usage == 0x0002 and caps.UsagePage == 0xFF01 and nothing else.
- **Cite:** fw110 lengths are compile-time immediates: pushl $0x411 at 0x4018a5, 0x4018e0, 0x401996, 0x401a4e, 0x401ae6, 0x401b05, 0x401b4f, 0x401b67, 0x401d31; pushl $0x40 at 0x40193a, 0x401a89, 0x401bc8. Windows HidD_SetFeature fails with ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER (0x57 — one of the five codes retried at fw110 0x4012dd) when the length disagrees with the descriptor, so a working vendor tool implies agreement; that implication is the G part.
- **REFUTED IF:** The 0x1977 descriptor in the capture declares different feature report lengths or different report ids, or the vendor tool is seen retrying on GetLastError 0x57 during the flash.
- **Matters:** Our flasher opens the bootloader device and immediately sends a 1041-byte frame. If the bootloader's declared lengths differ, hidapi rejects every write and we would have no diagnostic for why.

#### 17. `flash-phase-timing` — [D]

**The block-write phase costs about 14.3 seconds of host sleeping, and the whole A0 03 to A1 13 span is roughly 19 to 25 seconds.**

- **Expect:** Per block the host sleeps 50 ms after A0 06 (0x401a68 pushl $0x32) + 50 ms after the A0 07 send (0x401b2e pushl $0x32) + 30 ms after the A0 07 response (0x401d78 pushl $0x1e) + 90 ms at the end of the loop (0x403b43 pushl $0x5a) = 220 ms; times 65 = 14,300 ms. Add Sleep(3000) inside A0 03 (0x401917 pushl $0xbb8), Sleep(500) before the loop (0x403a41 pushl $0x1f4), Sleep(200) before A1 08 (0x403b65 pushl $0xc8), Sleep(100) inside A1 08 (0x401bfe pushl $0x64), Sleep(50) in the A1 09 loop, and Sleep(900) before the discarded A1 13 read (0x403de9 pushl $0x384). The progress bar value is (100*i)/65 + 2, i.e. 2 at the first block and exactly 100 at the last (0x403b18–0x403b3d).
- **Cite:** All sleep values read as immediates at the cited fw110 addresses; loop structure 0x403a48–0x403b5f; progress arithmetic 0x403b18 (movl -0x98(%ebp)), 0x403b1f (idivl 0x21c(%esi)), 0x403b2d (addl $0x2), 0x403b51 (addl $0x64).
- **REFUTED IF:** Consecutive A0 06 frames are separated by markedly less than about 220 ms, or the block phase completes in well under 14 s, or the progress bar does not end at exactly 100.
- **Matters:** It separates protocol requirement from vendor padding, which is CLAUDE.md §5's 'real inter-command timing vs the vendor's padding' item, and it lets the user confirm they captured a whole flash rather than a truncated window.

#### 18. `a003-retry-budget` — [D]

**If the device does not answer A0 03 with status 0x01, the tool sends A0 03 exactly 5 times and then aborts without ever sending a block.**

- **Expect:** ebx starts at 0x64 (0x403996); each failure does Sleep(ebx) then ebx += 100, looping while ebx <= 0x1F4. Sleeps are 100, 200, 300, 400, 500 ms across 5 attempts, and each attempt also contains its own internal Sleep(3000), so the worst case is 5x3000 + 1500 = 16.5 s before the failure string is set at 0x4039df and the handle is closed at 0x4039f6.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403996 (movl $0x64,%ebx), 0x4039a0–0x4039c6 (retry loop with cmpl $0x1f4 / jle), 0x4039b6 (cmpb $0x1,%al).
- **REFUTED IF:** More or fewer than 5 A0 03 frames appear on a failing run, or any A0 06 is emitted after a non-0x01 A0 03 response.
- **Matters:** A0 03 is our point of no return (notes §10.2). We need to know exactly how many times the vendor pushes before concluding nothing happened, because our own rule is to keep driving rather than abort.

### Rejected by audit (4) — not pre-registered

| id | fault | auditor's evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `no-image-bytes-outside-setfeature` | `citation-wrong` | The §1.2a blind-spot paragraph states: "Neither updater imports the LoadLibrary+GetProcAddress pair against hid.dll (the config tools do; the updaters do not), which is the only construction that would exercise that blind spot." I enumerated both import directories from the loader-authoritative tables (my own walker, not .analysis/pe_res.py). fw110 (566 names, 18 DLLs, 0 delay-imports) imports BOTH KERNEL32!LoadLibraryW and KERNEL32!GetProcAddress. fw104 (595 names) imports LoadLibraryA, LoadLibraryW AND GetProcAddress. So the pair is present in both updaters and the stated exclusion is false as written; the sentence that is supposed to close the blind spot does not close it. The conclusion is still safe, but only via the DIFFERENT evidence already committed in notes/updater-protocol.md §9.4, which I reproduced independently: scanning every Hid[DP]_* name string in the file and looking for its ADDRESS as a 4-byte literal in .text gives fw110 7 strings / 0 referenced, fw104 7 / 0, and the single 'HID.DLL' string (fw110 VA 0x55ab8a, fw104 0x5ba3d2) has zero .text references; by contrast cfg107 has 18 strings, 11 referenced at 0x4027f0-0x4028e7, and its 'HID.DLL' at 0x5568dc referenced from 0x4027d1. This matters because §9.3 is a committed CORRECTION recording that an import-presence-style test is exactly what missed the config tools' dynamic resolution; the prediction reverts to the discredited test and states its result wrongly. |
| `repair-path-never-fires-cleanly` | `citation-wrong` | exact_expectation states "So the loop runs all 20 passes (sleep accumulator 0, 100 … 1900) and returns 2". The cited bytes do not do that in the branch the prediction's own byte[1] table describes. Disassembled fw110 0x401df7-0x401e41: 0x401e0d calls the A0 06 builder; 0x401e15 cmpb $1,%al; 0x401e17 jne 0x401e34 (EBX untouched); on al==1, 0x401e1f 'movzbl 0x1(%eax),%ebx' REASSIGNS EBX to the firmware source byte[1] every pass, 0x401e26/0x401e2b fall through to 0x401e34 when that byte is neither 0x01 nor 0x04, and the exit at 0x401e41 'movb %bl,%al' returns whatever EBX last held. So when the repair write is accepted (the case the byte[1] argument is about), the function returns 0xC4/0x84/0x07 etc. — I recomputed the full byte[1] table over FWFILE/140 and it is C4 28 DF 33 FD A2 E7 CE FC EE 35 05 84 C4 3A D7 75 8A CA 81 1D 51 57 92 CC AF D2 95 56, then 0x84 x35, then 0x07, none 0x01 or 0x04, matching the prediction. It returns 2 only in the OTHER branch, where all 20 repair writes returned != 0x01 and EBX kept the value set at 0x401ddd/0x401deb. The prediction's chain uses one branch to argue exhaustion and the other to state the return. The conclusion survives (0x403b14 cmpb $1,%al / jne 0x403ab3 aborts on 0x84 as well as on 0x02), and notes §5.6 — which I read in full — does not claim 'returns 2' either, so the number is the prediction's own addition. |
| `wire-is-ciphertext` | `mistagged` | Tagged [D], but the claim sentence is "What goes on the wire is CIPHERTEXT, not plaintext firmware. The host never decrypts; the device does." Only the middle clause is derivable. I disassembled fw110 0x403200-0x4033a0 in full and confirmed 0x403295 'movl $0x1,%ebx' and the 4x-unrolled movzbl/movb loop at 0x4032c0-0x403313 — a verbatim byte copy, so 'the host never decrypts' is [D] with an address. 'The bytes are ciphertext' and 'the device decrypts' have no address anywhere in any .exe: they rest on the entropy statistics (which I recomputed and which all hold: 7.9444 bits/byte, 256 distinct, min 108 / max 524, longest identical run 2, 255x 0xFF, 279x 0x00, dword0 0xEAD0C429, dword1 0x1CD6E88A) plus notes §8.0, where the mechanism is expressly '[G] as to mechanism' and the sentence 'The device decrypts. We never need to.' carries no tag at all. CLAUDE.md §1.2 defines [D] as 'traced to a specific location in an .exe. Cite the address'; a statement about what the device does can only be [O] or [G]. Operationally harmless (we ship the bytes verbatim on either reading), but §1.3 keys off the tag, and the same overclaim, untagged, is what §1.2 calls a bug. |
| `fwfile-census-1-10` | `not-falsifiable` | refuted_if reads: "The .exe actually run on Windows has a different SHA-256, a different FWFILE name set, or any FWFILE of a size other than 66560." The claim's subject is pinned by SHA-256 inside the citation itself ('sha256 97cd23d8…'), so all three conditions describe a DIFFERENT file rather than a false statement about this one; no outcome of the capture, and no outcome of hashing the .exe, can show 'file 97cd23d8… holds six 66,560-byte FWFILE leaves named 133/135/137/140/142/143 at lang 2052' to be false. It is a scoping precondition, not a test. The substance is exactly right — my independently written PE walker (DataDirectory[2] at RVA 0x16c000) returns 64 leaves total, the six FWFILE/lang-2052 leaves at file offsets 0x162d50, 0x173150, 0x183550, 0x193950, 0x1a3d50, 0x1b4150, all 66560 bytes, all six SHA-256s byte-for-byte as predicted, and no other leaf of any type is 66560 bytes — which is precisely why the refuted_if has nothing left to do. Note also that the older updaters carry only FIVE FWFILE names (133/135/137/140/142; 143 is new in 1.10), a checkable asymmetry the census does not state. |

### Cannot predict — what the capture will teach us (8)

- **Which physical flash address each block index 0x34..0x74 corresponds to on the device.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The index is a bare byte in the frame; the mapping to a flash address lives entirely in the device's bootloader ROM. notes §3.8 tags the 1024-byte block size [D] and the index-to-address mapping [G]. No .exe contains the mapping in any form.
  - how a capture answers it: It will NOT answer it, and saying so in advance matters so that a clean capture is not misread as having settled it. The only thing that could settle it is an A0 07 read of a block we deliberately did not write, compared against a flash layout we do not have.
- **Whether the device validates the image it is handed — signature, magic, version gate, or a decryption-failure check.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The host computes only a 32-bit additive sum over the bytes it is about to send (fw110 0x403580), so any device-side comparison can only prove received == sent. Nothing in the host distinguishes 'device accepted a valid image' from 'device accepted anything'.
  - how a capture answers it: A normal capture cannot answer it, because the image sent is correct by construction. The only experiment that would is deliberately sending a wrong image, which CLAUDE.md §2 forbids and which risks the only mouse. This stays [G] permanently, and it is exactly why every guard must be ours and must run before the first byte.
- **The full 64-byte content of the A1 3A (enter-bootloader) response, the A0 03 response, the A1 09 response and the A1 13 response.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The updater reads only byte [1] of A0 03 / A0 06 / A0 07 / A1 08 / A1 09; it reads NOTHING from the A1 3A response (0x4037e1 tests only the API return value); and it fetches the A1 13 response at 0x403e10 after Sleep(900) then discards it without examining a single byte. Static analysis can only see fields that code touches.
  - how a capture answers it: The capture contains all 64 bytes of every one of these responses. This is the highest-value thing the capture will teach us about the bootloader — it may carry a bootloader version, an identity or an error code, and CLAUDE.md §4.2's preflight requirement ('if the bootloader reports a version or an identity of any kind, refuse anything unrecognised') currently has nothing to check against.
- **The bootloader device's HID report descriptor, its bcdDevice, its string descriptors, and how many collections it presents.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: PID 0x1977 is a separate device mode whose descriptors live in device ROM. The updater never queries a report length; it passes hardcoded 0x411 and 0x40 everywhere.
  - how a capture answers it: USBPcap records the full enumeration of the 0x1977 device — GET_DESCRIPTOR device, configuration and HID report descriptor. This is the only way to obtain it short of entering the bootloader ourselves (CLAUDE.md §4.4 stage 2), and here it comes for free during the flash.
- **Whether the device ever returns status 0x04 (busy) during the block loop, and at which block indices.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The 0x04 handling exists in FUN_00401330's busy-poll (Sleep 0, 100 … 1900, 20 passes) but whether the device ever uses that state is device behaviour, not code.
  - how a capture answers it: Repeated GET_REPORTs of the same command returning [1]=0x04 with roughly 100 ms increments between them would show it directly. It matters because our own retry design has to know whether busy is a live state or dead code.
- **Whether the flash itself disturbs the settings region, independently of the trailing A1 13 factory reset.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The updater writes only blocks 0x34..0x74 and issues no settings command; where the device stores settings is [G] (working-memory, 'Flagged for decision' #2).
  - how a capture answers it: Only indirectly, and only if the config blob is captured with an A1 12 / A0 11 read BEFORE the flash and again after. That 'before' half is a one-shot opportunity that disappears the moment the flash runs.
- **Whether a 1041-byte control transfer is delivered in one data stage, its real timing, and whether the device NAKs during it.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: All of it is below the HidD_SetFeature API boundary; nothing in the .exe describes wire behaviour.
  - how a capture answers it: Directly, from the URB records and timestamps. It bounds how fast our own flasher can safely drive the device and whether hidapi's blocking behaviour will differ from the vendor's.
- **Whether any traffic appears on the device's other vendor collections — 0xFF02/0x01 Input 0x03, and the unidentified 0xFF02/0x02 Input 0x08 — while the flash is running.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: No Endgame binary opens 0xFF02/0x02 at all; the updaters contain zero 4-byte 0xff02 immediates. Static analysis of software that never listens cannot describe what the device sends.
  - how a capture answers it: USBPcap captures interrupt IN traffic whether or not any application reads it. This is the cheapest possible look at the unidentified 64-byte event channel recorded in notes/device-predictions.md, and it costs nothing extra on a capture we are taking anyway.

### Gaps the auditor flagged (6)

- The A1 09 'bootloader complete' frame is never predicted, although it is the command that fires between the last block and the re-enumeration and will be plainly visible in the capture. fw110 0x403be4 'movl $0x9a1,-0x50(%ebp)' and 0x403beb 'movl $0x0,-0x4c(%ebp)' over a 64-byte memset build A1 09 00 00 + 60 zeros; 0x403bfa Sleep(50); 0x403c1d/0x403c24 issue a 64-byte A1 GET; 0x403c30 'cmpb $0x1,-0x8f(%ebp)' requires resp[1]==0x01; the retry at 0x403c39-0x403c4c runs up to 11 attempts with Sleep(1), Sleep(2), … Sleep(10). Every byte of that is a compile-time immediate and none of it is registered.
- The A1 13 factory-reset frame appears only as prose in irreversible_warnings, with no id, no exact_expectation and no refuted_if — so the one command in the sequence that destroys user state is the one the document cannot be scored on. It is fully derivable: fw110 0x403dcf 'movl $0x13a1,-0x50(%ebp)' over a 64-byte memset gives A1 13 00 00 + 60 zeros, sent at 0x403ddd, then Sleep(900) at 0x403de9 and a 64-byte A1 GET at 0x403e10 whose bytes are never read. Worth registering with its own refuted_if because notes §5.4a records a reachable path (gate (c) fails at 0x403cc3, returns 1 at 0x403ead) in which the tool reports success and A1 13 is NEVER sent — so 'A1 13 present in the capture' is a real yes/no outcome, not a foregone one.
- The per-block wire sequence is only half specified. a006-frame-shape describes the 1041-byte A0 06 SET but not the 64-byte A1 GET_REPORT that always follows it (fw110 0x401a89 pushl $0x40, 0x401a9f writes the report id passed at 0x403a86 = 0xA1, status read at -0x43 = offset 1). A reader matching the capture will see four control transfers per block — A0 06 SET 1041, A1 GET 64, A0 07 SET 1041, A0 GET 1041 — i.e. 260 transfers across the block phase, and only three of the four are registered.
- Nothing in the document accounts for transport-level frame duplication, which can make a correct capture look like a refutation. The SetFeature wrapper FUN_004012a0 re-sends the SAME frame up to three more times (0x4012ea 'movl $0x1,%edi' … 0x40131b 'cmpl $0x4,%edi' / jl, with Sleep(10) at 0x401312) whenever HidD_SetFeature fails with GetLastError in {0x15,0x17,0x1d,0x57,0x65b}; the GetFeature wrapper FUN_00401330 re-issues the same GET up to 20 times on a 0x04 busy status (0x401385-0x4013a3, ESI 0,100,…,1900); and the block loop retries a failed write five times (0x403aae 'cmpl $0x5,%eax'). repair-path-never-fires-cleanly asserts flatly 'there will be exactly 65 A0 06 frames', which those three mechanisms can break with no repair-path involvement at all. payload-total-bytes guards itself with 'distinct-index'; this one does not, and should say what a duplicate frame looks like so it is not misread.
- only-140-reaches-wire closes EnumResourceNamesW/A but says nothing about FindResourceExW, which notes §8.2 singles out as the one alternative API that IS imported and IS referenced (fw110 0x4f37fe, fw104 0x4b4a01) and which it calls 'a real hole' had it been ignored. The once-in-the-whole-file count of the L"FWFILE" address does cover it, so the conclusion is safe, but a pre-registration that omits the API the committed notes flag as the near-miss is weaker than the notes it is drawn from.
- cannot_predict #6 identifies the pre-flash config blob as a one-shot observation, and capture_requirements asks for it, but no prediction states what that exchange should look like — so the 'before' half of the irreversible capture has nothing to be scored against. If the A1 12 request and the 1041-byte A0 response shape are derivable from cfg107 (notes/config-protocol.md), registering them costs nothing and makes the one-shot capture testable; if they are not, saying so explicitly is what stops a mismatch later being explained away.

### Irreversible / one-shot in this area

- A REAL FLASH IS IRREVERSIBLE AND THE 1.07 STATE IS OBSERVABLE EXACTLY ONCE. The device is currently at firmware 1.07 (working-memory, [O]). Once updater 1.10 runs, the mouse is at 1.10 and the 1.07-to-1.10 transition can never be captured again with this device. Everything about the pre-flash device — its config blob, its A1 12 response, its bcdDevice — is a one-shot observation.
- THE TRAILING A1 13 IS A FACTORY RESET AND WIPES SETTINGS. fw110 0x403dcf builds a 64-byte A1 13 00 00 frame byte-for-byte identical to the Configuration Tool's Factory Reset. On a normal successful update it IS sent, after three gates: checksum match at 0x403b95, A1 09 status at 0x403c30, and re-enumeration at 0x403c8d–0x403cb9. Capture the config blob before the flash or it is gone.
- ENTERING THE BOOTLOADER LEAVES THE MOUSE WITH NO APPLICATION FIRMWARE for the duration of the flash. That the device survives this and stays enumerable at PID 0x1977 is [G] (notes §10.2), argued from the vendor tool's own mode==2 path and never observed. There is one mouse and no spare.
- DO NOT SEND A MODIFIED OR SUBSTITUTED IMAGE to test whether the device validates it. CLAUDE.md §2 assumes it does not; the host's additive checksum proves only received == sent, so a wrong-but-well-formed image would be accepted silently and there is no recovery path we have any evidence for.
- DO NOT RUN A DOWNGRADE (updater 1.07 or 1.04 onto a device already at 1.10) to test the alternate-payload predictions. The tool does not gate on version and would attempt it, but nothing in the binaries says the device tolerates a downgrade, and that prediction is not worth the only mouse.
- DO NOT RUN THE CONFIGURATION TOOL AND THE UPDATER AT THE SAME TIME. cfg107 wraps every send and read in a critical section on 0x00578c08, so the vendor treats a command and its response as atomic within a process — but two separate executables cannot share that lock. Whether the device tolerates interleaving is untested (working-memory, 'Flagged for decision' #3).
- THE BOOTLOADER'S REPORT DESCRIPTOR IS VISIBLE ONLY DURING THE FLASH WINDOW. If the root-hub filter is wrong or the capture starts late, that descriptor is lost for this run, and re-obtaining it costs another bootloader entry.


---

## Config 1.07 — startup, device open, reading the settings record

*area key: `config-read` — 43 written, 14 rejected*


### Pre-registered (30)

#### 1. `startup-exactly-four-feature-transfers` — [D]

**Plug in mouse, launch config tool 1.07, let the UI populate, change nothing, close it: the vendor HID collection sees EXACTLY FOUR feature-report control transfers, in one fixed order, and nothing else for the whole session.**

- **Expect:** In order: (1) SET_REPORT Feature id 0xA1, 64 bytes; (2) GET_REPORT Feature id 0xA1, 64 bytes; (3) SET_REPORT Feature id 0xA1, 64 bytes; (4) GET_REPORT Feature id 0xA0, 1041 bytes. Total feature transfers for the session = 4. No fifth transfer at any point, including at window close.
- **Cite:** cfg107 OnInitDialog 0x00412fb0: call 0x4035f0 at 0x4130b8 (open), call 0x4045e0 at 0x4130c5 (A1 02), call 0x403b20 at 0x413152 (A1 12), call 0x412d00 at 0x413191 (event channel). Raw E8 scan of all .text finds only six call sites into the four command functions: 0x4130c5 and 0x413885 -> 0x4045e0; 0x413152 and 0x413914 -> 0x403b20; 0x413f2f and 0x414356 -> 0x404180; 0x413faf -> 0x404720.
- **REFUTED IF:** The count is not 4, or the order differs, or any additional feature transfer appears after the UI has populated, or anything is sent when the window is closed.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.4 stage 1 is 'one read-only command round trip'. This fixes exactly what our own EGGConfigCore startup must emit and is the tightest whole-session invariant the capture can check.

#### 2. `a1-02-request-frame` — [D]

**The first SET_REPORT carries the A1 02 query, 64 bytes, with only two non-zero bytes.**

- **Expect:** data[0]=0xA1, data[1]=0x02, data[2..63]=0x00. Length exactly 64 (0x40). Setup: bmRequestType=0x21, bRequest=0x09 (SET_REPORT), wValue=0x03A1 (type Feature=3, id 0xA1), wLength=64.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x4045e0: memset(-0x94(%ebp),0,0x40) at 0x404654, then movl $0x2a1,-0x94(%ebp) at 0x40466c (little-endian a1 02 00 00), esi=0x40 at 0x404660, ebx=&buf at 0x404666, call send 0x403850 at 0x404676; 0x403885 calll *0x52d1dc = HidD_SetFeature(handle, ebx, esi).
- **REFUTED IF:** Any byte other than [0] and [1] is non-zero, or [1] != 0x02, or the length is not 64, or wValue != 0x03A1.
- **Matters:** This is the first byte sequence our own tool will put on the wire in stage 1. Every byte of it is currently an immediate read out of a Windows .exe with no independent check.

#### 3. `a1-12-request-frame` — [D]

**The second SET_REPORT carries the A1 12 settings-read request, 64 bytes, with only two non-zero bytes.**

- **Expect:** data[0]=0xA1, data[1]=0x12, data[2..63]=0x00. Length exactly 64. bmRequestType=0x21, bRequest=0x09, wValue=0x03A1, wLength=64. Byte-for-byte identical to the A1 02 frame except data[1].
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x403b20: memset(-0x50(%ebp),0,0x40) at 0x403b79, movl $0x12a1,-0x50(%ebp) at 0x403b8a, esi=0x40 at 0x403b81, ebx=&buf at 0x403b87, call 0x403850 at 0x403b91.
- **REFUTED IF:** data[1] != 0x12, or any of data[2..63] is non-zero, or the length is not 64.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.1 requires read-modify-write, which starts with this exact read request. If the device needs anything more than two bytes here, our read fails and §4.1 forbids proceeding to a write.

#### 4. `a1-12-response-is-1041-on-report-a0` — [D]

**The A1 12 answer comes back on a DIFFERENT report id from the request: report 0xA0, 1041 bytes, not report 0xA1.**

- **Expect:** GET_REPORT with bmRequestType=0xA1, bRequest=0x01, wValue=0x03A0 (Feature, id 0xA0), wLength=1041 (0x411). Data stage first byte = 0xA0. Total 1041 bytes.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x403b20: pushl $0x411 at 0x403bb3 (memset size), movl $0x411,%esi at 0x403bc9 (length), movb $0xa0,-0x468(%ebp) at 0x403bd4 (objdump prints $-0x60), edi=&buf at 0x403bce, call recv 0x403920 at 0x403bdb; 0x403955 calll *0x52d1d8 = HidD_GetFeature(handle, edi, esi). Device-side corroboration: macOS MaxFeatureReportSize = 1041 [O], notes/device-predictions.md row 6.
- **REFUTED IF:** wValue low byte != 0xA0, or wLength != 1041, or the response arrives on report 0xA1.
- **Matters:** Request and response use different report ids. A transport that assumes a request/response pair shares a report id — the obvious design — is wrong for this device, and this is where it would break.

#### 5. `a1-02-response-is-64-on-report-a1` — [D]

**The A1 02 answer comes back on report 0xA1, 64 bytes — the report id tracks transfer size, not direction.**

- **Expect:** GET_REPORT with bmRequestType=0xA1, bRequest=0x01, wValue=0x03A1, wLength=64. Data stage first byte = 0xA1.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x4045e0: movl $0x40,%esi at 0x4046a8, movb $0xa1,-0x54(%ebp) at 0x4046b0 (objdump prints $-0x5f), edi=-0x54(%ebp) at 0x4046ad, call 0x403920 at 0x4046b4.
- **REFUTED IF:** The A1 02 response is 1041 bytes, or comes back on report 0xA0, or has a length other than 64.
- **Matters:** Together with the previous prediction it establishes the rule 'response report id is chosen by payload size', which our transport has to encode as a per-command property rather than a constant.

#### 6. `wlength-includes-report-id-byte` — [G]

**Windows' hidusb puts the report-ID byte INSIDE the control-transfer data stage, so the wire lengths are 64 and 1041 (not 63 and 1040) and the first data byte repeats the report id already in wValue.**

- **Expect:** wLength = 0x0040 for the three 0xA1 transfers and 0x0411 for the 0xA0 GET. data[0] = 0xA1 / 0xA0 respectively. The 1041-byte IN data stage splits into 17 packets on the bus (16x64 + 1x17) if EP0 wMaxPacketSize is 64.
- **Cite:** Windows HID stack convention, not the binary. Cross-check against the device's own descriptor, which declares Feature 0xA0 as 8320 bits = 1040 payload bytes plus one Report ID byte (notes/device-predictions.md rows 6-7 and the verbatim descriptor).
- **REFUTED IF:** wLength is 63/1040, or the first data byte is not the report id.
- **Matters:** macOS hidapi also prepends the report id to the buffer. If Windows strips it and macOS does not, or vice versa, every buffer we build is off by one byte at the very first write — the cheapest possible way to brick a device.

#### 7. `status-byte-01-in-both-responses` — [D]

**Both GET responses carry status 0x01 in byte 1, and the tool requires it before using the data.**

- **Expect:** A1 02 response byte[1] == 0x01; A1 12 response (report 0xA0) byte[1] == 0x01. Not 0x00, not 0x02, not 0x03.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x403920 recv wrapper: movzbl 0x1(%edi),%eax at 0x40396a, cmpl $0x3 (busy) at 0x40396e, cmpl $0x1 at 0x4039ef. Callers re-check: 0x403be4 cmpb $0x1,-0x467(%ebp) (report 0xA0 byte 1); 0x4046be cmpb %al,-0x53(%ebp) with al=1 (report 0xA1 byte 1).
- **REFUTED IF:** Either response has byte[1] != 0x01 while the tool still shows a populated UI, or byte[1]==0x03 appears at all.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.1 says never let a reported success stand in for verifying the data. resp[1]==1 is the vendor's own structural-plausibility gate and we must mirror it; if the byte means something else, our gate is wrong.

#### 8. `a1-02-carries-firmware-version-at-0x11-0x12` — [D]

**The A1 02 64-byte response carries the device firmware version as two bytes at offsets 0x11 (minor) and 0x12 (major), and this device is at 1.07.**

- **Expect:** response[0x11] = 0x07, response[0x12] = 0x01. The tool computes (resp[0x12]<<8)|resp[0x11] = 0x0107, prints it as a HEX string ('107'), re-parses that string as DECIMAL (107), divides by 100.0 and formats with 'V%.2f' — so the main window reads exactly 'V1.07'.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x4045e0 copies resp[0x10..0x1D] into the caller struct (movl -0x44/-0x40/-0x3c, movw -0x38, base -0x54) at 0x4046c9-0x4046e3. OnInitDialog 0x4130dc-0x413144: movzbl 0x26(%esp) and movzbl 0x31(%esp) resolve to struct+2 and struct+1, shlw $8, or; _itow_s(value, buf, 10, radix=0x10) at 0x5087f1; parse back at 0x508831; fildl / fdivl 0x558c18 (=100.0); format string 0x5589dc = 'V%.2f'. Device is at bcdDevice 0x0107 [O].
- **REFUTED IF:** response[0x11] != 0x07 or response[0x12] != 0x01, or the UI shows a version other than V1.07 while bcdDevice is still 0x0107.
- **Matters:** This is the only version the config tool reads from the device and the cheapest structurally-plausible round trip for CLAUDE.md §4.2 preflight. It also tests the BCD-not-division reading that device-predictions.md row 9 settled only from the descriptor side.

#### 9. `record-only-111-bytes-are-understood` — [D]

**The host understands only 111 of the 1024 record bytes: offset 0x00 and offsets 0x05 through 0x72. Bytes 0x01-0x04 and 0x73-0x3FF are neither read nor written by cfg107.**

- **Expect:** Unpacker 0x00403c40 performs exactly 111 loads from 0x0057f340+k for k in {0x00} union [0x05,0x72]; packer 0x004042d0 performs exactly 111 stores to those same 111 offsets. Holes: k = 0x01, 0x02, 0x03, 0x04 and every k >= 0x73 (909 bytes).
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00403c40-0x00404173 (unpack: 63 byte stores plus 24 sixteen-bit fields = 111 source bytes) and 0x004042d0-0x004045dc (pack: 111 stores, max record offset 0x72, holes exactly 0x01-0x04). Both extracted mechanically from raw objdump, not from decompiled C.
- **REFUTED IF:** A control in the vendor UI is shown to change a record byte outside {0x00} union [0x05,0x72].
- **Matters:** It says exactly how much of the record we must understand to do a safe read-modify-write, and exactly how much we must preserve untouched under CLAUDE.md §1.3.

#### 10. `sleep-50ms-between-a1-02-set-and-get` — [D]

**There is a fixed 50 ms host-side sleep between the A1 02 SET_REPORT and its GET_REPORT.**

- **Expect:** Timestamp(transfer 2) minus Timestamp(transfer 1) >= 50 ms and below about 60 ms on an unloaded machine. The sleep is unconditional and happens even when the send failed.
