- **Cite:** cfg107 0x40467b pushl $0x32; 0x40467f calll *0x52d22c (Sleep). Issued immediately after the send returns, before the failure test at 0x404685.
- **REFUTED IF:** The gap is under 40 ms or over 100 ms on an otherwise idle machine.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §5 lists real inter-command timing as device-gated. These two constants are the only pacing data we have, and mirroring them is the conservative default until the device says otherwise.

#### 11. `sleep-80ms-between-a1-12-set-and-get` — [D]

**There is a fixed 80 ms host-side sleep between the A1 12 SET_REPORT and the 1041-byte GET_REPORT — a different constant from the A1 02 pair.**

- **Expect:** Timestamp(transfer 4) minus Timestamp(transfer 3) >= 80 ms and below about 95 ms. The two inter-pair gaps differ: 50 ms for A1 02, 80 ms for A1 12.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x403b96 pushl $0x50; 0x403b9a calll *0x52d22c.
- **REFUTED IF:** The two inter-pair gaps are equal, or the A1 12 gap is not about 80 ms.
- **Matters:** Two different sleeps for two commands means the delay is per-command, not a transport constant. Encoding it as a single constant in EGGCore would be wrong, and this is the observation that decides it.

#### 12. `gap-between-the-two-pairs-is-small` — [D]

**The A1 02 GET and the A1 12 SET are separated only by UI work, with no sleep between them.**

- **Expect:** Timestamp(transfer 3) minus Timestamp(transfer 2) below 50 ms, typically a few milliseconds. All four transfers complete within roughly 200 ms of each other.
- **Cite:** cfg107 OnInitDialog 0x4130ca-0x413152: between the A1 02 return and the call to 0x403b20 there is only 0x4143c0 (EnableWindow calls) and the version-string formatting. No Sleep call in that stretch.
- **REFUTED IF:** There is a delay of 100 ms or more between transfers 2 and 3.
- **Matters:** It shows the device tolerates back-to-back commands with no inter-command gap, which bounds how much of the 50/80 ms padding is really about the device rather than about the previous command.

#### 13. `no-busy-retry-expected` — [D]

**None of the four transfers is retried; the busy convention (status 0x03) does not fire.**

- **Expect:** No duplicate GET_REPORT with identical setup about 100 ms after a first one. Were the device to answer 0x03, the tool would sleep 100 ms, re-issue the identical GET, and on repeated busy add an extra Sleep(accumulator) with the accumulator stepping 0, 100, 200 ... 900 — at most 10 further GETs and 5.5 s of total sleep.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x403920: cmpl $0x3 at 0x40396e, Sleep(0x64) at 0x403980, re-GET at 0x4039a2, extra Sleep(acc) at 0x4039c8, acc += 0x64 at 0x4039d1, cmpl $0x3e8 at 0x4039d7 (10 iterations).
- **REFUTED IF:** Any GET_REPORT is repeated with the same wValue about 100 ms later.
- **Matters:** If the busy path never fires in practice it is dead code in our port too; if it does fire, notes/config-protocol.md §1's warning that the busy byte differs between the two tools (config 0x03/1000 ms, updater 0x04/2000 ms) becomes load-bearing rather than academic.

#### 14. `no-send-retry-expected` — [D]

**No SET_REPORT is retried; the four-attempt send retry loop does not fire.**

- **Expect:** Each SET_REPORT appears exactly once. The retry path fires only when HidD_SetFeature fails AND GetLastError is in {0x15 ERROR_NOT_READY, 0x17 ERROR_CRC, 0x1D ERROR_WRITE_FAULT, 0x57 ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER, 0x65B ERROR_SERVICE_REQUEST_TIMEOUT}; it then retries at most 3 more times with Sleep(50) between, so at most four SET_REPORTs of the same frame.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x403895 GetLastError; the five comparisons at 0x40389b, 0x4038a0, 0x4038a5, 0x4038aa, 0x4038af; counter set to 1 at 0x4038b6; Sleep(0x32) at 0x4038ed; incl and cmpl $0x4 at 0x4038f8-0x4038fc.
- **REFUTED IF:** A SET_REPORT with identical data appears two to four times about 50 ms apart.
- **Matters:** Those five Windows error codes have no macOS equivalent. Knowing whether the retry ever fires tells us whether we need an equivalent retry policy on IOKit errors or can drop the whole path.

#### 15. `nothing-sent-on-close` — [D]

**Closing the config tool sends nothing to the device and does not even close its handles cleanly — the process just exits.**

- **Expect:** Zero USB transfers attributable to the tool between the last populate transfer and process exit. No A1 13, no A0 11, no final read. The command-channel handle global 0x0057f338 is never closed after a successful match, the event-channel hid_device* at 0x00580188 is never freed, and the 'channel running' flag 0x0057f196 is written twice and read nowhere.
- **Cite:** cfg107 exhaustive 4-byte .text scan: 0x0057f338 referenced only at 0x40360c, 0x403710, 0x40372e, 0x403755, 0x4037d6, 0x4037e3, 0x4037eb, 0x403875, 0x4038cc, 0x403945, 0x403992, 0x403a35, 0x403a47, 0x404276 — the CloseHandle at 0x4037db is inside the enumerate loop for NON-matching devices. 0x00580188 referenced only at 0x412c91 and 0x412d2c. 0x0057f196 referenced only at 0x412d50 and 0x412d67.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any control transfer or a close-triggered IOCTL burst appears at window close.
- **Matters:** It tells us the device needs no shutdown handshake, so our tool can exit at any moment during a read without leaving the device in a bad state — which is the premise behind §4.2's 'before erase, abort is always correct'.

#### 16. `enumeration-produces-no-usb-traffic` — [D]

**The device-selection walk generates no USB bus traffic at all: it is SetupAPI plus HID-class calls that hidclass.sys answers from cached descriptor data.**

- **Expect:** Between process start and the first SET_REPORT there is no control transfer to the OP1 other than possibly string-descriptor reads. HidD_GetAttributes, HidD_GetPreparsedData and HidP_GetCaps produce zero URBs.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x004035f0, with every slot resolved from the PE import table rather than from Ghidra names: HidD_GetHidGuid (*0x52d1e4) at 0x40363f into buffer 0x57f1bc; SetupDiGetClassDevsW (*0x52d4cc) with flags 0x12 = DIGCF_PRESENT|DIGCF_DEVICEINTERFACE at 0x40364e; SetupDiEnumDeviceInterfaces (*0x52d4c8) at 0x40367e and 0x403817; SetupDiGetDeviceInterfaceDetailW (*0x52d4d0) at 0x4036a8 and 0x4036ef; CreateFileW (*0x52d270) at 0x403709; HidD_GetAttributes (*0x52d1e0) at 0x403722; HidD_GetPreparsedData (*0x52d1d4) at 0x40375e; HidP_GetCaps (*0x52d1d0) at 0x403771; HidD_FreePreparsedData (*0x52d1cc) at 0x4037b6; SetupDiDestroyDeviceInfoList (*0x52d4d4) at 0x40382e.
- **REFUTED IF:** Control transfers to the OP1 appear before the first SET_REPORT that are not GET_DESCRIPTOR(STRING).
- **Matters:** If enumeration is silent on the bus, our macOS enumerator can be as chatty as it likes without touching the device — which is what makes stage 1 genuinely read-only.

#### 17. `command-channel-uses-setupapi-not-hidapi` — [D]

**The CORRECTION in the notes cuts both ways: the config tool has TWO enumerators, and the one that finds the command channel is the SetupAPI matcher, not hidapi. Dynamic GetProcAddress resolution belongs to the hidapi half only.**

- **Expect:** A Process Monitor trace shows two distinct enumeration bursts. First, before the four feature transfers: SetupDiGetClassDevsW / EnumDeviceInterfaces / GetDeviceInterfaceDetailW (WIDE variants) plus CreateFileW with dwDesiredAccess=0xC0000000, dwShareMode=3, OPEN_EXISTING, dwFlagsAndAttributes=0 on each present HID interface path. Second, after them: SetupDiGetClassDevsA / GetDeviceInterfaceDetailA / EnumDeviceInfo / GetDeviceRegistryPropertyA (ANSI variants) plus CreateFileA with dwDesiredAccess=0 and FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED (0x40000000). None of the eleven dynamically resolved hid.dll entry points serves the command path — that path uses the STATIC IAT slots 0x52d1dc and 0x52d1d8.
- **Cite:** cfg107 matcher 0x4035f0 uses W-variant slots 0x52d4cc/0x52d4c8/0x52d4d0/0x52d4d4 and CreateFileW 0x52d270 (push order 0x403700-0x403709: 0, 0, 3, 0, 3, 0xC0000000, path). hid_enumerate 0x402980 uses A-variant slots 0x52d4b8/0x52d4c4/0x52d4bc/0x52d4c0 and CreateFileA 0x52d230 (push order 0x402b89-0x402b9c: 0, 0x40000000, 3, 0, 3, 0, path) and calls dynamic slots *0x57f16c, *0x57f160, *0x57f184, *0x57f17c, *0x57f178, *0x57f170, *0x57f188. Send wrapper 0x403850 calls *0x52d1dc at 0x403885 and 0x4038dc; recv 0x403920 calls *0x52d1d8 at 0x403955, 0x4039a2, 0x403a40, 0x403a54.
- **REFUTED IF:** A ProcMon or API trace shows CreateFileA-with-zero-access before the first SET_REPORT, or shows the four feature transfers issued through a handle opened by the ANSI path.
- **Matters:** notes/config-protocol.md §2 was corrected twice for scanning the wrong search space. This says which of the two enumerators our own EGGCore enumerator should model — and the answer is the one that matches on the usage pair, not the hidapi one.

#### 18. `matcher-opens-every-hid-interface-rw` — [D]

**The matcher brute-forces every present HID interface with GENERIC_READ|GENERIC_WRITE, so it is denied on the system keyboard and mouse collections — including three of the OP1's own — before it reaches the vendor collection.**

- **Expect:** ProcMon shows CreateFile on many \\?\\hid#... paths with Desired Access: Generic Read/Write. ACCESS DENIED on the OP1's keyboard collection (usage 01/06), its consumer collection (0C/01) and its mouse interface (01/02), and on every other attached keyboard or mouse. SUCCESS on the OP1's vendor collections. The walk stops at the first FF01/02 match and leaves that handle open for the process lifetime.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x403709 CreateFileW(path, 0xC0000000, 3, 0, 3, 0, 0); 0x403714 cmpl $-0x1,%eax skips on failure; on a full match 0x4037aa movb %al,-0x2d(%ebp) with al=1 and 0x4037d0 cmpb %bl,-0x2d(%ebp) / jne 0x403825 exits the loop WITHOUT reaching the CloseHandle at 0x4037db.
- **REFUTED IF:** ProcMon shows only one CreateFile, or shows the vendor collections opened with access 0.
- **Matters:** macOS has its own version of this question — whether opening the vendor collection needs Input Monitoring (CLAUDE.md §5, still untested). Knowing that Windows denies the keyboard collections but allows the vendor ones tells us which macOS failures to expect and which would be genuinely new.

#### 19. `string-descriptor-burst-after-the-four` — [G]

**After the four feature transfers, hidapi's own enumeration runs and asks the OP1 for its USB string descriptors — the only other USB traffic the launch produces.**

- **Expect:** A burst of GET_DESCRIPTOR(STRING) control transfers (bmRequestType=0x80, bRequest=0x06, wValue high byte 0x03) to the OP1, occurring AFTER transfer 4 and before the tool goes idle. Three per matching HID collection interface — serial number, then manufacturer, then product — preceded by a wValue=0x0300 LANGID read. If Windows exposes one device interface per top-level collection, that is six matching interfaces (five on interface A, one on interface B) and up to eighteen string reads.
- **Cite:** cfg107 hid_enumerate 0x402980 filters VID at 0x402bce and PID at 0x402be2 (hid_open passes 0x3367 and 0x1978 at 0x412d14 and 0x412d0f), then calls *0x57f178 HidD_GetSerialNumberString at 0x402cbf, *0x57f170 HidD_GetManufacturerString at 0x402cf4, *0x57f188 HidD_GetProductString at 0x402d29, each with a 0x400-byte buffer. hid_open is called only from 0x412d00, which OnInitDialog calls at 0x413191 — after the A1 12 read at 0x413152.
- **REFUTED IF:** No GET_DESCRIPTOR(STRING) transfers appear at all, or they appear BEFORE the four feature transfers.
- **Matters:** The ordering half is [D] and is what matters: the command channel is opened and fully read before the event channel exists. That is the order EGGCore should use, and it means a config read never has to contend with an open event channel.

#### 20. `event-channel-opens-ff02-01` — [D]

**The event channel is opened last, on a DIFFERENT collection from the command channel — usage page 0xFF02, usage 0x01 — and it never uses feature reports.**

- **Expect:** ProcMon shows a CreateFileA on the OP1 collection whose usage pair is FF02/01, Desired Access Generic Read/Write, FILE_FLAG_OVERLAPPED, followed by HidD_SetNumInputBuffers with 64 and then a permanently pending ReadFile of 8 bytes. No SET_REPORT or GET_REPORT is ever issued on that handle.
- **Cite:** cfg107 hid_open 0x402e70: movl $0xff02,%ecx at 0x402eb5, cmpw %cx,0x18(%esi) at 0x402eba, cmpw $0x1,0x1a(%esi) at 0x402ec0. hid_open_path 0x402f60: CreateFileA (*0x52d230) at 0x403025 with 0xC0000000 / 3 / OPEN_EXISTING / 0x40000000; pushl $0x40 then calll *0x57f168 (HidD_SetNumInputBuffers) at 0x403039-0x40303c. Poll thread 0x412c60: pushl $0x8 at 0x412ca1, calll hid_read_timeout 0x4031c0 at 0x412ca8.
- **REFUTED IF:** The tool opens FF02/02 (the third collection), or issues any feature report on the FF02/01 handle.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §3 says the enumerator must not stop at the first vendor collection. This capture shows the vendor running two channels on two collections simultaneously, which is the shape our own enumerator has to support.

#### 21. `poll-thread-read-is-blocking-not-polling` — [D]

**The event channel is NOT an 80 ms poll — it is a BLOCKING read, so an idle mouse produces no host-driven traffic; the 80 ms sleep only throttles the loop AFTER a report has been delivered.**

- **Expect:** hid_read_timeout is called with milliseconds = -1 (infinite), because hidapi's dev->blocking at offset +4 is initialised to 1 and the code computes 0 minus (blocking != 0) via negl/sbbl. The ReadFile stays pending indefinitely and Sleep(80) runs only after it returns.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x412c99 movl 0x4(%eax),%ecx; 0x412c9c negl %ecx; 0x412c9e sbbl %ecx,%ecx (yielding 0xFFFFFFFF when blocking != 0); 0x412ca0 pushes it as the timeout. hid_open_path 0x402fde movl $0x1,0x4(%esi). Sleep(0x50) at 0x412ce4-0x412ce6 is after the read returns.
- **REFUTED IF:** The capture shows a regular ~80 ms cadence of interrupt-IN activity attributable to the tool on an otherwise idle mouse.
- **Matters:** notes/config-protocol.md §10.3 describes this as '80 ms between polls'. If it is really a blocking read, that section needs correcting and our event channel should be a blocking reader, not a poller that wakes the device 12 times a second.

#### 22. `record-0x05-is-polling-rate-one-hot` — [D]

**Settings-record byte 0x05 is the polling rate as a ONE-HOT byte, bit 0 = 8000 Hz through bit 6 = 125 Hz.**

- **Expect:** record[0x05] has exactly one bit set, from {0x01 = 8000, 0x02 = 4000, 0x04 = 2000, 0x08 = 1000, 0x10 = 500, 0x20 = 250, 0x40 = 125}. Whatever the value, the 'Polling Rate' combo (control 1085 on page 135) shows the matching label out of '125Hz','250Hz','500Hz','1000Hz','2000Hz','4000Hz','8000Hz'.
- **Cite:** Packer cfg107 0x4042d5-0x4042d9: record[0x05] <- struct[0x06]. Restore 0x40f152: movzbl 0x57f216 (struct base 0x57f210 plus 0x06), decl, cmpl $0x3f, index table 0x40f70c, jump table 0x40f6ec, CB_SETCURSEL (0x14E) index 0..6 on the window at page135+0x12f0; DDX_Control binds control 1085 to member +0x12d0 at 0x40bb65 and m_hWnd is +0x20. Event-channel corroboration: notes/config-protocol.md §10.4.
- **REFUTED IF:** record[0x05] has zero or more than one bit set, or the UI's polling-rate selection does not match the table.
- **Matters:** It is the first settings field to be located in the wire record from both directions — the record map and the event channel — and it is the anchor that lets the rest of the record be read off against the UI.

#### 23. `record-0x72-and-0x6f-0x71-are-appended-out-of-order` — [D]

**The wire record is not laid out in struct order: four fields are appended at the end, out of sequence, after the assignment table.**

- **Expect:** record[0x6F] <- struct[0x2B], record[0x70] <- struct[0x2C], record[0x71] <- struct[0x2D], record[0x72] <- struct[0x08]. record[0x00] <- struct[0x00]. record[0x0F..0x22] is a twenty-byte contiguous block from struct[0x6E..0x81]. So a change to a single UI control can move a byte near the very end of the meaningful span.
- **Cite:** Packer cfg107 0x4045c1-0x4045d9 (the last four stores, ending movb 0x8(%ecx),%cl then movb %cl,0x72(%eax)) and 0x40431b onward for the 0x0F..0x22 block; mirrored in the unpacker at 0x403cad-0x403d75 and 0x40414b-0x404170.
- **REFUTED IF:** Changing one setting is shown to move a record byte the map does not predict.
- **Matters:** It kills the tempting assumption that the record is the host struct memcpy'd. Any diff-verify in EGGConfigCore has to compare in record space using this map, not in struct space.

#### 24. `onitdialog-single-shot-devicechange-retries` — [D]

**Startup does NOT retry; a device-arrival event DOES. The two paths are otherwise the same sequence.**

- **Expect:** At launch, each of A1 02 and A1 12 is attempted exactly once and failure just leaves the UI at host defaults. If the mouse is unplugged and replugged while the tool is open, the WM_DEVICECHANGE handler repeats open, A1 02, A1 12, event-channel start, retrying EACH of A1 02 and A1 12 up to five times with Sleep(100) between attempts.
- **Cite:** cfg107 OnInitDialog 0x4130c5 and 0x413157: cmpb $0x1,%al then jne, with no loop. WM_DEVICECHANGE 0x413880-0x413899: call 0x4045e0, cmpb $0x1, on failure pushl $0x64 then Sleep, incl %esi, cmpl $0x5,%esi, jl 0x413880; the same shape at 0x413914-0x413925 for 0x403b20.
- **REFUTED IF:** A replug produces a different transfer count than a fresh launch in a way five-attempt retries do not explain.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.2 says re-enumeration must be handled explicitly. This is the vendor's own re-enumeration policy on the config side — five attempts, 100 ms apart — and it is the closest reference we have for what the device needs after it reappears on the bus.

#### 25. `replug-leaks-a-second-poll-thread` — [D]

**Each device-arrival event starts an ADDITIONAL event-channel thread and leaks the previous hid_device and the previous command-channel handle — there is no guard.**

- **Expect:** After N replugs with the tool open there are N+1 threads blocked in ReadFile on the FF02/01 collection and N+1 open handles to it. The tool never calls hid_close and never clears 0x0057f196.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x412d00 has no entry guard: it unconditionally calls hid_open at 0x412d23, stores the result to 0x580188 at 0x412d2b, sets 0x57f196 = 1 at 0x412d4e, calls _beginthreadex at 0x412d55 and CloseHandle(thread) at 0x412d75. Matcher 0x4035f0 zeroes 0x57f338 at 0x40360a without closing the previous handle.
- **REFUTED IF:** A second replug produces no second open of the FF02/01 collection in ProcMon.
- **Matters:** It shows the device tolerates several simultaneous open handles on the same collection, which bears directly on Flagged-for-decision #3 — whether our flasher and config tool may hold the device open at the same time.

#### 26. `no-timer-driven-refresh` — [D]

**There is no periodic re-read: the config tool sets no timer in its own code.**

- **Expect:** No feature transfers on any fixed cadence while the window sits open. An exhaustive 4-byte scan of cfg107's whole .text for the SetTimer IAT slot 0x0052d5cc finds 29 references, all at VA 0x431b74 or above — none below 0x420000, none in the vendor band [0x401000, 0x420000).
- **Cite:** cfg107 import table: 0x52d5cc = USER32!SetTimer. Exhaustive scan of the raw .text bytes for that slot address; lowest hit at file offset 0x31b74 = VA 0x431b74.
- **REFUTED IF:** Feature transfers recur at a regular interval with no user action. Blind spot per CLAUDE.md §1.2a: the scan sees the slot address as a 4-byte literal, so a SetTimer reached through an MFC wrapper living above 0x420000 stays invisible to the band argument — the observation, not the scan, is authoritative here.
- **Matters:** It settles whether the vendor believes the device's settings can change under it. No timer means the vendor relies entirely on the event channel for that, which is a design decision our tool should copy or consciously reject.

#### 27. `device-is-high-speed` — [G]

**The OP1 8k v2 enumerates at USB High Speed, so EP0 wMaxPacketSize is 64 and the 1041-byte GET_REPORT splits into 17 packets on the bus.**

- **Expect:** Device descriptor bcdUSB at least 0x0200, speed High (480 Mbps). Interface B's mouse interrupt endpoint has bInterval = 1 (125 microsecond microframes) to reach 8000 Hz.
- **Cite:** Not from the binary. Inference from the product's 8000 Hz claim, which is unreachable at Full Speed (1 ms frames cap at 1000 Hz), plus the seven-entry polling-rate list ending at '8000Hz' in cfg107 (strings 0x557ea8 through 0x557efc; notes/config-protocol.md §10.4).
- **REFUTED IF:** The capture shows the device at Full Speed, or bInterval is not 1 on the mouse endpoint.
- **Matters:** It sets how a 1041-byte feature transfer is fragmented and therefore what a partial or stalled transfer would look like — the failure mode our mock device harness has to be able to inject.

#### 28. `four-transfers-share-one-interface` — [D]

**All four feature transfers go to the SAME USB interface — the one carrying the 156-byte five-collection report descriptor — and none goes to the mouse interface.**

- **Expect:** Identical wIndex on all four setup packets. That wIndex is the bInterfaceNumber of the interface carrying report ids 0xA0 and 0xA1; the other interface (Generic Desktop / Mouse, 69-byte descriptor, Input 0x01) receives no control transfers from the tool.
- **Cite:** Device side [O]: two HID interfaces, interface A carrying the vendor collections (notes/device-predictions.md). Host side [D]: cfg107 matches on HIDP_CAPS UsagePage and Usage at 0x40377c and 0x403785, which selects one collection and therefore one interface.
- **REFUTED IF:** The four transfers do not share a wIndex, or any is addressed to the mouse interface.
- **Matters:** On macOS we address a collection, not an interface, and hidapi picks the device path. Confirming that all vendor traffic lands on one interface is what lets us treat the FF01/02 collection as the single command endpoint.

#### 29. `software-version-string` — [D]

**The window caption and the Software Version readout are static strings; the Firmware Version readout is the only device-derived text in the UI.**

- **Expect:** Caption 'Endgame Gear OP1 8k v2 Configuration Tool' set from the literal at 0x00558988. The Firmware Version field (control 1027 on DIALOG 102) reads 'V1.07'.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x413035 pushl $0x558988 then 0x41303c call 0x41b535 (SetWindowText); UTF-16 at 0x558988 = 'Endgame Gear OP1 8k v2 Configuration Tool'. Version format string at 0x5589dc = 'V%.2f'; divisor double 100.0 at 0x558c18; target window at page-object+0x2ccc set at 0x41313e.
- **REFUTED IF:** The Firmware Version field shows something other than V1.07 for a device at bcdDevice 0x0107.
- **Matters:** It gives a zero-cost visual confirmation, visible in a screenshot, that the A1 02 round trip actually reached the device — useful for judging the capture even before the pcap is opened.

#### 30. `critical-section-brackets-each-command` — [D]

**Each command-plus-response is wrapped in one critical section, so the four transfers cannot interleave — but the allocator calls inside the send and receive wrappers are NOT that critical section.**

- **Expect:** Transfers 1 and 2 form one atomic pair, 3 and 4 another; no other feature traffic can appear between a SET and its matching GET. EnterCriticalSection(0x00578C08) is called by the four COMMAND functions, not by the transport wrappers.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x403b4e and 0x403b67 Enter, 0x403ba9 and 0x403c0e Leave (A1 12); 0x404611 and 0x404630 Enter, 0x40468e / 0x4046e7 / 0x4046f6 Leave (A1 02); 0x4041ae and 0x4041c9 Enter, 0x40425e and 0x4042a7 Leave (A0 11); 0x4047eb and 0x4047ff Leave (A1 13). CORRECTION to the implication in notes/config-protocol.md §8.1 about the wrappers: 0x0041466c inside 0x403850 and 0x403920 tail-jumps to 0x0041463d, which calls malloc at 0x00507bff and retries through the new-handler at 0x00573000 — it is operator new; 0x00414677 tail-jumps to 0x00507b17 = free. They allocate a scratch copy of the caller's buffer so a failed HidD_SetFeature can be retried with the original bytes.
- **REFUTED IF:** A GET appears between a SET and its matching GET, or the two pairs interleave.
- **Matters:** notes/config-protocol.md §8.1 rests a design argument on where that critical section is. Getting the boundary right decides whether our transport layer or our command layer owns the mutual exclusion — and §4.3 says the write phase must be small enough to audit by eye, which means the locking must be in one obvious place.

### Rejected by audit (14) — not pre-registered

| id | fault | auditor's evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `record-0x09-is-lod-index` | `citation-wrong` | The cited restore code contradicts the claim twice. (1) The list is ELEVEN entries in normal mode, not twelve. Disassembling 0x40ee80-0x40f215 (dis.sh cfg107), the 12-entry branch at 0x40ef2a pushes exactly eleven CB_ADDSTRING (0x143) strings: 0x557f0c,0x557f18,0x557f24,0x557f30,0x557f3c,0x557f48,0x557f54,0x557f60,0x557f6c,0x557f78,0x557f84 at 0x40ef32,0x40ef47,0x40ef5c,0x40ef71,0x40ef86,0x40ef9b,0x40efb0,0x40efc5,0x40efda,0x40efef,0x40f004. I dumped those UTF-16 strings from the file: '0.7mm'..'1.7mm'. The twelfth string 0x557f90='2.0mm' is pushed ONLY at 0x40eef2, inside the TWO-entry branch (with 0x557f30 '1.0mm' at 0x40eedd). The prediction's cited range '0x40eedd-0x40f011' straddles both branches and counts 11+1 as one twelve-item list. (2) The index range is 0..10, not 0..11: 0x40f013 movzbl %bl,%eax; 0x40f016 cmpl $0xa,%eax; 0x40f019 ja 0x40f0ce (default arm pushes CB_SETCURSEL index 3), then jmpl *0x40f6c0(,%eax,4). I read that jump table from the file: exactly 11 entries (0x40f026,0x40f03b,0x40f050,0x40f056,0x40f068,0x40f07a,0x40f080,0x40f092,0x40f0a4,0x40f0aa,0x40f0bc), pushing wParam 0..10. So a record byte of 11 would display '1.0mm', not '2.0mm', and the prediction's own refuted_if ('the LOD combo shows an entry whose index does not equal record[0x09]') is triggered by its own cited code. This also corrupts capture_requirements, which instructs the observer to record 'whether the LOD combo has 12 entries or 2'. |

| `record-0x08-and-0x0a-are-two-named-checkboxes` | `citation-wrong` | The sense is INVERTED for record[0x08] (struct+0x26, control 1066 'Disable LED on Lift-Off'); the prediction states 'The checkbox is checked exactly when the byte is 1' for both bytes. Three independent sites in cfg107 say otherwise. (a) Restore 0x40ee80: cmpb $0x1,0x57f236; 0x40ee92 pushl $0 (lParam); 0x40ee94 jne 0x40eea6. Fall-through (byte==1) reaches 0x40ee9c pushl $0x0 = BM_SETCHECK wParam 0 -> UNCHECKED; the jne arm (byte!=1) reaches 0x40eeac pushl $0x1 -> CHECKED. (b) The BN_CLICKED handler for 1066 at 0x410000: SendMessage(hwnd,0xF0 BM_GETCHECK,0,0) then 0x410018 sete %cl; 0x41001b movb %cl,0x57f236 -> struct+0x26 = 1 when UNCHECKED. (c) The APPLY-time harvest 0x40ec20: 0x40ecd2 sete %cl; 0x40ecd7 movb %cl,0x26(%edi) -> same inversion. By contrast record[0x0A] (struct+0x28, 'Angle Snapping') is non-inverted: 0x40ecb8 setne %dl / movb %dl,0x28(%edi), and restore 0x40f0e0 cmpb $0x1 falls through to 0x40f0f1 pushl $0x1 (CHECKED). The vendor's own default writer at 0x413e37 movl $0x301,0x26(%eax) sets struct+0x26=1, i.e. box unchecked, consistent with the inversion. Control captions confirmed with dlgdump: 1066 AUTOCHECKBOX 'Disable LED on Lift-Off', 1064 AUTOCHECKBOX 'Angle Snapping'. |
| `settings-record-at-report-offset-0x10` | `citation-wrong` | The exact_expectation contains a false arithmetic statement: '0x10 + 0x400 = 0x410, plus the report-id byte = 1041 exactly, so the record ends flush with the report.' The report-ID byte is buffer index 0x00 and is already inside the 0x10-byte prefix, so it cannot be added again. 16 + 1024 = 1040 = 0x410, but the buffer is 0x411 = 1041 bytes (indices 0x000..0x410). Verified from the bytes: 0x403bb3 pushl $0x411 (memset size on -0x468(%ebp)), 0x403bc9 movl $0x411,%esi (GetFeature length), 0x403bed movl $0x100,%ecx / 0x403bf2 leal -0x458(%ebp),%esi (= base -0x468 + 0x10) / 0x403bf8 movl $0x57f340,%edi / 0x403c02 rep movsl. The record therefore occupies 0x10..0x40F and report byte 0x410 lies AFTER it, unread by cfg107 and unmentioned anywhere in the document. On the write side 0x404180 memsets the full 0x411 buffer at 0x4041f9 and rep movsl's 0x400 bytes to base+0x10 at 0x404233, so byte 0x410 goes out as 0x00. The document's report-level accounting (14 header bytes + 913 unknown record bytes) leaves an unenumerated 1-byte residue — the 'partition, not arithmetic' failure CLAUDE.md §6 names. |

The other half ('no CreateFile on the FF02/02 collection') is genuinely [D]. |
