So no number in the exact_expectation is checkable against a successful capture. The structural claim itself is confirmed exactly by my mechanical extraction of the packer: record 0x3D<-struct 0x34, then +7 on the record and +8 on the struct through record 0x67<-struct 0x64, lone byte record 0x6E<-struct 0x6C, and record 0x37..0x3C<-struct 0x2E..0x33 contiguous. |
| `record-0x0d-0x0e-are-cpi-stage-selector-and-count` | `contradicts-notes` | The prediction (correctly) maps struct+0x0A to RECORD offset 0x0D, but notes/config-protocol.md §10.5 point 4 states that 0x57f2aa — the shadow of struct+0x0A — is 'settings-record byte 0x0a', and likewise calls 0x57f2a6 'settings-record byte 0x06'. Those are struct offsets, not record offsets: I disassembled 0x404830 and it copies struct byte k to 0x57f2a0+k (0x404843 movzbl 0x6(%eax),%ecx / 0x404847 movb %cl,0x57f2a6), and my mechanical extraction of the packer gives record 0x05 <- struct 0x06 and record 0x0D <- struct 0x0A. So the prediction silently supersedes a committed [D] note that names two record bytes wrongly. The document explicitly flags its corrections to §2, §8.1, §10.3 and §10.5, so the omission here is inconsistent with its own practice, and CLAUDE.md §1.7 requires a noticed gap to be written down. Everything else in this prediction verifies: packer 0x40430d/0x404311 and 0x404314/0x404318; restore 0x40f10b movb 0x57f21b,%al with cmpb $1/$2/$3 driving CB_SETCURSEL 0/1/2/3 on 0xf0(%esi); DDX binds 1019 to +0xd0 at 0x40b80a-0x40b811 (+0x20 = 0xf0); DLGINIT for 1019 is '1','2','3','4'; default writer 0x413e31 movw $0x401,0xa(%eax). |
| `no-a0-11-write-at-startup` | `contradicts-notes` | The prediction's premise — that eight BN_CLICKED handlers tail-jump into a live A0 11 write — directly contradicts notes/config-protocol.md §12.2, which states of 'Factory Reset' (1039) and 'APPLY' (1043) on dialog 102: 'Those two are the entire write surface of the program.' The prediction does not say it is correcting that. I confirmed the prediction is the right side of the disagreement: 0x414010 reaches 0x414356 calll 0x404180 (in a 3-attempt loop, Sleep arg 0x12c stepping +0x32), its only entries are the eight E9 tail-jumps my raw scan found (0x410cf9, 0x410e29, 0x410f59, 0x411089, 0x4111ca, 0x41130a, 0x41144a, 0x41158a — e.g. 0x410cf9 e9 12 33 00 00 jmp 0x414010), and a whole-FILE 4-byte search for 0x414010, 0x404180, 0x4045e0, 0x403b20 and 0x404720 returns zero hits, so no address is taken anywhere and the E8/E9 edge sets are complete. The prediction itself is sound and is the safety case for the capture; the fault is the undeclared contradiction with a committed note that a later reader would take at face value. |

### Cannot predict — what the capture will teach us (8)

- **What is in the 913 record bytes the host never touches — record offsets 0x01-0x04 and 0x73-0x3FF?**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: cfg107 reads none of them (unpacker 0x403c40 loads exactly 111 bytes) and writes none of them (packer 0x4042d0 stores exactly 111 bytes into a buffer that was memset to zero first). No other code in cfg107 references the 0x57f340 record block outside those two functions. A byte a program never touches leaves no evidence of its meaning anywhere in that program.
  - how a capture answers it: Transfer 4's 1041-byte data stage IS those bytes. Reading them off the device is the single highest-value thing this capture produces and it costs nothing extra. If they are all zero, the record really is 111 bytes and APPLY is harmless. If they are non-zero — LED colours, per-button macros, calibration, a checksum — then the vendor's own APPLY destroys them, and CLAUDE.md §1.3 forbids us from copying that behaviour.
- **What do the other twelve of the fourteen bytes the A1 02 query returns mean (response offsets 0x10 and 0x13-0x1D)?**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: cfg107 copies resp[0x10..0x1D] into a caller struct at 0x4046c9-0x4046e3, and OnInitDialog consumes only struct bytes 1 and 2 (the firmware version). The remaining twelve bytes are copied and then read by nothing.
  - how a capture answers it: Transfer 2 contains them verbatim. Even without meanings, knowing which are zero, which look like a serial number, and which change between two runs would classify them. If any byte matches the device's bcdDevice, PID or a string descriptor, that identifies it outright.
- **Does the device ever answer status 0x03 (busy), and how long does it actually need after a SET before a GET succeeds?**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The 50 ms and 80 ms sleeps and the 0x03 / 1000 ms busy budget are host-side constants (cfg107 0x40467b, 0x403b96, 0x40396e, 0x4039d7). They are what the vendor chose to wait, not what the device requires. Nothing in any binary measures the device.
  - how a capture answers it: Timestamps on the four transfers give the vendor's actual pacing, and a busy retry, if it ever fires, appears as a repeated GET about 100 ms later. A passive capture cannot find the minimum the device needs, but it fixes an upper bound and tells us whether the busy path is live code or dead.
- **Does HidD_GetSerialNumberString / GetManufacturerString / GetProductString generate real USB GET_DESCRIPTOR(STRING) traffic, or does hidusb serve them from cache?**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: This is Windows kernel-stack behaviour. cfg107 only calls the APIs, at 0x402cbf, 0x402cf4 and 0x402d29 through the dynamic slots.
  - how a capture answers it: Directly: either the string reads appear on the bus after transfer 4 or they do not. Either answer is fine; what matters is being able to attribute every non-feature transfer in the capture and be sure nothing is unaccounted for.
- **Which bInterfaceNumber carries the vendor collections, and how many Windows HID device-interface paths the OP1 exposes.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The tool matches on the usage pair and never on interface number (cfg107 0x40377c and 0x403785). hid_enumerate extracts an interface number from the path string at 0x402d78-0x402da5, but hid_open never filters on it.
  - how a capture answers it: wIndex on the four setup packets gives the interface number. ProcMon's CreateFile paths give the full col01..colNN list, which also settles whether Windows splits one USB interface into five device interfaces — the thing that decides how many entries our own macOS enumerator should expect.
- **Does the mouse push vendor input reports (report 0x03 or 0x08) without being asked?**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: cfg107 only listens. It tells us what it would do with buf[1] in {0x02, 0x06, 0xB4} at 0x412cbf-0x412cde, but nothing about whether the device ever sends them, and nothing at all about report 0x08 on the third collection, which no binary opens.
  - how a capture answers it: Interrupt-IN transfers on interface A are in the capture whether or not any application reads them. This settles whether our read-modify-write races a device-side change (notes/config-protocol.md §10.5 point 2) and gives the first evidence of any kind about the third collection.
- **What the device's CURRENT settings actually are — the meaning of every record byte we have located but not named.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: The binary gives offsets, widths and which control each drives. It cannot give this device's values, and it cannot label a byte whose control has no caption — the four AUTORADIOBUTTONs on page 135 are captioned ''.
  - how a capture answers it: Only in combination with the UI. The record from transfer 4 plus a screenshot of all four tabs is a labelled example: every number visible in the UI can be searched for in the 1041 bytes. That single pairing would probably name more record bytes than another full day of disassembly.
- **Whether opening the vendor collection is what makes hidclass start polling interface A's interrupt endpoint, or whether Windows was already polling it for the keyboard and consumer collections.**
  - why the binaries cannot answer: Entirely a Windows HID class-driver question; no vendor binary influences it.
  - how a capture answers it: Compare bus traffic on interface A before the tool launches with traffic after it opens the FF02/01 collection. It bears on whether our macOS tool's open has any observable side effect on the device at all.

### Gaps the auditor flagged (5)

- The 1041st byte of report 0xA0. The record is 1024 bytes at report offset 0x10, so it ends at 0x40F, and report byte 0x410 is neither read (unpacker touches only 0x57f340+{0..0x72}) nor accounted for anywhere in the document. On the write side it is forced to 0x00 by the 0x411-byte memset at 0x4041f9. The capture will contain it and nothing predicts its value; the document's own accounting (14 header bytes plus 913 unknown record bytes) leaves exactly one unenumerated byte, which is the residue-must-be-zero discipline of CLAUDE.md §6 breaking down.
- A warning that the record-to-screenshot pairing is one-way wherever the host decoder clamps. capture_requirements rests most of the capture's value on 'the pairing is what turns located offsets into named fields', but three decoders collapse out-of-range bytes onto a default: LOD (0x40f016 cmpl $0xa / ja 0x40f0ce -> CB_SETCURSEL 3, i.e. any byte above 10 displays '1.0mm'), polling rate (index table 0x40f70c maps every non-one-hot value to arm 7 at 0x40f1d3 -> index 3, '1000Hz'), and CPI Levels (0x40f10b, anything not 1/2/3 -> index 3, '4'). An observer reading a byte back off a screenshot in any of those cases would key it wrongly, and this is exactly the situation a single irreversible capture cannot be re-run to fix.
- A prediction about 'Ripple Control' (control 1065 on page 135), which is cheap on the same screenshot and would be a real negative. dlgdump shows it as an AUTOCHECKBOX with a caption; DDX binds it to member +0xf78 (m_hWnd +0xf98); it has no entry in the page-135 message map I parsed; and an objdump grep over the whole of .text for '0xf98(%e' returns no hits outside that binding. That supports predicting that its state corresponds to no record byte at all — worth registering both as a testable claim and as a warning against spending capture-interpretation effort correlating it.
- DIALOG 137, the LED page. capture_requirements asks for screenshots of the four tabs (Basic 135, Advanced Sensor 140, Buttons 139, Button Mapping 153) but not of 137, which notes/config-protocol.md §12.2 records as created separately at 0x004056bb and which carries 'LED On / Off', an 'LED effect' combo, 'Scroll led', 'Logo led', 'DPI led' and R/G/B edits. Those settings must occupy some of the same 111 record bytes. If page 137 is reachable in the running UI, omitting it from the screenshot set loses that half of the pairing for a whole feature area, and the capture cannot be redone.
- A warning that DLGINIT is not what the user sees on the Advanced Sensor page. notes §12.3 records that combo 1082 ('CPI Downshift Tuning') ships a three-item DLGINIT but is refilled at run time from 0x00411980 with four items ('Force Off', 'Light Timer Only', 'Medium Timer Only', 'Default'). A reader matching a screenshot index against the dlgdump inventory would be off by one for every selection past the first. The capture protocol asks for the screenshots but does not carry this caveat.

### Irreversible / one-shot in this area

- DO NOT PRESS APPLY. The vendor's write is not read-modify-write: cfg107 0x404180 memsets the 1024-byte record to zero at 0x4041d1 before refilling only the 111 bytes it understands. Pressing APPLY once overwrites 913 record bytes with 0x00 whether or not anything was changed. If the device stores anything in record 0x01-0x04 or 0x73-0x3FF, it is gone — and the capture will have destroyed the one thing we are running it to see.
- DO NOT PRESS FACTORY RESET. It sends A1 13 and the device restores its own defaults; the mouse's current settings, which are the labelled sample we want, are unrecoverable afterwards. If a factory-reset capture is wanted it must come AFTER the read-only capture is safely saved, as a separate deliberate run.
- DO NOT CLICK the four unlabelled radio buttons or the 'CPI 1'..'CPI 4' labels on the Basic page. Those eight controls (ids 1062, 1067, 1068, 1069 and 1033/1039, 1034/1043, 1035/1052, 1036/1053) are the ONLY controls whose BN_CLICKED handler tail-jumps into the live-apply path at 0x414010, which writes A0 11 immediately, with no confirmation and no APPLY press. Message map at cfg107 file offset 0x156768-0x156884.
- Other controls — checkboxes, combos, edit boxes, trackbars — only enable the APPLY button via the dirty-check at 0x4144c0 and do not themselves write. That is a derived claim, not a licence: change nothing.
- DO NOT press the button on the mouse itself that cycles CPI or polling rate. It changes device state, and the change then lands in the very record we are trying to read as an unmodified baseline.
- Two hidden buttons exist on the Basic page — 1059 'Apply CPI settings' and 1061 'Surface Calibration' — shipped with WS_VISIBLE clear. If any tool or accessibility path makes them visible, do not press them. Surface Calibration in particular is a capability in no derived command set and its effect on the device is completely unknown.
- The 1041-byte record from transfer 4 is the closest thing to a settings backup that exists for this mouse. Save the pcapng somewhere durable before running any further experiment on the device.


---

## Config 1.07 — writing settings, and the differential experiment

*area key: `config-write` — 44 written, 9 rejected*


### Pre-registered (35)

#### 1. `apply-write-frame` — [D]

**Pressing APPLY emits exactly one HID SET_FEATURE of 1041 bytes whose first two bytes are A0 11, with bytes 0x02-0x0F all zero.**

- **Expect:** buffer[0]=0xA0, buffer[1]=0x11, buffer[0x02..0x0F]=0x00 (14 zero bytes), buffer[0x10..0x82]=the 115-byte settings record, buffer[0x83..0x410]=0x00 (910 zero bytes). Total length exactly 0x411=1041.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x404180 (fn entry); memset(payload,0,0x400) at 0x4041d1; memset(report,0,0x411) at 0x404206; movl $0x11a0,-0x46c(%ebp) at 0x404218 (bytes A0 11 00 00); rep movsl 0x100 dwords from -0x86c to -0x45c (=base+0x10) at 0x404222-0x404233; movl $0x411,%esi at 0x404238; calll 0x403850 at 0x404243
- **REFUTED IF:** The SET_REPORT length is not 1041 (or 1040, see usb-setup-fields), or byte[1] is not 0x11, or any of bytes 0x02-0x0F is non-zero, or any byte at or above 0x83 is non-zero.
- **Matters:** EGGConfigCore's write frame is this exact buffer. A single wrong offset here writes garbage into the mouse's settings region.

#### 2. `usb-setup-fields` — [G]

**The APPLY write appears as a control transfer SET_REPORT on interface A: bmRequestType 0x21, bRequest 0x09, wValue 0x03A0 (Feature<<8 | reportID 0xA0).**

- **Expect:** bmRequestType=0x21, bRequest=0x09, wValue=0x03A0, wIndex=<interface number of the vendor interface>, wLength=0x0411=1041 with the data stage starting 'A0 11 00 ...' (report ID present in the data). RIVAL ENCODING: wLength=0x0410=1040 with the data stage starting '11 00 ...' (hidusb strips the report ID into wValue). Distinguisher: the wLength field and whether the first data byte is 0xA0 or 0x11.
- **Cite:** cfg107 calll *0x52d1dc (HidD_SetFeature) at 0x403885 with (handle, buffer, esi=0x411); import-table slot per notes/config-protocol.md §8. The USB-level framing itself is a Windows HID-stack convention, not derived from the .exe.
- **REFUTED IF:** bRequest is not 0x09, or wValue is not 0x03A0, or wLength is neither 1041 nor 1040, or the transfer is on an interrupt OUT endpoint rather than EP0.
- **Matters:** Tells us whether hidapi on macOS (IOHIDDeviceSetReport) will produce a byte-identical wire transaction to the vendor's, i.e. whether our 1041-byte buffer including the leading report ID is the right shape.

#### 3. `apply-status-readback` — [D]

**After the write the tool sleeps 300 ms and then issues one 64-byte GET_FEATURE on report 0xA1; it does NOT re-read the 1024-byte settings blob.**

- **Expect:** Exactly one GET_REPORT with wValue 0x03A1 and length 64 (or 63), issued 300 ms +/-15 ms after the SET_REPORT returns. Its request buffer is 64 bytes with [0]=0xA1 and [1..63]=0. Success requires response[1]==1. No 1041-byte GET_REPORT follows an APPLY at any point.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x404248 movzwl 0x8(%ebp),%edx then calll *0x52d22c (Sleep) at 0x40424f, with 0x12c=300 pushed at 0x413f25/0x413f29 (from movl $0x12c,0x10(%esp) at 0x413ed1); memset 64 at 0x404270; movb $-0x5f(=0xA1),-0x54(%ebp) at 0x40427d; calll *0x52d1d8 (HidD_GetFeature) at 0x40428c with length 0x40; movb -0x53(%ebp),%bl at 0x404292; cmpb $0x1,%al at 0x413f37
- **REFUTED IF:** A 1041-byte GET_FEATURE follows an APPLY, or the gap between the write and the 64-byte read is not ~300 ms, or no read follows the write at all.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.1 says read back and verify after every write. The vendor does NOT read back the data — only a 1-byte status. Our tool must do strictly more (re-read A1 12 and diff), and we must know that the vendor's status byte is the only thing it checks.

#### 4. `no-read-before-write` — [D]

**The config tool never reads the settings blob immediately before writing it. A1 12 is issued only at OnInitDialog and on WM_DEVICECHANGE.**

- **Expect:** Across the whole session, every 1041-byte GET_FEATURE is immediately preceded by a 64-byte A1 12 SET_FEATURE, and every such pair occurs only (a) within ~1 s of the tool's window appearing, or (b) within ~1 s of a device arrival/removal notification. No A1 12 pair appears between a UI change and its APPLY.
- **Cite:** Raw E8-edge scan of cfg107 .text: the only callers of 0x403b20 (the A1 12 read path) are 0x413152 (inside OnInitDialog 0x412fb0) and 0x413914 (inside the WM_DEVICECHANGE handler 0x413600).
- **REFUTED IF:** Any A1 12 / 1041-byte GET_FEATURE pair appears on the wire adjacent to an APPLY, or at a periodic interval.
- **Matters:** It settles that the vendor's 'read-modify-write' is a read ONCE at startup, then blind writes from a host-side shadow. Our design (§4.1) must re-read before every write, which is a deliberate divergence, and this is the evidence that it is a divergence.

#### 5. `apply-retry-shape` — [D]

**If the write or the status read fails, APPLY retries the whole write up to 3 times total, with the post-write sleep growing 300 -> 350 -> 400 ms.**

- **Expect:** On failure: at most 3 SET_FEATURE(0xA0,0x11,1041) frames with identical payloads, each followed by a 64-byte 0xA1 GET_FEATURE, with inter-frame sleeps of 300, 350 and 400 ms. Never a 4th.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x413f3b addl $0x32,0x10(%esp); 0x413f40 incl %esi; 0x413f41 cmpl $0x3,%esi; 0x413f44 jl 0x413f25. Same loop in the live-apply path at 0x414362-0x414369 with esi starting 0x12c (0x41403f) and +0x32 per pass.
- **REFUTED IF:** A failing APPLY produces a number of A0 11 frames other than 3, or the sleeps are not 300/350/400 ms.
- **Matters:** Bounds how many identical writes our own retry policy may legitimately emit before declaring failure.

#### 6. `record-is-115-bytes` — [D]

**The settings record the host composes is exactly 0x73 = 115 bytes wide; the rest of the 1024-byte payload is always zero.**

- **Expect:** Payload bytes 0x00-0x72 (report bytes 0x10-0x82) carry data; payload bytes 0x73-0x3FF (report bytes 0x83-0x410) are 0x00 in every A0 11 frame in the capture, in every experiment step, including after Factory Reset.
- **Cite:** cfg107 packer 0x4042d0-0x4045dc writes destination offsets 0x00 and 0x05..0x72 only (111 stores, extracted mechanically); the dirty-check 0x4144c0 memsets two 0x72-byte buffers at 0x4144db/0x4144ec and compares indices 0..0x72 (cmpl $0x73,%esi at 0x414550).
- **REFUTED IF:** Any A0 11 frame has a non-zero byte at report offset >= 0x83.
- **Matters:** Our dry-run golden file and the invariant 'the byte stream for a given input is identical every run' need the exact extent of the meaningful record.

#### 7. `payload-hole-01-04` — [D]

**Payload bytes 0x01, 0x02, 0x03, 0x04 (report bytes 0x11-0x14) are never written by the packer and are always zero.**

- **Expect:** Report bytes 0x11,0x12,0x13,0x14 = 00 00 00 00 in every A0 11 frame, in every experiment step.
- **Cite:** cfg107 packer 0x4042d0 writes wire offset 0x00 then jumps straight to 0x05 (movb %dl,0x5(%eax) at 0x4042d9). Destination offsets 0x01-0x04 have no store anywhere in 0x4042d0-0x4045dc.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any of report bytes 0x11-0x14 is non-zero in an outgoing A0 11 frame.
- **Matters:** A 4-byte hole in the middle of a record is a strong structural signal (reserved field, or a field the 1.07 UI does not expose). If the device returns non-zero there on a read, our read-modify-write must preserve it — the vendor does not.

#### 8. `passthrough-bytes-00-07` — [D]

**Payload bytes 0x00 and 0x07 (report bytes 0x10 and 0x17) also have no UI control behind them, but unlike 0x0F-0x22 they ARE forced to zero by Factory Reset.**

- **Expect:** Report bytes 0x10 and 0x17 equal the corresponding bytes of the start-up read in every A0 11 frame — until Factory Reset, after which the next A0 11 frame has 0x00 at both.
- **Cite:** cfg107 struct offsets 0x00 and 0x0c. Literal scan for 0x57f210 and 0x57f21c finds only base-pointer uses; register-offset grep for '0xc(%reg)' in 0x401000-0x430000 finds only 0x403c64 (unpacker), 0x40430a (packer) and 0x413dfc (movb %cl,0xc(%eax) in the default writer, cl=0). Default for struct[0x00] is movb %cl,(%eax) at 0x413df7.
- **REFUTED IF:** Report byte 0x10 or 0x17 changes in response to any UI control, or is non-zero in the first A0 11 issued after a Factory Reset.
- **Matters:** Distinguishes 'unknown but host-resettable' from 'unknown and never host-written'. Our factory-reset implementation must not invent values for either.

#### 9. `polling-rate-byte` — [D]

**The Polling Rate combo (page 135 'Basic', control 1085) writes payload byte 0x05 = report byte 0x15 as a one-hot mask, and it needs APPLY (it does not write immediately).**

- **Expect:** Combo index -> report byte 0x15: 0 ('125Hz')=0x40, 1 ('250Hz')=0x20, 2 ('500Hz')=0x10, 3 ('1000Hz')=0x08, 4 ('2000Hz')=0x04, 5 ('4000Hz')=0x02, 6 ('8000Hz')=0x01. Selecting a rate produces NO USB traffic; the next APPLY carries the new value.
- **Cite:** cfg107 CBN_SELCHANGE handler for id 1085 is 0x004115a0 (MFC message-map entry at .rdata 0x00557d50: msg 0x111, code 1, id 1085, pfn 0x004115a0). It sends CB_GETCURSEL (0x147) to page135+0x12f0 at 0x4115aa, bounds to <=6 at 0x4115b6, and jumps through the table at 0x411618 to eight movb sites 0x4115c2/ce/da/e6/f2, 0x41160a, 0x4115fe storing 0x40/0x20/0x10/0x08/0x04/0x02/0x01 to 0x0057f216 (= struct[0x06]). Packer 0x4042d5-0x4042d9: wire[0x05] <- struct[0x06]. Each arm then jmps to the dirty-check 0x4144c0, not to a send path.
- **REFUTED IF:** Report byte 0x15 takes any value other than the seven listed, or the index->mask mapping differs (in particular if index 5 gives 0x01 and index 6 gives 0x02, i.e. the last two swapped), or selecting a rate emits an A0 11 without pressing APPLY.
- **Matters:** Polling rate is the setting most likely to be wanted first, and bit 0 = 8000 Hz is counter-intuitive enough that getting it backwards would silently set 4000 Hz.

#### 10. `lod-two-item-mode` — [D]

**There is a second LOD mode in which the combo holds only '1.0mm' and '2.0mm' and the byte is 1 or 2; it is gated on payload byte 0x6F (report byte 0x7F) being 1.**

- **Expect:** If report byte 0x7F == 0x01 in the start-up read, the LOD combo shows exactly two items and report byte 0x19 takes only 0x01 or 0x02. If report byte 0x7F != 1, the combo shows eleven items '0.7mm'..'1.7mm'.
- **Cite:** cfg107 restore 0x40eeb6 cmpb $0x1,0x0057f23b (=struct[0x2b]) selecting the 2-item fill (0x557f30 '1.0mm', 0x557f90 '2.0mm') at 0x40eed7-0x40ef01 versus the 11-item fill at 0x40ef2c-0x40f011; harvest 0x40ec42 tests the same byte and yields 1/2. Packer: wire[0x6f] <- struct[0x2b] (0x4045c1-0x4045c5).
- **REFUTED IF:** The combo item count does not match the value of report byte 0x7F in the start-up read.
- **Matters:** It is the first evidence of a device-reported capability flag that changes what the host may legally send. Our tool must read it before offering LOD values.

#### 11. `disable-led-liftoff-inverted` — [D]

**'Disable LED on Lift-Off' (page 135, control 1066) is payload byte 0x08 = report byte 0x18 and it is INVERTED: checked sends 0, unchecked sends 1.**

- **Expect:** Report byte 0x18 = 0x00 when the box is ticked, 0x01 when it is clear. Factory default is 0x01 (box clear).
- **Cite:** cfg107 harvest 0x40ecc0-0x40ecd7: BM_GETCHECK to page135+0x127c (DDX member 0x125c = control 1066, bound at 0x40bb2f region) then 'sete %cl' (0x40ecd2) -> movb %cl,0x26(%edi). Packer wire[0x08] <- struct[0x26] (0x4042ea-0x4042ee). Default movl $0x301,0x26(%eax) at 0x413e37 gives struct[0x26]=1.
- **REFUTED IF:** Ticking the box makes report byte 0x18 go to 1 rather than 0.
- **Matters:** An inverted boolean is exactly the kind of thing that compiles clean and writes the wrong value. It is also a caught-early check on my reading of setne vs sete.

#### 12. `cpi-level-count-byte` — [D]

**The 'CPI Levels' combo (page 135, control 1019, items '1'..'4') writes payload byte 0x0E = report byte 0x1E as index+1, and needs APPLY.**

- **Expect:** Report byte 0x1E = 1, 2, 3 or 4 for combo items '1','2','3','4'. Factory default 4.
- **Cite:** cfg107 CBN_SELCHANGE for 1019 is 0x0040ca70 (.rdata 0x00557b88): CB_GETCURSEL to page135+0xf0 (DDX member 0xd0 = control 1019, bound at 0x40b7xx) then leal 0x1(%eax),%ebx and movb %bl,0x0057f21b at 0x40ca9c, then jmp 0x4144c0. Harvest 0x40ecda-0x40ed43 reproduces the same +1 mapping. Packer wire[0x0e] <- struct[0x0b] (0x404314-0x404318). Default movw $0x401,0xa(%eax) at 0x413e31 gives struct[0x0b]=4.
- **REFUTED IF:** Report byte 0x1E equals the zero-based index rather than index+1.
- **Matters:** Off-by-one on a count field is the classic transcription error, and this one bounds how many CPI stages the device will accept.

#### 13. `cpi-table-layout` — [D]

**The four CPI stages are four 5-byte records at payload offsets 0x23, 0x28, 0x2D, 0x32 (report offsets 0x33, 0x38, 0x3D, 0x42), each {u8 flag, u16 X little-endian, u16 Y little-endian}.**

- **Expect:** Report bytes 0x33..0x46 = [f1][X1lo][X1hi][Y1lo][Y1hi][f2][X2lo][X2hi][Y2lo][Y2hi][f3]...[Y4hi]. Typing 1600 into the 'CPI 3' X edit (control 1022) and pressing APPLY changes report bytes 0x3E,0x3F to 40 06 and nothing else except possibly 0x3D. Factory defaults: 400/400, 800/800, 1600/1600, 3200/3200 i.e. 90 01 90 01 / 20 03 20 03 / 40 06 40 06 / 80 0c 80 0c.
- **Cite:** cfg107 packer 0x4043ad-0x404436 maps struct 0x0e,0x10-0x14,0x16-0x1a,0x1c-0x20,0x22-0x25 onto wire 0x23-0x36 contiguously; unpacker 0x403c40+ builds struct u16 fields at 0x10,0x12,0x16,0x18,0x1c,0x1e,0x22,0x24 from wire byte pairs low-then-high (e.g. 0x403e0d-0x403e26: wire[0x2f]<<8 | wire[0x2e] -> struct u16 0x1c). Defaults movw $0x190/$0x320/$0x640/$0xc80 at 0x413db9-0x413def. Harvest 0x40ed47-0x40edda binds DDX members 0x4e8/0x4ec/0x4f0/0x4f4 (controls 1020-1023, X) and 0xe7c/0xe80/0xe84/0xe88 (controls 1025,1026,1027,1042, Y).
- **REFUTED IF:** The CPI values are not 16-bit little-endian at those offsets, or the stride is not 5, or the flag byte is not first.
- **Matters:** CPI is the setting users change most; a wrong endianness or stride writes a nonsense DPI to the sensor.

#### 14. `cpi-xy-flag-byte` — [D]

**The per-stage flag byte is 1 exactly when that stage's X and Y differ, and it is computed by the host, not chosen by the user.**

- **Expect:** With 'X/Y Settings' ticked and CPI 1 set to X=800 Y=1600, report byte 0x33 = 0x01. With X=Y for that stage, report byte 0x33 = 0x00.
- **Cite:** cfg107 harvest 0x40ed5d-0x40ed6e: movl 0x4e8(%esi),%edx; cmpl 0xe7c(%esi),%edx; setne %al; movb %al,0xe(%edi). Same shape at 0x40ed87/0x40edb1 for stages 2 and 3.
- **REFUTED IF:** The flag byte does not track X!=Y, or is settable independently.
- **Matters:** Our tool must derive this byte rather than expose it, or the device will get an inconsistent record.

#### 15. `cpi4-flag-vendor-bug` — [D]

**There is a copy-paste bug in the APPLY harvest: the CPI-4 flag byte is computed from CPI 3's X and Y, not CPI 4's.**

- **Expect:** Set CPI 3 to X=800 Y=1600 (different) and CPI 4 to X=3200 Y=3200 (equal), then press APPLY. Report byte 0x42 (CPI-4 flag) = 0x01 even though CPI 4's X equals its Y. Conversely set CPI 3 X=Y and CPI 4 X!=Y and report byte 0x42 = 0x00. NOTE the live-apply path is correct: clicking the CPI-4 radio afterwards sends report byte 0x42 = (CPI4 X != CPI4 Y).
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x40edc8-0x40eded: movzwl 0x4f4(%esi),%eax (CPI4 X) -> struct 0x22; movzwl 0xe88(%esi),%ecx (CPI4 Y) -> struct 0x24; then movl 0x4f0(%esi),%edx (CPI *3* X) ; cmpl 0xe84(%esi),%edx (CPI *3* Y) ; setne %al ; movb %al,0x20(%edi). Compare the correct version at 0x411574-0x411584 which uses 0x4f4 vs 0xe88.
- **REFUTED IF:** Report byte 0x42 tracks CPI 4's own X!=Y after an APPLY.
- **Matters:** If confirmed it proves the capture is measuring the exact code path I read, down to a one-instruction operand mistake. It is the highest-information single observation in this document. It also means our tool must NOT mirror the vendor here.

#### 16. `xy-settings-mirrors` — [D]

**The 'X/Y Settings' checkbox (page 135, control 1063) writes no record byte of its own; unticking it copies each X trackbar position into the corresponding Y control.**

- **Expect:** Unticking 'X/Y Settings' then pressing APPLY makes all four Y values equal their X values (report bytes 0x36-0x37 == 0x34-0x35, 0x3B-0x3C == 0x39-0x3A, 0x40-0x41 == 0x3E-0x3F, 0x45-0x46 == 0x43-0x44) and all four flag bytes 0x33,0x38,0x3D,0x42 become 0x00. No byte outside the CPI table changes.
- **Cite:** cfg107 BN_CLICKED for 1063 is 0x0040cab0 (.rdata 0x00557bd0). It sends TBM_GETPOS (0x400) to the four X trackbars at members 0x164/0x1d8/0x24c/0x2c0 and TBM_SETPOS (0x405, wParam 1) to the four Y trackbars at 0x75c/0x7d0/0x844/0x8b8 (0x40cac7-0x40cb58). It stores to no 0x0057f2xx global.
- **REFUTED IF:** Toggling 'X/Y Settings' changes a byte outside report range 0x33-0x46.
- **Matters:** It tells us the checkbox is a UI convenience with no wire representation, so our tool needs no field for it — and it is a candidate for a byte we might wrongly go looking for.

#### 17. `cpi-clamp-quantise` — [D]

**CPI values typed into the edit boxes are clamped to [10, 30000] and quantised half-up to a multiple of 10 below 10000 and a multiple of 50 above, before they reach the record.**

- **Expect:** Typing 5 gives report u16 = 10 (0x000A). Typing 40000 gives 30000 (0x7530). Typing 806 gives 810 (0x032A). Typing 12345 gives 12350 (0x303E). Typing 12324 gives 12300 (0x300C).
- **Cite:** cfg107 EN_CHANGE handler 0x0040fb80 for control 1020 (.rdata 0x00557c78): cmpl $0xa/jae, cmpl $0x7530/jbe, cmpl $0x2710/jbe at 0x40fb90-0x40fbb1; reciprocal /50 via $0x51eb851f + shrl $4 with 'cmpl $0x19' half-up at 0x40fbb3-0x40fbca; reciprocal /10 via $0xcccccccd + shrl $3 with 'cmpl $0x5' half-up at 0x40fbcf-0x40fbe9. Identical body at 0x0040e410 (notes/config-protocol.md §9.1).
- **REFUTED IF:** A typed value reaches the wire unquantised, or the boundary at 10000 behaves differently.
- **Matters:** Our own tool must apply the same clamp or refuse the value; sending an unquantised DPI is a [G] write to the sensor.

#### 18. `cpi-edit-no-live-write` — [D]

**Typing in a CPI edit box produces no USB traffic at all, on any keystroke.**

- **Expect:** Zero SET_FEATURE frames between the first keystroke in a CPI edit and the APPLY press, however many characters are typed.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x0040fb80 ends 'jmp 0x4144c0' at 0x40fbfc; 0x4144c0 packs both host records and only calls EnableWindow on the APPLY button (0x41456d / 0x41454a). It never calls 0x404180.
- **REFUTED IF:** Any A0 11 frame appears while typing.
- **Matters:** If the tool wrote on every keystroke, the device's flash would take many more writes than we assume, and our own edit-then-apply model would be wrong about how the device is normally driven.

#### 19. `motion-sync-byte` — [D]

**'Motion Sync' (page 140 'Advanced Sensor', control 1028) is payload byte 0x0C = report byte 0x1C, 0/1.**

- **Expect:** Report byte 0x1C flips 0x00 <-> 0x01 with the checkbox; factory default 0x00.
- **Cite:** cfg107 page-140 harvest 0x411ab4-0x411ad6: BM_GETCHECK to page140+0xd8 (DDX member 0xb8 = control 1028, bound at 0x4118aa/0x4118b1 'pushl $0x404') then setne -> movb %cl,0x2a(%ebx). Packer wire[0x0c] <- struct[0x2a] (0x404306-0x40430a). Default movb %cl,0x2a(%eax) with cl=0 at 0x413dff.
- **REFUTED IF:** Report byte 0x1C does not change when Motion Sync is toggled.
- **Matters:** Second independent confirmation that a different page's harvest maps to the offsets I derived.

#### 20. `downshift-smoothing-nibbles` — [D]

**Payload byte 0x0B (report byte 0x1B) packs two page-140 combos: low nibble = 'Smoothing Tuning' (control 1084), high nibble = 'CPI Downshift Tuning' (control 1082).**

- **Expect:** Smoothing Tuning index 0 ('Force Off') -> low nibble 2; index 1 ('Ripple Control Off') -> 0; index 2 ('Ripple Control On') -> 1; anything else -> 2. CPI Downshift index 0 ('Force Off') -> high nibble 0x8; index 1 ('Light Timer Only') -> 0xC; index 2 ('Medium Timer Only') -> 0x4; index 3 ('Default') -> 0x0. Factory default report byte 0x1B = 0x00.
- **Cite:** cfg107 page-140 harvest 0x411b28-0x411b51 (CB_GETCURSEL to page140+0x40c, DDX member 0x3ec = control 1084 bound at 0x411953 'pushl $0x43c') yields the local at -0x1(%ebp) = 2/0/1/2; 0x411b51-0x411bb9 (CB_GETCURSEL to page140+0x398, member 0x378 = control 1082 bound at 0x411941 'pushl $0x43a') selects among four arms 0x411b70/0x411b84/0x411b98/0x411bac which do andb $-0x10,0x29(%ebx) then orb $0x8 / $0xc / $0x4 / $0x0 then orb the low-nibble local. Packer wire[0x0b] <- struct[0x29] (0x4042ff-0x404303).
- **REFUTED IF:** The two combos do not share one byte, or the nibble order is reversed, or 'Force Off' on Smoothing does not give low nibble 2.
- **Matters:** Same packed-byte hazard as byte 0x06, and the non-monotonic index->value maps (0->2, 1->0, 2->1 and 0->8, 1->C, 2->4, 3->0) cannot be guessed.

#### 21. `force-max-fps-byte` — [D]

**'Force max Sensor fps' (page 140, control 1032) is payload byte 0x71 = report byte 0x81, 0/1.**

- **Expect:** Report byte 0x81 flips 0x00 <-> 0x01; factory default 0x00.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x411af5-0x411b10: BM_GETCHECK to page140+0x320 (member 0x300 = control 1032, bound at 0x411935 'pushl $0x408') then setne -> movb %cl,0x2d(%ebx). Packer wire[0x71] <- struct[0x2d] (0x4045cf-0x4045d3).
- **REFUTED IF:** Report byte 0x81 does not change, or a different byte does.
- **Matters:** Confirms the tail of the packer (the last four bytes are pulled from struct offsets far from the wire position), which is the part of the map most likely to be transcribed wrong.

#### 22. `sensor-angle-tuning-byte` — [D]

**'Sensor Angle Tuning' (page 140, trackbar 1038) is payload byte 0x70 = report byte 0x80 and carries the raw trackbar position.**

- **Expect:** Report byte 0x80 equals the trackbar's TBM_GETPOS value truncated to 8 bits. Moving the slider to its extremes gives the two ends of its range; factory default 0x00.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x411b13-0x411b25: SendMessage(page140+0x238, 0x400 /*TBM_GETPOS*/, 0, 0) -> movb %al,0x2c(%ebx), where member 0x218 is control 1038 (bound at 0x4118f9 'pushl $0x40e'). Packer wire[0x70] <- struct[0x2c] (0x4045c8-0x4045cc).
- **REFUTED IF:** Report byte 0x80 does not track the slider, or the value is not the raw position (e.g. it is signed-offset or scaled).
- **Matters:** It is the only free-numeric non-CPI field, and whether it is signed matters for our validation.

#### 23. `button-records-8x7` — [D]

**Payload offsets 0x37-0x6E (report 0x47-0x7E) are eight 7-byte records, one per input, laid out {u8 type, u8 code, u16, u16, u8}.**

- **Expect:** Factory-default report bytes 0x47..0x7E = 00 01 00 00 00 00 08 | 00 02 00 00 00 00 08 | 00 04 00 00 00 00 08 | 00 10 00 00 00 00 08 | 00 08 00 00 00 00 08 | 09 f1 00 00 00 00 08 | 01 01 00 00 00 00 08 | 01 ff 00 00 00 00 08. Record order is Left, Right, Middle, Forward, Back, DPI-button, Wheel-Up, Wheel-Down.
- **Cite:** cfg107 packer 0x404439-0x4045ba groups struct 0x2e..0x34, 0x36..0x3c, 0x3e..0x44, 0x46..0x4c, 0x4e..0x54, 0x56..0x5c, 0x5e..0x64, 0x66..0x6c onto wire 0x37+7k. Default writer 0x413db0: movb %bl,0x2f(%eax) (bl=1) at 0x413e27; movl $0x200/$0x400/$0x1000/$0x800/$0xf109/$0x101/$0xff01 at 0x413e48/0x413e52/0x413e5c/0x413e66/0x413e74/0x413e82/0x413e90; movb $0x8 to 0x34/0x3c/0x44/0x4c/0x54/0x5c/0x64/0x6c. The button-mask reading (0x01 L, 0x02 R, 0x04 M, 0x10 Forward, 0x08 Back) is corroborated by page 153's harvest 0x406180 writing the +6 byte of records 0..4 from the five 'Left/Right/Middle/Forward/Back Button Multiclick Filter' trackbars.
- **REFUTED IF:** The stride is not 7, or the eight defaults do not match the bytes listed, or the trailing byte of each record is not 0x08 on a factory-fresh device.
- **Matters:** Button remapping is the largest single block of the record; if the stride is wrong our writes would shift every binding by one.

#### 24. `multiclick-and-spdt-byte` — [D]

**The trailing byte of each of the first five button records (report 0x4D, 0x54, 0x5B, 0x62, 0x69) is the per-button Multiclick Filter value from page 153, overwritten by 0xF1 or 0xF0 when that button's 'SPDT:' combo selects 'GX Speed Mode' or 'GX Safe Mode'.**

- **Expect:** Moving the 'Left Button Multiclick Filter' trackbar (control 1066 on page 153) and pressing APPLY changes only report byte 0x4D, to the trackbar's raw position. Setting the left 'SPDT:' combo (control 1062) to 'GX Speed Mode' sets report byte 0x4D = 0xF1; 'GX Safe Mode' sets 0xF0; 'OFF' returns it to the trackbar value. The right button's equivalent is report byte 0x54 driven by controls 1067 and 1063. Middle/Forward/Back (report 0x5B/0x62/0x69) have trackbars only (1033/1035/1037) and no SPDT combo.
- **Cite:** cfg107 page-153 harvest 0x0040618d-0x40629f: CB_GETCURSEL to page153+0x560 gives 0xf1 (index 1) / 0xf0 (index 2) else TBM_GETPOS(0x400) of page153+0xd8, stored movb %al,0x34(%ebx); same shape for 0x5d4/0x14c -> 0x3c(%ebx); then plain TBM_GETPOS of 0x1c0/0x234/0x2a8 -> 0x44/0x4c/0x54(%ebx). Packer: struct 0x34/0x3c/0x44/0x4c/0x54 -> wire 0x3d/0x44/0x4b/0x52/0x59 -> report 0x4d/0x54/0x5b/0x62/0x69.
- **REFUTED IF:** An SPDT selection does not produce 0xF1/0xF0 at the predicted offset, or a Multiclick trackbar moves a byte other than the one predicted.
- **Matters:** It is a field where one control can write either an enumeration or a raw number into the same byte — our data tables must model that or we will validate it wrongly.

#### 25. `ack-checkbox-byte` — [D]

**The long 'I understand that the Multiclick Filter is not a traditional debounce slider...' checkbox (page 153, control 1064) is payload byte 0x72 = report byte 0x82, the last non-zero byte of the record.**

- **Expect:** Report byte 0x82 flips 0x00 <-> 0x01 with that checkbox and is the highest-numbered non-zero byte in the frame.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x4061a7-0x4061c2: BM_GETCHECK to page153+0x6bc then setne -> movb %dl,0x8(%ebx). Packer wire[0x72] <- struct[0x08] via movb 0x8(%ecx),%cl / movb %cl,0x72(%eax) at 0x4045d6-0x4045d9 — the packer's last instruction before retl. APPLY also tests it at 0x413f57 (cmpb $0x1,0x0057f218) to decide a UI action.
- **REFUTED IF:** Report byte 0x82 does not track that checkbox, or a non-zero byte appears above 0x82.
- **Matters:** It pins the upper end of the record, which is the other half of the 115-byte-length claim.

#### 26. `a1-12-read-frame` — [D]

**The settings read is a 64-byte SET_FEATURE 'A1 12' followed 80 ms later by a 1041-byte GET_FEATURE on report 0xA0.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT: 64 bytes, [0]=0xA1, [1]=0x12, [2..63]=0x00. Then Sleep(80 ms). Then GET_REPORT wValue 0x03A0, 1041 bytes; response[0]=0xA0 and response[1]=0x01 for success, and response[0x10..0x82] is the device's 115-byte settings record.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x403b20: memset 0x40 at 0x403b79; movl $0x12a1,-0x50(%ebp) at 0x403b8a; calll 0x403850 at 0x403b91; pushl $0x50 / Sleep at 0x403b96-0x403b9a; memset 0x411 at 0x403bc1; movb $-0x60(=0xA0),-0x468(%ebp) at 0x403bd4; movl $0x411,%esi at 0x403bc9; calll 0x403920 at 0x403bdb; cmpb $0x1,-0x467(%ebp) at 0x403be4; rep movsl 0x100 dwords from -0x458 (=base+0x10) to 0x0057f340 at 0x403bed-0x403c02.
- **REFUTED IF:** The gap is not ~80 ms, or the response report id is not 0xA0, or response[1] != 1, or the response length is not 1041/1040.
- **Matters:** This is stage-1 of CLAUDE.md §4.4 — the one read-only round trip our transport must reproduce byte for byte before anything else happens.

#### 27. `a1-02-version-query` — [D]

**Immediately before the first settings read, the tool issues a 64-byte 'A1 02' query and reads a 64-byte 0xA1 response; the firmware version shown in the title area is bytes 0x11 and 0x12 of that response.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT 64 bytes [0]=0xA1 [1]=0x02 rest zero; Sleep(50 ms); GET_REPORT 64 bytes report 0xA1 with response[1]==1. On this mouse (firmware 1.07) response[0x11] = 0x07 and response[0x12] = 0x01, because the host computes v = (resp[0x12]<<8)|resp[0x11], prints v in HEXADECIMAL, re-parses that text as DECIMAL, divides by 100.0 and formats 'V%.2f' — which yields 'V1.07' only for v = 0x0107.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x4045e0: movl $0x2a1,-0x94(%ebp) at 0x40466c; Sleep(0x32) at 0x40467b; movb $-0x5f(=0xA1),-0x54(%ebp) at 0x4046b0; recv 0x40 at 0x4046b4; copies resp[0x10..0x1d] into the caller's 14-byte struct at 0x4046c3-0x4046e3. Caller OnInitDialog 0x4130bd 'leal 0x28(%esp),%ecx' then 0x4130dc movzbl 0x26(%esp) (=out[2]) shlw $8, or'd with 0x4130ea movzbl 0x31(%esp) (=out[1]); _itoa_s(v, buf, 10, 16) at 0x4130f9 (radix 16 pushed at 0x4130e1), atoi at 0x413103, fdivl 0x558c18 (=100.0) at 0x41311a, format string 0x5589dc = L'V%.2f'. Same code at 0x4138a1-0x4138f6.
- **REFUTED IF:** The A1 02 response does not carry 0x07 at offset 0x11 and 0x01 at offset 0x12 while the tool displays V1.07, or A1 02 is not the first command sent.
- **Matters:** It is the cheapest possible identity check before a flash: we can read the running firmware version over the config channel without entering the bootloader.

#### 28. `startup-command-order` — [D]

**The complete start-up command sequence is exactly: (device open) -> A1 02 -> A1 12 -> 1041-byte GetFeature -> open the second HID collection -> start an 80 ms input-report poll. No other feature report is sent until the user acts.**

- **Expect:** In capture order: SET 0xA1/0x02 (64), GET 0xA1 (64), SET 0xA1/0x12 (64), GET 0xA0 (1041), then only interrupt IN activity. Total four control transfers before idle. On OnInitDialog each is issued once; on WM_DEVICECHANGE each is retried up to 5 times with 100 ms between attempts.
- **Cite:** cfg107 OnInitDialog 0x412fb0: 0x4130b8 calll 0x4035f0 (open/match, PID 0x1978), 0x4130c5 calll 0x4045e0 (A1 02), 0x413152 calll 0x403b20 (A1 12), 0x413160 calll 0x403c40 (unpack into the second record), 0x413191 calll 0x412d00 (hid_open on UsagePage 0xFF02/Usage 0x01 and _beginthreadex of the 0x412c60 poll thread). WM_DEVICECHANGE 0x413600: retry loops at 0x413880-0x413899 and 0x413914-0x413925, both 'cmpl $0x5' with Sleep(0x64).
- **REFUTED IF:** A fifth control transfer appears at start-up, or the order differs, or the tool polls the device periodically over the feature channel.
- **Matters:** It is the whole of the vendor's connect handshake. Ours should be a superset, never a subset, and knowing there is no periodic feature polling means we need not fear racing a background poll.

#### 29. `event-channel-poll` — [D]

**While the tool is running there is a standing 8-byte interrupt IN read on the vendor interface, re-armed every 80 ms.**

- **Expect:** Interrupt IN URBs of length 8 on the vendor interface, present only while the config tool is running, and only carrying data when the user touches the mouse's own DPI button or similar. Report id byte = 0x03 on any delivered report.
- **Cite:** cfg107 poll thread 0x00412c60: movb $0x3,-0xc(%ebp) at 0x412c7e; calll 0x4031c0 (hid_read_timeout, 8 bytes) at 0x412ca8; pushl $0x50 / Sleep at 0x412ce4. Started by 0x412d00 which hid_opens the collection filtered on 0xFF02/0x01 at 0x402eb5-0x402ec0.
- **REFUTED IF:** No interrupt IN activity appears on the vendor interface while the tool runs, or the requested length is not 8.
- **Matters:** Our config tool needs the same channel to notice user-initiated changes, and CLAUDE.md §4.1 requires we re-read before writing precisely because of it.

#### 30. `factory-reset-frame` — [D]

**Factory Reset (main dialog 102, button 1039, at x=368 y=53) sends exactly one 64-byte 'A1 13' with no payload, then waits 1100 ms and reads one 64-byte 0xA1 status report. It sends no A0 11 and no A1 12.**

- **Expect:** SET_REPORT 64 bytes: [0]=0xA1, [1]=0x13, [2..63]=0x00. Then a gap of 1100 ms +/-30 ms. Then GET_REPORT 64 bytes on report 0xA1, whose byte 1 must be 0x01 for the tool to consider it successful. Nothing else on the wire attributable to the button press.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00413f90: cmpw $0x0,0x0057f194 / pushl $0x1978 / calll 0x4035f0 at 0x413f9d-0x413fa2; calll 0x404720 at 0x413faf. 0x404720: memset 0x3f at 0x404782 and memset 0x40 at 0x40478e; movl $0x13a1,-0x50(%ebp) at 0x40479f; calll 0x403850 at 0x4047a6; je 0x4047fa (failure) at 0x4047ad; pushl $0x44c (=1100) / Sleep at 0x4047af-0x4047b4; memset 64 at 0x4047c4; movb $-0x5f(=0xA1) at 0x4047d2; calll 0x403920 at 0x4047d9; cmpb $0x1,-0x8f(%ebp) at 0x4047e2.
- **REFUTED IF:** More than one SET_FEATURE follows the button press, or the command byte is not 0x13, or the 64-byte read comes at some interval other than ~1100 ms, or an A0 11 or A1 12 appears.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §4.1 makes factory reset the first write path we implement and the undo for bad config state. Every byte and every timing of it has to be right before we send anything else.

#### 31. `factory-reset-no-reread` — [D]

**After the reset command the tool does NOT re-read the device; it resets its two host-side copies to compiled-in defaults and disables the APPLY button.**

- **Expect:** No A1 12 / 1041-byte GET_FEATURE after the reset. The APPLY button becomes greyed out. The four tab pages repaint to defaults (CPI 400/800/1600/3200, CPI Levels 4, active stage = CPI 2, LOD '1.0mm', polling rate '8000Hz', Angle Snapping off, Motion Sync off).
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x413fb4-0x413ffc: movl $0x57f210,%eax / calll 0x413db0; movl $0x57f2a0,%eax / calll 0x413db0; four restore calls 0x411bd0 / 0x40ee80 / 0x4062b0 / 0x409290; pushl $0x0 / leal 0x2e9c(%edi),%ecx / calll 0x41b642 (EnableWindow FALSE). Raw E8 scan: 0x403b20 is not called from anywhere in 0x413f90-0x414003.
- **REFUTED IF:** An A1 12 pair appears within a second of the Factory Reset press, or APPLY stays enabled.
- **Matters:** It means the vendor's post-reset UI shows HOST defaults, not the device's actual state. Our implementation must re-read after reset instead — and the capture is the only way to learn whether the two agree.

#### 32. `factory-reset-device-effect` — [G]

**The device's own post-reset record, read on a fresh launch of the tool, will differ from the host defaults in at least the 20-byte passthrough region.**

- **Expect:** Quit and relaunch the tool after the reset. The new A1 12 response's bytes 0x1F..0x32 will differ from what the host would have sent, and possibly bytes 0x10 and 0x17 too. Everything the UI exposes (0x15,0x16,0x18-0x1E,0x33-0x46,0x47-0x7E,0x7F-0x82) should match the default vector above if the device's reset agrees with the host's idea of defaults.
- **Cite:** By construction: 0x413db0 writes no value to struct 0x6e..0x81, so the host cannot know what the device put there. Any agreement or disagreement in the exposed fields is a fact only the device can supply.
- **REFUTED IF:** The relaunched read is byte-identical to the host default vector including the passthrough region — which would mean the device's factory defaults happen to be zero there.
- **Matters:** It is the only way to learn whether the vendor's host-side defaults are the device's real defaults. If they disagree, a host-composed 'restore defaults' would be a silent misconfiguration, which is the argument for CLAUDE.md §4.1's read-modify-write.

#### 33. `send-wrapper-error-retry` — [D]

**If HidD_SetFeature fails with error 0x15, 0x17, 0x1D, 0x57 or 0x65B the transport retries the same frame up to three more times with 50 ms between, giving at most four identical SET_REPORTs.**

- **Expect:** At most 4 SET_REPORT frames with byte-identical data and ~50 ms spacing, per logical command. Any other GetLastError value gives exactly one.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x403850: calll *0x52d1dc at 0x403885; GetLastError at 0x403895; cmpl against 0x15/0x17/0x1d/0x57/0x65b at 0x40389b-0x4038af; counter initialised to 1 at 0x4038b6; loop body 0x4038c0-0x4038ff with Sleep(0x32) at 0x4038ed and cmpl $0x4 at 0x4038fc.
- **REFUTED IF:** More than four identical SET_REPORTs appear for one logical command, or the retry spacing is not 50 ms.
- **Matters:** Distinguishes vendor transport-level retries from vendor command-level retries in the capture, so we do not mistake one for the other when counting frames.

#### 34. `busy-status-03` — [D]

**If the device answers a GET_FEATURE with byte 1 == 0x03 the config tool treats it as busy and re-reads, up to a 1000 ms budget in 100 ms steps — note this is 0x03/1000 ms for the CONFIG tool, versus 0x04/2000 ms for the updater.**

- **Expect:** A busy response produces repeated GET_REPORTs of the same length at ~100 ms and then growing intervals, at most ten, then failure. If the device is never busy, this path produces nothing and the prediction is untested rather than refuted.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x403920: movzbl 0x1(%edi),%eax at 0x40396a; cmpl $0x3 at 0x40396e; Sleep(0x64) at 0x403980; accumulator +0x64 at 0x4039d1; cmpl $0x3e8 at 0x4039d7. Updater counterpart cited in notes/config-protocol.md §1 (fw110 0x40135d cmpb $0x4, 0x40139d cmpl $0x7d0).
- **REFUTED IF:** A repeated GET_REPORT sequence appears whose trigger byte is 0x04 rather than 0x03, or whose budget is 2000 ms.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md-derived design note: the busy byte is a parameter, not a constant. If the device is ever busy in the capture we learn which convention it actually honours.

#### 35. `config-tool-never-flashes` — [D]

**The configuration tool 1.07 will emit no report on the 0xA0 channel other than command 0x11, and nothing resembling the updater's command set, for the whole session.**

- **Expect:** Across the entire capture, the only command bytes seen on the vendor feature channel are 0x02, 0x11, 0x12 and 0x13. Never 0x03, 0x06, 0x07, 0x08, 0x09 or 0x3A.
- **Cite:** cfg107: the only two references to the HidD_SetFeature IAT slot 0x0052d1dc are inside 0x00403850, whose callers recovered from raw E8 edges are exactly 0x403b20 (A1 12), 0x404180 (A0 11), 0x4045e0 (A1 02) and 0x404720 (A1 13). hidapi's own hid_send_feature_report 0x00403320 has zero callers and its address occurs zero times in the whole file (notes/config-protocol.md §7.1a).
- **REFUTED IF:** Any other command byte appears on the wire from this process.
- **Matters:** It bounds the blast radius of running the config tool during the capture: it cannot start a firmware operation, so this experiment is safe to run before the flasher work.
