## What the vendor software looks like on screen

*area key: `ui-surface` — 44 written, 9 rejected*


### Pre-registered (36)

#### 1. `upd-window-one-dialog` — [D]

**The firmware updater's entire UI is one non-resizable-content window with exactly four controls: a progress bar, one push button captioned 'Update Firmware', a static reading 'Status:', and an edit box.**

- **Expect:** Title bar text exactly 'Endgame Gear OP1 8k v2 Firmware Updater'. Controls, with dialog-unit rects: progress bar (36,35) 294x23; button 'Update Firmware' (245,67) 86x19; static 'Status:' (36,21) 24x8; edit (63,17) 266x14. Dialog is 375x100 dialog units, font MS Shell Dlg 8pt, so at 100% scaling the client area is ~562x162 px. No other control of any kind.
- **Cite:** fw110 RT_DIALOG 102 (rsrc RVA 0x1e7f14, 352 bytes, DIALOGEX, style 0x90CE0048, exStyle 0x00040000); dlgdump.py fw110; DDX at fw110 0x00403060 binds 1000->this+0xbc, 1001->this+0x130, 1017->this+0x1a4
- **REFUTED IF:** A screenshot of the updater shows any fifth control (a Cancel button, a file path box, a version label, a checkbox, a logo bitmap), or a different window title, or the button caption is not exactly 'Update Firmware'.
- **Matters:** It is the whole-program check on §11.1's 'there is no second flow'. A fifth control means a code path we have not read, and our flasher's state machine is modelled on there being exactly one.

#### 2. `upd-no-menu-no-about` — [D]

**The updater has no menu bar, no system-menu 'About' item, no context menu, no keyboard accelerators and no tray icon.**

- **Expect:** Right-clicking the title bar shows the stock Windows system menu only (Restore/Move/Size/Minimize/Maximize/Close) with NO appended 'About …' item and no separator above one. Right-clicking the client area shows nothing. Alt+<any letter> does nothing. fw110 contains zero RT_MENU and zero RT_ACCELERATOR resources.
- **Cite:** rsrc.py fw110 (resource type census: 0 RT_MENU, 0 RT_ACCELERATOR); fw110 OnInitDialog 0x004030c0 is 63 bytes and does only two SendMessageW(WM_SETICON) calls — the AppWizard AppendMenu(IDM_ABOUTBOX) block is absent
- **REFUTED IF:** The system menu contains an 'About' entry, or any menu bar / context menu appears anywhere in the updater.
- **Matters:** §1.2a says a shipped capability has a button somewhere. If a menu exists, the 'seven commands, one flow' negative was scoped to the wrong surface.

#### 3. `upd-resizable-no-reflow` — [D]

**The updater window is resizable and minimizable but not maximizable, and resizing does NOT move or stretch any control.**

- **Expect:** Style 0x90CE0048 = WS_POPUP|WS_VISIBLE|WS_CAPTION|WS_SYSMENU|WS_THICKFRAME|WS_MINIMIZEBOX|DS_SETFONT|DS_FIXEDSYS. WS_MAXIMIZEBOX (0x00010000) is clear, so the title bar has a minimize box and a close box but the maximize box is greyed/absent. Dragging the border resizes the frame; the progress bar, button, edit and 'Status:' label stay at their original pixel offsets from the top-left, leaving empty space at the right and bottom. (fw110 ships AFX_DIALOG_LAYOUT 102 as two zero bytes = an empty MFC dynamic layout.)
- **Cite:** fw110 DIALOGEX 102 header, style dword at template offset 12 = 0x90CE0048; rsrc.py fw110 shows resource type AFX_DIALOG_LAYOUT name 102, size 2, sha256 96a296d224f285c6 (two 0x00 bytes)
- **REFUTED IF:** The window cannot be resized at all, or the maximize button is enabled, or the controls reflow/stretch when the window is resized.
- **Matters:** Cheap, zero-risk falsifier of the resource parse. If the template bits we read do not match what Windows draws, every other byte-level reading of the same template is suspect.

#### 4. `upd-status-edit-is-editable` — [D]

**The updater's status field is a real, editable, borderless single-line EDIT — the user can click into it, get a caret, select and type — not a read-only or greyed label.**

- **Expect:** Control 1017 style 0x50010080 = WS_CHILD|WS_VISIBLE|WS_TABSTOP|ES_AUTOHSCROLL. ES_READONLY (0x0800) is clear; WS_BORDER (0x00800000) is clear; extended style is 0, so no WS_EX_CLIENTEDGE sunken border. It renders as a plain white rectangle with black text and no frame, and clicking it places a caret.
- **Cite:** fw110 RT_DIALOG 102, DLGITEMTEMPLATEEX for id 1017: exStyle 0x00000000, style 0x50010080
- **REFUTED IF:** The status field is greyed/read-only, refuses a caret, or is drawn with a sunken 3-D border.
- **Matters:** Same as above — a direct check that the per-control style dwords are being read at the right offsets, which is what the whole resource-derived UI inventory rests on.

#### 5. `upd-idle-state` — [D]

**At launch, before any click, the status box is empty, the progress bar is at 0, and the 'Update Firmware' button is enabled — regardless of whether the mouse is plugged in.**

- **Expect:** Empty edit, progress bar showing 0% (no filled segment), button not greyed. The updater does not enumerate the device at startup: fw110's OnInitDialog only sets the two window icons and returns TRUE. No status text such as 'Device found' or a version number appears until Update is pressed.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x004030c0 (OnInitDialog: two SendMessageW(hwnd, WM_SETICON=0x80, 1/0, hIcon) then mov eax,1; ret); fw110 0x00402f20 ctor stores 0 to +0x25a24 and +0x25a38 at 0x00402ff9/0x00403003 and calls no enumerator
- **REFUTED IF:** The updater shows any status text, a firmware version, or a non-zero progress bar before the button is clicked, or the button starts greyed.
- **Matters:** It fixes when the vendor first touches the device. Our flasher's preflight ordering assumes nothing is sent before the user commits.

#### 6. `upd-click-sequence-app-mode` — [D]

**With the mouse in application mode, the observable sequence after one click of 'Update Firmware' is: button greys, status blanks, progress goes to 0, status becomes 'Mouse firmware current version 1.07', then a silent gap of a few seconds with no status change, then status becomes 'send firmware block data...' and the progress bar jumps to 2%.**

- **Expect:** In order: (1) EnableWindow(button,FALSE) at 0x4033f2; (2) SetWindowText(status, L"") at 0x403423 using the empty string at 0x005443B8; (3) SendMessageW(progress, PBM_SETPOS=0x402, 0, 0) at 0x403450; (4) the resource is loaded (no visible change); (5) the device is found and the status shows the version line; (6) the bootloader-entry thread runs with NO status update at all — expect roughly 2-5 s of dead UI (up to nine Sleep(2*i) ms rounds i=1..9, then a poll loop, then Sleep(1000) twice); (7) status 'send firmware block data...' and progress 2. There is no 'entering bootloader' message.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x004033b0 (0x4033dc busy check, 0x4033f2 disable, 0x403423 blank, 0x403450 PBM_SETPOS 0, 0x403456 load, 0x403499 find, 0x4034e6 version text, 0x4034f4 AfxBeginThread(0x402f00)); 0x00403750 (0x403780-0x4037f3 nine tries, 0x4038fb/0x403902 two Sleep(1000)); 0x00403960 (0x403a15 PBM_SETPOS 2, 0x403a31 status)
- **REFUTED IF:** Any additional status line appears between the version line and 'send firmware block data...', or the progress bar shows a value other than 0 then 2 before the block loop, or the button does not grey on click.
- **Matters:** It pins where the vendor's UI is silent, which is exactly the window in which our flasher must decide it has entered the bootloader. If a message we do not know about appears there, our model of §5.3 is wrong.

#### 7. `upd-version-line-1-07` — [D]

**On this specific mouse (bcdDevice 0x0107), the status line reads exactly 'Mouse firmware current version 1.07' — decimal 1.07, not 2.63.**

- **Expect:** The literal characters: M-o-u-s-e space f-i-r-m-w-a-r-e space c-u-r-r-e-n-t space v-e-r-s-i-o-n space 1 . 0 7. The decode is: HID VersionNumber -> wsprintf L"%x" -> __wtol -> truncate to 16 bits -> divide by 100.0 (double at 0x00544C78 = 100.0) -> "%.2f". A naive integer/100 would print 2.63.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x004011f0 (0x401245-0x401263: mov 0x56a0f4; push $0x5443b0 = L"%x"; call CString::Format 0x4014a0; call __wtol 0x4f7965; movzwl %ax,%edi), caller 0x4034ab-0x4034e6 (fildl, fdivl 0x544c78, push $0x544840); device bcdDevice 0x0107 observed in notes/device-predictions.md
- **REFUTED IF:** The status shows '2.63', or '1.7', or '1.070', or any value other than 1.07 while the mouse is still on firmware 1.07.
- **Matters:** It is the only place the vendor's own code prints its interpretation of bcdDevice. Our tools display a firmware version to the user and must use the same convention, or a user will read a downgrade as an upgrade.

#### 8. `upd-success-text` — [D]

**On success the status becomes 'Update Succeed, current firmware version is V' followed by the NEW firmware version formatted %.2f, and the button is re-enabled before that text appears.**

- **Expect:** Exact prefix 'Update Succeed, current firmware version is V'. The number is the re-enumerated device's bcdDevice put through the same %x/__wtol/÷100 round trip, so if FWFILE 140 in updater 1.10 is firmware 1.10 the line reads 'Update Succeed, current firmware version is V1.10'. Order of operations on this path: re-enumerate at PID 0x1978 -> EnableWindow(button,TRUE) -> read version -> send the 64-byte A1 13 factory-reset frame -> Sleep(900) -> one ignored 0xA1 read -> set the success text.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x403c7c-0x403cb9 (re-enumerate 0x1978), 0x403cc9 (EnableWindow TRUE), 0x403d43-0x403d67 (version), 0x403dcf (movl $0x13a1 = A1 13), 0x403de9 (Sleep 0x384), 0x403e2a (push $0x544a50), 0x403e45 (SetWindowText)
- **REFUTED IF:** The success line names a version other than the one the device reports afterwards in Device Manager / a re-run of the updater, or the wording differs, or the button is still greyed when the success text is showing.
- **Matters:** It is the vendor's own confirmation of which firmware FWFILE 140 actually is — the single fact §8.5a could not close from the binary. It is also the only external check that A1 13 fired.

#### 9. `upd-wedge-after-device-not-found` — [D]

**After a 'Device not found' result, the Update button is left ENABLED but is permanently dead: clicking it again does nothing at all, even after the mouse is plugged back in. Only restarting the updater recovers it.**

- **Expect:** Run the updater with the mouse unplugged, click Update. Status shows 'Device not found' and the button un-greys. Plug the mouse in. Click Update again, and again: no status change, no progress movement, no USB traffic. This is a vendor bug: the not-found path clears the busy flag at 0x40351f and then falls through to 0x403541 which sets it back to 1, and nothing on that path ever clears it again.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x004033b0: 0x4033dc 'cmpl %edi,0x25a38(%esi); jne 0x40356b' (edi=0, so a non-zero flag returns immediately); 0x40351a-0x403539 not-found arm; 0x40353e falls through to 0x403541 'movl $0x1,0x25a38(%esi)'. Exhaustive scan of the whole objdump for 0x25a38 finds writes only at 0x403003(0), 0x40351f(0), 0x403541(1), 0x403855, 0x403883, 0x40393a(0), 0x4039d0(0), 0x403abb(0), 0x403ba5(0), 0x403c56(0), 0x403cd1(0), 0x403e77(0) — every clearing site other than 0x40351f is inside the worker thread.
- **REFUTED IF:** Clicking Update a second time after a 'Device not found' produces any status change, progress movement, or USB traffic.
- **Matters:** It is free to test, it costs nothing, and it is a hard falsifier of our reading of the busy-flag control flow — the same flow that decides whether the vendor can re-enter the flash path after a failure. If we are wrong here we are probably wrong about the retry structure.

#### 10. `upd-two-reenumerations` — [D]

**One successful update causes exactly two USB re-enumerations, and Windows plays its device-disconnect/connect sounds twice.**

- **Expect:** Disconnect+connect #1 shortly after the version line (application 0x1978 -> bootloader 0x1977). Disconnect+connect #2 after the progress bar reaches 100 (bootloader 0x1977 -> application 0x1978). Device Manager shows the mouse vanish and reappear twice. Between them the device is present under VID_3367&PID_1977.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x00403750 (A1 3A at 0x403793 then poll FUN_00401000(0x1977) at 0x403800/0x403831/0x4038d1) and 0x00403960 gate (c) polling FUN_00401000(0x1978) at 0x403c81/0x403c97
- **REFUTED IF:** The device re-enumerates three or more times, or once, or not at all, during a single successful update.
- **Matters:** Our post-erase phase re-opens the device by identity after every failure. If the real device bounces more often than twice, our re-open logic sees transitions we did not plan for.

#### 11. `upd-already-in-bootloader-no-version-line` — [D]

**If the updater is started while the mouse is already at PID 0x1977, the version line never appears — the status goes straight from blank to 'send firmware block data...'.**

- **Expect:** No 'Mouse firmware current version …' text at any point; no re-enumeration before flashing; only one disconnect/connect (the return to 0x1978 at the end).
- **Cite:** fw110 0x004033b0: mode==1 arm at 0x4034a3-0x4034fe prints the version and spawns 0x402f00; mode==2 arm at 0x403500-0x403518 spawns 0x402f10 which calls 0x403960 directly with no version formatting
- **REFUTED IF:** A version line appears when the tool is started with the device already in the bootloader, or the tool refuses to flash such a device.
- **Matters:** This is the recovery case our flasher depends on (working-memory Open gap #6): a device stuck at 0x1977 is still flashable. Confirming the vendor does it with no handshake is what makes the erase boundary survivable.

#### 12. `upd-file-properties` — [D]

**The updater's Windows file properties show CompanyName 'Endgame Gear', FileDescription 'OP1 8k v2 Firmware Updater', FileVersion '1.1.0.0', OriginalFilename 'OP1 8k v2 Firmware Updater.exe'.**

- **Expect:** Exactly those strings, language 040904B0. The config tool 1.07 shows FileDescription 'OP1 8k v2 Configuration Tool', FileVersion '1.0.7.0', OriginalFilename 'OP1 8k v2 Configuration Tool.exe'. Note the updater's FileVersion is 1.1.0.0, not 1.10.0.0.
- **Cite:** VS_VERSION_INFO block in each binary's RT_VERSION resource (fw110 rsrc RVA 0x1e9dc4, 880 bytes; cfg107 rsrc RVA 0x1a0484, 836 bytes)
- **REFUTED IF:** Any of those fields differs in the Details tab of the file's properties.
- **Matters:** It is the identity check a user will actually perform on a downloaded .exe. Our ingest guard hashes .text and the FWFILE set; knowing what the human-readable identity looks like keeps the two consistent.

#### 13. `cfg-main-window` — [D]

**The config tool's main window has exactly eight controls: an 'Info' group box, four static texts (two labels, two values), a tab control, and two push buttons 'Factory Reset' and 'APPLY'.**

- **Expect:** Title exactly 'Endgame Gear OP1 8k v2 Configuration Tool'. Inside: GROUPBOX 'Info' at (13,7) 419x69; static 'Firmware Version :' (22,25); value static 1027 (87,26) 42x12; static 'Software Version :' (22,46); value static 1029 (87,47) 42x12; 'Factory Reset' button (368,53) 54x14; 'APPLY' button (372,410) 50x14 — bottom right; tab control 1060 (12,88) 420x308. Dialog 445x437 dialog units, so ~667x710 px client at 100% scaling. Window style 0x90CE0048 (resizable, minimize box, no maximize box); exStyle 0x00040000 = WS_EX_APPWINDOW (NOT topmost).
- **Cite:** cfg107 RT_DIALOG 102 (rsrc RVA 0x19c8f8, 550 bytes); DDX at cfg107 0x00412f30 binds 1027->this+0x2ccc, 1060->this+0x2d40, 1029->this+0x2db4, 1039->this+0x2e28, 1043->this+0x2e9c; window text set explicitly at 0x00413035 (push $0x558988 = L'Endgame Gear OP1 8k v2 Configuration Tool')
- **REFUTED IF:** The main window shows any control outside this list (a profile selector, a logo, a status bar, a firmware-update button), or the button captions differ, or 'APPLY' is not upper-case.
- **Matters:** 'Factory Reset' and 'APPLY' are stated in the notes to be the entire write surface of the program. A ninth control on the main window would falsify that directly.

#### 14. `cfg-four-tabs-exact-labels` — [D]

**The tab control shows exactly four tabs, drawn as buttons rather than notebook tabs, with captions that include leading and trailing spaces.**

- **Expect:** Left to right: '  Basic  ', '  Advanced Sensor  ', '  Buttons  ', '  Button Mapping  ' — two spaces before and after each word group, which shows as visibly wide padding. Tab style 0x50800300 = WS_CHILD|WS_VISIBLE|WS_BORDER|TCS_BUTTONS|TCS_MULTILINE, so with the shipped manifest (comctl32 v6) they render as themed buttons in a row, not as a notebook. No fifth tab, no 'LED' tab, no scroll arrows.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00413300: four and only four calls to CTabCtrl::InsertItem 0x0041c350 at 0x413399 (index 0, L'  Basic  ' @0x5589e8), 0x4133b1 (1, L'  Advanced Sensor  ' @0x5589fc), 0x4133c9 (2, L'  Buttons  ' @0x558a24), 0x4133e1 (3, L'  Button Mapping  ' @0x558a3c); tab control style from RT_DIALOG 102 item 1060
- **REFUTED IF:** There are three or five tabs, or a tab is captioned 'LED', or the captions have no visible extra padding, or the tabs are drawn as classic notebook tabs.
- **Matters:** The tab count was the constraint that caught the CB_SETCURSEL/TCM_SETCURSEL error in config-protocol.md §10.4. If the count is wrong the correction it forced is unsafe.

#### 15. `cfg-tab-to-page-mapping-corrected` — [D]

**Tab 2 ('  Buttons  ') displays DIALOG 153 — the Slamclick/Multiclick/SPDT page — and tab 3 ('  Button Mapping  ') displays DIALOG 139, the six binding buttons. This is the OPPOSITE of the table in notes/config-protocol.md §12.2, which is wrong.**

- **Expect:** Clicking '  Buttons  ' shows: 'Slamclick Filter' checkbox, five edit+slider pairs labelled 'Left/Right/Middle/Forward/Back Button Multiclick Filter', two 'SPDT:' combos, and the long acknowledgement checkbox 'I understand that the Multiclick Filter is not a traditional debounce slider, lower values don't affect click latency.' Clicking '  Button Mapping  ' shows: 'Left-handed Mode' checkbox, six labels 'Right Button / Middle Button / Forward Button / Back Button / Wheel Up / Wheel Down' and six wide push buttons.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00413c50 (TCN_SELCHANGE): hides +0xc8/+0x1980/+0x1de0/+0x2500/+0x166c, sends TCM_GETCURSEL (0x130B) at 0x413cb3, then jumps through the 4-entry table at 0x00413d20 = {0x413ccb, 0x413ce1, 0x413cf7, 0x413d0a}. Arm 2 (0x413cf7) shows the object saved from 0xc(%ebp) = this+0x1de0; arm 3 (0x413d0a) shows the object saved at -0x4(%ebp) = this+0x2500. Creation in 0x00413300 pairs this+0x1de0 with IDD 0x99=153 (0x413457) and this+0x2500 with IDD 0x8b=139 (0x413483)
- **REFUTED IF:** Tab 2 shows the six binding buttons, or tab 3 shows the Multiclick sliders — i.e. the notes' original mapping is what appears on screen.
- **Matters:** It is a documented error in our own notes found by re-deriving from the jump table rather than from dialog-id order. One screenshot settles it, and it calibrates how much of §12.2 was assumed rather than derived.

#### 16. `cfg-version-fields` — [D]

**Both version fields show 'V' followed by two decimals: Firmware Version 'V1.07' (read from the device) and Software Version 'V1.07' (a compile-time constant).**

- **Expect:** Static 1027 = 'V1.07', static 1029 = 'V1.07', formatted with L'V%.2f' at 0x005589dc. The software value is the float constant at 0x00558C10 = 1.07f. The firmware value is two bytes from the A1 12 settings read, combined as (hi<<8)|lo, printed with %x, re-parsed with _wtoi, divided by the double 100.0 at 0x00558C18 — the same BCD round trip the updater uses. With the mouse unplugged, static 1027 stays empty.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00413035 title, 0x0041335a-0x00413381 (fldl 0x558c10; Format L'V%.2f'; SetWindowText on this+0x2db4 = id 1029), 0x004130dc-0x00413144 (assemble word, _itow base 16 at 0x5087f1, _wtoi at 0x508831, fdivl 0x558c18, SetWindowText on this+0x2ccc = id 1027)
- **REFUTED IF:** Either field shows something other than V1.07 on this mouse (e.g. 'V2.63', '1.07' without the V, or the two fields swapped so that Software shows the device's version).
- **Matters:** It cross-checks the BCD decode from a second, independent code base, and it identifies which of the two look-alike statics is device-sourced — the one our own tool must reproduce.

#### 17. `cfg-apply-starts-disabled` — [D]

**The APPLY button is greyed out when the tool starts, becomes enabled the instant any setting differs from what the device reports, and greys again if the change is reverted. Factory Reset is always enabled.**

- **Expect:** APPLY greyed at launch. Change one control (e.g. tick 'Angle Snapping') -> APPLY enables immediately, with no click needed. Untick it -> APPLY greys again. The comparison is a byte-for-byte diff of 0x73 = 115 bytes between two rendered copies of the settings record.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00413198 'pushl $0; leal 0x2e9c(%edi),%ecx; call 0x41b642' = CWnd::EnableWindow(FALSE) on control 1043 in OnInitDialog; 0x004144c0 builds two 115-byte images from the globals 0x0057f210 and 0x0057f2a0 via 0x004042d0, then the loop 0x414530-0x414553 compares bytes 0..0x72 and calls EnableWindow(TRUE) at 0x414565 on the first difference, EnableWindow(FALSE) at 0x414542 when all 115 match. 0x004144c0 has 55 direct callers across the page handlers.
- **REFUTED IF:** APPLY is enabled at startup, or stays greyed after a setting is changed, or does not grey again when the change is undone.
- **Matters:** It says the vendor treats the settings record as 115 comparable bytes and that the tool holds a device copy and a pending copy — exactly the read-modify-write shape §4.1 requires of us, and it names the length.

#### 18. `cfg-led-page-never-appears` — [D]

**DIALOG 137 — the LED page with 'LED On / Off', an 'LED effect' combo, 'Scroll led', 'Logo led', 'DPI led', Red/Green/Blue edits and 'Apply led settings' — is never created and never shown. The config tool 1.07 exposes NO LED controls anywhere.**

- **Expect:** None of those captions appears on any tab, in any popup, or after any click. In particular the vendor's typo 'Singel color' (the sole DLGINIT item for combo 1038) is never visible on screen. SEARCH SPACE for this negative: (a) the LED page object is a member of the main dialog at this+0x166c, constructed at 0x00412dfa; a grep of the complete objdump -D for a modrm displacement 0x166c against any base register returns exactly three sites — 0x412ae0 (destructor), 0x412def (constructor), 0x413c9e (ShowWindow(SW_HIDE) in the tab-change handler) — and none of them is a Create or DoModal; (b) the IDD 0x89 = 137 appears as an immediate at exactly two sites in the whole file, 0x004056bb (inside the page's own constructor, i.e. m_lpszTemplateName) and 0x00425849 (MFC library); (c) the four page-create sites in 0x00413300 push 0x87, 0x8c, 0x99, 0x8b and never 0x89. WHAT THIS METHOD CANNOT SEE: an address for the object synthesised in a register (e.g. 'mov eax,esi; add eax,0x166c') rather than used as a displacement.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00412d90 main constructor (0x412def 'leal 0x166c(%esi),%ecx; call 0x405690'); 0x00405690 page-137 constructor (0x4056bb 'pushl $0x89'); 0x00413300 page creation (0x4133ff 0x87, 0x41342b 0x8c, 0x413457 0x99, 0x413483 0x8b); 0x00413c9e hide-only; dlgref.py cfg107 DIALOG 137 = 2 immediate sites
- **REFUTED IF:** Any LED control, or the string 'Singel color', is visible anywhere in the config tool.
- **Matters:** The OP1 8k v2 has LEDs and the binary contains a whole LED page. If it is reachable, there is an unread command family for LED control, and 'the command set is exactly four' is wrong.

#### 19. `cfg-hidden-buttons-stay-hidden` — [D]

**'Apply CPI settings' (1059) and 'Surface Calibration' (1061) never become visible on the Basic page, and cannot be reached by any input. 'Surface Calibration' additionally has a do-nothing handler; 'Apply CPI settings' has a real one.**

- **Expect:** Neither caption appears at the bottom of the Basic page — the area around dialog-unit (129,294) and (322,294) stays empty. Tabbing through the page never lands on them (hidden windows are skipped). SEARCH SPACE: both are shipped with WS_VISIBLE clear (style 0x40010000), and a scan of the complete objdump -D of cfg107 for the printed immediates $0x423 (1059) and $0x425 (1061) — covering push imm8/imm32, mov r32/imm32, mov r/m32/imm32 and the ALU imm forms at once — returns ZERO hits, so neither id is ever passed to GetDlgItem, DDX_Control or ShowWindow. They do have message-map entries, which store the id as a plain dword in .rdata: id 1059 -> handler 0x0040c1b0 (a real function), id 1061 -> handler 0x00413d90, which is a single 0xC3 'ret'. Since cfg107 has no RT_ACCELERATOR and no RT_MENU, no keystroke can post their WM_COMMAND either. WHAT THIS METHOD CANNOT SEE: an id computed at run time or read from a table.
- **Cite:** cfg107 RT_DIALOG 135 items 1059 (322,294) 90x18 and 1061 (129,294) 84x15, both style 0x40010000; exhaustive scan of the objdump for $0x423/$0x425 = 0 hits; AFX_MSGMAP_ENTRY at .rdata 0x00557b58 {0x0111,0,1059,1059,57,0x0040c1b0} and 0x00557bb8 {0x0111,0,1061,1061,57,0x00413d90}; 0x00413d90 disassembles to 'c3'
- **REFUTED IF:** Either button is visible on the Basic page in any state, or a click anywhere on the page makes one appear.
- **Matters:** 'Surface Calibration' would be a device command outside the four-command set, and 'Apply CPI settings' has a live handler that never runs. If they can be shown, we have an unexecuted vendor code path that touches the device.

#### 20. `cfg-basic-page-hidden-controls` — [D]

**On the Basic page, the 'Ripple Control' checkbox is present in the resource but hidden by code at page-init, so it is not on screen.**

- **Expect:** The Basic page shows 'Angle Snapping' and 'Disable LED on Lift-Off' and 'X/Y Settings' checkboxes, but NOT 'Ripple Control' — the area at dialog-unit (337,39) 60x10 is empty. (Consistent with its BN_CLICKED handler being the 'ret' stub 0x00413d90.)
- **Cite:** cfg107 page-135 DDX 0x0040baf3 binds id 0x429 = 1065 to this+0xf78; page-135 OnInitDialog 0x0040bbd0 begins 'pushl $0; leal 0xf78(%esi),%ecx; call 0x41b600' at 0x40bbd9-0x40bbe1 = CWnd::ShowWindow(SW_HIDE); message map .rdata 0x00557c60 {0x0111,0,1065,1065,57,0x00413d90}
- **REFUTED IF:** 'Ripple Control' is visible on the Basic page.
- **Matters:** It is a second, independent instance of the vendor hiding a shipped control. Three such cases in one program says the record has fields the current firmware does not honour — which bears on which bytes we must preserve untouched under §1.3.

#### 21. `cfg-advanced-sensor-hidden-controls` — [D]

**On the Advanced Sensor page, 'Motion Jitter Filter' and 'Sensor Glass Mode' are hidden by code and do not appear; 'Motion Sync' and 'Force max Sensor fps' do appear.**

- **Expect:** Visible on that page: 'Motion Sync' checkbox, 'Force max Sensor fps' checkbox, 'Sensor Angle Tuning' label + edit + slider, 'CPI Downshift Tuning' label + combo, 'Smoothing Tuning' label + combo. NOT visible: 'Motion Jitter Filter' (would be at dialog-unit (203,45)) and 'Sensor Glass Mode' (at (203,70)) — the whole right-hand column of checkboxes is empty. The Sensor Angle Tuning slider runs -127..+127 with a page size of 1.
- **Cite:** cfg107 page-140 DDX 0x004118a0 binds 0x406=1030 -> this+0x1a4, 0x407=1031 -> this+0x130, 0x408=1032 -> this+0x300, 0x40e=1038(slider) -> this+0x218; OnInitDialog 0x00411980 at 0x411a19 ShowWindow(this+0x1a4, 0), 0x411a26 ShowWindow(this+0x130, 0), 0x411a33 ShowWindow(this+0x300, 5), 0x411a40-0x411a4c SetRange(-0x7f, 0x7f, FALSE) on this+0x218, 0x411a51 TBM_SETPAGESIZE(0x414)=1. Control 1031's message-map handler is the 'ret' stub 0x00413d90 (.rdata 0x005581f8)
- **REFUTED IF:** Either 'Motion Jitter Filter' or 'Sensor Glass Mode' is visible on the Advanced Sensor page, or the Sensor Angle Tuning slider's range is not symmetric about zero.
- **Matters:** Two more settings the firmware apparently no longer supports. Each is a byte in the 115-byte record that the vendor writes but never lets the user change — exactly the class of byte our read-modify-write must carry through untouched.

#### 22. `cfg-combo-cpi-downshift` — [D]

**The 'CPI Downshift Tuning' combo shows exactly FOUR items — 'Force Off', 'Light Timer Only', 'Medium Timer Only', 'Default' — with 'Default' selected, NOT the three items in its DLGINIT resource.**

- **Expect:** Dropping the combo open shows, top to bottom: Force Off / Light Timer Only / Medium Timer Only / Default. The DLGINIT list for dialog 140 control 1082 in the .rsrc is 'Force Off' / 'Medium' / 'Default' — 'Medium' alone never appears, because OnInitDialog sends CB_RESETCONTENT (0x14B) before adding the four. Initial selection index 3 = 'Default'.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00411980: 0x411995 SendMessageW(this+0x398, 0x14B, 0, 0); 0x4119a7/0x4119bc/0x4119d1/0x4119e6 four CB_ADDSTRING (0x143) of 0x5582a8 'Force Off', 0x5582bc 'Light Timer Only', 0x5582e0 'Medium Timer Only', 0x558304 'Default'; 0x4119ff CB_SETCURSEL (0x14E) index 3. DDX 0x0041193a binds 0x43a=1082 to this+0x378, m_hWnd at +0x398. RT_DLGINIT 140 (rsrc RVA 0x1a0a6c) lists 1082: 'Force Off'/'Medium'/'Default'
- **REFUTED IF:** The dropdown shows three items, or contains the item 'Medium', or the initial selection is not 'Default'.
- **Matters:** It is the cleanest case in the program where the resource and the code disagree, and a screenshot settles it in one second. If the resource wins, our whole method of preferring code over DLGINIT is wrong.

#### 23. `cfg-combo-smoothing` — [D]

**The 'Smoothing Tuning' combo shows exactly THREE items from its DLGINIT — 'Force Off', 'Ripple Control Off', 'Ripple Control On' — with 'Force Off' selected initially. Unlike its neighbour, this one is NOT refilled from code.**

- **Expect:** Dropping it open shows exactly: Force Off / Ripple Control Off / Ripple Control On. Initial selection index 0 = 'Force Off'. No CB_RESETCONTENT and no CB_ADDSTRING is ever sent to it — the only message OnInitDialog sends is CB_SETCURSEL(0).
- **Cite:** cfg107 DDX 0x0041194e-ish binds 0x43c=1084 to this+0x3ec (m_hWnd +0x40c); 0x00411980 at 0x411a07-0x411a17 sends only SendMessageW(this+0x40c, 0x14E, 0, 0). RT_DLGINIT 140 lists 1084: 'Force Off'/'Ripple Control Off'/'Ripple Control On'
- **REFUTED IF:** The Smoothing Tuning dropdown has a number of items other than three, or different wording, or does not start on 'Force Off'.
- **Matters:** It is the control for the same claim as the previous prediction — two adjacent combos, one overridden by code and one not. If both behave the same way, our reading of which one gets CB_RESETCONTENT is wrong.

#### 24. `cfg-combo-polling-rate` — [D]

**The 'Polling Rate' combo has no DLGINIT and is filled from code with exactly seven items in ascending order.**

- **Expect:** Dropping it open shows, top to bottom: 125Hz, 250Hz, 500Hz, 1000Hz, 2000Hz, 4000Hz, 8000Hz — no space before 'Hz', no 'kHz'. Seven items, never six or eight. At page-init the last item (8000Hz) is selected; it is then overwritten by whatever the device reports (event byte 0x06, one-hot, bit 6 = 125 Hz … bit 0 = 8000 Hz).
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x0040bbd0: seven CB_ADDSTRING (0x143) to this+0x12f0 at 0x40bc04/0x40bc19/0x40bc2e/0x40bc43/0x40bc58/0x40bc6d/0x40bc82 of 0x557ea8 '125Hz', 0x557eb4 '250Hz', 0x557ec0 '500Hz', 0x557ecc '1000Hz', 0x557edc '2000Hz', 0x557eec '4000Hz', 0x557efc '8000Hz'; then CB_GETCOUNT (0x146) at 0x40bc9b and CB_SETCURSEL(count-1) at 0x40bcad. DDX 0x0040bb5f binds 0x43d=1085 to this+0x12d0, m_hWnd +0x12f0. Device-side one-hot table in config-protocol.md §10.4
- **REFUTED IF:** The dropdown shows a number of rates other than seven, or a rate not on the list (e.g. 100Hz, 500Hz missing), or the labels are formatted differently.
- **Matters:** The seven-item list is what makes the one-hot event byte a decoded field rather than a guess. An eighth entry means the mask has a meaning we have not assigned.

#### 25. `cfg-combo-lod` — [D]

**The 'LOD' combo does NOT show the twelve items the notes describe. It shows either exactly eleven items 0.7mm..1.7mm in 0.1 steps, or exactly two items '1.0mm' and '2.0mm' — and which one depends on a byte the device reports.**

- **Expect:** Case A (device byte at global 0x0057f23b != 1): eleven items, 0.7mm 0.8mm 0.9mm 1.0mm 1.1mm 1.2mm 1.3mm 1.4mm 1.5mm 1.6mm 1.7mm, and '2.0mm' is NOT among them. Case B (byte == 1): exactly two items, '1.0mm' and '2.0mm'. In both cases the combo is CB_RESETCONTENT'd first, so the eleven-item list built at page-init does not survive alongside. A twelve-item list containing both 1.7mm and 2.0mm cannot occur.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x0040bbd0 fills eleven strings 0x557f0c..0x557f84 into this+0x6e8 and sets index 3; the device-refresh routine 0x0040ee80 (called from OnInitDialog at 0x413176, from WM_DEVICECHANGE at 0x41393a and from Factory Reset at 0x413fd9) tests 'cmpb $0x1,0x57f23b' at 0x40eeb6, sends CB_RESETCONTENT (0x14B) at 0x40eecd, then either adds only 0x557f30 '1.0mm' and 0x557f90 '2.0mm' (0x40eedd, 0x40eef2) or adds the eleven 0x557f0c..0x557f84 (0x40ef32..0x40f011). 0x557f90 '2.0mm' has exactly one reference in the whole .text, at 0x40eef2
- **REFUTED IF:** The LOD dropdown shows twelve items, or shows 1.7mm and 2.0mm together, or shows a count other than 11 or 2.
- **Matters:** It corrects config-protocol.md §12.2's 'twelve' and, more importantly, it is the first case found where a device byte changes the SHAPE of the UI. That means the record has a mode field, and a blind write of the wrong value could put the device into a mode the UI does not describe.

#### 26. `cfg-cpi-levels-combo-and-rows` — [D]

**The 'CPI Levels' combo shows exactly four items '1','2','3','4' from its DLGINIT, and the number of visible CPI rows follows the selection.**

- **Expect:** Dropdown items are the bare digits 1, 2, 3, 4 — no 'levels', no 'stage'. Selecting '1' leaves exactly one radio button visible at the left of the Basic page (the top one, id 1062, checked); selecting '4' shows four. The rows for hidden stages disappear rather than grey out. Initial selection at page-init is index 3 = '4'.
- **Cite:** RT_DLGINIT 135 (rsrc RVA 0x1a0a28) control 1019 = '1','2','3','4'; cfg107 DDX 0x0040b811 binds 0x3fb=1019 to this+0xd0 (m_hWnd +0xf0); 0x0040bbf2 CB_SETCURSEL(3) on this+0xf0; CBN_SELCHANGE handler 0x0040ca70 does CB_GETCURSEL (0x147), adds 1, and calls 0x00410030, whose arm for count==1 at 0x410045-0x41008c does BM_SETCHECK(1) on this+0x13f4 then ShowWindow(SW_SHOW) on this+0x13d4 and ShowWindow(SW_HIDE) on this+0x1448/+0x14bc/+0x1530
- **REFUTED IF:** The CPI Levels dropdown contains anything other than 1/2/3/4, or changing it does not change how many CPI rows are on screen.
- **Matters:** It ties a visible count to the record byte that CBN_SELCHANGE stores at 0x0057f21b, giving a field whose meaning is confirmed by eye rather than inferred.

#### 27. `cfg-hidden-combos-on-139` — [D]

**The six comboboxes on the '  Button Mapping  ' page (ids 1072, 1020, 1021, 1022, 1023, 1024) are never visible; the popup menu replaces them.**

- **Expect:** No dropdown control appears in the right-hand column of that page (dialog-unit x=304, width 97) at any time. All six are shipped with WS_VISIBLE clear (styles 0x40210003 / 0x40210002), and page-139's OnInitDialog contains no ShowWindow call at all. They still carry CBN_SETFOCUS handlers (0x407420, 0x407490, 0x407500, 0x407570, 0x4075e0, 0x407650) which pop the same menu — dead code, since a hidden window cannot take focus.
- **Cite:** cfg107 RT_DIALOG 139 items 1072/1020/1021/1022/1023/1024, all style 0x402100xx with WS_VISIBLE clear; page-139 DDX 0x00407000 binds them to this+0xb8/+0x12c/+0x1a0/+0x214/+0x288/+0x2fc; 0x00407130 makes no ShowWindow call; message map .rdata 0x00557188-0x00557200 shows WM_COMMAND code 7 (CBN_SETFOCUS) for those six ids
- **REFUTED IF:** A dropdown appears anywhere on that page, or the menu is replaced by a combo when a button is clicked.
- **Matters:** Two parallel input mechanisms for the same six fields, one dead. Which one is live decides which handler actually writes the record, and only one of them was traced.

#### 28. `cfg-button-captions-reflect-binding` — [D]

**The six buttons on the '  Button Mapping  ' page do not keep their resource captions — they are rewritten from the device's settings to show the current binding of each physical button.**

- **Expect:** With a default device they read RIGHT CLICK / MIDDLE CLICK / FORWARD / BACK / SCROLL UP / SCROLL DOWN (matching the resource, which is why the default is indistinguishable). After assigning a different action via the popup menu, the button's caption changes to that action's name immediately. A button bound to FIXED CPI shows 'CPI: <n>' (or 'X: <n>, Y: <n>' with X/Y split enabled) rather than 'FIXED CPI'; a button bound to a keyboard key shows a modifier-prefixed key name built from 'Shift+' / 'CTRL+' / 'Win+' / 'Alt+'.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00409290 calls the caption formatter 0x00408e40 six times with edi = 0x40f..0x414 (ids 1039-1044) and ecx = 0x0057f23e / 0x24e / 0x256 / 0x25e / 0x26e / 0x276; 0x00408e40 references the action strings at 0x5572e0..0x55746c and the formats 'CPI: %d' (0x005574b0, referenced at 0x409133) and 'X: %d, Y: %d' (0x005574c0, referenced at 0x409148); modifier prefixes at 0x00556a94/0x00556aa4/0x00556ab0/0x00556abc
- **REFUTED IF:** A button's caption does not change after its binding is changed, or a FIXED CPI binding leaves the caption reading 'FIXED CPI'.
- **Matters:** Each caption is a direct rendering of one settings-record byte at a known global. Reading them off screen while diffing the record is the cheapest way to map those six fields.

#### 29. `cfg-left-handed-flips-first-label` — [D]

**Ticking 'Left-handed Mode' changes the first row's label on that page from 'Right Button' to 'Left Button'.**

- **Expect:** With Left-handed Mode off, the top label reads 'Right Button'; with it on, the same static reads 'Left Button'. No other label text changes; the remaining five stay 'Middle Button', 'Forward Button', 'Back Button', 'Wheel Up', 'Wheel Down'.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00409290: 0x4092b9 SetWindowText(this+0x3a4, 0x557498 = L'Left Button') on the left-handed arm and 0x4092f0 SetWindowText(this+0x3a4, 0x55747c = L'Right Button') otherwise; page-139 DDX 0x0040707d binds 0x434 = 1076 to this+0x3a4
- **REFUTED IF:** The label does not change, or more than one label changes, or the wording differs.
- **Matters:** It is a one-bit setting with an unambiguous visual effect, which makes it the ideal first field to locate by diffing two settings-record captures.

#### 30. `cfg-buttons-tab-contents` — [D]

**The '  Buttons  ' tab (which shows DIALOG 153) starts with 'Slamclick Filter' CHECKED, both 'SPDT:' combos showing 'OFF', and five Multiclick Filter sliders whose range is 0..25.**

- **Expect:** 'Slamclick Filter' checkbox is ticked at page-init. Each 'SPDT:' combo contains exactly three items in this order — OFF, GX Speed Mode, GX Safe Mode — with OFF selected. The five sliders (Left/Right/Middle/Forward/Back Button Multiclick Filter) have minimum 0 and maximum 25, page size 1, tick marks every 8. The acknowledgement checkbox 1064 is present with its full sentence caption.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00405f30 (page-153 OnInitDialog): loop 0x405f78-0x405fab does CSliderCtrl::SetRange(0, 0x19, FALSE) then TBM_SETTICFREQ(0x414, wParam 1) and **TBM_SETPOS(0x405, wParam 1, lParam 8)** on the five sliders [two message names corrected 2026-09-06: 0x414 is WM_USER+20 TBM_SETTICFREQ, not SETPAGESIZE, and 0x405 is WM_USER+5 TBM_SETPOS, not SETTICFREQ. The second one matters -- it is not a tick spacing, it is the DEFAULT VALUE 8, which independently corroborates config-protocol.md 7.3's `0x08` at `+6`] at this+0xb8/+0x12c/+0x1a0/+0x214/+0x288; 0x405fad-0x406000 adds 0x556f70 'OFF', 0x556f78 'GX Speed Mode', 0x556f94 'GX Safe Mode' to both this+0x560 and this+0x5d4; 0x406005/0x406017 CB_SETCURSEL(0) on both; 0x406029 BM_SETCHECK(1) on this+0x648, whose CWnd this+0x628 is bound by DDX 0x00405ee9 to id 0x405 = 1029 'Slamclick Filter'
- **REFUTED IF:** 'Slamclick Filter' starts unticked, or an SPDT combo has a number of items other than three or different wording, or a Multiclick slider's range is not 0..25.
- **Matters:** It converts config-protocol.md §12.2a from a resource reading into an observed one, and 0..25 is a concrete clamp our own writes would have to respect under §4.1.

#### 31. `cfg-only-messagebox` — [D]

**The only message box the config tool's own code can raise says 'RegisterDeviceNotification Failed!', and it will not be seen in normal use.**

- **Expect:** No 'Device not found', 'Please connect your mouse', 'Settings applied' or error popup appears when the mouse is unplugged, when APPLY is pressed with no device, or when Factory Reset is pressed. The tool simply does nothing visible. USER32!MessageBoxW is imported at IAT slot 0x0052d738 and is dereferenced at exactly one site in the whole file, 0x004169a0, inside MFC's own AfxMessageBox; the only vendor call that reaches it is the RegisterDeviceNotification failure path.
- **Cite:** cfg107 IAT 0x0052d738 = USER32!MessageBoxW, one call site 0x004169a0 (grep of the whole objdump); vendor path cfg107 0x00413020 'call 0x403550; test eax,eax; jne' then 0x41302b 'pushl $0x558940' = L'RegisterDeviceNotification Failed!' into 0x0041daaf
- **REFUTED IF:** Any message box, balloon or toast appears from the config tool during normal operation, including when the device is absent.
- **Matters:** If the vendor tool reports failure silently, a user cannot tell a failed write from a successful one — which is a design input for our own tool, and it also means a capture is the only way to see failures.

#### 32. `cfg-startup-with-no-device` — [D]

**With the mouse unplugged, the config tool still opens fully: all four tabs are present and clickable, the Firmware Version field is blank, the Software Version field still reads V1.07, and APPLY stays greyed.**

- **Expect:** No error, no modal dialog, no disabled tabs. Static 1027 empty, static 1029 'V1.07'. Plugging the mouse in while the tool is open causes it to notice via WM_DEVICECHANGE and populate the fields; the handler also explicitly disables APPLY again on that event.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00412fb0 (OnInitDialog): 0x4130ac calls the enumerator with PID 0x1978 and, if it fails (0x4130d0 'jne 0x41314b'), skips the whole version/settings block and still calls 0x00413300 to build the tabs; WM_DEVICECHANGE (0x0219) handler is 0x00413600 per .rdata message map entry at 0x005588c0, and at 0x41383f it calls EnableWindow(this+0x2e9c, FALSE)
- **REFUTED IF:** The tool refuses to start, shows an error, hides its tabs, or leaves the Software Version field blank when no device is attached.
- **Matters:** It tells us the vendor tool never gates its UI on a successful read. Ours must, because §4.1 forbids writing after a failed read — and the difference is worth stating deliberately rather than by accident.

#### 33. `cfg-tab-pages-clip` — [D]

**All four tab pages are child dialogs parented to the tab control itself, positioned inside it, and only one is visible at a time.**

- **Expect:** Switching tabs does not resize the main window; the page content is clipped by the tab control's rectangle at dialog-unit (12,88) 420x308. Pages 135 (418x334 du) and 140/153 (424x316 du) are LARGER than that rectangle, so the bottom and/or right edge of each page is clipped — expect controls near the bottom of the Basic page to be cut off or partially hidden, and the window to be too small to show a page in full at the default size. Enlarging the window does NOT reveal more, because the layout is fixed.
- **Cite:** cfg107 0x00413300: GetDlgItem(0x424 = 1060) at 0x4133ec/0x413415/0x413441/0x413474 supplies the parent for each CDialog::Create; MoveWindow (0x0041b5c0) at 0x4134dc/0x41350a/0x413538/0x413566 places them; ShowWindow(5) only on this+0xc8, ShowWindow(0) on the other three (0x4134e9, 0x413511, 0x41353f, 0x41356d). Page template sizes from RT_DIALOG 135/139/140/153 headers vs the tab control's 420x308 rect in RT_DIALOG 102
- **REFUTED IF:** Every control on the Basic and Advanced Sensor pages is fully visible at the default window size with nothing clipped, or the window resizes when tabs are switched.
- **Matters:** If nothing is clipped, our reading of the page template sizes or of MoveWindow's arguments is wrong, and those same numbers are what tell us which controls exist at all.

#### 34. `cfg-manifest-themed` — [D]

**Both programs render with modern themed common controls, not the Windows-95 flat look.**

- **Expect:** Both binaries ship an RT_MANIFEST (607 bytes, identical sha256 45cb3493020782cf in both), so comctl32 v6 is used: the progress bar is the themed green/blue gradient bar, checkboxes and buttons are themed, and the tab control's TCS_BUTTONS tabs are themed buttons.
- **Cite:** rsrc.py fw110 and cfg107, MANIFEST(24) name 1 lang 1033, size 607, sha256 45cb3493020782cf (same leaf in both files)
- **REFUTED IF:** Either program renders with classic non-themed controls on a normally themed Windows desktop.
- **Matters:** It is a trivially checkable cross-binary identity claim (the same manifest leaf hash in both files), and it confirms the resource walker is reading the right leaves in both.

#### 35. `upd-no-file-dialog` — [D]

**The updater never opens a file-open dialog and never asks the user which firmware to use.**

- **Expect:** Clicking 'Update Firmware' produces no file picker, no dropdown of firmware versions, no 'Browse…' step. The image comes from the resource named 140 of type FWFILE inside the .exe, hardcoded. The binary carries six such resources (133, 135, 137, 140, 142, 143), all exactly 66,560 bytes, and the user is offered no choice among them.
- **Cite:** fw110 0x00403200 FindResourceW(NULL, (LPCWSTR)0x8c, L"FWFILE") — the FWFILE type string has exactly one .text reference and it pushes $0x8c = 140 (updater-protocol.md §4, §8.2); rsrc.py fw110 lists FWFILE 133/135/137/140/142/143 each 66560 bytes; COMDLG32 is imported but GetOpenFileNameW is not reached from the vendor band
- **REFUTED IF:** A file-open dialog, a firmware chooser, or any UI naming a version other than the one being installed appears during an update.
- **Matters:** CLAUDE.md §1.4 makes the image selection a compile-time constant in our code. If the vendor lets a user choose, the rule was written against a false premise.

#### 36. `upd-icon` — [D]

**Both programs show a real Endgame-supplied application icon in the title bar and taskbar, not the generic Windows default.**

- **Expect:** Each binary carries exactly one RT_GROUP_ICON, name 128, language 2052 (Chinese-Simplified), 76 bytes describing five images starting at 16x16 — and the two group-icon leaves are byte-identical between the updater and the config tool (sha256 9595be7d246f12c7 in both). So both programs show the SAME icon.
- **Cite:** rsrc.py fw110 (GROUP_ICON 128, lang 2052, RVA 0x1e9d78, 76 bytes, sha256 9595be7d246f12c7) and rsrc.py cfg107 (GROUP_ICON 128, lang 2052, RVA 0x1a0438, 76 bytes, same sha256); fw110 0x00402fed LoadIconW(hInst, 0x80=128), applied by SendMessageW(WM_SETICON) at 0x4030e1/0x4030f5
- **REFUTED IF:** The two programs show different icons, or either shows the default Windows application icon.
- **Matters:** An identical icon leaf in two separately built binaries is another concrete instance of the shared source tree the notes claim, and it is checkable from two screenshots.

| id | fault | auditor's evidence |
